# zimr cheatsheet

Every public function in zimr, paired with the raylib (or Dear ImGui)
function it corresponds to.  Generated by `scripts/build_cheatsheet.py`
from the source — re-run after any API change.

Each entry shows the zimr signature, the doc comment if there is one,
and `→ raylib: FunctionName  (rationale or detail)`.  When a doc comment
isn't present, that's because the zimr name + signature already say
everything raylib's docs would.  When the raylib equivalent line is
missing, the function is zimr-specific (state plumbing, the wasm-only
`Loader` polling API, browser-only fns like `setWindowIconPng`, etc.).

For the smallest end-to-end demo see `examples/basic.zig`.  For the
user-facing intro and ten quick examples see `README.md`.

---

## The skeleton

Every zimr app boils down to one call.  At module scope, declare a
single `var zimr_app: z.AppBridge = .{};` — the user-owned bridge
the framework reaches via `@import("root").zimr_app` at comptime.
The framework itself has zero mutable globals; `zimr_app` is the
ONE module-scope variable in user code, in plain sight.

```zig
const std = @import("std");
const z = @import("zimr");

const State = struct {
    shapes_texture: z.ShapesTextureState = .{},
    font_cache: z.FontCache = .{},
    scratch: std.heap.ArenaAllocator,
    counter: u32 = 0,
};

var zimr_app: z.AppBridge = .{};

pub fn main(init: std.process.Init) !void {
    try zimr_app.run(init.gpa, .{
        .window = .{ .title = "demo", .width = 800, .height = 450 },
    }, State, initState, update);
}

fn initState(gpa: std.mem.Allocator, _: *z.Frame, s: *State) !void {
    s.* = .{ .scratch = std.heap.ArenaAllocator.init(gpa) };
    try z.loadFontDefault(gpa, &s.font_cache);
}

fn update(f: *z.Frame, state: *State) void {
    _ = state.scratch.reset(.retain_capacity);
    state.counter += 1;
    z.beginDrawing(f.gl);
    z.clearBackground(f.gl, z.colors.slate_950);
    z.drawCircle(f.gl, &state.shapes_texture, 400, 225, 60, z.colors.sky_400);
    const txt = std.fmt.allocPrint(
        state.scratch.allocator(),
        "frame: {d}",
        .{state.counter},
    ) catch return;
    z.drawText(f.gl, &state.font_cache, 2, txt, 12, 12, 20, z.colors.amber_300);
    z.endDrawing(f.gl);
}
```

Zig 0.16's juicy main (`pub fn main(init: std.process.Init) !void`)
hands `init.gpa` (plus `init.arena`, `init.environ_map`, `init.io`)
straight to `zimr_app.run`.  No `pub export fn main`; Zig's
start.zig wrapper exports `_initialize` (wasi-reactor entry) that
JS calls right after instantiation.

`initState` takes `(gpa, *z.Frame, *State)` with the state pointer as
an out-parameter; `update` takes `(*z.Frame, *State)`.  Frame is a
5-field pure-data struct, zero methods:

| Field             | Type                  | Purpose                          |
|-------------------|-----------------------|----------------------------------|
| `f.gl`            | `*GlState`            | rlgl matrix + batch state        |
| `f.input`         | `*InputState`         | keyboard / mouse / touch         |
| `f.window`        | `*const WindowState`  | canvas size, focus               |
| `f.time`          | `*const TimeState`    | `current`, `frame` (dt)          |
| `f.audio_device`  | `*AudioDeviceState`   | AudioContext bridge to JS        |

Everything else (clock, scratch, font cache, shapes texture, RNG,
logger, loader, gestures FSM, skybox cache) lives on the user's
State as owned fields.  All operations are free functions taking
`f.gl` (and `f.window` where viewport math is involved):

```zig
z.beginDrawing(f.gl)             // bracket every frame
z.endDrawing(f.gl)               //   (flushes the rlgl batch)
z.clearBackground(f.gl, color)   // raylib: ClearBackground
z.beginMode2D(f.gl, cam)         // raylib: BeginMode2D / EndMode2D
z.beginMode3D(f.gl, cam)         // raylib: BeginMode3D / EndMode3D
z.beginTextureMode(f.gl, rt)            // raylib: BeginTextureMode
z.endTextureMode(f.gl, f.window)        //   (window for vp restore)
z.beginShaderMode(f.gl, shader)         // raylib: BeginShaderMode
z.beginBlendMode(f.gl, mode)            // raylib: BeginBlendMode
z.beginScissorMode(f.gl, f.window, x, y, w, h)
```

## **zimr.zig** — public surface: `App`, `Frame`, `run`, top-level loaders

#### `init`

```zig
pub fn init(cfg: Config) !*App
```

#### `loadImageFromMemory`

```zig
pub fn loadImageFromMemory(
    gpa: Allocator,
    bytes: []const u8,
) LoadError!Image
```

Decode in-memory PNG bytes into a CPU-side `Image` (RGBA8). Caller owns the returned image's pixel buffer - release with `unloadImage(gpa, image)`.

→ **raylib:** `LoadImageFromMemory` — Load image from memory buffer, fileType refers to extension: i.e. '.png'

#### `loadTextureFromMemory`

```zig
pub fn loadTextureFromMemory(
    gpa: Allocator,
    bytes: []const u8,
) LoadError!Texture
```

Decode an in-memory image and upload directly to a GPU texture. Caller owns the resulting texture - release with `drawing.textures.unloadTexture(tex)` (or the re-exported `z.textures.unloadTexture(tex)`).  The intermediate CPU pixel buffer is freed before returning.

#### `setWindowIcon`

```zig
pub fn setWindowIcon(gpa: Allocator, image: Image) void
```

Set the favicon from an `Image`.  Encodes to PNG via `textures.exportImageToMemory`, then swaps the `<link rel="icon">`. On encode failure (compressed format that can't round-trip through PNG, or OOM during encoding), the call is a silent no-op - favicons are best-effort UX. Reads `image` (CPU pixel data).  Mutates `gpa.*` (transient PNG buffer, freed before return).  Mutates the document's `<link rel="icon">` href.

→ **raylib:** `SetWindowIcon` — Set icon for window (single image, RGBA 32bit)

#### `setWindowIconPng`

```zig
pub fn setWindowIconPng(png_bytes: []const u8) void
```

Set the favicon from raw PNG bytes.  The byte-shape variant avoids a re-encode round-trip if you already have the PNG useful when loading a favicon directly from your asset bundle. Reads `png_bytes` (the PNG payload).  Mutates the document's `<link rel="icon">` href via the `dom.set_window_icon_png` JS bridge.  No allocator involved - JS owns the blob URL.

#### `setWindowIcons`

```zig
pub fn setWindowIcons(
    gpa: Allocator,
    images: []const Image,
) void
```

Set the favicon from multiple Images, picking the largest by area.  Browsers don't support multi-resolution favicons via blob URLs, so we just use the highest-quality one.  Matches raylib's `SetWindowIcons(Image *images, int count)` shape. Reads `images` (slice of CPU pixel data).  Picks the entry with largest `width * height`, then forwards to `setWindowIcon`. Mutates `gpa.*` (transient PNG buffer in the forwarded call).

→ **raylib:** `SetWindowIcons` — Set icon for window (multiple images, RGBA 32bit)

### `zimr.App`

#### `App.create`

```zig
    pub fn create(cfg: Config) !*App
```

Initialise the GL context, set up the canvas, and return an `*App` pointer that lives in `cfg.gpa`.  The pointer is returned to the caller (`AppBridge.run`) which stores it on the user-owned bridge.

#### `App.destroy`

```zig
    pub fn destroy(self: *App) void
```

Tear down everything `create` brought up.  Idempotent.

#### `App.start`

```zig
    pub fn start(self: *App, opts: StartOptions) !void
```

Register a per-frame callback and yield control to the host. On wasm this returns immediately - JS owns the loop and will invoke `zimr_frame` (an export below) on every requestAnimationFrame. Native builds (for testing) loop here themselves.  The JS frame dispatch finds the live app via `@import("root").zimr_app.app.?`, populated on the user-owned bridge by `AppBridge.run` before this method is invoked.  `opts.update` / `opts.state` are recorded on App but redundant — `AppBridge` holds the canonical copies and the dispatch path reads from there.  Kept as fields for the moment; a future turn can clean them up.

### `zimr.AppBridge`

#### `AppBridge.dispatchFrame`

```zig
    pub fn dispatchFrame(self: *AppBridge) void
```

Drive one frame.  Called by the `zimr_frame` wasm export. No-op when the bridge hasn't been initialized via `run` yet — protects against JS firing a RAF tick during the brief window between wasm instantiation and the user's `main` running.  Body delegates to `dispatchFrameOn(app, state, update)` so future host-backend dispatch paths can share the per-frame driver.  AppBridge.dispatchFrame is currently the only caller, but factoring it out keeps the frame-construction logic in one place.

#### `AppBridge.run`

```zig
    pub fn run(
        self: *AppBridge,
        gpa: Allocator,
        cfg: Config,
        comptime State: type,
        comptime init_fn: fn (Allocator, *Frame, *State) anyerror!void,
        comptime update_fn: fn (*Frame, *State) void,
    ) !void
```

Build the runtime, allocate State, call init_fn, hand off to the RAF loop.  The single entry point through which a zimr application starts.  The `gpa` argument is explicit (not inside `cfg`) so that callers using juicy main can write `try zimr_app.run(init.gpa, .{...}, ...)` — the start.zig wrapper hands them an allocator and they pipe it straight through.  Any value in `cfg.gpa` is overridden by this explicit parameter.

## **drawing.zig** — `shapes`, `textures`, `text`, `models`, `shaders`, `colors`, `skybox`

### `drawing.models`

#### `models.checkCollisionBoxSphere`

```zig
    pub fn checkCollisionBoxSphere(
        box: BoundingBox,
        center: Vec,
        radius: f32,
    ) bool
```

AABB-vs-sphere overlap test. Compute the closest point on the box to the sphere center; collision iff that point is within radius.

→ **raylib:** `CheckCollisionBoxSphere` — Check collision between box and sphere

#### `models.checkCollisionBoxes`

```zig
    pub fn checkCollisionBoxes(box1: BoundingBox, box2: BoundingBox) bool
```

AABB-vs-AABB overlap test on three axes.

→ **raylib:** `CheckCollisionBoxes` — Check collision between two bounding boxes

#### `models.checkCollisionSpheres`

```zig
    pub fn checkCollisionSpheres(
        c1: Vec,
        r1: f32,
        c2: Vec,
        r2: f32,
    ) bool
```

Sphere-vs-sphere overlap test. Uses squared distances to avoid sqrt.

→ **raylib:** `CheckCollisionSpheres` — Check collision between two spheres

#### `models.drawBillboard`

```zig
    pub fn drawBillboard(
        gl: anytype,
        camera: Camera3D,
        texture: Texture,
        position: Vec,
        scale: f32,
        tint: Color,
    ) void
```

Draw a 2D texture as a camera-facing quad in 3D world space. The quad's "right" axis is taken from the camera's view matrix so the texture always faces the camera, regardless of camera position.

→ **raylib:** `DrawBillboard` — Draw a billboard texture

#### `models.drawBillboardPro`

```zig
    pub fn drawBillboardPro(
        gl: anytype,
        camera: Camera3D,
        texture: Texture,
        source_in: Rectangle,
        position: Vec,
        up_in: Vec,
        size_in: Vec2,
        origin_in: Vec2,
        rotation_deg: f32,
        tint: Color,
    ) void
```

Full-control billboard: arbitrary up vector (for off-axis or stuck-to-axis billboards), origin offset (for non-centered rotation pivot), and rotation around the camera-facing forward axis.  Underlies `drawBillboard` and `drawBillboardRec`.

→ **raylib:** `DrawBillboardPro` — Draw a billboard texture defined by source and rotation

#### `models.drawBillboardRec`

```zig
    pub fn drawBillboardRec(
        gl: anytype,
        camera: Camera3D,
        texture: Texture,
        source: Rectangle,
        position: Vec,
        size: Vec2,
        tint: Color,
    ) void
```

Draw a sub-region of a texture as a camera-facing quad with arbitrary on-screen size.  `source` is in pixel coordinates inside `texture`; `size` is in world units.

→ **raylib:** `DrawBillboardRec` — Draw a billboard texture defined by source

#### `models.drawBoundingBox`

```zig
    pub fn drawBoundingBox(
        gl: anytype,

```

Draw a wireframe AABB as a 12-edge cube.  Useful for visualizing `getMeshBoundingBox` / `getModelBoundingBox` / the primitive AABB helpers.

→ **raylib:** `DrawBoundingBox` — Draw bounding box (wires)

#### `models.drawCapsule`

```zig
    pub fn drawCapsule(
        gl: anytype,
        startPos: Vec,
        endPos: Vec,
        radius: f32,
        slices_in: i32,
        rings: i32,
        color: Color,
    ) void
```

Draw a filled capsule (two hemispheres + cylinder).  `radius` is the sphere/tube radius.  `slices` is the around-axis subdivision (≥ 3 enforced).  `rings` is the per-cap latitude count.

→ **raylib:** `DrawCapsule` — Draw a capsule with the center of its sphere caps at startPos and endPos

#### `models.drawMesh`

```zig
    pub fn drawMesh(
        gl: anytype,
        mesh: Mesh,
        material: Material,
        transform: Matrix,
    ) void
```

Render a Mesh with a Material at a given world-space transform. Pipeline: bind shader → upload material colors + textures → enable VAO (or per-attribute VBOs as fallback) → set MVP uniform → draw → unbind everything → restore matrix state.

→ **raylib:** `DrawMesh` — Draw a 3d mesh with material and transform

#### `models.drawMeshInstanced`

```zig
    pub fn drawMeshInstanced(
        gl: anytype,
        gpa: Allocator,
        mesh: Mesh,
        material: Material,
        transforms: []const Matrix,
    ) Allocator.Error!void
```

Render the same mesh + material `instances` times, with each instance's per-instance data (typically a model transform) streamed into a per-instance vertex buffer.  Mirrors raylib's `DrawMeshInstanced`. **Shader requirements:** the material's shader must declare an `instanceTransform` mat4 vertex attribute at location `SHADER_LOC_VERTEX_INSTANCETRANSFORM` (29) and use it to compute the per-instance MVP.  The default shader does NOT have this callers using `drawMeshInstanced` must compile their own shader that reads the attribute, otherwise all instances will render at the same (model) transform and look like one mesh.  See `examples/instancing.zig` for a working shader. **Lifetime:** the per-instance VBO is allocated and uploaded here, then freed at the end of the call.  Per-frame allocation is fine for the small instance counts typical of debug demos (hundreds to a few thousand); for stable instance sets, callers can build the VBO themselves once and skip this function.

→ **raylib:** `DrawMeshInstanced` — Draw multiple mesh instances with material and different transforms

#### `models.drawMeshWires`

```zig
    pub fn drawMeshWires(
        gl: anytype,
        mesh: Mesh,
        tint: Color,
    ) void
```

Walk a Mesh's CPU-side vertex + (optional) index arrays and emit every triangle's three edges as line segments.  Uses the rlgl immediate-mode batch - caller must NOT have called rlBegin before this. Skips meshes whose `vertices` pointer is null (GPU-only meshes from streaming-load paths) - there's no CPU data to walk.

#### `models.drawModel`

```zig
    pub fn drawModel(
        gl: anytype,
        model: Model,
        position: Vec,
        scale: f32,
        tint: Color,
    ) void
```

Render a Model - a Mesh + Material bundle - with a uniform scale and tint.  Thin wrapper around `drawModelPro`.

→ **raylib:** `DrawModel` — Draw a model (with texture if set)

#### `models.drawModelPro`

```zig
    pub fn drawModelPro(
        gl: anytype,
        model: Model,
        position: Vec,
        rotation_axis: Vec,
        rotation_angle_deg: f32,
        scale: Vec,
        tint: Color,
    ) void
```

Render a Model with full position + rotation axis/angle + per-axis scale.

#### `models.drawModelWires`

```zig
    pub fn drawModelWires(
        gl: anytype,
        model: Model,
        position: Vec,
        scale: f32,
        tint: Color,
    ) void
```

Render a Model as a wireframe - every triangle's three edges drawn as line segments. On desktop OpenGL, raylib uses `glPolygonMode(GL_LINE)` to rasterize triangles as line loops.  WebGL2 has no equivalent, so this implementation walks each mesh's CPU-side vertex + index buffers and emits explicit line segments via the rlgl immediate- mode batch (`rlBegin(RL_LINES)` / `rlVertex3f`). Cost note: this is O(triangles × 3) vertex submissions per draw, vs. drawModel's one-VAO-bind + one-drawElements call.  Fine for debug visualization, slow for thousands of meshes.  If you need fast wires at scale, build a separate edge-index buffer at upload time - for now this matches raylib's user-facing API and gets the visual right.

→ **raylib:** `DrawModelWires` — Draw a model wires (with texture if set)

#### `models.drawModelWiresPro`

```zig
    pub fn drawModelWiresPro(

```

#### `models.drawSkybox`

```zig
    pub fn drawSkybox(
        gl: anytype,
        cache: *SkyboxCache,
        camera: Camera3D,
        cubemap: Texture,
    ) void
```

Render `cubemap` as the visual background for `camera`.  Call inside a `beginMode3D` / `endMode3D` block, BEFORE drawing the scene if you want correct depth ordering, or AFTER if you've cleared depth between passes. Internally lazy-builds the shader + cube VAO on first call. Subsequent calls are a few bound state changes plus one drawArrays(36).  Cheap enough to call every frame. `gl` is the rlgl state - read for the modelview/projection matrices the skybox shader inherits, mutated through the `rl_models` shader-load + uniform-set path.  `cache` is the long-lived skybox state (compiled program + VAO/VBO ids, uniform locations); the first call mutates it to fill in these handles, subsequent calls only read.

#### `models.exportMeshAsObj`

```zig
    pub fn exportMeshAsObj(
        gpa: Allocator,
        mesh: Mesh,
        name: []const u8,
    ) Allocator.Error![]u8
```

Encode a `Mesh` as Wavefront OBJ text.  Caller owns the returned memory (use `gpa.free(...)`).  Returns `error.OutOfMemory` on allocation failure - previously this swallowed the error and returned an empty slice that was indistinguishable from a successful zero-byte output.

#### `models.genMeshCube`

```zig
    pub fn genMeshCube(
        gpa: Allocator,
        width: f32,
        height: f32,
        length: f32,
    ) Allocator.Error!Mesh
```

Indexed cuboid centered at the origin. 24 vertices (4 per face × 6 faces, faces unshared so each has its own normal/texcoords) and 36 indices (12 triangles × 3). Texcoords map [0,1]² to each face.

→ **raylib:** `GenMeshCube` — Generate cuboid mesh

```zig

        gpa: Allocator,
        radius: f32,

```

#### `models.genMeshHeightmap`

```zig
    pub fn genMeshHeightmap(
        gpa: Allocator,
        heightmap: Image,
        size: Vec,
    ) Allocator.Error!Mesh
```

Generate a terrain mesh from a grayscale heightmap. Each pixel becomes a vertex; pixel brightness scales the +Y height. Triangles are NOT shared between adjacent quads - each quad gets its own 6 vertices so per-face normals stay flat (no smoothing). `size[0]`, `size[2]` give horizontal extent; `size[1]` is the maximum height (when the pixel value averages to 255).

→ **raylib:** `GenMeshHeightmap` — Generate heightmap mesh from image data

```zig
    ) Allocator.Error!Mesh
```

Half-sphere (top hemisphere only, no bottom cap).

→ **raylib:** `GenMeshHemiSphere` — Generate half-sphere mesh (no bottom cap)

#### `models.genMeshKnot`

```zig
        rad_seg: i32,

    ) Allocator.Error!Mesh
```

Trefoil knot mesh.  `radius` is the dominant scale; `size` is the tube radius.  rad_seg = curve sample count, sides = tube sides.

#### `models.genMeshPlane`

```zig

    ) Allocator.Error!Mesh
```

Subdivided plane in the XZ plane. `res_x` × `res_z` give the face-grid resolution (cells), the vertex grid is one larger in each dimension. Vertices are shared between adjacent quads (indexed mesh).

→ **raylib:** `GenMeshPlane` — Generate plane mesh (with subdivisions)

#### `models.genMeshPoly`

```zig
    pub fn genMeshPoly(
        gpa: Allocator,
        sides: i32,
        radius: f32,
    ) Allocator.Error!Mesh
```

Convex polygon ("fan" of triangles meeting at center). The mesh is in the XZ plane with normals pointing +Y, so it can be drawn as a floor disc. `sides < 3` returns an empty mesh.

→ **raylib:** `GenMeshPoly` — Generate polygonal mesh

#### `models.genMeshSphere`

```zig
    pub fn genMeshSphere(
        gpa: Allocator,
        radius: f32,
        rings: i32,
        slices: i32,
    ) Allocator.Error!Mesh
```

Sphere centered at origin.  `rings` is the latitude count (poles excluded), `slices` is the longitude count.  Each ring × slice quad emits 2 triangles = 6 vertices (unshared).  Min 3 / 3.

→ **raylib:** `GenMeshSphere` — Generate sphere mesh (standard sphere)

#### `models.genMeshTangents`

```zig
    pub fn genMeshTangents(
        gpa: Allocator,
        mesh: *Mesh,
    ) Allocator.Error!void
```

Compute per-vertex tangent vectors from `mesh.vertices`, `mesh.normals`, `mesh.texcoords`.  Required input for normal- mapped shaders that read a tangent-space normal map and reconstruct the world-space normal. Allocates `mesh.tangents` as a flat array of `vertexCount × 4` floats - three components for the tangent direction plus a fourth "handedness" value (`+1.0` or `-1.0`) that disambiguates the bitangent's sign.  If `mesh.tangents` is already populated it's freed first to avoid a leak. Algorithm: accumulate per-triangle (sdir, tdir) contributions into temporary `tan1` / `tan2` arrays, then for each vertex run Gram-Schmidt orthogonalization against the normal.  Skips gracefully if `vertices`, `normals`, or `texcoords` are null, returning without modification. Note: this CPU-side computation only.  Uploading the new tangents to the GPU as an additional vertex attribute is the caller's job - re-call `uploadMesh` after if the mesh is being rebuilt for use with a normal-mapped shader.  Future work will add a paired `rlUpdateVertexBuffer` path to update an existing VAO in place (mirrors raylib's tail-end logic).

→ **raylib:** `GenMeshTangents` — Compute mesh tangents

```zig
    ) Allocator.Error!Mesh
```

Torus: major radius `radius`, tube thickness `size`.  `rad_seg` is the segment count along the major circle, `sides` along the tube.

#### `models.isModelValid`

```zig
    pub fn isModelValid(model: Model) bool
```

Validate a Model: needs meshes, materials, mesh→material map, and every CPU-side vertex array must have a matching uploaded VBO. Returns false if any vertex array exists but its vboId is 0 (= "data on CPU but never uploaded to GPU").

→ **raylib:** `IsModelValid` — Check if a model is valid (loaded in GPU, VAO/VBOs)

#### `models.loadImageColors`

```zig
    pub fn loadImageColors(
        gpa: Allocator,
        image: Image,
    ) Allocator.Error![]Color
```

Convert any uncompressed Image to a flat RGBA Color slice, allocating fresh memory with `gpa`.  Free with `gpa.free(slice)`. Returns an empty slice for empty images or compressed pixel formats.  Supported formats: GRAYSCALE, GRAY_ALPHA, R5G5B5A1, R5G6B5, R4G4B4A4, R8G8B8, R8G8B8A8.  16-bit and 32-bit float formats are not supported.

#### `models.loadMaterialDefault`

```zig
    pub fn loadMaterialDefault(
        gl: anytype,
        gpa: Allocator,
    ) Allocator.Error!Material
```

Construct a default Material referencing rlgl's built-in shader and 1×1 white texture.  The `maps` array is heap-allocated; free with `unloadMaterial(gpa, mat)` using the same allocator.

→ **raylib:** `LoadMaterialDefault` — Load default material (Supports: DIFFUSE, SPECULAR, NORMAL maps)

#### `models.loadModelAnimations`

```zig
    pub fn loadModelAnimations(
        gpa: Allocator,
        bytes: []const u8,
    ) LoadError![]ModelAnimation
```

Free an array of ModelAnimations and the per-animation per-keyframe pose arrays they own.  Same Phase-D-and-zgltf-adoption note as Decode glTF animations into a heap-allocated `[]ModelAnimation`. Each glTF `animation` becomes one `ModelAnimation` whose keyframes are the union of its samplers' input timestamps, taken from the first sampler as the canonical set.  At each keyframe, each channel's `target_path` (translation/rotation/ scale) is sampled with linear interpolation and written into the destination bone's slot. `boneCount` is computed as `max(channel.target_node) + 1` for the animation, which means each glTF node referenced as an animation target gets one Transform slot in every keyframe. This matches raylib's mental model where bones and nodes are the same thing for animation purposes. Caller MUST free with `unloadModelAnimations(gpa, slice)` using the same allocator.

#### `models.loadModelFromMemory`

```zig
    pub fn loadModelFromMemory(
        gl: anytype,
        gpa: Allocator,
        bytes: []const u8,
    ) LoadError!Model
```

#### `models.loadModelFromMesh`

```zig
    pub fn loadModelFromMesh(
        gl: anytype,
        gpa: Allocator,
        mesh: Mesh,
    ) Allocator.Error!Model
```

Build a Model that bundles a single Mesh with a default Material. The Mesh is moved by value into a one-slot heap array; the Model owns the meshes/materials/meshMaterial arrays it allocates here. Free with `unloadModel(gpa, model)` using the same allocator.

→ **raylib:** `LoadModelFromMesh` — Load model from generated mesh (default material)

#### `models.prepareMeshFor`

```zig
    pub fn prepareMeshFor(
        comptime VsIface: type,
        gpa: Allocator,
        mesh: *Mesh,
    ) Allocator.Error!void
```

Parse a glTF (.gltf or .glb) byte stream and return a `Model`. Auto-detects the format via magic bytes.  Each glTF primitive becomes one mesh in the model.  Per-primitive materials are honoured via the glTF `material` index - `materialsFromGltf` extracts PBR colour + diffuse texture (PNG decode + GPU upload). On success, the model owns its meshes (CPU-side data + GPU resources after upload).  Free with `unloadModel(gpa, model)`. Host build returns `LoadError.GltfParseFailed` (no GPU upload possible without a GL context). Walk the vertex schema's `Attributes` and auto-fulfill any attribute that the mesh is missing but the shader will reference.  Conventional field names drive the dispatch:  `vertex_tangent` → call `genMeshTangents` if normals + UVs are present and tangents are absent. (other names)   → no-op — caller's responsibility to provide them via the source data.  Conceptually this is the schema-driven generalization of the hardcoded auto-tangent block that used to live inline in `loadModelFromMemory`.  Adding a new auto-fulfill kind (e.g. face-normal computation for meshes lacking `vertex_normal`) is one new branch here, ~10 lines.  See Phase 5 in `src/notes/typesafe_zig_shaders.md`.  Notes on `vertex_color`: drawMesh's per-draw fallback at `web_gl.vertexAttrib4fv(COLOR_LOC, white)` already handles meshes without a COLOR_0 attribute by binding white as the VAO's default — so we don't synthesize a per-vertex white color buffer here (would just waste VRAM upload time for data the engine already substitutes at draw time).


#### `models.unloadMesh`

```zig
    pub fn unloadMesh(
        gpa: Allocator,
        mesh: Mesh,
    ) void
```

Free GPU + CPU memory for a Mesh. Safe to call even if some buffers are null (raylib's malloc/free pattern allows that).

→ **raylib:** `UnloadMesh` — Unload mesh data from CPU and GPU

#### `models.unloadModel`

```zig
    pub fn unloadModel(
        gpa: Allocator,
        model: Model,
    ) void
```

Free a Model - recursively free meshes, free each material's maps (but not the shader/textures: caller owns those if shared), then free the top-level arrays and skeleton data. Per raylib's contract: shaders and textures are NOT unloaded because the caller may share them across models. The meshes / materials / meshMaterial / per-material maps arrays are gpa-allocated (by `loadModelFromMesh` / `loadMaterialDefault`). The skeleton arrays are libc-allocated by the underlying model loaders.

→ **raylib:** `UnloadModel` — Unload model (including meshes) from memory (RAM and/or VRAM)

#### `models.unloadModelAnimations`

```zig
    pub fn unloadModelAnimations(
        gpa: Allocator,
        animations: []ModelAnimation,
    ) void
```

Free a model-animation slice and all its owned keyframe poses. Pass the same allocator used for `loadModelAnimations`.

→ **raylib:** `UnloadModelAnimations` — Unload animation array data

#### `models.unloadSkybox`

```zig
    pub fn unloadSkybox(cache: *SkyboxCache) void
```

Release the skybox cache's GL resources.  Optional - most apps can let the GL context teardown handle it.  Call this if you're rotating cubemaps a lot and want deterministic cleanup. Only `cache` is touched - no rlgl state needed because the `rlUnload*` fns talk directly to GL handles.

#### `models.updateMeshBuffer`

```zig
    pub fn updateMeshBuffer(
        mesh: Mesh,
        buffer_index: usize,
        data: []const u8,
        offset: usize,
    ) void
```

Re-upload a region of an existing mesh's per-attribute VBO. `buffer_index` is one of the `RL_DEFAULT_SHADER_ATTRIB_LOCATION_*` slots (vertex / texcoord / normal / colour / etc.) - same indexing as `mesh.vboId[i]`.  `data` is the raw bytes to upload starting at `offset` bytes into the VBO. Silent no-op when the mesh hasn't been uploaded yet, the buffer slot is empty (slot doesn't exist for this mesh's attribute set), or `buffer_index` is out of range.  The caller is responsible for ensuring the VBO was created with `dynamic = true` (the default `uploadMesh(gpa, mesh, false)` makes them STATIC and updates will silently no-op on the GPU).

→ **raylib:** `UpdateMeshBuffer` — Update mesh vertex data in GPU for a specific buffer index

#### `models.updateModelAnimation`

```zig
    pub fn updateModelAnimation(
        gl: anytype,
        gpa: Allocator,
        model: *Model,
        anim: ModelAnimation,
        frame: i32,
    ) Allocator.Error!void
```

Apply a single keyframe of `anim` to `model`.  `frame` is a 0-indexed keyframe number; values outside `[0, anim.keyframeCount)` wrap modulo (so callers can drive it from a frame counter without bounds-checking). Pipeline: 1. Look up keyframe pose at index `frame % keyframeCount` 2. Compute Matrix from each Transform in the pose 3. Allocate `boneMatrices: []Matrix` if not already on the model 4. Upload to skinned shader's `boneMatrices[]` uniform via rlSetUniformMatrices 5. Swap each material's shader to the skinned variant On host this is mostly a no-op (rlgl forwarders return null/-1) but the path is exercised end-to-end so smoke tests catch regressions.

→ **raylib:** `UpdateModelAnimation` — Update model animation pose (vertex buffers and bone matrices)

#### `models.updateModelAnimationBlend`

```zig
    pub fn updateModelAnimationBlend(
        gl: anytype,
        gpa: Allocator,
        model: *Model,
        anim_a: ModelAnimation,
        frame_a: i32,
        anim_b: ModelAnimation,
        frame_b: i32,
        blend: f32,
    ) Allocator.Error!void
```

Cross-fade two animations at given keyframes.  `blend ∈ [0, 1]`: 0 = entirely animA at frameA, 1 = entirely animB at frameB. Per-bone: - translation: linear lerp - rotation: quaternion slerp (proper spherical interpolation, not lerp+normalize - preserves angular velocity for slow blends) - scale: linear lerp `boneCount` is taken from `animA` - the two animations should agree on bone topology (same skeleton).  If they don't, bones past `min(animA.boneCount, animB.boneCount)` get only the animA contribution.

#### `models.uploadMesh`

```zig
    pub fn uploadMesh(
        gpa: Allocator,
        mesh: *Mesh,
        dynamic: bool,
    ) Allocator.Error!void
```

Upload a CPU-side Mesh to the GPU.  Allocates a VAO + per-attribute VBOs, sets up vertex attribute pointers for the default shader's attribute layout, and stores the GL ids back into `mesh.vaoId` / `mesh.vboId[]`.  After this call the mesh can be rendered with drawMesh. `dynamic = true` enables `glBufferSubData` updates via `updateMeshBuffer`.  Set `false` for static meshes (most cases). Calling on an already-uploaded mesh (vaoId != 0) is a no-op with a warning - matches raylib's safety check.

→ **raylib:** `UploadMesh` — Upload mesh vertex data in GPU and provide VAO/VBO ids

### `drawing.shaders`

#### `shaders.beginBlendMode`

```zig
        gl: *GlState,

    ) void
```

#### `shaders.beginScissorMode`

```zig
    pub fn beginScissorMode(
        gl: anytype,
        window: *const @import("zig").WindowState,
        x: i32,
        y: i32,
        width: i32,
        height: i32,
    ) void
```

Begin scissor mode - restrict subsequent drawing to the rectangle (x, y, width, height) in screen coordinates.  Note that raylib defines the scissor rect with its origin at the upper-left, so we flip Y here to match GL's bottom-left expectation. `gl` is the rlgl state (the active batch is flushed and a scissor command is queued onto it).  `window` is read-only the y-flip needs `getRenderHeight` to map upper-left coords to GL's lower-left convention.

→ **raylib:** `BeginScissorMode` — Begin scissor mode (define screen area for following drawing)


→ **raylib:** `IsShaderValid` — Check if a shader is valid (loaded on GPU)

#### `shaders.loadShaderFromMemory`

```zig
    pub fn loadShaderFromMemory(
        gl: *GlState,
        gpa: Allocator,
        vs_code: []const u8,
        fs_code: []const u8,
    ) LoadShaderError!Shader
```

Load a shader from in-memory GLSL source.  Either source string can be empty: an empty `vs_code` reuses raylib's default vertex shader (handy when only the fragment stage is custom), and an empty `fs_code` likewise reuses the default fragment shader for vertex-only effects. On success returns a `Shader` whose `id` is the GL program and whose `locs` is a freshly-allocated, fully-populated location table (one slot per `SHADER_LOC_*` enum value).  Free with `unloadShader(gpa, shader)` using the same gpa. Returns `error.CompileFailed` when the GLSL didn't compile/link; `error.OutOfMemory` if the location table allocation fails.

→ **raylib:** `LoadShaderFromMemory` — Load shader from code strings and bind default locations

### `drawing.shapes`

#### `shapes.checkCollisionCircleLine`

```zig
    pub fn checkCollisionCircleLine(
        center: Vec2,
        radius: f32,
        p1: Vec2,
        p2: Vec2,
    ) bool
```

Test if a circle overlaps a line segment.

→ **raylib:** `CheckCollisionCircleLine` — Check if circle collides with a line created between two points [p1] and [p2]

#### `shapes.checkCollisionCircleRec`

```zig
    pub fn checkCollisionCircleRec(
        center: Vec2,
        radius: f32,
        rec: Rectangle,
    ) bool
```

Test if a circle overlaps a rectangle. Clamps the circle center to the rectangle's nearest edge and checks the squared distance.

→ **raylib:** `CheckCollisionCircleRec` — Check collision between circle and rectangle

#### `shapes.checkCollisionCircles`

```zig
    pub fn checkCollisionCircles(
        center1: Vec2,
        radius1: f32,
        center2: Vec2,
        radius2: f32,
    ) bool
```

Test if two circles overlap.

→ **raylib:** `CheckCollisionCircles` — Check collision between two circles

#### `shapes.checkCollisionLines`

```zig
    pub fn checkCollisionLines(
        startPos1: Vec2,
        endPos1: Vec2,
        startPos2: Vec2,
        endPos2: Vec2,
    ) ?Vec2
```

Test if two line segments intersect. If `collisionPoint` is non-null and the segments do intersect, the intersection point is written through it. Uses the parametric form: each line is parameterized as `start + t*r`, solve for t and u (parameters along line 1 and line 2 respectively), and require both to lie in [0, 1] for the intersection to fall within both segments. Same algorithm as raylib's C version. Find the intersection point of two line segments, or `null` if they don't intersect.  Replaces raylib's `(bool, *Vec2)` shape with a single typed-optional return - the caller naturally gets the point from `if (checkCollisionLines(...)) |hit| { ... }`.

→ **raylib:** `CheckCollisionLines` — Check the collision between two lines defined by two points each, returns collision point by reference

#### `shapes.checkCollisionPointCircle`

```zig
    pub fn checkCollisionPointCircle(
        point: Vec2,
        center: Vec2,
        radius: f32,
    ) bool
```

Test if a point lies inside a circle.

→ **raylib:** `CheckCollisionPointCircle` — Check if point is inside circle

#### `shapes.checkCollisionPointLine`

```zig
    pub fn checkCollisionPointLine(
        point: Vec2,
        p1: Vec2,
        p2: Vec2,
        threshold: i32,
    ) bool
```

Test if a point lies on a line within `threshold` pixels (Euclidean).

→ **raylib:** `CheckCollisionPointLine` — Check if point belongs to line created between two points [p1] and [p2] with defined margin in pixels [threshold]

#### `shapes.checkCollisionPointPoly`

```zig
    pub fn checkCollisionPointPoly(
        point: Vec2,
        points: []const Vec2,
    ) bool
```

Test if a point lies inside a polygon. Uses the Jordan-curve ray-cast algorithm: count how many polygon edges the half-line going right from `point` crosses; odd → inside, even → outside.

→ **raylib:** `CheckCollisionPointPoly` — Check if point is within a polygon described by array of vertices

#### `shapes.checkCollisionPointRec`

```zig
    pub fn checkCollisionPointRec(point: Vec2, rec: Rectangle) bool
```

Test if a point lies inside a rectangle (inclusive on top/left, exclusive on bottom/right per the original - we use `<=` for parity with raylib).

→ **raylib:** `CheckCollisionPointRec` — Check if point is inside rectangle

#### `shapes.checkCollisionPointTriangle`

```zig

        p1: Vec2,
        p2: Vec2,
        p3: Vec2,
    ) bool
```

Test if a point lies inside (or on the edge of) a triangle. Uses the barycentric-coordinate sign test - robust for any triangle winding.

→ **raylib:** `CheckCollisionPointTriangle` — Check if point is inside a triangle

#### `shapes.drawTriangleGradient`

```zig
    pub fn drawTriangleGradient(
        gl: anytype,
        v1: Vec2,
        v2: Vec2,
        v3: Vec2,
        c1: Color,
        c2: Color,
        c3: Color,
    ) void
```

Draw a single triangle whose three vertices each carry their own colour, with linear interpolation across the face by the GPU.  The CPU-side equivalent (writes into an `Image`) is `imageDrawTriangleGradient` in the textures namespace. Goes through the rlgl batch path - three `rlColor4ub`/`rlVertex2f` pairs inside a single `rlBegin(RL_TRIANGLES)` scope.  No batch flush.  Degenerate (collinear) input is silently accepted; the GPU rasterises zero pixels.

→ **raylib:** `DrawTriangleGradient` — Draw triangle with interpolated colors (vertex in counter-clockwise order!)

#### `shapes.drawTriangleLines`

```zig
    pub fn drawTriangleLines(
        gl: anytype,
        v1: Vec2,
        v2: Vec2,
        v3: Vec2,
        color: Color,
    ) void
```

Draw a triangle outline using GL lines.

→ **raylib:** `DrawTriangleLines` — Draw triangle outline (vertex in counter-clockwise order!)

#### `shapes.drawTriangleStrip`

```zig
    pub fn drawTriangleStrip(
        gl: anytype,
        shapes_state: *const ShapesTextureState,
        points: []const Vec2,
        color: Color,
    ) void
```

Draw a triangle ribbon (sequence of pairs of vertices on opposite edges) as a strip of quads.  Used by drawLineThick + drawSpline* + drawLineBezier — all of which produce ribbon-style strips of 4+ vertices where pairs `(2k, 2k+1)` are the two ends of one cross-section.  Why RL_QUADS, not RL_TRIANGLES: WebGL2's textured batch path in rlgl only samples the bound texture for RL_QUADS draw calls. RL_TRIANGLES emits geometry but the shader path either doesn't bind texture0 properly or doesn't honor UV interpolation, so fragments render with zero (invisible) alpha.  raylib's `rshapes.c` header comment is explicit: "Use QUADS instead of TRIANGLES for drawing when possible," and issue #4347 spells out "Texturing is only supported on RL_QUADS."  zimr inherits the same WebGL2 batch shader so the same constraint applies.  Ribbon → quads mapping: vertices `[i, i+1]` form one cross- section, `[i+2, i+3]` form the next, and together they make one quad in order `(i, i+1, i+3, i+2)` to keep consistent winding. The loop strides by 2 since each iteration advances by one cross-section.  Input with fewer than 4 vertices is a no-op (one cross-section alone doesn't form a quad).

→ **raylib:** `DrawTriangleStrip` — Draw a triangle strip defined by points

#### `text.bakeFontAtlas`

```zig
    pub fn bakeFontAtlas(
        gpa: Allocator,
        font: *const Font,
        font_size: i32,
        codepoints: []const u21,
        padding: i32,
    ) !FontAtlas
```

Rasterize `codepoints` into a packed atlas at `font_size` pixel height.  Returns the atlas image + parallel glyph-metric and atlas-rect arrays, all gpa-allocated. `padding` is added between glyphs in the atlas so adjacent slots don't bleed into each other under bilinear sampling.  Typical values: 0 for nearest sampling, 1-2 for bilinear.

No-op when the cache is unloaded (texture id 0). Reads: `font_cache.font` (the loaded default `Font`), `line_spacing` (forwarded to `drawWithFont`). Mutates: `gl.*` (rlgl batch via `drawWithFont`).

```zig
    pub fn drawCodepoints(
        gl: anytype,
        line_spacing: i32,
        font: Font,
        codepoints: []const u21,
        position: Vec2,
        font_size: f32,
        spacing: f32,
        tint: Color,
    ) void
```

Draw a sequence of pre-decoded codepoints.  Same layout rules as `drawWithFont` - '\n' starts a new line, advancing y by `font_size + line_spacing`. **Caller must pass a loaded `Font`** - no fallback to default. Reads: `line_spacing` (px gap between lines, only consulted when codepoints contain `'\n'`). Mutates: `gl.*` (rlgl batch - one quad per renderable codepoint).

```zig
        pos_y: i32,
    ) void
```

Draw the current FPS at (`pos_x`, `pos_y`).  Color codes: ≥ 30 fps  →  lime    (good) 15-29 fps →  orange  (warning) < 15 fps  →  red     (low) Uses 20-pixel default font. Reads `fps.average` (rolling FPS via `core.getFPS`), `font_cache.font` (default `Font` for the digit glyphs). Mutates `gl.*` (rlgl batch - one quad per digit + " FPS" suffix).

→ **raylib:** `DrawFPS` — Draw current FPS

Draw `s` rotated `rotation` degrees around `origin` (relative to `position`). **Caller must pass a loaded `Font`** - no fallback to default. Reads: `line_spacing` (forwarded to drawWithFont). Mutates: `gl.*` - matrix stack push/translate/rotate is balanced (caller's transform is restored at return); the rlgl batch is appended via `drawWithFont`.

```zig
        spacing: f32,
```

Draw a slice of decoded codepoints with `font` at `position`, honouring `\n` (newline advances by `fontSize + line_spacing`). Pure forwarder over `drawCodepoint` - useful when the caller already has a decoded codepoint buffer (e.g. shaping output) and wants to skip the UTF-8 redecoding `drawWithFont` would do. Reads: `line_spacing` (vertical gap between wrapped lines). Writes: implicit GL draw state via `drawCodepoint`.

Reads: `line_spacing` (px gap between lines, only consulted when the string contains '\n'). Mutates: `gl.*` (rlgl batch - one quad per renderable codepoint).

```zig
        position: Vec2,
        fontSize: f32,
        spacing: f32,
        tint: Color,
    ) void
```

CPU-side equivalent of `drawTextEx`. **Limitation:** only honors the font's *base* size during rasterization - user-requested `fontSize` smaller than `font.baseSize` gets clamped up. #### `text.loadCodepoints`

```zig
    pub fn loadCodepoints(
        gpa: Allocator,
        s: []const u8,
    ) Allocator.Error![]i32
```

Decode a UTF-8 string into a heap-allocated array of codepoints. The returned slice carries its length; free with `gpa.free(slice)`. Empty input returns an empty slice (not an error). Invalid UTF-8 sequences yield a `'?'` (0x3F) replacement codepoint, matching `nextCodepoint`'s lenient behaviour and what raylib does at the public boundary.

#### `text.loadFontFromMemory`

```zig
    pub fn loadFontFromMemory(
        gpa: Allocator,
        ttf_bytes: []const u8,
        font_size: i32,
        codepoints: []const u21,
        padding: i32,
    ) LoadFontError!Font
```

raylib-name alias for `loadFontFromTtfData` - `LoadFontFromMemory` in raylib's vocabulary.  raylib's signature takes a `fileType` string (".ttf" / ".otf") that we ignore (we only support TTF / OTF via stb_truetype anyway).  Provided for callers porting from raylib who want familiar names.

→ **raylib:** `LoadFontFromMemory` — Load font from memory buffer, fileType refers to extension: i.e. '.ttf'

#### `text.loadFontFromTtfBytes`

```zig
    pub fn loadFontFromTtfBytes(
        gpa: Allocator,
        state: *FontCache,
        ttf_bytes: []const u8,
        bake_size: i32,
        codepoints: []const u21,
        oversample: u8,
    ) LoadFontError!void
```

Eagerly bake the default-font atlas into `state`.  Called Eager-load **zimr's branded default font** - Atkinson Hyperlegible Mono, regular weight, baked at 16 px, ASCII codepoints 32-127.  Allocates an atlas image and glyph data via `gpa`, uploads the atlas to the GPU as a texture, populates `state.font`.  Idempotent - second call returns immediately because `state.loaded` is true. `state` is the user's owned `FontCache`, typically zero- initialised on the user's `State` struct and threaded here from `initState`.  Per the thin-frame principle (see `src/notes/thin-frame-plan.md`), zimr does not own the cache. Errors: - `error.OutOfMemory` - allocator failed during parse or atlas bake. - `error.TtfParseFailed` - should not happen for the embedded Atkinson bytes (they're shipped and verified in this codebase), but the error path exists. - `error.AtlasOverflow` - codepoints + padding don't fit in the chosen atlas size. - `error.GpuUploadFailed` - `rlLoadTexture` returned 0 (no GPU on host builds; real wasm-side failure elsewhere). On non-wasm host builds this is a no-op that returns success - `rlLoadTexture` is a host stub that returns 0, which would surface as `GpuUploadFailed`, so we short- circuit at the top to keep host examples allocator-free. Reads: `atkinson_mono_ttf` (compile-time constant). Mutates: `state.*` (sets `loaded`, installs the live `Font` struct including its GPU texture id and allocator-owned `glyphs` / `recs` slices). Step turn-335 (font-default sweep, Option A): bake `ttf_bytes` into `state` as the active font.  This is the explicit form the old `loadFontDefault` papered over - the user brings their own TTF, picks their own bake size and codepoint coverage, and gets a font cache ready for `u.text` and `drawText`. `ttf_bytes`: TTF/OTF byte slice.  Typically the result of `@embedFile("path/to/font.ttf")` at the call site. `bake_size`: atlas rasterization size in pixels.  Use 2× your typical render size for crisp downscaling (e.g. 32 for a UI rendering text at 14-16 px). `codepoints`: which characters to include in the atlas. `default_codepoints_ascii` covers printable ASCII (32-126). `oversample`: anti-aliasing oversample.  1 = none. Idempotent guarded by `state.loaded` - second call no-ops rather than re-baking + leaking the previous atlas.  If the user wants to replace the font, they call `unloadFont` first or manage their own cache. On non-wasm host builds: no-op + success (returns without allocating).  `rlLoadTexture` is a host stub returning 0, which would surface as `GpuUploadFailed`; we short-circuit at the top to keep host examples allocator-free.  Host PNG screenshots therefore CAN'T render text - separate concern, see `src/notes/font-default-plan.md`.

#### `text.loadFontFromTtfData`

```zig
    pub fn loadFontFromTtfData(
        gpa: Allocator,
        ttf_bytes: []const u8,
        font_size: i32,
        codepoints: []const u21,
        padding: i32,
    ) LoadFontError!Font
```

Load a custom font from in-memory TTF/OTF bytes.  Combines: 1. `truetype.loadFontFromTtf` - parse the table directory. 2. `bakeFontAtlas` - rasterize requested codepoints + pack into a CPU atlas image. 3. GPU upload via `rlgl.fwd.rlLoadTexture`. 4. Free the CPU atlas image; the GPU keeps the only copy. Returns a `Font` shaped like raylib's, which means the existing `drawTextEx` / `measureTextWithFont` / `unloadFont` all-work-unchanged path is the immediate consumer.

#### `text.loadFontRaylibBitmap`

```zig
    pub fn loadFontRaylibBitmap(gpa: Allocator, state: *FontCache) !void
```

**Legacy alternative:** load the original raylib 8×10 bitmap font as the default.  Available for callers who want the pixel-perfect retro aesthetic (or who can't afford the ~34 KB Atkinson TTF in their wasm).  Most code should use `loadFontDefault(gpa, state)` instead. Lazily allocates `state.buffers` (~114 KB).  The four big arrays previously lived inline on FontCache; moving them behind a pointer dropped ~228 KB of vestigial zero-init templates from the wasm binary (the TTF path doesn't need them and didn't use them). Mutates: `state.*` (atlas, glyphs, font, `loaded` flag). On non-wasm host builds this is a no-op.

#### `text.loadUTF8`

```zig
    pub fn loadUTF8(
        gpa: Allocator,
        codepoints: []const i32,
    ) Allocator.Error![:0]u8
```

Encode a codepoint slice back to a heap-allocated, sentinel- terminated UTF-8 string.  The returned slice carries its length (excluding the null sentinel); free with `gpa.free(slice)`. Empty input returns an empty sentinel-terminated slice. Invalid codepoints (above 0x10FFFF or in the surrogate range) are emitted as the replacement character (U+FFFD).

```zig
        font: Font,
        codepoints: []const i32,

    ) Vec2
```

Measure rendered dimensions of a slice of decoded codepoints at `fontSize` and per-glyph `spacing`.  Multi-line strings (codepoints containing `'\n'`) advance the height by `fontSize + line_spacing` per newline. Reads: `line_spacing` (only matters when `codepoints` contains '\n').

→ **raylib:** `MeasureTextCodepoints` — Measure string size for an existing array of codepoints for Font


Measure the width × height (px) of a string with explicit font, size, and per-glyph spacing.  Width returned in `.x`, height in `.y`.  Multi-line strings ('\n' separated) yield the max-line width and the cumulative height including line gaps. Reads: `line_spacing` (px gap between lines, only consulted when the string contains '\n').

#### `text.nextCodepoint`

```zig
    pub fn nextCodepoint(s: []const u8) DecodedCodepoint
```

Decode the next UTF-8 codepoint from the start of `s`.  Returns `'?'` and `bytes=1` on decode error or empty input.  Lenient matches raylib's behaviour and tolerates malformed sequences. Implementation: delegates to the vendored DFA-based decoder (zg/code_point.zig) which implements the Unicode-recommended Maximal Subparts algorithm.  Compared to the previous hand-rolled decoder this is more correct on truncated multibyte sequences: e.g. for the input "Я" (0xD0, 0xAF) followed by a stray 0xE0, the old code consumed all 3 bytes; the new code stops cleanly after 2.


snake_case → PascalCase.

#### `text.unloadFont`

```zig
    pub fn unloadFont(
        gpa: Allocator,
        font_cache: *const FontCache,
        font: Font,
    ) void
```

Free GPU + CPU memory for a Font.  No-op if the font is the rlgl default (which is owned by `App.create` / `App.destroy`). Pass the same allocator used to load the font. Reads: `font_cache.font.texture.id` (used to recognise the default font and skip its teardown). Mutates: nothing in zimr state - frees `font.glyphs` / `font.recs` from `gpa` and the font's GL texture via JS bridge.

→ **raylib:** `UnloadFont` — Unload font from GPU memory (VRAM)

#### `text.unloadFontCache`

```zig
    pub fn unloadFontCache(gpa: Allocator, state: *FontCache) void
```

Tear down `state.font` - free its GPU texture and the allocator-owned glyph data.  Resets `state.loaded` to false so a subsequent `loadFontFromTtfBytes` call will re-bake.  No-op when nothing's loaded. On non-wasm host builds: no-op (the load path was a no-op too).

#### `text.unloadFontData`

```zig
    pub fn unloadFontData(
        gpa: Allocator,
        glyphs: []GlyphInfo,
    ) void
```

Free per-glyph CPU images (reverse of `loadFontData`).  Empty slice is a no-op.  Pass the same allocator used to create the glyph images.

→ **raylib:** `UnloadFontData` — Unload font chars info data (RAM)

#### `text.unloadFontRaylibBitmap`

```zig
    pub fn unloadFontRaylibBitmap(gpa: Allocator, state: *FontCache) void
```

Pair with `loadFontRaylibBitmap`.  Frees the on-demand `state.buffers` allocation (the 114 KB block holding the atlas + glyphs scratch).

#### `text.unloadFontRaylibBitmapImpl`

```zig
    pub fn unloadFontRaylibBitmapImpl(gpa: Allocator, state: *FontCache) void
```

Free the GPU texture backing the default font and reset the cache to its uninitialized state.  After this call, `getFontDefault` returns a zero `Font` until `loadFontDefault` runs again. Mutates: `state.*` (clears `loaded`, zeroes the live `Font`). Side effect: a `rlgl.fwd.rlUnloadTexture` JS-bridge call. No `gl: *GlState` parameter - the texture deletion is performed by JS directly via the GL texture id, not via any rlgl batch state.

#### `text.upper`

```zig
    pub fn upper(allocator: Allocator, s: []const u8) Allocator.Error![]u8
```

Convert ASCII letters to upper-case. Caller owns the returned slice.

### `drawing.textures`

#### `textures.beginTextureMode`

```zig
    pub fn beginTextureMode(
        gl: anytype,
        target: RenderTexture2D_t,
    ) void
```

Begin rendering to a `RenderTexture` instead of the canvas. Pairs with `endTextureMode`.  Pattern: beginTextureMode(rt); defer endTextureMode(); // … draw calls go to rt.texture instead of canvas … What it does: 1. Flush the current rlgl batch (so prior draw calls hit the canvas, not the RT) 2. Bind `target.id` as the active framebuffer 3. Set the viewport to the RT's size 4. Push the projection matrix and load an ortho matching the RT's coordinate space (Y-down so 2D draw fns line up the same way they do on the canvas) 5. Reset the modelview matrix to identity Bind `target` as the active framebuffer.  Saves the current projection matrix on the rlgl stack, installs an ortho that matches the render texture's dimensions, and sets the viewport.  Pair with `endTextureMode(gl, window)` to restore. `gl` is the rlgl state to mutate (matrix stack + active framebuffer).  This fn writes - never reads-without-writing so the parameter is `*GlState`, not `*const`.

→ **raylib:** `BeginTextureMode` — Begin drawing to render texture

```zig

        gl: anytype,
        window: *const @import("zig").WindowState,
    ) void
```

Restores the canvas viewport and pops the projection matrix stacked at begin. `window` is read-only here - only `getRenderWidth` / `getRenderHeight` are called on it to restore the viewport to the current canvas dimensions.  `gl` is mutated (matrix stack pop + framebuffer rebind).

→ **raylib:** `EndTextureMode` — End drawing to render texture

#### `textures.exportImageToMemory`

```zig
    pub fn exportImageToMemory(
        gpa: Allocator,
        image: Image,
        file_type: []const u8,
    ) ExportImageError![]u8
```

Encode `image` to bytes in the format named by `file_type` (e.g. `".png"`).  Returns owned bytes the caller must `gpa.free` matches raylib's `ExportImageToMemory(image, fileType, *fileSize)` shape but slice-shape instead of pointer + out-size. Currently supports `.png` only on the web target.  Other formats (BMP, TGA, JPG, QOI) would each need their own encoder; we have only PNG internally because that's what `getClipboardImage` / `setWindowIcon` round-trip through. The image is normalised to RGBA8 in a scratch buffer before encoding (we don't mutate `image`).  Compressed inputs return `error.UnsupportedPixelFormat`.

#### `textures.fade`

```zig
    pub fn fade(color: Color, alpha_in: f32) Color
```

→ **raylib:** `Fade` — Get color with alpha applied, alpha goes from 0.0f to 1.0f

#### `textures.genImageCellular`

```zig
    pub fn genImageCellular(
        gpa: Allocator,
        width: i32,
        height: i32,
        tile_size: i32,
    ) ImageGenError!Image
```

Generate a 2D cellular (Worley) noise image, grayscale RGBA8. Each pixel's value is the distance to the nearest random "seed" point, normalized to 0..255. Parameters mirror raylib's `GenImageCellular`: - `width` / `height` - image dimensions - `tile_size` - grid cell size in pixels.  One random seed point per cell; smaller tiles = more cells = denser pattern.  Default raylib uses ~32. Implementation: divide the image into a grid of `tile_size`-square cells.  Each cell holds one randomly-positioned point.  For each pixel, compute distance to the nearest point in the 9 surrounding cells (current + 8 neighbours).  Normalize by `tile_size * sqrt(2)` so output covers most of the 0..255 range. Random seeds use a deterministic hash of `(cell_x, cell_y)` so the result is reproducible - same parameters produce identical pixels, no `Rng` parameter needed.

→ **raylib:** `GenImageCellular` — Generate image: cellular algorithm, bigger tileSize means bigger cells

#### `textures.genImageChecked`

```zig
    pub fn genImageChecked(
        gpa: Allocator,
        width: i32,
        height: i32,
        checks_x: i32,
        checks_y: i32,
        col1: Color,
        col2: Color,
    ) ImageGenError!Image
```

Checkerboard pattern.  `checks_x` / `checks_y` are the *cell sizes* (in pixels), not counts - raylib's naming is misleading.  Caller frees with `unloadImage(gpa, img)`.

→ **raylib:** `GenImageChecked` — Generate image: checked

#### `textures.genImageColor`

```zig
    pub fn genImageColor(
        gpa: Allocator,
        width: i32,
        height: i32,
        color: Color,
    ) ImageGenError!Image
```

Generate a `width × height` image filled with a single color.  Format is always RGBA8 for predictability.  Caller frees with `unloadImage(gpa, img)` using the same allocator.

→ **raylib:** `GenImageColor` — Generate image: plain color

#### `textures.genImageGradientLinear`

```zig
    pub fn genImageGradientLinear(
        gpa: Allocator,
        width: i32,
        height: i32,
        direction: i32,
        start: Color,
        end: Color,
    ) ImageGenError!Image
```

Linear gradient.  `direction` is in degrees: 0° goes top→bottom, 90° left→right, etc. (counter-clockwise from "up").  The math: rotate the gradient axis by 90°-direction, then for each pixel project its position onto that axis and lerp between `start` and `end`.  Caller frees with `unloadImage(gpa, img)`.

→ **raylib:** `GenImageGradientLinear` — Generate image: linear gradient, direction in degrees [0..360], 0=Vertical gradient

#### `textures.genImageGradientRadial`

```zig
    pub fn genImageGradientRadial(
        gpa: Allocator,
        width: i32,
        height: i32,
        density: f32,
        inner: Color,
        outer: Color,
    ) ImageGenError!Image
```

Radial gradient from `inner` (centre) to `outer` (edge).  `density` is a [0, 1] knob: at 0 the gradient starts at the centre; closer to 1 the inner color spreads further before fading.  Caller frees with `unloadImage(gpa, img)`.

→ **raylib:** `GenImageGradientRadial` — Generate image: radial gradient

#### `textures.genImageGradientSquare`

```zig
    pub fn genImageGradientSquare(
        gpa: Allocator,
        width: i32,
        height: i32,
        density: f32,
        inner: Color,
        outer: Color,
    ) ImageGenError!Image
```

Square (Manhattan-distance) gradient from `inner` (centre) to `outer` (edge).  `density` works the same as in `genImageGradientRadial`.  Caller frees with `unloadImage(gpa, img)`.

→ **raylib:** `GenImageGradientSquare` — Generate image: square gradient

#### `textures.genImagePerlinNoise`

```zig
    pub fn genImagePerlinNoise(
        gpa: Allocator,
        width: i32,
        height: i32,
        offset_x: i32,
        offset_y: i32,
        scale: f32,
    ) ImageGenError!Image
```

Generate a 2D Perlin noise image, grayscale RGBA8.  Output range is mapped 0..255; smooth gradient noise - no jagged edges, no repeated tiling pattern. Parameters mirror raylib's `GenImagePerlinNoise`: - `width` / `height` - image dimensions - `offset_x` / `offset_y` - sample-space offset (lets you sample different regions of the same noise field; useful for animating "scrolling" terrain) - `scale` - controls the feature size.  Higher = larger features (zoomed in); lower = smaller (zoomed out).  Typical values 1.0-10.0; raylib uses ~10 in its example. Implementation: classical Perlin with a fixed permutation table (Ken Perlin's original 256-entry table).  Pure CPU, deterministic for the same offset/scale - same call reproduces identical pixels.  ~80 LOC including the table.

→ **raylib:** `GenImagePerlinNoise` — Generate image: perlin noise

#### `textures.genImageText`

```zig
    pub fn genImageText(
        gpa: Allocator,
        width: i32,
        height: i32,
        s: []const u8,
    ) ImageGenError!Image
```

Generate a grayscale `Image` whose pixels are the bytes of `text` - a debug/utility helper for visualising raw byte data, NOT for rendering text glyphs.  For "render this string into an image" you want `text.imageDrawText` (which uses the default font and produces a proper RGBA8 anti-aliased result). Output is `width × height` bytes of single-channel grayscale; text bytes are copied left-to-right, top-to-bottom; if `text` is shorter than the image the remaining pixels stay zero (allocator zeroes); if longer the excess is silently truncated.

→ **raylib:** `GenImageText` — Generate image: grayscale image from text data

#### `textures.genImageWhiteNoise`

```zig
        rng: Rng,
        width: i32,
        height: i32,
        factor: f32,
    ) ImageGenError!Image
```

White-noise binary image.  `factor` in [0, 1] controls the white/black ratio (0.5 ≈ even).  Uses the explicit `Rng` for reproducibility - pass `Rng.Seeded` for deterministic output, or whatever `Rng` your Frame carries for production use.

→ **raylib:** `GenImageWhiteNoise` — Generate image: white noise

```zig
    pub fn imageColorReplace(
```

#### `textures.imageCopy`

```zig
    pub fn imageCopy(
        gpa: Allocator,
        image: Image,
    ) ImageGenError!Image
```

Deep-copy an Image, including all mipmap levels.  The returned Image owns its pixel buffer independently of the source.  Free with `unloadImage(gpa, copy)` using the same allocator.

→ **raylib:** `ImageCopy` — Create an image duplicate (useful for transformations)

#### `textures.imageCrop`

```zig
    pub fn imageCrop(
        gpa: Allocator,
        image: *Image,
        crop_in: Rectangle,
    ) Allocator.Error!void
```

Crop an image to a sub-rectangle, in place.  Out-of-bounds rect is silently clamped to the image extents; if the rect is entirely outside, the call is a no-op (matches raylib semantics).  Pass the same allocator the image was created with - the old pixel buffer is freed after the new (cropped) buffer is built.

→ **raylib:** `ImageCrop` — Crop an image to a defined rectangle

#### `textures.imageDither`

```zig
    pub fn imageDither(
        gpa: Allocator,
        image: *Image,
        rBpp: i32,
        gBpp: i32,
        bBpp: i32,
        aBpp: i32,
    ) Allocator.Error!void
```

Floyd-Steinberg dither an RGBA8 image down to the specified bit depth per channel.  Output pixel format is determined by the per-channel bit depths: - rBpp + gBpp + bBpp + aBpp == 16 → R5G6B5 (or R5G5B5A1, R4G4B4A4) - others → unchanged (function is a no-op for unsupported combos) Error diffusion: 7/16 right, 3/16 below-left, 5/16 below, 1/16 below-right (classic Floyd-Steinberg coefficients).

→ **raylib:** `ImageDither` — Dither image data to 16bpp or lower (Floyd-Steinberg dithering)

#### `textures.imageDraw`

```zig
    pub fn imageDraw(
        dst: *Image,
        src: Image,
        src_rec_in: Rectangle,
        dst_rec_in: Rectangle,
        tint: Color,
    ) void
```

Composite a source Image onto a destination Image with optional tint.  The source rectangle `src_rec` is sampled from `src`; the destination rectangle `dst_rec` defines where it lands in `dst`. **Limitation:** when source and destination rectangles have different dimensions, this implementation falls back to nearest-neighbor scaling.  A high-quality bilinear resampler is deferred to a future `imageResize` and will upgrade this fast path automatically once available. Source rectangles outside `src` bounds are clipped.  Destination rectangles partially outside `dst` are clipped.  Tinted source pixels are alpha-blended onto the destination via `colorAlphaBlend`.

→ **raylib:** `ImageDraw` — Draw a source image within a destination image (tint applied to source)

#### `textures.imageFormat`

```zig
    pub fn imageFormat(
        gpa: Allocator,
        image: *Image,
        new_format: PixelFormat,
    ) ImageGenError!void
```

Convert `image.data` from its current pixel format to `new_format` in place.  Allocates a fresh destination buffer at the new format's size, walks each pixel through `getPixelColor` (source-format→Color) then `setPixelColor` (Color→destination- format), then frees the old buffer and installs the new. Strong exception guarantee: on `error.OutOfMemory` from the allocator, `image` is left untouched. Compressed formats (DXT/ETC/PVRT/ASTC) are silent no-ops on either source or destination - matches raylib's behaviour. Mipmaps beyond level 0 are dropped (raylib parity).  Float formats lose precision because the through-Color path is RGBA8 - also raylib parity. Pass the same allocator that was used to create `image`.

→ **raylib:** `ImageFormat` — Convert image data to desired format

#### `textures.imageFromChannel`

```zig
    pub fn imageFromChannel(
        gpa: Allocator,
        image: Image,
        selected_channel: i32,
    ) ExportImageError!Image
```

Extract a single channel from a multi-channel image as a new grayscale image.  Channel index: 0=R, 1=G, 2=B, 3=A. raylib: `ImageFromChannel(Image image, int selectedChannel)`. The output is `uncompressed_grayscale` (1 byte/pixel).  Source channels beyond the source format's actual channel count return 255 (fully opaque) - matches raylib.

→ **raylib:** `ImageFromChannel` — Create an image from a selected channel of another image (GRAYSCALE)

#### `textures.imageFromImage`

```zig
    pub fn imageFromImage(
        gpa: Allocator,
        image: Image,
        rec: Rectangle,
    ) ImageGenError!Image
```

Extract a rectangular sub-region of an image as a new Image. Result has `mipmaps = 1` regardless of the source.  No bounds clamping - caller must keep `rec` within the source.  Free with `unloadImage(gpa, sub)` using the same allocator.

→ **raylib:** `ImageFromImage` — Create an image from another image piece

#### `textures.imageKernelConvolution`

```zig
    pub fn imageKernelConvolution(
        gpa: Allocator,
        image: *Image,
        kernel: []const f32,
    ) Allocator.Error!void
```

Apply an arbitrary square convolution kernel to an RGBA8 image. `kernelSize` is the side length (must be odd: 3, 5, 7, ...). Edges replicate the nearest valid pixel (clamp-to-edge).  `gpa` is used only for a scratch buffer; image data is mutated in place. `kernel` is a flat row-major odd×odd matrix; pass e.g. a 3×3 kernel as `&[_]f32{ 0, -1, 0, -1, 5, -1, 0, -1, 0 }`.

→ **raylib:** `ImageKernelConvolution` — Apply custom square convolution kernel to image

#### `textures.imageMipmaps`

```zig
    pub fn imageMipmaps(
        gpa: Allocator,
        image: *Image,
    ) ExportImageError!void
```

Generate a mipmap chain for `image` in place.  Each level is a 2× box-filter downscale of the previous level until we reach 1×1 (or a 1×N / N×1 strip stops shrinking on one axis). raylib: `ImageMipmaps(Image *image)`. On success `image.data` points at a fresh allocation containing the original level-0 followed by all generated levels, packed contiguously.  Caller must `unloadImage(gpa, image)` with the SAME allocator.  The original `image.data` is freed. Compressed formats return `error.UnsupportedPixelFormat`.  Only uncompressed RGBA8 is currently supported (the most common case for textures used as mip pyramids).  Other uncompressed formats are converted to RGBA8 first via the caller's choice of `imageFormat`.

→ **raylib:** `ImageMipmaps` — Compute all mipmap levels for a provided image

#### `textures.imageResize`

```zig
    pub fn imageResize(
        gpa: Allocator,
        image: *Image,
        newWidth: i32,
        newHeight: i32,
    ) Allocator.Error!void
```

Resize image using bilinear filtering.  Quality is appropriate for photographic content; for pixel art use `imageResizeNN`. Pass the same allocator the image was created with - the old pixel buffer is freed after the new (resized) buffer is built.

→ **raylib:** `ImageResize` — Resize image (Bicubic scaling algorithm)

#### `textures.imageResizeCanvas`

```zig
    pub fn imageResizeCanvas(
        gpa: Allocator,
        image: *Image,
        new_w: i32,
        new_h: i32,
        off_x: i32,
        off_y: i32,
        fill: Color,
    ) Allocator.Error!void
```

Resize the image's canvas to (`new_w`, `new_h`), preserving pixel content.  The original image is positioned at (`off_x`, `off_y`) in the new canvas; new area is filled with `fill`.  Negative offsets clip the source.  Pass the same allocator the image was created with - the old buffer is freed after the new canvas is built.

→ **raylib:** `ImageResizeCanvas` — Resize canvas and fill with color

#### `textures.imageResizeNN`

```zig
    pub fn imageResizeNN(
        gpa: Allocator,
        image: *Image,
        newWidth: i32,
        newHeight: i32,
    ) Allocator.Error!void
```

Resize image using nearest-neighbor sampling.  Best for pixel art or when crisp pixel boundaries are desired.  Faster than `imageResize`.  Pass the same allocator the image was created with.

→ **raylib:** `ImageResizeNN` — Resize image (Nearest-Neighbor scaling algorithm)

#### `textures.imageRotate`


Rotate an image by an arbitrary angle in degrees.  Output Image dimensions grow to fit the rotated content (no clipping).  Areas outside the source are zero-filled.  Sampling is bilinear. Reallocates `image.data`; the old buffer is freed via `gpa`. No-op for compressed pixel formats.

→ **raylib:** `ImageRotate` — Rotate image by input angle in degrees (-359 to 359)

```zig
    pub fn imageRotateCW(

    ) Allocator.Error!void
```

Rotate image 90° clockwise.  Allocates a new buffer with `gpa`, frees the old one with `freeImageData`.  Pass the same allocator the image was created with.

→ **raylib:** `ImageRotateCW` — Rotate image clockwise 90deg

#### `textures.imageText`

```zig
    pub fn imageText(
        gpa: Allocator,
        font_cache: *const @import("zig").FontCache,
        line_spacing: i32,
        s: []const u8,
        font_size: i32,
        color: Color,
    ) ImageGenError!Image
```

Allocate a new RGBA8 `Image` containing `text` rendered with the default font.  Defaults `spacing` to `fontSize / 10` (matching raylib).  Caller frees with `unloadImage(gpa, image)` using the same allocator. Empty string returns a 1×1 transparent image (so the result is always valid for `imageDraw` etc.). Allocate a new RGBA8 `Image` sized to fit `s` rendered in the default font.  Convenience wrapper around `imageTextWithFont`: picks default-font, derives spacing from `font_size / 10` (default font's 10 px base height). Reads: `line_spacing` (forwarded to `imageTextWithFont`, used only when `s` contains '\n'). Empty string returns a 1×1 transparent image (so the result is always valid for `imageDraw` etc.).

→ **raylib:** `ImageText` — Create an image from text (default font)

#### `textures.imageTextWithFont`

```zig
    pub fn imageTextWithFont(
        gpa: Allocator,
        line_spacing: i32,
        font: Font,
        s: []const u8,
        font_size: f32,
        spacing: f32,
        tint: Color,
    ) ImageGenError!Image
```

Allocate a new RGBA8 `Image` sized to fit `s` rendered with `font`, then composite the text onto it via `imageDrawTextWithFont`. Background is fully transparent (alpha 0); glyphs tinted by `tint`. Reads: `line_spacing` (forwarded to `text.measureWithFont` to size the destination image - only matters when `s` contains '\n'). Returns a 1×1 transparent image for empty input or an empty font - always a valid, drawable result.

#### `textures.loadImageFromScreen`

```zig
    pub fn loadImageFromScreen(
        gl: anytype,
        window: *const @import("zig").WindowState,
        gpa: Allocator,
    ) LoadError!Image
```

Read pixels back from the default framebuffer (canvas backbuffer) into a CPU `Image`.  Always returns RGBA8. Reads the entire canvas at the runtime's reported render dimensions.  The result is "what was last drawn this frame up to the call site" - call AFTER all draw commands you want captured; AFTER `f.clear` followed by AT LEAST one `rlDrawRenderBatchActive()` flush (or `endTextureMode` / `f.present`-equivalent) so the pixels actually exist in the backbuffer. On host builds, returns `error.GpuReadbackFailed`.

→ **raylib:** `LoadImageFromScreen` — Load image from screen buffer (screenshot)

#### `textures.loadImageFromTexture`

```zig
    pub fn loadImageFromTexture(
        gl: anytype,
        gpa: Allocator,
        texture: Texture2D_t,
    ) LoadError!Image
```

Read pixels back from a GPU texture into a CPU `Image`.  Always returns RGBA8 (4 bytes per pixel). Implementation: bind the texture as a colour attachment to a transient FBO, verify completeness, `glReadPixels` into a fresh CPU buffer, unbind+unload the FBO.  Caller owns the returned `Image` and must free it with `unloadImage(gpa, image)` using the same allocator. On host builds (no GL context), returns `error.GpuReadbackFailed` immediately.

→ **raylib:** `LoadImageFromTexture` — Load image from GPU texture data

#### `textures.loadRenderTexture`

```zig
    pub fn loadRenderTexture(
        width: i32,
        height: i32,
    ) LoadError!RenderTexture2D_t
```

Allocate a render-target framebuffer with a colour texture (RGBA8) and a depth renderbuffer.  Returns a populated `RenderTexture`, or `error.GpuUploadFailed` if framebuffer completeness fails. Bundles three rlgl calls (`rlLoadFramebuffer`, `rlLoadTexture`, `rlLoadTextureDepth`, `rlFramebufferAttach`×2, `rlFramebufferComplete`) into one call.  Matches raylib's `LoadRenderTexture(width, height)` shape. The depth attachment is always a renderbuffer (not a texture) faster to allocate, can't be sampled, which matches what post-processing pipelines actually want.  Examples that need a depth-as-texture should construct the FBO manually.

→ **raylib:** `LoadRenderTexture` — Load texture for rendering (framebuffer)

#### `textures.loadTextureCubemap`

```zig
    pub fn loadTextureCubemap(
        gpa: Allocator,
        faces: [6]Image,
    ) LoadError!Texture
```

Build a cubemap texture from six face images. Faces must be supplied in raylib's canonical order: [0] +X (right) [1] -X (left) [2] +Y (top) [3] -Y (bottom) [4] +Z (front) [5] -Z (back) All six faces must share the same square dimensions and the same pixel format.  Returns a cubemap texture with `width = height = face_size`; the `id` field is bound to the new GL texture name. Failure modes (returns a Texture with `id == 0`): - Any face is non-square or differs in size from face[0] - Any face has a different format from face[0] - Allocation for the contiguous face buffer fails - The underlying `rlLoadTextureCubemap` returns 0 (driver issue, unsupported format, etc.) The caller still owns the input images - this function reads them but doesn't free them.  Pair with `unloadImage` per face after. Implementation note: the rlgl primitive expects all six faces laid out contiguously in memory.  We allocate a single buffer of `face_size² × bpp × 6` bytes, copy each face's pixels into the right slot, then call `rlLoadTextureCubemap` once.  After the upload, the staging buffer is freed - the GPU has its own copy.

→ **raylib:** `LoadTextureCubemap` — Load cubemap from image, multiple image cubemap layouts supported

#### `textures.loadTextureFromImage`

```zig
    pub fn loadTextureFromImage(image: Image) LoadError!Texture2D_t
```

Promote a CPU `Image` to a GPU `Texture`.  Single call: uploads the pixel buffer via `rlLoadTexture` and wraps the resulting id + dims in a `Texture`. The caller still owns the source `image`; this function reads but doesn't free it.  Dispose with `unloadImage(gpa, image)` after. Returns `error.GpuUploadFailed` if `rlLoadTexture` returns id 0 (driver OOM, unsupported format, exceeded `GL_MAX_TEXTURE_SIZE`). On the host test harness, the fake rlgl always returns a non-zero id, so this error path only fires under a real driver.

→ **raylib:** `LoadTextureFromImage` — Load texture from image data

#### `textures.resizeRenderTexture`

```zig
    pub fn resizeRenderTexture(
        target: RenderTexture2D_t,
        new_width: i32,
        new_height: i32,
    ) LoadError!RenderTexture2D_t
```

Re-create `target` at the new size, releasing the old GL resources first.  This is the practical "resize FBO" path users actually want - raylib's `rlResizeFramebuffer` is software-only and a no-op on WebGL2. Returns the new RenderTexture; callers should overwrite the original (the old IDs are gone after this call).

```zig
    pub fn unloadImage(
        gpa: Allocator,
        image: Image,
    ) void
```

Free an Image's pixel buffer. No-op on null `image.data`. Free an Image's CPU pixel buffer.  Pass the same allocator that was used to create the image (e.g. the gpa given to `genImageColor`). The byte count is derived from `image.width × image.height × format`, summed across mipmap levels.

→ **raylib:** `UnloadImage` — Unload image from CPU memory (RAM)

```zig

```

Free a render texture: framebuffer + colour texture + depth renderbuffer all released to the GL driver.  Idempotent - safe to call on a `RenderTexture` with `id == 0` (no-op).

→ **raylib:** `UnloadRenderTexture` — Unload render texture from GPU memory (VRAM)

#### `textures.unloadTexture`

```zig
    pub fn unloadTexture(texture: Texture2D_t) void
```
