→ **raylib:** `UnloadTexture` — Unload texture from GPU memory (VRAM)

#### `textures.updateTexture`

```zig
    pub fn updateTexture(tex: Texture2D_t, pixels: ?*const anyopaque) void
```

Re-upload an entire texture's pixel data, replacing every texel. `pixels` must hold `width * height * bytesPerPixel(format)` bytes in the texture's existing format - we don't re-allocate the GPU buffer, so dimensions and format must match what was originally uploaded.  Use `updateTextureRec` for a partial update. Common use: animated dynamic textures, video frames, paint apps.

→ **raylib:** `UpdateTexture` — Update GPU texture with new data (pixels should be able to fill texture)

#### `textures.updateTextureRec`

```zig
    pub fn updateTextureRec(
        tex: Texture2D_t,
        rec: Rectangle_t,
        pixels: ?*const anyopaque,
    ) void
```

Re-upload a sub-rectangle of an existing texture.  `pixels` must hold `rec.width * rec.height * bytesPerPixel(format)` bytes only the rectangle's worth of data, not the whole texture.

→ **raylib:** `UpdateTextureRec` — Update GPU texture rectangle with new data (pixels and rec should fit in texture)

---

## **runtime.zig** — `core`, `input`, `gestures`, time, fps, log, rng, loader

### `runtime.allocator`

#### `allocator.calloc`

```zig
    pub fn calloc(nmemb: usize, size: usize) ?*anyopaque
```

libc calloc - like malloc(nmemb * size) followed by memset to 0.

#### `allocator.free`

```zig
    pub fn free(ptr: ?*anyopaque) void
```

libc free.  No-op on null; tolerant on host.

#### `allocator.malloc`

```zig
    pub fn malloc(size: usize) ?*anyopaque
```

libc malloc.  Returns null on OOM, on size=0, or on non-wasm targets.

#### `allocator.realloc`

```zig
    pub fn realloc(ptr: ?*anyopaque, size: usize) ?*anyopaque
```

libc realloc. - realloc(NULL, n) == malloc(n) - realloc(p, 0) frees p and returns NULL - otherwise: allocate, copy min(old, new), free old.  This is simpler than the libc semantics where realloc *may* extend in place; callers that rely on grow-in-place would be incorrect anyway.

### `runtime.camera`

#### `camera.beginMode2D`

```zig
    pub fn beginMode2D(
        gl: *GlState,
        cam: Camera2D,
    ) void
```

Set up a 2D rendering context (offset/zoom/rotation around target). Pairs with `endMode2D(gl)`.  This is purely a model-view transform no projection change.

→ **raylib:** `BeginMode2D` — Begin 2D mode with custom camera (2D)

#### `camera.beginMode3D`

```zig
    pub fn beginMode3D(
        gl: *GlState,
        cam: Camera3D,
    ) void
```

Set up a 3D rendering context using `cam`.  Pushes the current projection matrix, installs a perspective or orthographic projection based on `cam.projection`, applies the look-at view, and enables depth testing.  Pair with `endMode3D(gl)`. `gl` is mutated - pushes projection, installs perspective/ortho, sets the modelview to the camera's view matrix, enables depth.

→ **raylib:** `BeginMode3D` — Begin 3D mode with custom camera (3D)


→ **raylib:** `GetCameraMatrix` — Get camera transform matrix (view matrix)

```zig

```

Get the 2D cam transform matrix.  Translates by -target, rotates, scales by zoom, then translates by +offset.

→ **raylib:** `GetCameraMatrix2D` — Get camera 2d transform matrix

```zig
    pub fn getCameraRight(cam: *Camera3D) Vec
```

#### `camera.getCameraUp`

```zig

```

→ **raylib:** `GetCameraUp`

#### `camera.getScreenToWorld2D`

```zig
    pub fn getScreenToWorld2D(position: Vec2, cam: Camera2D) Vec2
```

Inverse: screen-space pixel → world-space point under a Camera2D.

→ **raylib:** `GetScreenToWorld2D` — Get world space position for a 2d camera screen space position

#### `camera.getScreenToWorldRay`

```zig
    pub fn getScreenToWorldRay(
        gl: *const GlState,
        position: Vec2,
        cam: Camera3D,
    ) Ray
```

Get a ray from a screen-space pixel position (e.g. mouse) into the world, using the current framebuffer dimensions.  Useful for click-to-pick in 3D.  See `getScreenToWorldRayWithViewport` for an arbitrary-viewport variant. Reads `gl.framebuffer_width` / `gl.framebuffer_height` (current canvas dimensions), `gl.cull_distance_near` / `gl.cull_distance_far` (clip planes forwarded to `Ex`), `position`, `cam`.  No mutation.

→ **raylib:** `GetScreenToWorldRay` — Get a ray trace from screen position (i.e mouse)

#### `camera.getScreenToWorldRayWithViewport`

```zig
    pub fn getScreenToWorldRayWithViewport(
        gl: *const GlState,
        position: Vec2,
        cam: Camera3D,
        width: i32,
        height: i32,
    ) Ray
```

As `getScreenToWorldRay`, but with an explicit viewport size. Use this when rendering into an off-screen render target whose dimensions don't match the framebuffer. Reads `gl.cull_distance_near` / `gl.cull_distance_far` (clip planes for the projection matrix), `position`, `cam`, `width`, `height`.  No mutation. Implementation follows raylib's: convert pixel position to NDC, build view + projection matrices, unproject z=0 (near) and z=1 (far) points to get a world-space line.  For perspective cameras the ray origin is the cam position; for ortho cameras the origin is the unprojected near-plane point (since orthographic rays don't converge).

#### `camera.getWorldToScreen`

```zig
    pub fn getWorldToScreen(
        gl: *const GlState,
        position: Vec,
        cam: Camera3D,
    ) Vec2
```

Project a world-space point onto the screen.  Returns pixel coordinates relative to the active framebuffer's top-left.  If the point is behind the cam or outside the frustum, the result will be clamped or off-screen - caller is responsible for visibility culling. Project a world-space 3D point to screen-space pixel coordinates using the current framebuffer dimensions.  Useful for HUD pin labels onto 3D objects.  See `getWorldToScreenWithViewport` for an arbitrary-viewport variant. Reads `gl.framebuffer_width` / `gl.framebuffer_height` (current canvas dimensions used by the renderer), `gl.cull_distance_near` / `gl.cull_distance_far` (clip-plane distances forwarded to the `Ex` variant), `position`, `cam`.  No mutation. Returned coordinate may be clamped or off-screen - caller is responsible for visibility culling.

→ **raylib:** `GetWorldToScreen` — Get screen space position for a 3d world space position

#### `camera.getWorldToScreen2D`

```zig
    pub fn getWorldToScreen2D(position: Vec2, cam: Camera2D) Vec2
```

Project a world-space 2D point into screen-space using a Camera2D.

→ **raylib:** `GetWorldToScreen2D` — Get screen space position for a 2d camera world space position

#### `camera.getWorldToScreenWithViewport`

```zig
    pub fn getWorldToScreenWithViewport(
        gl: *const GlState,
        position: Vec,
        cam: Camera3D,
        width: i32,
        height: i32,
    ) Vec2
```

As `getWorldToScreen`, but with explicit `width` and `height`. Reads `gl.cull_distance_near` / `gl.cull_distance_far` (clip planes for the projection matrix), `position`, `cam`, `width`, `height`.  No mutation.

#### `camera.updateCamera`

```zig
    pub fn updateCamera(
        cam: *Camera3D,
        mode: CameraMode,
        dt: f32,
        input_state: *const InputState,
    ) void
```

Drive the cam using keyboard + mouse + (if available) gamepad input.  Mode picks one of the high-level behaviors (free / orbital / first-person / third-person).  `dt` is seconds since last frame (pass `f.time.delta_time`); used to make movement speed framerate-independent.

→ **raylib:** `UpdateCamera` — Update camera position for selected mode

#### `camera.updateCameraPro`

```zig
    pub fn updateCameraPro(
        cam: *Camera3D,
        movement: Vec,
        rotation: Vec,
        zoom: f32,
    ) void
```

Lower-level cam drive: pass desired movement (forward/right/up in world units), rotation (pitch/yaw/roll in degrees), and zoom delta directly.  Useful when the app handles input itself.

→ **raylib:** `UpdateCameraPro` — Update camera movement/rotation


#### `core.getShaderResolution`

```zig
    pub fn getShaderResolution(window: *const WindowState) [2]f32
```

Canvas size in BACKING pixels (= logical × DPR), packed as `[2]f32` for direct push into a `setShaderValue(..., .vec2)` call.  Backing pixels are the units of `gl_FragCoord.xy` in GLSL, so this is the right value for a `u_resolution`-style uniform when the shader compares against `gl_FragCoord`.  For Zig-shader-pipeline shaders that use `frag_tex_coord * u_resolution` (no `gl_FragCoord`), any consistent unit works — logical px (`getScreenWidth` / `getScreenHeight`) is simpler in that case.  See `examples/mandelbrot.zig` for that style and `examples/shader_uniforms.zig` for the `gl_FragCoord` style.


#### `core.isWindowResized`

```zig

```

Read-and-clear the "canvas was resized since last call" flag. raylib: `IsWindowResized`.  Backed by a `ResizeObserver`; first call after a resize returns true and clears the flag, subsequent calls return false until the next resize.

→ **raylib:** `IsWindowResized` — Check if window has been resized last frame

#### `core.loadDroppedFiles`

```zig
    pub fn loadDroppedFiles(gpa: Allocator) Allocator.Error!DroppedFiles
```

Pull all dropped files into Zig-owned memory.  Returns a slice of `DroppedFile`; both `name` and `bytes` are owned by `gpa` and must be freed via `unloadDroppedFiles(gpa, files)`. raylib: `LoadDroppedFiles()`.  After this returns, the JS-side table is NOT yet cleared - call `unloadDroppedFiles` to clear.

→ **raylib:** `LoadDroppedFiles` — Load dropped filepaths

#### `core.loadRandomSequence`

```zig
    pub fn loadRandomSequence(
        gpa: Allocator,
        count: u32,
        min_in: i32,
        max_in: i32,
    ) Allocator.Error![]i32
```

Return an owned slice of `count` distinct integers chosen at random from the inclusive range [`min`, `max`].  Caller must `unloadRandomSequence(allocator, seq)` to free. Returns `error.OutOfMemory` if either the output slice or the internal pool buffer can't be allocated.  Returns `&.{}` (empty non-error slice) when `count == 0` or `count > range` - those are well-defined "no work to do" cases, not failures. The slice element type is `i32` rather than `i32`; on every target zimr supports these are the same width but `i32` is the idiomatic Zig name.  Internally the body still works in `i32` because `getRandomValue` (the rcore RNG) is typed that way; the cast happens at the slice-write boundary. Algorithm is reservoir-style for small counts; sufficient for shuffles/permutations.

#### `gestures.update`

```zig
    pub fn update(
        state: *GesturesState,
        input_state: *const InputState,
        time: *const TimeState,
    ) void
```

Per-frame update.  Called by the runtime BEFORE the user's `update` so they see fresh gesture state.  Internally: 1. Reads `input.state.touch` to detect touch-set transitions since last frame, calls `process` for each. 2. Applies HOLD escalation: if a TAP/DOUBLETAP was emitted last frame and a finger is still down (count > 0), transition to HOLD. 3. Resets transient gestures (SWIPE_*) to NONE - they fire once per finger lift.

### `runtime.input`

#### `input.disableCursor`

```zig
    pub fn disableCursor(state: *InputState) void
```

#### `input.getCharPressed`

```zig
    pub fn getCharPressed(state: *InputState) ?u21
```

Drain the unicode-codepoint queue, returning oldest first. Returns `null` when empty.  `u21` covers the full Unicode range (max codepoint `0x10FFFF` = 1 114 111 fits in 21 bits). Mutating: drains the queue.

→ **raylib:** `GetCharPressed` — Get char pressed (unicode), call it multiple times for chars queued, returns 0 when the queue is empty

#### `input.getGamepadAxisCount`

```zig
    pub fn getGamepadAxisCount(
        state: *const InputState,
        gamepad: i32,
    ) i32
```

Number of axes the gamepad reports.  We always allocate `MAX_GAMEPAD_AXES` slots in the input state but a Web Gamepad API device might report fewer - the surplus stay at 0.0.  For now we just return `MAX_GAMEPAD_AXES` if the gamepad is connected, matching what the JS host populates.  Future work: track per-pad axis count via a separate state field.

→ **raylib:** `GetGamepadAxisCount` — Get axis count for a gamepad


#### `input.getGamepadButtonPressed`

```zig
    pub fn getGamepadButtonPressed(state: *const InputState) ?@import("zig").GamepadButton
```

Returns the index of the last-pressed gamepad button (across all connected gamepads), or `null` if no button is currently pressed. `null` replaces the old `0` sentinel which collided with the real `@import("types.zig").GamepadButton.unknown` value (raylib used 0 here even though it's a legitimate enum tag).

→ **raylib:** `GetGamepadButtonPressed` — Get the last gamepad button pressed

#### `input.getGamepadName`

```zig
    pub fn getGamepadName(
        state: *const InputState,
        gamepad: i32,
    ) ?[*:0]const u8
```

Return a static name string for the gamepad, or `null` if not connected.  In a browser environment the Gamepad API exposes `gamepad.id` (a free-form string); for now we return a generic "Gamepad N" placeholder.  Future work: pass the JS-side id through to wasm via a string pool.

#### `input.getKeyName`

```zig
    pub fn getKeyName(key: KeyboardKey) ?[]const u8
```

Map a `KeyboardKey` to its display name.  Returns null for unknown keys.  Names follow JS `KeyboardEvent.code` / W3C UI Events convention (e.g. `KEY_F1` → "F1", `KEY_LEFT_BRACKET` → "BracketLeft", `KEY_KP_0` → "Numpad0"), which is the natural choice in a browser runtime - matches what the user actually sees in devtools when they inspect a keypress event. Letters and digits return their character form ("A", "5") for ergonomic display in keybinding overlays.

#### `input.getKeyPressed`

```zig
    pub fn getKeyPressed(state: *InputState) ?KeyboardKey
```

Drain the keycode press queue, returning oldest first.  Returns `null` when the queue is empty - `null` replaces the old `0` sentinel which collided with the real `KeyboardKey.null` value. Mirrors raylib's destructive read semantics. `KeyboardKey` is non-exhaustive: keycodes pushed by the JS shim that don't correspond to a named tag still round-trip through `@intFromEnum` cleanly, callers just won't get a meaningful tag match for them. Mutating: drains the queue.

→ **raylib:** `GetKeyPressed` — Get key pressed (keycode), call it multiple times for keys queued, returns 0 when the queue is empty

#### `input.getMouseCursor`

```zig
    pub fn getMouseCursor(state: *const InputState) MouseCursor
```

Return the last cursor value set via `setMouseCursor`.  Defaults to `.default` for a freshly-constructed `InputState`.  Pure read - never touches the JS bridge.

#### `input.getMouseDelta`

```zig
    pub fn getMouseDelta(state: *const InputState) Vec2
```

→ **raylib:** `GetMouseDelta` — Get mouse delta between frames

#### `input.getMouseDragDelta`

```zig
    pub fn getMouseDragDelta(
        state: *InputState,
        button: @import("zig").MouseButton,
        lock_threshold: f32,
    ) Vec2
```

Cursor displacement since the most recent press of `button`. Returns `(0, 0)` if the button isn't held OR the threshold hasn't been exceeded yet - same gating as `isMouseDragging` so the two can be paired without conditionals.  Threshold semantics match `isMouseDragging`. Once the threshold is crossed on a given press, the sticky `drag_started` flag means subsequent reads return the live displacement even if the caller has reset the anchor and the next move is small.  Important for slow touch drags. Common use: a custom-widget drag handler reads delta each frame, applies it as a position offset, and calls `resetMouseDragDelta` to make the next read incremental.

#### `input.getShaderMouse`

```zig
    pub fn getShaderMouse(
        state: *const InputState,
        window: *const WindowState,
    ) [2]f32
```

Mouse position in BACKING pixels with Y flipped to GL convention (origin at bottom-left).  Drop-in for `u_mouse`-style shader uniforms compared against `gl_FragCoord`.  `getMousePosition` returns logical (CSS) pixels with browser-space Y (origin at top) — the units the rest of the draw API expects. `gl_FragCoord` in GLSL is in backing pixels with Y up, so mixing the two silently works on desktop (DPR=1, height ≈ logical) but is off by a factor of DPR on phones.  Use this helper to keep shader-uniform math unit-clean.

#### `input.isKeyChordPressed`

```zig
    pub fn isKeyChordPressed(
        state: *const InputState,
        chord: KeyChord,
        opts: ShortcutOpts,
    ) bool
```

True if `chord.key` was pressed THIS frame AND the modifier state matches exactly.  Edge-triggered on the key, level- triggered on modifiers.  Pass `opts.repeat = true` to also fire on auto-repeat events. Modifier rules: each `chord.{ctrl,shift,alt,super}` field must match the held state of EITHER the left- or right-side physical key.  `chord.ctrl = true` matches if `left_control` OR `right_control` is held.  `chord.ctrl = false` requires BOTH released - Ctrl+S won't fire a bare-S chord.

#### `input.isMouseDragging`

```zig
    pub fn isMouseDragging(
        state: *InputState,
        button: @import("zig").MouseButton,
        lock_threshold: f32,
    ) bool
```

True when `button` is held AND the cursor has moved more than `lock_threshold` pixels from the position it was pressed at. Pass `-1` to use `MOUSE_DRAG_THRESHOLD_DEFAULT`.  Use this to distinguish a click (small movement) from a drag (significant movement) - typical pattern: `if (isMouseDragging(state, .left, -1)) continueDragOp(getMouseDragDelta(state, .left, -1))`. Threshold uses L∞ (Chebyshev) distance - same shape as imgui's, matching dx² + dy² > t² with a single comparison on |Δ| max.

#### `input.isMouseHoveringRect`

```zig
    pub fn isMouseHoveringRect(
        state: *const InputState,
        x0: f32,
        y0: f32,
        x1: f32,
        y1: f32,
    ) bool
```

True if the current mouse position is inside the axis-aligned rectangle `(x0..x1, y0..y1)`.  Edges are inclusive on the near side, exclusive on the far side (imgui semantics - half- open interval to avoid double-counting adjacent rects in a gallery layout).  Coordinates use the same space as `getMousePosition` - canvas CSS pixels in `.responsive` scale mode, the init-time logical box in `.stretch` mode.


#### `input.setMouseCursor`

```zig
    pub fn setMouseCursor(state: *InputState, cursor: MouseCursor) void
```

Set the canvas cursor style.  raylib: `SetMouseCursor(int cursor)`. Maps the MouseCursor enum to a CSS cursor keyword on the JS side AND mirrors the value on `state.mouse.current_cursor` so a later `getMouseCursor` returns the same answer (CSS doesn't let us query the live canvas cursor - the spec returns the computed value, not the inline style, and Chrome reports it as `auto` regardless of what was set). Reads: `state.mouse.current_cursor` (no-op shortcut - repeated `setMouseCursor(.default)` calls only hit the JS bridge once). Mutates: `state.mouse.current_cursor`; calls `dom.set_mouse_cursor` (which writes `canvas.style.cursor`).

→ **raylib:** `SetMouseCursor` — Set mouse cursor

Read a rectangle of pixels from the active framebuffer into the caller-owned `pixels` buffer.  raylib's `rlCopyFramebuffer` thin- wraps `glReadPixels`; same semantics here - caller picks format (e.g. `GL_RGBA = 0x1908`) and type (e.g. `GL_UNSIGNED_BYTE = 0x1401`). `pixels` must be at least `width * height * pixel_size` bytes, where pixel_size depends on `format`/`type`.  For the common RGBA/UNSIGNED_BYTE case, that's 4 bytes/pixel.

→ **raylib:** `rlCopyFramebuffer` — Copy framebuffer pixel data to internal buffer

```zig

    source_ptr: [*c]const u8,
    source_len: usize,

) u32
```

#### `rlLoadShaderCode`

```zig
pub fn rlLoadShaderCode(
    state: *GlState,
    vs_code_ptr: [*c]const u8,
    vs_code_len: usize,
    fs_code_ptr: [*c]const u8,
    fs_code_len: usize,
) u32
```

Compile + link a vertex+fragment shader pair from source code. Either `vs_code` or `fs_code` may be empty (length 0) - in that case raylib's default vertex/fragment shader is used in its place, which is the common pattern for post-process shaders that only override the fragment stage. Pure-byte-slice inputs match the wasm ABI cleanly and avoid the raylib C-string convention which doesn't survive the JS boundary.

#### `rlLoadShaderProgramEx`

```zig
pub fn rlLoadShaderProgramEx(vs_id: u32, fs_id: u32) u32
```

#### `rlLoadTexture`

```zig
pub fn rlLoadTexture(
    data: ?*const anyopaque,
    width: i32,
    height: i32,
    format: i32,
    mipmap_count: i32,
) callconv(.c) u32
```

Upload pixel data to a fresh GPU texture, configure default parameters, return the new id.  Returns 0 on failure (unsupported format, GL allocation failure).  `mipmapCount` indicates how many mipmap levels the caller has data for in `data` - pass 1 if you only have the base level.  Pre-existing mipmaps are uploaded in-line; new ones get generated by `rlGenTextureMipmaps` if you want them. Mirrors raylib's `rlLoadTexture(data, width, height, format, mipmapCount)`.

→ **raylib:** `rlLoadTexture` — Load texture data

#### `rlLoadTextureCubemap`

```zig
pub fn rlLoadTextureCubemap(
    face_data: ?*const anyopaque,
    face_size: i32,
    format: i32,
) callconv(.c) u32
```

Create a cubemap texture and upload all six face images.  Each face is `face_size × face_size` pixels in `format`; the caller passes a pointer to the start of `face_size² × bytes_per_pixel × 6` bytes laid out face-by-face in raylib's canonical face order: 0: +X (right) 1: -X (left) 2: +Y (top) 3: -Y (bottom) 4: +Z (front) 5: -Z (back) `face_data` may be null to allocate empty faces (useful for FBO color targets that the GPU writes into via cubemap face attachments). Mipmap levels beyond level 0 aren't uploaded - callers wanting mipmaps can call `gl.generateMipmap(TEXTURE_CUBE_MAP)` after this.  Filtering defaults to LINEAR + clamp-to-edge wrapping (matches the typical skybox use case where wrapping artefacts at face seams are unacceptable).

→ **raylib:** `rlLoadTextureCubemap` — Load texture cubemap data

#### `rlLoadTextureDepth`

```zig
pub fn rlLoadTextureDepth(
    width: i32,
    height: i32,
    use_renderbuffer: bool,
) u32
```

Allocate a depth attachment - either a real depth texture (so it's sampleable, useful for shadow mapping) or a renderbuffer (cheaper, write-only).  Returns the new id (interpret per `use_renderbuffer`). Mirrors raylib's `rlLoadTextureDepth` for the WebGL2 path: uses `DEPTH_COMPONENT24` as the storage format (WebGL2 supports it directly).  WebGL1's quirky `DEPTH_COMPONENT` unsized format isn't needed here.

→ **raylib:** `rlLoadTextureDepth` — Load depth texture/renderbuffer (to be attached to fbo)

```zig
) void
```

Multiply the current matrix by an orthographic projection.  Like `rlFrustum`, operand order is math `ortho * current`.

→ **raylib:** `rlOrtho`

#### `rlPopMatrix`

```zig
pub fn rlPopMatrix(state: *GlState) void
```

Pop the most recently pushed matrix back into the current target. On the final pop, clears `transformRequired` and (in modelview mode) switches the target back from `transform` to the modelview matrix. **transformRequired clears unconditionally on an empty stack.** The C source gates the clear on `currentMatrixMode == RL_MODELVIEW`, which leaks state when a push happens in MODELVIEW but the matching pop happens after a switch to PROJECTION: the flag stays true into the next frame, redirecting subsequent modelview writes to `state.transform` and breaking 3D rendering whose matrices land in the wrong slot.  The flag is only meaningful while at least one matrix is pushed; the empty-stack invariant must zero it.

→ **raylib:** `rlPopMatrix` — Pop latest inserted matrix from stack

#### `rlPushMatrix`

```zig
pub fn rlPushMatrix(state: *GlState) void
```

Push the current matrix onto the matrix stack.  Switches subsequent rl* matrix writes onto the per-draw `transform` matrix while in MODELVIEW mode - see the design note at the top of this file.

→ **raylib:** `rlPushMatrix` — Push the current matrix to stack

#### `rlResizeFramebuffer`

```zig
pub fn rlResizeFramebuffer(width: i32, height: i32) void
```

raylib parity stub - `rlResizeFramebuffer(width, height)` is a no-op on hardware-accelerated GL paths (it only does work in raylib's software renderer, behind `#if defined(GRAPHICS_API_OPENGL_SOFTWARE)`). On WebGL2, framebuffers are owned by the user - to resize a render texture, call `z.textures.resizeRenderTexture(target, new_w, new_h)` which unloads + reloads the FBO and its attachments.

→ **raylib:** `rlResizeFramebuffer` — Resize internal framebuffer

#### `rlRotatef`

```zig
pub fn rlRotatef(
    state: *GlState,
    angle: f32,
    x_in: f32,
    y_in: f32,
    z_in: f32,
) void
```

Post-multiply the current matrix by a rotation: math `current = current * R`.  `angle` is in degrees; raylib's API choice - we keep it for parity.

→ **raylib:** `rlRotatef` — Multiply the current matrix by a rotation matrix

#### `rlScalef`

```zig
pub fn rlScalef(
    state: *GlState,
    x: f32,
    y: f32,
    z: f32,
) void
```

Post-multiply the current matrix by a scale: math `current = current * S`.

→ **raylib:** `rlScalef` — Multiply the current matrix by a scaling matrix

#### `rlScissor`

```zig
pub fn rlScissor(x: i32, y: i32, width: i32, height: i32) void
```

Set the scissor rectangle (in pixels, framebuffer coords).  Has no effect until `rlEnableScissorTest` is called.  WebGL2's `glScissor` throws `GL_INVALID_VALUE` on a negative width or height.  We assert in debug so the *caller* gets blamed at the site that computed the bad rect (otherwise the failure surfaces here, far from the source), and clamp to 0 in release so prod degrades to "empty clip" instead of a hard GL error. ui_dock_basic hit this once: dockspace size was computed as `sh - margin - 180` and went negative on small viewports.

→ **raylib:** `rlScissor` — Scissor test


```zig
pub fn rlSetMatrixModelview(

    m: Matrix,

```

```zig
pub fn rlSetMatrixProjection(

```

#### `rlSetPointSize`

```zig
pub fn rlSetPointSize(
    state: *GlState,
    size: f32,
) void
```

Set the point size for `RL_POINTS` primitives.  On desktop GL this calls `glPointSize`; in WebGL2 there's no API for it (the shader writes `gl_PointSize`).  zimr stores the value so `rlGetPointSize` can return it; user shaders that want to honour it should read a uniform set from this value.

→ **raylib:** `rlSetPointSize` — Set the point drawing size

#### `rlSetShader`

```zig
pub fn rlSetShader(
    state: *GlState,
    id: u32,
    locs: ?[*]i32,
) void
```

Switch the active shader for the batched renderer.  Unlike `rlEnableShader`, this DOES flush the current batch first (since the upload uniforms / per-vertex layout differ between programs). `locs` may be null - in that case the default shader's locs are reused, which is appropriate for shaders that share the default's attribute layout (most post-process shaders do).

→ **raylib:** `rlSetShader` — Set shader currently active (id and locations)

#### `rlSetTexture`

```zig
pub fn rlSetTexture(
    state: *GlState,
    id: u32,
) void
```

Bind a texture for the current draw call.  If `id` differs from the active draw call's texture, a new draw call slot is opened. `id == 0` rebinds the default white texture. In raylib's full implementation this also handles the QUAD-overflow → flush case; we leave that to rlgl_gpu's check at flush time so this function can stay GL-free.

→ **raylib:** `rlSetTexture` — Set current texture for render batch and check buffers limits

#### `rlSetUniform`

```zig
pub fn rlSetUniform(
    loc: i32,
    value: ?*const anyopaque,
    uniform_type: i32,
    count: i32,
) callconv(.c) void
```

Type-dispatched uniform setter.  `value` points to `count` packed elements of `uniform_type`.  E.g. for `RL_SHADER_UNIFORM_VEC3` with `count=2`, `value` must point at 6 floats.  Mirrors raylib's `rlSetUniform(loc, value, uniformType, count)` exactly - including the SAMPLER2D case which uploads `count` ints (sampler unit indices) the same way `RL_SHADER_UNIFORM_INT` does.

→ **raylib:** `rlSetUniform` — Set shader value uniform

#### `rlSetUniformMatrices`

```zig
pub fn rlSetUniformMatrices(loc: i32, mats: []const Matrix) void
```

Upload an array of matrices to a `uniform mat4 name[N]` slot. Used for skinning bone palettes and any "many transforms" pattern.  `mats.len` is the number of mat4s, not floats internally flattened to N×16 floats and pushed in one call. No-op for empty `mats` or invalid `loc`.

→ **raylib:** `rlSetUniformMatrices` — Set shader value matrices

#### `rlSetUniformMatrix`

```zig
pub fn rlSetUniformMatrix(loc: i32, mat: Matrix) void
```

Set a 4×4 matrix uniform.  WebGL2 disallows the GL3+ transpose flag, so we always upload column-major.  raylib's `Matrix` is already in that layout (see types.zig), and `matrixToFloatV` produces the right ordering.

→ **raylib:** `rlSetUniformMatrix` — Set shader value matrix

#### `rlSetUniformSampler`

```zig
pub fn rlSetUniformSampler(
    state: *GlState,
    loc: i32,
    texture_id: u32,
) void
```

Set a sampler-uniform pointing at an additional texture (i.e. something other than the default `texture0`).  Allocates the next free slot in `state.activeTextureId[]`, uploads the unit index to the shader, and lets the next batch flush bind the texture to that unit. If the texture is already bound to a slot, reuses it.

→ **raylib:** `rlSetUniformSampler` — Set shader value sampler

#### `rlSetVertexAttribute`

```zig
pub fn rlSetVertexAttribute(
    index: u32,
    comp_size: i32,
    @"type": i32,
    normalized: bool,
    stride: i32,
    offset: usize,
) void
```

Configure the vertex attribute layout for the currently-bound VAO + VBO.  Mirrors `glVertexAttribPointer`; `offset` is the byte offset into the VBO (0 for tightly-packed attributes).

→ **raylib:** `rlSetVertexAttribute` — Set vertex attribute data configuration

#### `rlSetVertexAttributeDivisor`

```zig
pub fn rlSetVertexAttributeDivisor(index: u32, divisor: u32) void
```

Set the per-instance step rate for a vertex attribute.  `divisor=0` (the default) means the attribute advances per-vertex; `divisor=1` means it advances per-instance (the same value is used for every vertex of one instance, then advances for the next instance). Higher divisors are valid but rarely useful.

→ **raylib:** `rlSetVertexAttributeDivisor` — Set vertex attribute data divisor

#### `rlTexCoord2f`

```zig

```

Set the active texcoord - applies to every subsequent rlVertex* until changed.

#### `rlTextureParameters`

```zig
pub fn rlTextureParameters(
    id: u32,
    param: i32,
    value: i32,
) void
```

Set wrap or filter parameter on a texture.  `param`/`value` use the `RL_TEXTURE_*` constants from `rlgl.zig` (which match GL's `pname` / `param` enum values). Anisotropic filter is silently ignored; the `EXT_texture_filter_anisotropic` extension isn't probed.  Mipmap- bias is also skipped (extension-only on WebGL2).

→ **raylib:** `rlTextureParameters` — Set texture parameters (filter, wrap)

#### `rlTranslatef`

```zig

```

→ **raylib:** `rlTranslatef` — Multiply the current matrix by a translation matrix

#### `GlState.setTexture`

```zig
    pub fn setTexture(self: *GlState, id: u32) void
```

Bind a texture by GL id.  On rlsw the same method name takes a `Handle(Texture)` - drawing code that needs rlsw-compatible texture binding has to dispatch on the renderer's type. For ui drawing today, rlsw runs without any user texture bound - sampler reads white, so vertex-colored quads come out correctly even for textured-quad cmds (glyphs render as solid colored blocks rather than rasterized glyphs).

---

## **sound.zig** — audio device + Wave/Sound/Music

### `sound.audio_device`

#### `audio_device.close`

```zig
    pub fn close(state: *AudioDeviceState) void
```

Tear down the audio device.  Stops all in-flight sounds. Idempotent.  After `close()`, callers must `init()` again before any further sound ops.

→ **raylib:** `CloseAudioDevice` — Close the audio device and context

#### `audio_device.getContextId`

```zig
    pub fn getContextId(state: *const AudioDeviceState) ContextId
```

Internal accessors for sibling namespaces.  Not part of the raylib parity surface.

#### `audio_device.getMasterVolume`

```zig
    pub fn getMasterVolume(state: *const AudioDeviceState) f32
```

Get the master volume (1.0 = unity).  Returns 1.0 if the device isn't ready.

→ **raylib:** `GetMasterVolume` — Get master volume (listener)

#### `audio_device.getSampleRate`

```zig
    pub fn getSampleRate(state: *const AudioDeviceState) u32
```

#### `audio_device.init`

```zig
    pub fn init(state: *AudioDeviceState) void
```

Initialize the audio device.  Must be called once before any Wave/Sound/Music op.  Idempotent - calling twice is harmless; the second call is a no-op. On wasm: creates a real Web Audio AudioContext.  Browsers start contexts suspended; the first user gesture (click / touch / keydown) auto-resumes via `resumeFromGesture` (Phase 4).  Until then, sounds queue silently. On host: marks ready with sample_rate = 48000 and a synthetic ctx_id so wrappers don't bail with the "no context" path.

→ **raylib:** `InitAudioDevice` — Initialize audio device and context

#### `audio_device.isReady`

```zig
    pub fn isReady(state: *const AudioDeviceState) bool
```

True iff the device is initialized and ready to accept Sound ops.  Higher-level wrappers gate every op on this.

→ **raylib:** `IsAudioDeviceReady` — Check if audio device has been initialized successfully

#### `audio_device.resumeFromGesture`

```zig
    pub fn resumeFromGesture(state: *const AudioDeviceState) void
```

Try to resume a suspended AudioContext.  Browsers reject the resume unless called from a user-gesture handler; the runtime wires this into the input layer.  Direct callers can also invoke it from their own click handler.

#### `audio_device.setMasterVolume`

```zig
    pub fn setMasterVolume(
        state: *const AudioDeviceState,
        volume: f32,
    ) void
```

Set the master volume.  Clamped to `[0, 10]` (matches the underlying `web.audio.setMasterVolume`).  Silent if the device isn't ready.

→ **raylib:** `SetMasterVolume` — Set master volume (listener)

### `sound.composer`

#### `composer.silence`

```zig
    pub fn silence(
        waves_state: *AllocTable,
        gpa: Allocator,
        duration_ms: u32,
        sample_rate: u32,
    ) !Wave
```

Silence-Wave generator.  Useful as a sequencer pad or as a time-aligned spacer between tones.  Caller owns the Wave; `waves.unload(waves_state, w)` to free it. Reads `duration_ms`, `sample_rate`.  Mutates `gpa.*` (allocates the zeroed PCM buffer).  Mutates `waves_state.entries` (registers the new buffer for tracking).

#### `composer.tone`

```zig
    pub fn tone(
        waves_state: *AllocTable,
        gpa: Allocator,
        opts: ToneOptions,
    ) !Wave
```

Generate a tone Wave according to `opts`.  Output is 16-bit signed PCM mono at the requested sample rate; caller `unload`s. The envelope shapes amplitude over the whole duration; with `.attack_ms = 0` and `.release_ms = 0` the output is a hard- gated tone (audible click at start/end at higher amplitudes). Sub-millisecond attack/release is fine - even 2 ms is enough to suppress the click. Synthesize a single tone Wave at the given frequency / shape / duration.  The result is 16-bit signed PCM at `opts.sample_rate`, `default_channels` (1) channels.  Caller owns the Wave and must `waves.unload(waves_state, w)` to free it. Reads `opts` (frequency, shape, duration, envelope, amplitude, sample_rate).  Mutates `gpa.*` (allocates the PCM buffer + registers it into `waves_state`).  Mutates `waves_state.entries` (registers the new buffer for tracking, so a later `waves.unload` can find + free it).

### `sound.music`

#### `music.getTimeLength`

```zig
    pub fn getTimeLength(
        state: *const MusicTable,
        track: Music,
    ) f32
```

Total track length in seconds.  Returns 0 for OGG tracks still decoding asynchronously (post-decode duration isn't surfaced - see `isReady` doc).

#### `music.getTimePlayed`

```zig
    pub fn getTimePlayed(
        state: *const MusicTable,
        track: Music,
    ) f32
```

Elapsed playback time in seconds.  For looped tracks, modulo the track's duration.  Returns 0 for stopped / not-yet-loaded tracks.

#### `music.isPlaying`

```zig
    pub fn isPlaying(
        state: *const MusicTable,
        track: Music,
    ) bool
```

Whether the track is currently playing (not stopped, not paused, not naturally finished - though looped tracks never finish naturally).

→ **raylib:** `IsMusicStreamPlaying` — Check if music is playing

#### `music.isReady`

```zig
    pub fn isReady(
        state: *MusicTable,
        track: Music,
    ) bool
```

Whether the music has loaded and is ready to play. Promotes async OGG decodes to bound buffers as a side effect when polling.

#### `music.isValid`

```zig
    pub fn isValid(
        state: *const MusicTable,
        track: Music,
    ) bool
```

Whether the Music slot is allocated and not yet unloaded.

#### `music.loadFromMemory`

```zig
    pub fn loadFromMemory(
        state: *MusicTable,
        device: *const AudioDeviceState,
        waves_state: *AllocTable,
        gpa: Allocator,
        file_type: []const u8,
        bytes: []const u8,
    ) !Music
```

Load a `Music` stream from in-memory bytes.  Dispatches via `Format.detect`: sync decode for WAV (full PCM upload as a Web Audio buffer), async for OGG (kicks off `web.audio.decodeOggBytes`; entry sits in "loading" until `isReady` promotes it). Reads `device.ctx_id` (sample-rate target + Web Audio context for buffer upload), `device.is_ready` (early-out on closed device).  Mutates `state.entries` (allocates one slot per successful load).  Mutates `waves_state.entries` for the WAV path (transient - the temporary `Wave` is unloaded before return).  Mutates `gpa.*` (decode buffers + resampled output + table allocations). `file_type` is currently unused - format dispatch uses byte sniffing.  Kept on the signature to mirror raylib's `LoadMusicStreamFromMemory` shape.

→ **raylib:** `LoadMusicStreamFromMemory` — Load music stream from data

#### `music.pause`

```zig
    pub fn pause(
        state: *MusicTable,
        track: Music,
    ) void
```

Pause the current play.  Resume with `resumeMusic`.

→ **raylib:** `PauseMusicStream` — Pause music playing

#### `music.play`

```zig
    pub fn play(
        state: *MusicTable,
        track: Music,
    ) void
```

Start (or restart) playback.  Stops any in-flight playback of THIS track first (raylib parity - Music is single-shot per track, unlike Sound which can layer). If `seek` was previously called, playback starts at the requested offset rather than from 0; the offset is consumed (subsequent plays without a new seek start at 0 again).

→ **raylib:** `PlayMusicStream` — Start music playing

#### `music.resumeMusic`

```zig
    pub fn resumeMusic(
        state: *MusicTable,
        track: Music,
    ) void
```

Resume paused playback.  Named `resumeMusic` to dodge Zig's `resume` keyword.

#### `music.seek`

```zig
    pub fn seek(
        state: *MusicTable,
        track: Music,
        position_seconds: f32,
    ) void
```

Seek to `position_seconds` within the track.  Stops any in-flight playback (Web Audio source nodes can't be re- positioned after `start`) and starts a fresh source from the requested offset.  Position is clamped to `[0, duration]` JS-side. Whether playback resumes from the new position depends on whether the track was playing before - `seek` preserves the playing/paused state.  Seeking a stopped track just records the new start position; the next `play` will use it.

→ **raylib:** `SeekMusicStream` — Seek music to a position (in seconds)

#### `music.setLooping`

```zig
    pub fn setLooping(
        state: *MusicTable,
        track: Music,
        looping: bool,
    ) void
```

Set whether the track loops.  Takes effect on the NEXT `play` call (Web Audio's source-node loop flag is set at construction time).

#### `music.setPan`

```zig
    pub fn setPan(
        state: *MusicTable,
        track: Music,
        pan: f32,
    ) void
```

Set per-track pan.  Clamped to `[-1, 1]`.

#### `music.setPitch`

```zig
    pub fn setPitch(
        state: *MusicTable,
        track: Music,
        pitch: f32,
    ) void
```

Set per-track pitch.  Clamped to `[0.0625, 16.0]`.

#### `music.setVolume`

```zig
    pub fn setVolume(
        state: *MusicTable,
        track: Music,
        volume: f32,
    ) void
```

Set per-track volume.  Stored on the entry; takes effect on the next `play`.  Clamped to `[0, 10]`.

#### `music.stop`

```zig
    pub fn stop(
        state: *MusicTable,
        track: Music,
    ) void
```

Stop playback.  Position resets - subsequent `play` starts from the beginning.

→ **raylib:** `StopMusicStream` — Stop music playing

#### `music.unload`

```zig
    pub fn unload(
        state: *MusicTable,
        track: Music,
    ) void
```

Free the Music's resources.

→ **raylib:** `UnloadMusicStream` — Unload music stream

#### `music.update`

```zig
    pub fn update(
        state: *MusicTable,
        track: Music,
    ) void
```

Per-frame update hook.  raylib's Music backend uses this to keep the streaming buffer ring topped up; on the web our playback path doesn't need any per-frame work (it's all scheduled in the AudioContext).  Present for raylib parity - calling it has no effect here.

→ **raylib:** `UpdateMusicStream` — Update buffers for music streaming

### `sound.sounds`

#### `sounds.isPlaying`

```zig
    pub fn isPlaying(
        state: *const SoundTable,
        sound: Sound,
    ) bool
```

Whether the Sound's most-recently-started source is still playing (not stopped, not paused, not naturally finished).

→ **raylib:** `IsSoundPlaying` — Check if a sound is currently playing

#### `sounds.isReady`

```zig
    pub fn isReady(
        state: *SoundTable,
        sound: Sound,
    ) bool
```

Whether this Sound has finished loading (relevant for the async OGG path) and is ready for `play`.  WAV-loaded sounds are immediately ready.

#### `sounds.isValid`

```zig
    pub fn isValid(
        state: *const SoundTable,
        sound: Sound,
    ) bool
```

Whether this Sound's slot is allocated (has gone through loadFromWave / loadFromMemory / loadAlias and not yet unloaded).  Empty defaults / unloaded sounds report false.

→ **raylib:** `IsSoundValid` — Check if a sound is valid (data loaded and buffers initialized)

#### `sounds.loadAlias`

```zig
    pub fn loadAlias(
        state: *SoundTable,
        source: Sound,
    ) Sound
```

Build an alias of an existing Sound that shares its AudioBuffer.  On the web platform this is essentially free - Web Audio source nodes are already one-shot, so `play` on either the original or alias creates a fresh source node from the same buffer. Aliases do NOT own the buffer; `unload` on an alias just releases the table slot.  Unloading the original while aliases are alive leaves the JS-side buffer alive (Web Audio holds a ref via in-flight source nodes anyway), but future `play` calls on the alias will fail because the buffer_id is now stale on the JS side.  Don't do this; unload aliases before originals.

→ **raylib:** `LoadSoundAlias` — Load sound alias, new sound that shares the same sample data as the source sound, does not own the sound data

#### `sounds.loadFromMemory`

```zig
    pub fn loadFromMemory(
        state: *SoundTable,
        device: *const AudioDeviceState,
        waves_state: *AllocTable,
        gpa: Allocator,
        file_type: []const u8,
        bytes: []const u8,
    ) !Sound
```

Construct a Sound from raw bytes.  WAV path is sync (decodes immediately, returns ready Sound); OGG path is async (returns a Sound in "loading" state - `isReady` polls).  For OGG, the caller must keep `bytes` alive until the decode completes (we have a copy on the JS side, but we need the format for dispatch). Reads `device.is_ready` (early-out), `device.ctx_id` (sample rate + Web Audio context).  Mutates `state.entries` (one slot per successful load).  Mutates `waves_state.entries` for the WAV path (transient - the temporary Wave is unloaded before return).  Mutates `gpa.*` (decode + resample buffers + table allocations). Errors only on truly broken input (unknown format, decode errors for sync formats).

#### `sounds.loadFromWave`

```zig
    pub fn loadFromWave(
        state: *SoundTable,
        device: *const AudioDeviceState,
        gpa: Allocator,
        wave: Wave,
    ) !Sound
```

Build a Sound from an in-memory `Wave`.  Resamples to the device's sample rate, mixes/expands to stereo, uploads the buffer through the JS bridge.  The `Wave`'s sample data is NOT consumed - caller still owns its data and `unload`s it independently (or doesn't, if it wants to keep editing and reload). Reads `device.is_ready` (early-out on closed device), `device.ctx_id` (target sample rate + Web Audio context for upload).  Mutates `state.entries` (allocates one slot on success).  Mutates `gpa.*` (resampled buffer + table allocations; resampled buffer freed on return). Returns an empty Sound (`.stream.buffer = null`) if: - Audio device not initialized (`audio_device.init(device)` first) - Wave is invalid - Buffer upload failed (browser denied or out of memory)

→ **raylib:** `LoadSoundFromWave` — Load sound from wave data

#### `sounds.pause`

```zig
    pub fn pause(
        state: *SoundTable,
        sound: Sound,
    ) void
```

Pause the most-recently-started in-flight play.  Resume with `resumeSound` (named to dodge Zig's `resume` keyword collision; raylib calls this `ResumeSound`).

→ **raylib:** `PauseSound` — Pause a sound

#### `sounds.play`

```zig
    pub fn play(
        state: *SoundTable,
        sound: Sound,
    ) void
```

Start playing a Sound.  Each `play` call creates a fresh Web Audio source node (Web Audio nodes are one-shot); re-playing while a previous play is still active does NOT stop the previous one - you get two sources playing concurrently. `stop` stops only the most recently-started source (raylib parity).  For exhaustive cleanup, `unload`. Silent if the Sound isn't loaded yet (OGG decode pending) or has been unloaded.

→ **raylib:** `PlaySound` — Play a sound

#### `sounds.resumeSound`

```zig
    pub fn resumeSound(
        state: *SoundTable,
        sound: Sound,
    ) void
```

Resume a paused play.  Named `resumeSound` to dodge Zig's `resume` keyword.

→ **raylib:** `ResumeSound` — Resume a paused sound

#### `sounds.setPan`

```zig
    pub fn setPan(
        state: *SoundTable,
        sound: Sound,
        pan: f32,
    ) void
```

Set the stereo pan (`-1.0` = left, `0.0` = center, `+1.0` = right).  Same NEXT-`play` semantics as `setVolume`.

→ **raylib:** `SetSoundPan` — Set pan for a sound (-1.0 left, 0.0 center, 1.0 right)

#### `sounds.setPitch`

```zig
    pub fn setPitch(
        state: *SoundTable,
        sound: Sound,
        pitch: f32,
    ) void
```

Set the playback pitch (rate multiplier; 1.0 = native). Same NEXT-`play` semantics as `setVolume`.  Clamped to `[0.0625, 16.0]` matching `web.audio` conventions.

→ **raylib:** `SetSoundPitch` — Set pitch for a sound (1.0 is base level)

#### `sounds.setVolume`

```zig
    pub fn setVolume(
        state: *SoundTable,
        sound: Sound,
        volume: f32,
    ) void
```

Set the playback volume for this Sound.  Stored on the Sound's table entry; takes effect on the NEXT `play` call. (Web Audio's gain node lives inside the source-graph created by `play` - we'd need a per-Sound persistent gain node to retro-update in-flight plays, which costs CPU for the common case.  Future enhancement if needed.) Clamped to `[0, 10]` matching `web.audio` conventions.

→ **raylib:** `SetSoundVolume` — Set volume for a sound (1.0 is max level)

#### `sounds.stop`

```zig
    pub fn stop(
        state: *SoundTable,
        sound: Sound,
    ) void
```

Stop the most-recently-started in-flight play of this Sound. Idempotent / silent on already-stopped or never-played sounds.

→ **raylib:** `StopSound` — Stop playing a sound

#### `sounds.unload`

```zig
    pub fn unload(
        state: *SoundTable,
        sound: Sound,
    ) void
```

Free a Sound's resources.  For an owner: unloads the underlying buffer.  For an alias: releases the slot only.  Idempotent / silent on unloaded sounds.

→ **raylib:** `UnloadSound` — Unload sound

### `sound.streams`

#### `streams.isPlaying`

```zig
    pub fn isPlaying(
        state: *const StreamTable,
        stream: AudioStream,
    ) bool
```

Whether the stream is currently scheduling new audio. True iff `update` was called at least once and the stream isn't paused.

→ **raylib:** `IsAudioStreamPlaying` — Check if audio stream is playing

#### `streams.isProcessed`

```zig
    pub fn isProcessed(
        state: *const StreamTable,
        stream: AudioStream,
    ) bool
```

Whether the next chunk slot is "free" (head time is within `lookahead` of the current wall-clock).  Callers should produce + push more samples when this is true. On host (no real time advancing), always returns true the host stub doesn't model scheduling latency.

→ **raylib:** `IsAudioStreamProcessed` — Check if any audio stream buffers requires refill

#### `streams.isValid`

```zig
    pub fn isValid(
        state: *const StreamTable,
        stream: AudioStream,
    ) bool
```

Whether the stream has slot space for another chunk scheduled.  Always returns true on the web platform - we never run out of slots.  Present for raylib parity.

→ **raylib:** `IsAudioStreamValid` — Check if an audio stream is valid (buffers initialized)

#### `streams.load`

```zig
    pub fn load(
        state: *StreamTable,
        device: *const AudioDeviceState,
        sample_rate: u32,
        sample_size: u32,
        channels: u32,
    ) AudioStream
```

Create a new AudioStream at `(sample_rate, sample_size, channels)`.  `sample_size` is informational on the web callers always push f32 frames via `update`.  Channel counts other than 1 (mono) and 2 (stereo) are accepted but get downmixed to stereo on upload. Reads `device.is_ready` (early-out on closed device), `device.ctx_id` (Web Audio context for chunk uploads). Mutates `state.entries` (allocates one slot on success). Returns an empty AudioStream (`.buffer = null`) if the device isn't ready or the table is full.

→ **raylib:** `LoadAudioStream` — Load audio stream (to stream raw audio pcm data)

#### `streams.pause`

```zig
    pub fn pause(
        state: *StreamTable,
        stream: AudioStream,
    ) void
```

Pause the stream.  In-flight scheduled chunks continue playing (they're already in the browser's audio graph); new `update` calls become no-ops.

→ **raylib:** `PauseAudioStream` — Pause audio stream

#### `streams.play`

```zig
    pub fn play(
        state: *StreamTable,
        stream: AudioStream,
    ) void
```

Explicitly start the stream.  Streams already auto-start on their first `update` call, so this is mostly a no-op for already-streaming streams; for paused streams it has the same effect as `resumeStream`.  Provided for raylib parity.

→ **raylib:** `PlayAudioStream` — Play audio stream

#### `streams.resumeStream`

```zig
    pub fn resumeStream(
        state: *StreamTable,
        stream: AudioStream,
    ) void
```

Resume a paused stream.  Resets `primed` so the next `update` re-seeds `head_time` from the current wall-clock, avoiding a long catch-up if pause was held for a while.

#### `streams.setPan`

```zig
    pub fn setPan(
        state: *StreamTable,
        stream: AudioStream,
        pan: f32,
    ) void
```

Set per-stream pan.  Clamped to `[-1, 1]`.

→ **raylib:** `SetAudioStreamPan` — Set pan for audio stream (-1.0 left, 0.0 center, 1.0 right)

#### `streams.setPitch`

```zig
    pub fn setPitch(
        state: *StreamTable,
        stream: AudioStream,
        pitch: f32,
    ) void
```

Set per-stream pitch.  Clamped to `[0.0625, 16.0]`.

→ **raylib:** `SetAudioStreamPitch` — Set pitch for audio stream (1.0 is base level)

#### `streams.setVolume`

```zig
    pub fn setVolume(
        state: *StreamTable,
        stream: AudioStream,
        volume: f32,
    ) void
```

Set per-stream volume.  Applied to the next chunk pushed via `update`; in-flight chunks keep their original gain. Clamped to `[0, 10]`.

→ **raylib:** `SetAudioStreamVolume` — Set volume for audio stream (1.0 is max level)

#### `streams.stop`

```zig
    pub fn stop(
        state: *StreamTable,
        stream: AudioStream,
    ) void
```

Stop the stream and discard any in-flight scheduled chunks the bridge knows about.  Already-scheduled chunks in Web Audio's graph still play out (we have no per-chunk SourceId to cancel them).  After `stop`, the next `update` call re-primes head_time from the current wall-clock.

→ **raylib:** `StopAudioStream` — Stop audio stream

#### `streams.unload`

```zig
    pub fn unload(
        state: *StreamTable,
        stream: AudioStream,
    ) void
```

Free the stream's resources.  Stops any in-flight playback (Web Audio holds buffer references via source nodes anyway, so already-scheduled chunks may still play out).  Idempotent.

→ **raylib:** `UnloadAudioStream` — Unload audio stream and free memory

#### `streams.update`

```zig
    pub fn update(
        state: *StreamTable,
        stream: AudioStream,
        frames: []const f32,
    ) void
```

Push `frames` (interleaved f32 at the stream's sample_rate and channels) into the playback queue.  Schedules them to start at the stream's head time. Allocation: each `update` call allocates a Web Audio AudioBuffer.  Web Audio recycles its own buffer pool internally; we hold the last `RecycleRingSize` BufferIds and unload them in FIFO order so the JS side doesn't pile up garbage.  In the steady state this is a fixed-size working set. Silent if the stream isn't valid or the device isn't ready.

→ **raylib:** `UpdateAudioStream` — Update audio stream buffers with data

### `sound.waves`

#### `waves.copy`

```zig
    pub fn copy(
        state: *AllocTable,
        gpa: Allocator,
        wave: Wave,
    ) !Wave
```

Deep-copy a Wave (allocates fresh sample data).  The new wave is owned by `gpa`; caller `unload`s it.

#### `waves.crop`

```zig
    pub fn crop(
        state: *AllocTable,
        gpa: Allocator,
        wave: *Wave,
        init_frame: u32,
        final_frame: u32,
    ) !void
```

Crop the wave in-place to the frame range `[init_frame, final_frame)`. Frees the original sample data and replaces it with a freshly-allocated subrange.  Out-of-range frames are clamped to the wave's bounds; if the resulting range is empty the wave becomes empty (frameCount = 0, data = null).

#### `waves.exportToMemory`

```zig
    pub fn exportToMemory(
        gpa: Allocator,
        wave: Wave,
    ) ![]u8
```

Encode a Wave to RIFF/WAVE bytes.  Returns owned bytes; caller frees via `gpa.free`.  Output format mirrors the wave's input format - `wave.sampleSize` and the implicit format tag (PCM int for 8/16-bit, IEEE float for 32-bit) are written as-is. raylib's `ExportWave(wave, fileName)` writes to disk; on the web we have no filesystem, so this returns bytes the caller can hand to `dom.downloadBlob` or fetch upload.  Same name space, different I/O.

#### `waves.filter`

```zig
    pub fn filter(
        state: *AllocTable,
        gpa: Allocator,
        wave: *Wave,
        comptime filter_fn: anytype,
        args: anytype,
    ) !void
```

Apply a filter function in-place, freeing the original sample data once the filter has produced its replacement. Inspired by lightmix's `filter_with` pattern: filters take a Wave value, return a fresh Wave; the chain mutator handles ownership transfer + cleanup. The filter receives the current Wave and the args tuple; returns a fresh Wave (must be heap-allocated by the filter via `gpa`).  After this call, `wave.*` holds the new wave; the old one is freed. Composition example: try waves.filter(gpa, &w, fadeIn, .{ .ms = 50 }); try waves.filter(gpa, &w, normalize, .{}); try waves.filter(gpa, &w, lowpass, .{ .cutoff = 1500.0 });

#### `waves.format`

```zig
    pub fn format(
        state: *AllocTable,
        gpa: Allocator,
        wave: *Wave,
        sample_rate: u32,
        sample_size: u32,
        channels: u32,
    ) !void
```

Convert the wave to a desired sample rate / bit depth / channel count, in place.  Goes through the canonical f32 stereo intermediate via `toFloat32Stereo` + `resampleLinear`, then quantizes back to the requested bit depth. Supported `sample_size` values: 8, 16, 32.  Channel counts: 1 (mono), 2 (stereo).  Other values are unchanged. Note: 8-bit conversion is lossy; use 16-bit or 32-bit for quality.

#### `waves.isValid`

```zig
    pub fn isValid(wave: Wave) bool
```

Whether the Wave's parameters describe loaded, well-formed audio.

#### `waves.loadFromMemory`

```zig
    pub fn loadFromMemory(
        state: *AllocTable,
        gpa: Allocator,
        file_type: []const u8,
        bytes: []const u8,
    ) !Wave
```

Decode `bytes` into a `Wave`.  `file_type` is a hint string like ".wav" or ".ogg" - used to pick a codec when magic-byte sniffing is ambiguous.  Currently the magic-byte sniff (`Format.detect`) wins; `file_type` is informational. For sync-decodable formats (WAV) the decoded Wave is returned immediately.  For OGG (browser-only path) this errors with `error.OggRequiresAsyncDecode` - callers should use `sounds.loadFromMemory` instead, which has the async-friendly Sound type. Returns an empty Wave on decode failure (frameCount = 0, data = null) - matches raylib's contract that `IsWaveValid` distinguishes good from bad waves.

#### `waves.loadSamples`

```zig
    pub fn loadSamples(
        gpa: Allocator,
        wave: Wave,
    ) ![]f32
```

Decode a Wave to a fresh `[]f32` interleaved (per-channel layout matches the wave's channel count).  Mirror of raylib's `LoadWaveSamples`; caller frees via `unloadSamples`. For raylib parity the output channel count matches the input wave's.  For canonical-stereo output, callers should use `codecs.audio.wav.toFloat32Stereo` directly.

#### `waves.unload`

```zig
    pub fn unload(
        state: *AllocTable,
        wave: Wave,
    ) void
```

Free a Wave's sample data.  Idempotent on null/zero waves.

#### `waves.unloadSamples`

```zig
    pub fn unloadSamples(
        gpa: Allocator,
        samples: []f32,
    ) void
```

Free a buffer returned by `loadSamples`.

## **ui.zig** — immediate-mode UI (Dear ImGui-shaped)

#### `BoundedString`

```zig
pub fn BoundedString(comptime N: usize) type
```

Inline-buffered string with a fixed byte capacity and a stored length. The reflection inspector (P1.3) detects fields of this type and routes them through `inputText`, giving the caller mutable text editing without a heap allocator.  Layout: `[N]u8` buffer + `usize` length. Convertible to a `[]const u8` slice via `as_slice()` and writable in bulk via `set()`. Capacity is a comptime constant under the `capacity` declaration; `__is_bounded_string` is the marker the inspector checks for.  Usage: state: struct { player_name: z.ui.BoundedString(32) =.{}, } =.{}, //... u.inspect("Settings", &state); // player_name → text field  Why a wrapping struct instead of `[N:0]u8`? zimr's `inputText` takes a `(buf: []u8, len: *usize)` pair — the inspector can wire directly to those without rescanning the buffer for a sentinel every frame. And the public type is a clearer marker to a future reader than a sentinel byte.

#### `applyLongPressSynthesis`

```zig
pub fn applyLongPressSynthesis(
    ctx: *UiContext,
    snapshot: *InputSnapshot,
    input_state: *const InputState,
) void
```

Pure synthesis helper. Reads touch state from `input_state`, mutates `snapshot` to inject the right-click on the frame the long-press threshold crosses, and updates `ctx.long_press`.  Factored out of `beginFrame` so tests can drive it directly with a synthetic `InputState` + `InputSnapshot` pair, without needing a `Frame` / `GlState` harness.

#### `colorChannelToInt255`

```zig
pub fn colorChannelToInt255(v: f32) u8
```

Convert a 0.0–1.0 channel value to its 0–255 integer representation with imgui-compatible rounding (round-half- away-from-zero).  Out-of-range inputs clamp first.  Pure fn, used by the `.int_0_255` display path.

#### `ctxKeyPressedOrRepeat`

```zig
pub fn ctxKeyPressedOrRepeat(ctx: *UiContext, key: KeyCode) bool
```

`Ui.isKeyPressedOrRepeat` for callers that have a `*UiContext` but no `Ui` handle — same semantics, no construction ceremony. Initial-press case reads `keys[i].pressed_this_frame`; held- and-repeating case routes through raylib's OS-level timer.

#### `debugLogPush`

```zig
pub fn debugLogPush(
    ctx: *UiContext,
    kind: DebugEventKind,
    comptime fmt: []const u8,
    args: anytype,
) void
```

Append a debug event to the ring. Format-args are written into the inline buffer up to `DEBUG_LOG_MSG_MAX` bytes; longer messages truncate silently. When the ring is full, the oldest event is dropped via `orderedRemove(0)` so the latest cap-many events are always retained.  Cost when not at cap: one bufPrint + one struct copy. Cost at cap: same plus an O(N) shift inside `orderedRemove(0)`. Both well below the per-frame budget for realistic event rates.  Errors during format are swallowed: the event still lands with whatever bytes fit before the format ran out of room.

#### `debugLogSlice`

```zig
pub fn debugLogSlice(ctx: *const UiContext) []const DebugEvent
```

Borrow the populated events as a slice in oldest-to-newest order. Returned slice is valid until the next `debugLogPush`.

#### `dockWindowAsTab`

```zig
pub fn dockWindowAsTab(
    ctx: *DockContext,
    gpa: Allocator,
    leaf_id: Id,
    window_id: Id,
) Allocator.Error!void
```

Dock `window_id` as a new tab into the leaf `leaf_id`. Adds to the end of the tab list (rightmost tab); selects the new tab automatically. No-op if `leaf_id` doesn't refer to a leaf, or if the window is already in this leaf.

#### `effectiveItemWidth`

```zig
pub fn effectiveItemWidth(
    ctx: *UiContext,
    opts_width: f32,
    default_w: f32,
) f32
```

Resolve the width a widget should use for its primary input element. Priority (high to low): 1. `next_item_width` (set via `setNextItemWidth`; consumed) 2. top of `item_width_stack` (set via `pushItemWidth`) 3. `opts_width` (per-call-site option), if > 0 4. `default_w` (the widget's natural width if nothing else fires) `next_item_width` is cleared on read - the override is one-shot.

#### `except`

```zig
    pub fn except(self: DockNodeFlags, mask: DockNodeFlags) DockNodeFlags
```

Return `self` with every bit in `mask` cleared.

#### `findOrCreateWindow`

```zig
pub fn findOrCreateWindow(
    ctx: *UiContext,
    title: []const u8,
    opts: WindowOpts,
) *Window
```

#### `flushDockTabsToForeground`

```zig
pub fn flushDockTabsToForeground(ctx: *UiContext) void
```

walk the dockspace subtree rooted at `root_id` and emit a clickable tab bar across the top edge of every leaf. Tabs reflect the leaf's `window_ids` in order; the active tab matches `leaf.selected_window_id`. Click handling: if the mouse is over a tab AND the user pressed left this frame, set `selected_window_id` to that tab's window. The selected window's content shows from THIS frame's submission because findOrCreateWindow read pos/size from the leaf earlier, and openWindow's docked-and-selected check sees the just-updated selection. Tab clicks on a non-selected window therefore take effect on the SAME frame - no one-frame stutter. Tab labels: looked up via `ctx.windows.get(wid).name()`. Windows that haven't been submitted yet (dockBuilder ran before any `window()` call) get a fallback hex-id label so the bar is at least visible. Drains `ctx.dock.dockspaces_this_frame` and emits tabs into `ctx.foreground_dl`.  Called from `endFrame` in normal run-mode; called from the screenshot section's `renderToBytes` in host-test mode so single-frame PNG renders see the tabs.

#### `formatColorRgbHex`

```zig
pub fn formatColorRgbHex(
    rgba: [4]f32,
    has_alpha: bool,
    out: []u8,
) []const u8
```

Format an RGB triplet (or RGBA quad) into a `#RRGGBB[AA]` string.  Caller provides the destination buffer (must be at least 9 bytes for RGBA, 7 for RGB).  Returns the populated slice.  Channels are clamped to 0.0–1.0 before conversion so out-of-range inputs round-trip predictably.  Pure function — no UI state touched — so it's testable in isolation and reusable from non-widget code (e.g. logging, serialization).

#### `fromRaylib`

```zig
    pub fn fromRaylib(rk: KeyboardKey) ?KeyCode
```

Translate a raylib `KeyboardKey` to a zimr `KeyCode`. Returns `null` for raylib keys zimr doesn't surface (e.g. `kb_menu`, `null` sentinel). Translate a raylib `KeyboardKey` to a zimr `KeyCode`. Returns `null` for raylib keys zimr doesn't surface (e.g. `kb_menu`, `null` sentinel).

#### `hashInt`

```zig
pub fn hashInt(seed: Id, value: i64) Id
```

#### `hashStr`

```zig
pub fn hashStr(seed: Id, str: []const u8) Id
```

#### `intersect`

```zig
    pub fn intersect(self: DockNodeFlags, mask: DockNodeFlags) DockNodeFlags
```

Return only the bits set in BOTH `self` and `mask`. Used for the inheritor-transfer pattern above.

#### `isEdge`

```zig
    pub fn isEdge(self: Dir) bool
```

True for the four edge directions - useful for "split or tab" branches at call sites.

#### `isFirstChild`

```zig
    pub fn isFirstChild(self: Dir) bool
```

True when the dragged window should become the FIRST child (top of a vertical split, or left of a horizontal one). False for bottom/right. Determines child ordering in `splitNode`.

#### `keyEventSnapshot`

```zig
pub fn keyEventSnapshot(key: KeyCode) InputSnapshot
```

Test helper: returns an `InputSnapshot` with a single pressed-this-frame edge event for `key` set. Used by host- side tests that inject synthetic input via `beginFrameRaw`. Production code populates the snapshot via the inline-for loop in `beginFrame` — this helper exists only for tests.

#### `mergeItemFlags`

```zig
pub fn mergeItemFlags(parent: ItemFlags, child: ItemFlags) ItemFlags
```

Merge two `ItemFlags` per imgui's stack semantics: each bool is OR'd EXCEPT `auto_close_popups` which is AND'd (a parent disable of auto-close propagates). Used internally when pushing onto `item_flags_stack`.

#### `snapshotAltDown`

```zig
pub fn snapshotAltDown(s: *const InputSnapshot) bool
```

#### `snapshotCtrlDown`

```zig
pub fn snapshotCtrlDown(s: *const InputSnapshot) bool
```

#### `snapshotShiftDown`

```zig
pub fn snapshotShiftDown(s: *const InputSnapshot) bool
```

Convenience: held-state modifier checks that aggregate left+right sides. Same shape as the `Ui.isShiftDown` / `isCtrlDown` / `isAltDown` / `isSuperDown` helpers but takes a snapshot directly — used inside widget impl code that has a `*UiContext` but no `Ui` handle. Resolves to the same array lookup.

#### `snapshotSuperDown`

```zig
pub fn snapshotSuperDown(s: *const InputSnapshot) bool
```

#### `splitAxis`

```zig
    pub fn splitAxis(self: Dir) SplitAxis
```

The split axis implied by an edge direction. Top/bottom → vertical (stacked). Left/right → horizontal (side-by-side). Center isn't an edge - calling `splitAxis(.center)` is a programmer error; we return horizontal as a fallback rather than panicking so misuse degrades to wrong-but-not-crashing.

#### `toAttr`

```zig
    pub fn toAttr(self: InputMode) []const u8
```

Map to the HTML attribute string the JS bridge sends to the runtime.  All values are lowercase as the spec requires.

#### `toRaylib`

```zig
    pub fn toRaylib(kc: KeyCode) KeyboardKey
```

Inverse of `fromRaylib` — every `KeyCode` (except `MAX`) maps to a `types.KeyboardKey` of the same field name. Used by repeat-aware helpers that need to call back into the raylib input layer (which owns the OS-level repeat timer). Calling this on `KeyCode.MAX` is a logic error and will fail at compile time via the inline-for guard.

#### `undockWindow`

```zig
pub fn undockWindow(
    ctx: *DockContext,
    gpa: Allocator,
    window_id: Id,
) bool
```

Remove `window_id` from whatever leaf currently holds it. If that leaves the leaf empty AND it has a parent SPLIT, the sibling node takes the parent's slot and the parent is freed. Returns true if anything changed.

#### `widgetId`

```zig
pub fn widgetId(
    ctx: *UiContext,
    w: *Window,
    label: []const u8,
) Id
```

Compute a widget's ID by hashing `label` against the current ID scope. The scope is the top of `ctx.id_stack` if any `pushId`/`pushIdInt` is active, otherwise the current window's ID. ALL widget impls should use this rather than calling `hashStr(w.id, label)` directly - the difference matters when the caller wraps a loop in `pushIdInt(i)` to disambiguate IDs across iterations (e.g. `editArrayList`'s per-element delete buttons).

### `ui.CanvasTransform`

#### `CanvasTransform.applyLocal`

```zig
    pub fn applyLocal(self: CanvasTransform, p: Vec2) Vec2
```

Apply this transform to a point in child space: `out = p * scale + translate`.

#### `CanvasTransform.compose`

```zig
    pub fn compose(parent: CanvasTransform, child: CanvasTransform) CanvasTransform
```

Compose `child` ONTO `parent`. Result is the transform that applies `child` first, then `parent`. Used at push time so the stack always stores cumulative transforms — `top()` returns "everything stacked so far" directly, no walk needed.

#### `CanvasTransform.inverse`

```zig
    pub fn inverse(self: CanvasTransform) CanvasTransform
```

Invert the transform. Inverse exists iff scale[0] and scale[1] are both non-zero. Used by `localMouse` to pull screen-space mouse coords back into the canvas's authoring space. Caller responsibility: don't push a scale=0 transform if you plan to call localMouse.

### `ui.Clipper`

#### `Clipper.end`

```zig
    pub fn end(self: *Clipper) void
```

Manual termination, in case the caller breaks out of the step loop early. Equivalent to draining `step()` until it returns null. Safe to call multiple times. ImGui: `End()`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::End` — Child Windows

#### `Clipper.step`

```zig
    pub fn step(self: *Clipper) ?Range
```

Returns the row range to render this step, or null when done. Caller-side pattern: while (clip.step()) |r| {... render rows r.start..r.end... } On the first call (state ==.not_started): - If no rows are visible, advance cursor past the entire content height and return null. - Otherwise advance cursor to the visible-start row's position and return the visible range. On the second call (state ==.stepped): - Advance cursor past the remaining content (rows after visible_end through item_count). Return null.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::Step` — Call until it returns false. The DisplayStart/DisplayEnd fields will be set and you can process/draw those items.

### `ui.DrawList`

#### `DrawList.addArc`

```zig
    pub fn addArc(
        self: *DrawList,
        alloc: Allocator,
        center: Vec2,
        radius: f32,
        a0: f32,
        a1: f32,
        col: ColorU32,
        thickness: f32,
        segments: u32,
    ) void
```

Stroked arc from `a0` to `a1` radians on a circle of `radius` centered at `center`. When `a1 < a0`, sweep goes clockwise (matches naive interpretation). `segments = 0` auto-derives a count from the arc length and radius (matches the existing `n_segments = 0` convention on circles).

#### `DrawList.addArcFilled`

```zig
    pub fn addArcFilled(
        self: *DrawList,
        alloc: Allocator,
        center: Vec2,
        radius: f32,
        a0: f32,
        a1: f32,
        col: ColorU32,
        segments: u32,
    ) void
```

Filled arc (pie slice) — fan-triangulates from `center` to `segments + 1` points on the arc. When `a1 - a0 == 2π`, degenerates to a filled disk (equivalent to `addCircleFilled` minus the auto-segment-count convenience). `segments = 0` auto-derives.


Cubic Bezier curve. Drawn at replay via raylib's `drawLineBezierCubic`.


Filled polygon over the given points, fan-triangulated from `points[0]`. Assumes the polygon is convex; concave polygons will render with overlapping triangles (caller's problem). Returns silently with < 3 points.

#### `DrawList.addPolyline`

```zig
    pub fn addPolyline(
        self: *DrawList,
        alloc: Allocator,
        points: []const Vec2,
        col: ColorU32,
        thickness: f32,
        closed: bool,
    ) void
```

Record a polyline (line strip). Points are duplicated into `alloc` (the UiContext's frame arena) so they survive past the caller's frame. `closed = true` adds a final segment from `points[count-1]` back to `points[0]`. OOM drops the command silently.

Record a text command. String bytes are duplicated into `alloc` (which must be the UiContext's frame arena) so they outlive the caller's stack frame. `line_spacing` is captured at record time so the replay path doesn't need a separate parameter - multiple records in the same DrawList can have different line-spacing values if the user mutated `ctx.style.line_spacing` between calls.

```zig

```

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::Clear` — Clear selection

#### `DrawList.deinit`

```zig
    pub fn deinit(self: *DrawList, alloc: Allocator) void
```

Deinit the command list. Pass the same allocator used by the `add*` calls (in production, that's the UiContext's frame arena; in tests, it's typically `std.testing.allocator`). Safe to call with the arena allocator even though the arena will be deinit'd separately - `cmds.deinit` on arena memory is a no-op `free` plus a slice zero, which is harmless.

#### `DrawList.render`

```zig
    pub fn render(
        self: *const DrawList,
        gl: anytype,
        window: *const WindowState,
        shapes_state: *const ShapesTextureState,
        font_cache: *const FontCache,
    ) void
```

Replay all commands, emitting to rlgl through the existing `shapes.drawRectangleRec` / `text.draw` paths. rlgl batches the resulting vertex appends downstream. At replay time, the GL state (projection, viewport, framebuffer) must already be set up by the caller - this function just emits draw calls. `gl` is mutated for every shape/text/scissor command emitted. `window` is read-only - only the push_clip path needs canvas dimensions for the scissor y-flip. Each `.text` cmd carries its own `line_spacing` (captured at `addText` time). Replay all recorded commands. This is the deferred-render path: each `addRect`/`addText`/etc captured during widget submission gets translated into the corresponding `drawing` fn call here, in submission order. Reads `self.cmds` (the recorded command list); cmd payloads for variable-length data (`.text.s`, `.polyline.points`) carry slices into the DrawList's arena, used directly here. `shapes_state.*` provides the texture for every rect/quad; `font_cache.font` is the default font fallback for `.text` commands without a captured font. `gl` is mutated for every shape/text/scissor command emitted. `window` is read-only - only the push_clip path needs canvas dimensions for the scissor y-flip. Each `.text` cmd carries its own `line_spacing` (captured at `addText` time). `gl: anytype` - passes through to drawing.shapes / drawing.text which call method-style (`gl.begin(.quads)` etc). Both `*rlgl.GlState` and `*rlsw.Context` satisfy the trait, so the same DrawList drives either backend.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::Render` — ends the Dear ImGui frame, finalize the draw data. You can then get call GetDrawData().

### `ui.DrawListSplitter`

#### `DrawListSplitter.deinit`

```zig
    pub fn deinit(self: *DrawListSplitter, alloc: Allocator) void
```

Free channel storage. Pass the same allocator used by `split()` (and by any `add*` calls on the channels).

#### `DrawListSplitter.getCurrentChannel`

```zig
    pub fn getCurrentChannel(self: *DrawListSplitter) ?*DrawList
```

Get the active channel's DrawList — call `add*` methods on the returned pointer. Returns null when the splitter has no channels (split() wasn't called, or was called with n=0).

#### `DrawListSplitter.merge`

```zig
    pub fn merge(
        self: *DrawListSplitter,
        alloc: Allocator,
        target: *DrawList,
    ) !void
```

Concatenate all channel command lists into `target` in channel-index order (channel 0 first, channel N-1 last), then empty each channel (clearRetainingCapacity — the channels remain valid for further `add*` calls). The splitter is ready for a new `split()` after this. Allocates only to grow `target.cmds`. Errors propagate from the underlying append.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::Merge`

#### `DrawListSplitter.setCurrentChannel`

```zig
    pub fn setCurrentChannel(self: *DrawListSplitter, i: usize) void
```

Switch the active channel. Subsequent `getCurrentChannel()` calls return this index's DrawList. Out-of-range indices are clamped to the last channel — defensive default so a misconfigured call doesn't crash the render path. When the splitter has no channels (split() not yet called), `current` is forced to 0.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::SetCurrentChannel`

#### `DrawListSplitter.split`

```zig
    pub fn split(
        self: *DrawListSplitter,
        alloc: Allocator,
        n: usize,
    ) !void
```

Allocate `n` empty channels. Calling `split` again is allowed — the new layout supersedes any previous one (any unmerged commands are lost; channel storage is freed first). Errors propagate from the underlying ArrayList allocations.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::split`


Apply each request in `io.requests` to the selection. Conceptual O(N) per SetAll, O(R) per SetRange where N is `io.items_count` and R is range size. Imgui's optimized version (`imgui_widgets.cpp:8683-8810`) uses batched sorts for 100k+ item lists; the literal version below works fine for realistic UI scales. Re-do if/when someone has a performance complaint.
