### `ui.SplitResult`

#### `SplitResult.apply`

```zig
pub fn apply(
    gpa: Allocator,
    ctx: *UiContext,
    text: [:0]const u8,
) Error!void
```

Parse `text` (must be sentinel-terminated `[:0]const u8` per std.zon API contract) as a `PersistedState`, populate `ctx.pending_persistence` with deep-copies of each entry's name (so the names live in `gpa` independent of the parsed payload's lifetime). Version mismatch: silently drops the payload. Returns no error - calling code should be able to call `apply` on arbitrary user input without crashing the app. Parse failure: returns `error.InvalidPayload`. Callers generally log + ignore (start with empty state).

#### `SplitResult.clearPending`

```zig
pub fn clearPending(ctx: *UiContext) void
```

Free every entry in `ctx.pending_persistence` and `ctx.pending_tab_bar_selections`. Called from `UiContext.deinit`. After this call, the maps are empty but their backing allocations are left intact for the caller to release via their `deinit(gpa)`.

#### `SplitResult.comparePixels`

```zig
pub fn comparePixels(
    a: []const u8,
    b: []const u8,
    tolerance: u8,
) PixelDiff
```

Per-pixel RGBA8 byte-compare with a configurable per-channel tolerance.  `a` and `b` must be the same length, a multiple of 4. Not UI-specific; works on any same-size RGBA8 buffers.

#### `SplitResult.computeSplitChildSizes`

```zig
pub fn computeSplitChildSizes(split: DockNode.SplitData, avail: f32) [2]f32
```

work out child sizes for a SPLIT node given the available extent. Returns `[w_a, w_b]` (or `[h_a, h_b]` for vertical splits) summing to `avail`. Resolution order: 1. If exactly one child has a non-null `size_ref`, that child is locked at `clamp(size_ref, MIN, avail-MIN)` and the other child absorbs the remainder. This is the "fixed-width left panel, flexible center" UX - the hallmark feature this turn unlocks. 2. If both `size_ref` slots are null OR both are non-null (over-constrained), fall back to `ratio`. Imgui equivalent: per-node `SizeRef` field consulted by `DockNodeTreeUpdatePosSize` (imgui.cpp:20322-20338). Our model differs in two ways: (a) `size_ref` lives on the SPLIT (one-per-pair) rather than on each NODE - simpler, fewer places state can drift, easier to serialize. (b) No `WantLockSizeOnce` transient bool - splitter drag writes directly to size_ref or ratio, no two-phase protocol. Compute the two child sizes for a SPLIT node given the available pixels along the split axis. Returns `[size_of_child_a, size_of_child_b]`.  Rules: - Both children locked OR both free → fall back to `split.ratio`. - Exactly one locked → that child takes its locked size, the other takes the remainder. - Either child clamps to MIN_PANEL (24px) so a dragged splitter can't shrink a pane to a sliver.  Used both by the runtime dock-rendering path (twice) and by the docking persistence/test paths. Pre-turn-414 there was a `Public` wrapper alias here because `DockNode.SplitData` lived in a separate file; that wrapper is gone now that the dock layer is inlined.

#### `SplitResult.freePendingDockTree`

```zig
pub fn freePendingDockTree(gpa: Allocator, ctx: *UiContext) void
```

release every allocation owned by `ctx.pending_dock_tree` and reset it to empty. Called from `UiContext.deinit`, from `apply()` when re-staging, and from `tryRestoreDockTree` after a successful restore.

#### `SplitResult.layoutSubtree`

```zig
pub fn layoutSubtree(
    ctx: *const DockContext,
    root_id: Id,
    root_pos: Vec2,
    root_size: Vec2,
) void
```

#### `SplitResult.loadPng`

```zig
pub fn loadPng(
    gpa: Allocator,
    path: []const u8,
) !Image
```

Read a PNG file into an `Image`.  Caller owns the result; release with `image.deinit(gpa)`.

#### `SplitResult.nodeRect`

```zig
pub fn nodeRect(ctx: *const DockContext, node_id: Id) Rectangle
```

Get the final rect of `node_id` after the last layout pass. Returns a zero-sized rect at the origin if the id doesn't exist. Read-only helper for the future docked-window rendering path.

#### `SplitResult.processRequests`

```zig
pub fn processRequests(ctx: *DockContext, gpa: Allocator) Allocator.Error!void
```

Apply all queued requests in submission order. Called at a well-defined point in the frame (before window submission, after drag-drop releases have been observed). Drains `pending_requests`.

#### `SplitResult.removeNode`

```zig
pub fn removeNode(
    ctx: *DockContext,
    gpa: Allocator,
    node_id: Id,
) void
```

Remove an entire subtree rooted at `node_id`. Frees the node and all its descendants. If the node has a parent SPLIT, the parent collapses the same way as an undockWindow that empties a leaf. Use cases: a dockspace is torn down, or a layout resets via `dockBuilderRemoveNode`.

#### `SplitResult.renderToBytes`

```zig
pub fn renderToBytes(
    gpa: Allocator,
    ctx: *UiContext,
    w: u32,
    h: u32,
) ![]u8
```

Rasterize `ctx`'s current frame (every window's draw list) into a fresh RGBA8 buffer of size `w * h * 4`.  Caller owns the slice and must free with `gpa`. The contract: this is called AFTER `ctx.beginFrame(...)` and AFTER the caller has submitted every window's widgets, but BEFORE `ctx.endFrame(...)` (which would normally replay against real GL).  We replay against rlsw instead.

#### `SplitResult.renderToPng`

```zig
pub fn renderToPng(
    gpa: Allocator,
    ctx: *UiContext,
    w: u32,
    h: u32,
    out_path: []const u8,
) !void
```

Convenience: rasterize, encode, write to disk.

#### `SplitResult.serialize`

```zig
pub fn serialize(gpa: Allocator, ctx: *const UiContext) ![]u8
```

Walk `ctx.windows`, build a `PersistedState`, zon-stringify it. Caller owns the returned bytes (free with `gpa.free`). Skips windows whose `last_frame_active` is older than the current frame minus `STALE_FRAMES` (default 60) - keeps the payload free of windows the user has stopped submitting. Tooltip / popup windows (transient) are skipped via `flags.is_child` and other internal flags. Returns an empty `.{}` payload if no eligible windows exist callers shouldn't have to special-case "no state yet".

#### `SplitResult.snapshotPng`

```zig
pub fn snapshotPng(
    gpa: Allocator,
    ctx: *UiContext,
    w: u32,
    h: u32,
    ref_path: []const u8,
) !PixelDiff
```

Snapshot test: rasterize `ctx`, compare against the PNG at `ref_path`.  On mismatch — OR when the reference doesn't yet exist — write the current render to `ref_path` and return the diff so the caller can decide what to do.  The "first run writes the baseline" behavior is intentional: it makes adding a snapshot test as cheap as adding a regular test.  Refresh a snapshot by deleting the reference PNG and re-running.

#### `SplitResult.splitNode`

```zig
pub fn splitNode(
    ctx: *DockContext,
    gpa: Allocator,
    target_id: Id,
    dir: Dir,
    ratio: f32,
) SplitError!SplitResult
```

#### `SplitResult.takeFor`

```zig
pub fn takeFor(ctx: *UiContext, title: []const u8) ?PersistedWindow
```

Look up and remove the pending entry for `title`. Called by `findOrCreateWindow`'s first-time branch BEFORE applying `opts.initial_*` so persisted state takes precedence over the caller's defaults. Returns null when no persisted entry exists (new window, never seen before).

#### `SplitResult.takeTabBarSelection`

```zig
pub fn takeTabBarSelection(
    ctx: *UiContext,
    window_name: []const u8,
    str_id: []const u8,
) ?[]const u8
```

Pop the persisted selected-tab label for a `(window_name, str_id)` pair. Returns null when nothing was persisted for this bar. Returns an empty string when the persistence layer recorded an entry but no tab was selected - caller can treat empty as "no selection" (matches `state.selected_id = 0` semantics). Returned slice is owned by the caller; free with `ctx.gpa.free`.

#### `SplitResult.tryAutoLoad`

```zig
pub fn tryAutoLoad(ctx: *UiContext) void
```

Wasm-only: read localStorage for `ctx.persistence_key`, parse the payload, populate `ctx.pending_persistence`. No-op on host or when no key is set. Called from `beginFrameRaw` on the first frame.

#### `SplitResult.tryAutoSave`

```zig
pub fn tryAutoSave(ctx: *const UiContext) void
```

Wasm-only: serialize live state and write to localStorage. Called from `endFrame` every `SAVE_CADENCE_FRAMES` frames. No-op on host or without a key.

#### `SplitResult.tryRestoreDockTree`

```zig
pub fn tryRestoreDockTree(gpa: Allocator, ctx: *UiContext) bool
```

consume the pending dock tree (if any), rebuilding `ctx.dock` to match. Called by `dockSpace()` the first time a dockspace is submitted after load. Walks the flat `PersistedDockNode[]` and: 1. Creates every node in `ctx.dock.nodes` with the original `id` (preserves stable ids across reloads). 2. Wires split-vs-leaf role, ratio, axis, child_ids, size_ref, flags. 3. For leaves: resolves window NAMES to live `Window` ids via `findOrCreateWindow`. Sets `dock_node_id` on each window so they render in the docked path. 4. Bumps `ctx.dock.next_node_id` past the max observed id so future fresh allocations don't collide. On any structural inconsistency (a child_id that doesn't resolve, a leaf with no recoverable windows), the offending node is skipped but the rest of the tree is preserved. Persistence is a hint, never a hard requirement - a broken payload degrades to "fresh tree", not a crash. Returns true if the tree was restored (even partially), or false if pending_dock_tree was empty.

### `ui.SplitResult.PersistedStyle`

#### `SplitResult.PersistedStyle.applyTo`

```zig
    pub fn applyTo(self: PersistedStyle, target: *Style) void
```

Apply this persisted form back onto `target`. Preserves the caller's `font: ?*const Font` — the pointer cannot round-trip, so the caller's binding wins.

#### `SplitResult.PersistedStyle.fromStyle`

```zig
    pub fn fromStyle(s: *const Style) PersistedStyle
```

Capture a runtime `Style` into a serialization-ready form. Drops the `font` pointer (can't round-trip).


### `ui.Style`

#### `Style.applyAccent`

```zig
    pub fn applyAccent(self: *Style, accent: Color) void
```

Re-derive the interactive colors from a single accent color. Structural chrome (text, borders, window bg, base title bar) stays unchanged - that's neutral by design and shouldn't bend to the accent. Modeled on `ImGui::StyleColorsDark()`'s ratios: button at 40% alpha, hovered at full, active darkened 20%, etc. f.ui.style().applyAccent(z.colors.amber_500); // every interactive widget now reads as "amber"

#### `Style.applyPreset`

```zig
    pub fn applyPreset(self: *Style, preset: Preset) void
```

One-call theme switcher. Replaces every Color slot with the preset's values; leaves the numeric chrome (padding, spacing, font*) untouched so the user's typography/density choices survive a theme flip. ui.ctx.style.applyPreset(.light); Pair with `applyTailwind` for "neutral theme + brand accent": ui.ctx.style.applyPreset(.light); ui.ctx.style.applyTailwind(.emerald);

#### `Style.applyTailwind`

```zig
    pub fn applyTailwind(self: *Style, hue: TailwindHue) void
```

One-call retheme using a Tailwind-CSS-named hue at the 500 shade (Tailwind's standard "primary" weight - bright enough to read as the brand color, not so saturated it fights the chrome). Add hues here as we add them to `z.colors`; the 9 currently in the palette are listed.

### `ui.StyleGuard`

#### `StyleGuard.restore`

```zig
    pub fn restore(self: StyleGuard) void
```

Restore prior values for every field the matching `styleOverride` call modified. Safe to call exactly once; calling again is a no-op (all saved values were already written back, but no harm in re-writing them — the idempotence test verifies this).

#### `TabBarState.deinit`


#### `TabBarState.findTab`

```zig
    pub fn findTab(self: *const TabBarState, id: Id) ?usize
```

Linear search by id. Tab counts are small (~5-20 in practice); a HashMap would be overkill and add indirection.

#### `TabBarState.parentWindowName`

```zig
    pub fn parentWindowName(self: *const TabBarState) []const u8
```

Returns the bar's parent window name at allocation time. See `strId` for the same caveats around truncation.

#### `TabBarState.strId`

```zig
    pub fn strId(self: *const TabBarState) []const u8
```

Returns the bar's caller-given str_id (truncated to fit if the original was longer than `TAB_BAR_NAME_CAP`). Used by persistence to identify the bar by name rather than by hashed Id. Empty until openTabBar populates the buffer.



### `ui.TextFilter`

#### `TextFilter.build`

```zig
    pub fn build(self: *TextFilter) void
```

Parse `buf[0..buf_len]` into `segments`. Idempotent; safe to call every frame. `draw` does this automatically when inputText reports a change. Algorithm matches imgui's `Build`: split on `,`, trim each piece's whitespace, skip empty pieces, mark `-`-prefixed pieces as negated (and trim the `-` off the segment slice itself).

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::Build`

#### `TextFilter.clear`

```zig
    pub fn clear(self: *TextFilter) void
```

Reset to empty buffer + zero segments.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::Clear` — Clear selection

#### `TextFilter.draw`

```zig
    pub fn draw(
        self: *TextFilter,
        ui_handle: Ui,
        label: []const u8,
        hint: []const u8,
    ) bool
```

Render the filter's input box and re-parse on any change. Returns true if the filter (and therefore which lines pass) changed this frame - callers may use that to invalidate caches. `label` and `hint` follow imgui's `Draw("Filter (inc,-exc)")` convention; `hint` is shown inside the empty input so users discover the syntax.

#### `TextFilter.isActive`

```zig
    pub fn isActive(self: *const TextFilter) bool
```

True if any segment is parsed (filter does something).

#### `TextFilter.passFilter`

```zig
    pub fn passFilter(self: *const TextFilter, text: []const u8) bool
```

True if `text` should be displayed under the current filter. Empty filter → always true. Otherwise iterates segments in input order; first matching include returns true, first matching exclude returns false. If no include ever matches AND at least one include is defined, returns false (default- deny once includes are present). Matches imgui exactly.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::PassFilter`

#### `TooltipData.text`

```zig
    pub fn text(self: *const TooltipData) []const u8
```

### `ui.Ui`

#### `Ui.StateGetOrPut`

```zig
    pub fn StateGetOrPut(comptime T: type) type
```

Result of `getOrPutState`. Mirrors `GetOrPutResult` from `std.AutoHashMapUnmanaged` — the caller branches on `found_existing` to decide whether to write `value_ptr` with an initial value or read what's already there.  `value_ptr` is stable until the next `getOrPutState(T,...)` for the same `T`. Don't hold across calls.

#### `Ui.acceptDragDropPayload`

```zig
    pub fn acceptDragDropPayload(
        self: Ui,
        comptime T: type,
        opts: DragDropFlags,
    ) ?T
```

Try to take the active drag's payload as a `T`. Returns the payload value when: - The drag is active. - The current widget is the active drop target (i.e. called inside a `beginDragDropTarget` that returned true). - The payload's type tag matches `@typeName(T)`. - The user RELEASED the mouse this frame (release-edge), unless `opts.accept_before_delivery` is set, in which case mid-drag peeks succeed too. Returns null otherwise. After accepting, the payload is still readable for the rest of this frame; cleanup happens at endFrame.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::AcceptDragDropPayload` — accept contents of a given type. If ImGuiDragDropFlags_AcceptBeforeDelivery is set you can peek into the payload before the mouse button is released.

#### `Ui.alignTextToFramePadding`

```zig
    pub fn alignTextToFramePadding(self: Ui) void
```

Vertically align text to match the baseline of framed widgets (slider, drag, inputText) on the next line. When you intermix `text("Speed:")` with `slider(...)` on the same row via `sameLine`, the bare text otherwise sits flush at the cursor while the slider's frame extends both above and below - this padding compensates so the text reads centred-on-frame. ImGui: `AlignTextToFramePadding()`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::AlignTextToFramePadding` — vertically align upcoming text baseline to FramePadding.y so that it will align properly to regularly framed items (call if you have text on a line before a framed item)

#### `Ui.animated`

```zig
    pub fn animated(
        self: Ui,
        label: []const u8,
        opts: AnimatedOpts,
    ) f32
```

One-shot tween.  Returns the current interpolated value. Reads `ctx.input.delta_time` to advance the slot.  If `opts.to` differs from the previous frame's `to`, the tween restarts — useful for "snap to a new target" patterns.

#### `Ui.applyLayout`

```zig
    pub fn applyLayout(self: Ui, bytes: []const u8) !void
```

Restore window positions/sizes from a buffer previously produced by `serializeLayout`. Unknown magic / version is rejected with `error.LayoutFormatMismatch`. Window IDs not present in the current `ctx.windows` map create new entries with the saved geometry; IDs that DO exist get their pos / size / scroll_y / user_resized fields overwritten. Order of windows in the buffer doesn't matter. Safe to call before any window is submitted.

#### `Ui.arrowButton`

```zig
    pub fn arrowButton(
        self: Ui,
        str_id: []const u8,
        dir: ArrowDir,
    ) bool
```

Tiny directional button (square, font-sized, with a triangle glyph inside pointing in `dir`). Used for inline ±1 steppers next to numeric inputs, or for tab-bar / combo nav glyphs. `str_id` is the widget's unique label for state tracking; it is NOT drawn - only the arrow glyph is. ImGui: `ArrowButton(str_id, dir)`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::ArrowButton` — square button with an arrow shape

#### `Ui.beginCanvas`

```zig
    pub fn beginCanvas(
        self: Ui,
        label: []const u8,
        size: Vec2,
        opts: CanvasOpts,
    ) ?CanvasCtx
```

Open a canvas scope. Returns null when the canvas is clipped entirely outside the current window's content area (so the caller can skip the canvas body entirely). When non-null, the caller MUST pair with `endCanvas` — typically via `defer`.

#### `Ui.beginChild`

```zig
    pub fn beginChild(
        self: Ui,
        str_id: []const u8,
        size: Vec2,
        flags: ChildFlags,
    ) bool
```

Begin a child window scope inside the current window. Widgets submitted between `beginChild` / `endChild` are clipped to the child's bounds via the draw list's `push_clip` command, run their own layout from the child's top-left, and have their IDs hashed under `str_id` (so identical labels in different children don't collide). if (f.ui.beginChild("log",.{ 0, 120 },.{.border = true })) { defer f.ui.endChild(); for (lines) |line| f.ui.text("{s}",.{line}); } Layout: the child consumes its OUTER size from the parent's cursor - subsequent parent widgets sit below it. Width 0 auto-fills to the parent's content right edge; height 0 defaults to a comfortable 100px (caller can pass an explicit height for log windows / lists / preview panes). **Why this matters architecturally** - child windows are the foundation for tables (each scrollable area is a child) and for log/list views. Getting clipping right here proves the deferred-draw-list scissor pipeline works correctly in nested cases.

#### `Ui.beginCombo`

```zig
    pub fn beginCombo(
        self: Ui,
        label: []const u8,
        preview: []const u8,
        opts: ComboOpts,
    ) bool
```

Begin a combo dropdown that the caller fills with custom content. Sibling of `combo()` (which takes a string array); use `beginCombo` when rows need icons, color swatches, descriptions, or any other widget. Returns true when the dropdown is open - caller submits widgets, then MUST call `endCombo()` to close the scope. Returns false when closed; do NOT call endCombo in that case. ImGui: `BeginCombo(label, preview_value, flags)`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::BeginCombo`

#### `Ui.beginDisabled`

```zig
    pub fn beginDisabled(self: Ui, disabled: bool) void
```

Open a "disabled" scope. While the disabled stack is non-empty, widgets ignore input AND render with reduced alpha (matches `style.disabled_alpha`). Always pair with `endDisabled()` `defer` is the right way to spell that. Pass `false` to open a no-op scope (useful for conditional disabling without branching at every widget). ImGui: `BeginDisabled(bool disabled)`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::BeginDisabled`

#### `Ui.beginDragDropSource`

```zig
    pub fn beginDragDropSource(self: Ui, opts: DragDropFlags) bool
```

Open a drag-drop source scope. Call IMMEDIATELY after a widget that should be draggable - the widget's last_item_id is captured as the drag origin. Returns true when a drag is actively in flight from this widget THIS FRAME, in which case the caller should: 1. Call `setDragDropPayload` to register a typed payload. 2. Submit any preview widgets (text, icon) that should follow the cursor. 3. Call `endDragDropSource` to close the scope. if (ui.button("drag me")) {} if (ui.beginDragDropSource(.{})) { ui.setDragDropPayload(BuildId, &build.id); ui.text("Build #{d}",.{build.id}); ui.endDragDropSource(); } Drag begins on mouse-down + movement past `DragDropState.DRAG_THRESHOLD` pixels (matches imgui's 4 px default). Drag ends on mouse-up; if no target consumed the payload, it's simply discarded.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::BeginDragDropSource` — call after submitting an item which may be dragged. when this return true, you can call SetDragDropPayload() + EndDragDropSource()

#### `Ui.beginDragDropTarget`

```zig
    pub fn beginDragDropTarget(self: Ui, opts: DragDropFlags) bool
```

Open a drop-target scope. Call IMMEDIATELY after a widget that should accept drops. Returns true when: 1. A drag is currently active, AND 2. The cursor is over the just-submitted widget's rect. While the target scope is open the target's rect is outlined in `style.button_active` so the user sees where the drop will land. Pattern: // a slot/lane widget ui.child("lane:" ++ name,.{ 200, 80 }); if (ui.beginDragDropTarget(.{})) { if (ui.acceptDragDropPayload(BuildPayload,.{})) |build| { // user released over this lane → move build here } ui.endDragDropTarget(); } `opts: DragDropFlags` - pass `.{}` for default. Useful flags: `.accept_no_draw_default_rect` (suppress the dashed highlight rect), `.accept_draw_as_hovered` (render the target as if hovered).

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::BeginDragDropTarget` — call after submitting an item that may receive a payload. If this returns true, you can call AcceptDragDropPayload() + EndDragDropTarget()

#### `Ui.beginGroup`

```zig
    pub fn beginGroup(self: Ui) void
```

Open a "group" scope. Widgets submitted between `beginGroup` and `endGroup` lay out exactly as they normally would, but the group as a whole records a single bounding rect - so a follow-up `isItemHovered()` reports hover-over-the-whole-group rather than just hover-over-the-last-widget-in-it. Useful for labelled clusters that should act as one row. ImGui: `BeginGroup()`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::BeginGroup` — lock horizontal starting position

#### `Ui.beginItem`

```zig
    pub fn beginItem(
        self: Ui,
        label: []const u8,
        opts: ItemOpts,
    ) ?ItemCtx
```

Single-call widget primitive. Composes `widgetId` + `resolveCursor` + `itemAdd` + `buttonBehavior` into one "do all the boilerplate" call. Returns `null` if the widget's rect is fully outside the window (caller skips drawing); otherwise returns the full state needed to draw + react.  Pair with `endItem(ctx)` (which calls `itemSize`). Use `defer` to make pairing mistake-proof:  if (u.beginItem("knob",.{.size =.{ 40, 40 } })) |it| { defer u.endItem(it); // draw using it.hovered / it.held / it.rect return it.pressed; }  Imgui has no canonical equivalent — its widgets reimplement the boilerplate inline. Q3's two-path design (B for imgui-style + C for opinionated) gives extension authors the choice; C is the recommended starting point.

#### `Ui.beginItemTooltip`

```zig
    pub fn beginItemTooltip(self: Ui) bool
```

Shorthand for `if (isItemHovered()) beginTooltip()`. Open a tooltip scope ONLY when the just-submitted widget is currently hovered. Returns true when the tooltip is open - caller submits tooltip content then calls `endItemTooltip()`. _ = ui.button("?",.{}); if (ui.beginItemTooltip()) { defer ui.endItemTooltip(); ui.text("This button does the thing.",.{}); ui.text("(Press Enter to confirm)",.{}); }

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::BeginItemTooltip` — begin/append a tooltip window if preceding item was hovered.

#### `Ui.beginListBox`

```zig
    pub fn beginListBox(
        self: Ui,
        label: []const u8,
        size: Vec2,
    ) bool
```

Begin a fixed-height scrollable list box. Pair with `endListBox`. Inside the scope, the cursor lays out vertically as in any window; the box is clipped to its rect. Useful when you want a scrollable selection area inside a non-scrollable parent window. `size` interprets `0` as "auto" - width defaults to the available content region; height defaults to ~7 rows of the current font. ImGui: `BeginListBox(label, size)`.

#### `Ui.beginMainMenuBar`

```zig
    pub fn beginMainMenuBar(self: Ui) bool
```

Begin the main menu bar at the top of the canvas. Returns true if the bar is active (rare false: not enough memory to allocate the synthetic bar window). Pair with `endMainMenuBar`. ImGui: `BeginMainMenuBar()`. if (f.ui.beginMainMenuBar()) { defer f.ui.endMainMenuBar(); if (f.ui.beginMenu("File")) { defer f.ui.endMenu(); if (f.ui.menuItem("Open",.{.shortcut = "Ctrl+O" })) {} if (f.ui.menuItem("Quit",.{})) state.should_quit = true; } }

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::BeginMainMenuBar` — create and append to a full screen menu-bar.

#### `Ui.beginMenu`

```zig
    pub fn beginMenu(self: Ui, label: []const u8) bool
```

Begin a menu inside the main menu bar. Renders a clickable label in the bar; on click, opens a popup anchored just below the label. Returns true if the menu's popup is open (caller submits menu items, then calls `endMenu`). ImGui: `BeginMenu(label, enabled)`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::BeginMenu` — create a sub-menu entry. only call EndMenu() if this returns true!

#### `Ui.beginMenuBar`

```zig
    pub fn beginMenuBar(self: Ui) bool
```

Begin a per-window menu bar - a horizontal strip pinned to the top of the current window, just below the title bar. Pair with `endMenuBar`. Returns true so the caller can use `if (ui.beginMenuBar()) { defer ui.endMenuBar();... }` with a uniform shape. Unlike `beginMainMenuBar` (which floats at the top of the canvas regardless of which window is active), this bar belongs to the window - drag the window, the bar drags with it. ImGui: `BeginMenuBar()` paired with a window opened via `Begin(name, p_open, ImGuiWindowFlags_MenuBar)`. if (u.window("Tools",.{})) |w| { defer w.close(); if (u.beginMenuBar()) { defer u.endMenuBar(); if (u.beginMenu("File")) { defer u.endMenu(); _ = u.menuItem("Save",.{.shortcut = "Ctrl+S" }); } } u.text("Window content below the bar...",.{}); }

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::BeginMenuBar` — append to menu-bar of current window (requires ImGuiWindowFlags_MenuBar flag set on parent window).

#### `Ui.beginMultiSelect`

```zig
    pub fn beginMultiSelect(
        self: Ui,
        flags: MultiSelectFlags,
        selection_size: u32,
        items_count: u32,
    ) *const MultiSelectIO
```

Open a multi-select scope. Returns the IO view with any pending requests (from this frame's keyboard shortcuts or previous frame's box-select completion) ready to apply. `selection_size` and `items_count` are informational passed back via `io.items_count` for `applyRequests` to scope SetAll over. Pass `items_count = items.len` and `selection_size = sel.size()` from your storage. returns IO with empty requests + null anchor / nav_id. Real Begin-time logic (apply keyboard shortcuts, detect scope focus) lands.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::BeginMultiSelect`

#### `Ui.beginPopup`

```zig
    pub fn beginPopup(self: Ui, str_id: []const u8) bool
```

Begin a popup scope. Returns true if the popup is open; caller must match a true return with `endPopup()`. Pattern: if (ui.beginPopup("ctx_menu")) { defer ui.endPopup(); if (ui.menuItem("Copy",.{})) {... } } While submission is inside the popup scope, widgets render to the popup's draw list (which replays AFTER all regular windows in `endFrame`) and use the popup's persistent position + cursor. Click outside the popup OR a call to `closeCurrentPopup` dismisses it. ImGui: `BeginPopup(str_id)`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::BeginPopup` — return true if the popup is open, and you can start outputting to it.

#### `Ui.beginPopupContextItem`

```zig
    pub fn beginPopupContextItem(self: Ui, str_id: ?[]const u8) bool
```

Open + begin a context-menu popup if the user right-clicked on the most-recently-submitted widget this frame. Returns true once the popup is open; pair with `endPopup()`. `str_id` defaults to `"context"` when null. Pattern: _ = ui.button("Save",.{}); if (ui.beginPopupContextItem(null)) { defer ui.endPopup(); if (ui.menuItem("Reset",.{})) state.reset(); } ImGui: `BeginPopupContextItem(str_id, flags)`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::BeginPopupContextItem` — open+begin popup when clicked on last item. Use str_id==NULL to associate the popup to previous item. If you want to use that on a non-interactive item such as Text() you need to pass in an explicit ID here. read comments in .cpp!

#### `Ui.beginPopupModal`

```zig
    pub fn beginPopupModal(
        self: Ui,
        name: []const u8,
        open_ptr: ?*bool,
    ) bool
```

Begin a modal popup scope. Modals differ from regular popups in two ways: they render a fullscreen backdrop dim BEFORE the popup's chrome (visually disabling everything below), and they ignore click-outside-to-close - they must be dismissed explicitly via `closeCurrentPopup` OR by setting `*open_ptr` to false (typically wired to a Cancel/X button inside the modal body). if (state.show_about) ui.openPopup("about"); if (ui.beginPopupModal("about", &state.show_about)) { defer ui.endPopup(); ui.text("zimr v0.7.0",.{}); if (ui.button("OK",.{})) { state.show_about = false; ui.closeCurrentPopup(); } } ImGui: `BeginPopupModal(name, p_open, flags)`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::BeginPopupModal` — return true if the modal is open, and you can start outputting to it.

#### `Ui.beginTabBar`

```zig
    pub fn beginTabBar(
        self: Ui,
        str_id: []const u8,
        opts: TabBarFlags,
    ) bool
```

Begin a horizontal tab bar. Pair with `endTabBar`. Returns true so the caller can use `if (ui.beginTabBar(...,.{})) {... }` to skip the body when the bar can't open. ImGui: `BeginTabBar(str_id, flags)`. if (f.ui.beginTabBar("settings_tabs",.{})) { defer f.ui.endTabBar(); if (f.ui.beginTabItem("General", null,.{})) { defer f.ui.endTabItem(); //... general settings widgets } if (f.ui.beginTabItem("Audio", null,.{})) { defer f.ui.endTabItem(); //... audio settings widgets } } `opts: TabBarFlags` - pass `.{}` for default. See `TabBarFlags` for the flag inventory and current wired/ no-op status.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::BeginTabBar` — create and append into a TabBar

#### `Ui.beginTabItem`

```zig
    pub fn beginTabItem(
        self: Ui,
        label: []const u8,
        open_ptr: ?*bool,
        opts: TabItemFlags,
    ) bool
```

Begin a tab item inside the current tab bar. Returns true if THIS tab is the active one (caller submits widgets, then calls `endTabItem`). `open_ptr` (optional): non-null = render close-X. Clicking it (or middle-clicking the tab, unless suppressed) sets `*open_ptr = false` and removes the tab next frame. `opts: TabItemFlags` - pass `.{}` for default. Useful: `.unsaved_document` for `*` decoration, `.set_selected` to force-select. ImGui: `BeginTabItem(label, p_open, flags)`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::BeginTabItem` — create a Tab. Returns true if the Tab is selected.

#### `Ui.beginTable`

```zig
    pub fn beginTable(
        self: Ui,
        label: []const u8,
        n_columns: u32,
        opts: TableOpts,
    ) bool
```

Open a table scope. `n_columns` is the column count (each column gets `tableSetupColumn` between begin and the first row, or sensible defaults are used). Returns true when the table is active (always today; reserved for future auto-hide flags). Pattern: if (ui.beginTable("scores", 3,.{.borders = true })) { ui.tableSetupColumn("Name",.{.sizing =.stretch }); ui.tableSetupColumn("Score",.{.sizing =.fixed,.width = 80 }); ui.tableSetupColumn("Rank",.{.sizing =.fixed,.width = 50 }); for (rows) |r| { ui.tableNextRow(); ui.tableNextColumn(); ui.text("{s}",.{r.name}); ui.tableNextColumn(); ui.text("{d}",.{r.score}); ui.tableNextColumn(); ui.text("#{d}",.{r.rank}); } ui.endTable(); }

#### `Ui.beginTooltip`

```zig
    pub fn beginTooltip(self: Ui) void
```

Open a tooltip block. Widgets submitted between `beginTooltip` and `endTooltip` are routed into the tooltip's own draw list, which renders on top of everything else (above popups) at endFrame. Position is anchored at the current mouse position + (16, 16) offset. The tooltip's size auto-fits its content. Pattern: if (ui.isItemHovered(.{})) { ui.beginTooltip(); ui.text("Detailed info...",.{}); _ = ui.slider("preview", &p.x,.{}); ui.endTooltip(); } Multiple `beginTooltip` / `endTooltip` pairs in one frame are not supported - the last `beginTooltip` wins (its contents replace any previous tooltip block).

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::BeginTooltip` — begin/append a tooltip window.

#### `Ui.bullet`

```zig
    pub fn bullet(self: Ui) void
```

Tiny filled square + same-line cursor advance, ImGui-style. Common as a list-item glyph when you want a manually-bulleted paragraph.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::Bullet` — draw a small circle + keep the cursor on the same line. advance cursor x position by GetTreeNodeToLabelSpacing(), same distance that TreeNode() uses

#### `Ui.bulletText`

```zig
    pub fn bulletText(
        self: Ui,
        comptime fmt: []const u8,
        args: anytype,
    ) void
```

Bullet glyph + formatted text on the same line. Equivalent to `ui.bullet(); ui.sameLine(.{}); ui.text(fmt, args);`.

#### `Ui.button`

```zig
    pub fn button(
        self: Ui,
        label: []const u8,
        opts: ButtonOpts,
    ) bool
```

#### `Ui.buttonBehavior`

```zig
    pub fn buttonBehavior(
        self: Ui,
        rect: Rectangle,
        id: Id,
        opts: ButtonBehaviorOpts,
    ) ButtonResult
```

Run the press / release / repeat / keyboard-activation state machine for `id` over the rect `rect`. Returns edge events + level state in a `ButtonResult`. Doesn't draw anything — caller draws based on `result.hovered` / `held`. Doesn't claim the layout — caller pairs with `itemSize(rect_size)` after drawing.  Mirrors imgui's `ButtonBehavior(const ImRect&, ImGuiID, bool*, bool*, ImGuiButtonFlags)`. Output struct replaces imgui's out-pointer pattern.  The mouse hit test inside this fn uses `pointInRect` for consistency with `itemAdd`. Calling pattern:  const rect =...; const id = u.getId("knob"); u.itemSize(rect_size); // OR after the draw if you // want pixel-exact "current // line height" tracking if (!u.itemAdd(rect, id)) return; const r: Ui.ButtonResult = u.buttonBehavior(rect, id,.{}); // draw using r.hovered / r.held / r.pressed

#### `Ui.calcTextSize`

```zig
    pub fn calcTextSize(self: Ui, s: []const u8) Vec2
```

Public wrapper over the internal text-measurement machinery. Use to lay out custom widgets that need to reserve space for a piece of text without first drawing it. Honors the current `Style.font` + `font_size` + `font_spacing`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::CalcTextSize` — Color Utilities

#### `Ui.checkbox`

```zig
    pub fn checkbox(
        self: Ui,
        label: []const u8,
        v: *bool,
    ) bool
```

Toggleable boolean widget. Returns true if `*value` changed this frame (clicked while hovered). The function ALSO updates `*value` in place - caller can ignore the bool and just read `*value` after the call.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::Checkbox`

#### `Ui.clipper`

```zig
    pub fn clipper(
        self: Ui,
        item_count: usize,
        item_height: f32,
    ) Clipper
```

Open a virtualized clipper scope. Use when rendering long item lists (1000+ rows) where most rows would fall outside the visible viewport. Without a clipper, every row's widget submission cost is paid even for rows the user can't see. Idiomatic use: var clip = u.clipper(line_count, line_height); while (clip.step()) |range| { var i: usize = range.start; while (i < range.end): (i += 1) { u.text("Row {d}: {s}",.{ i, rows[i] }); } } **Precondition: `item_height` must be the rendered height of one item - including item_spacing.y.** The clipper computes the visible row range arithmetically from `(viewport_top - cursor.y) / item_height`; a wrong height silently miscalculates the range (renders the wrong rows or duplicates rows when the user scrolls). For variable- height rows the imgui idiom is "measure on first frame, virtualize on second frame" - not implemented in A2 (see `Clipper.measure_pending` field reserved for that). `item_count == 0` returns a clipper that completes immediately (zero step iterations). Negative item_height is clamped to 1 to avoid division-by-zero. ImGui: `ImGuiListClipper` (constructor + `Begin`).

#### `Ui.closeCurrentPopup`

```zig
    pub fn closeCurrentPopup(self: Ui) void
```

Close the topmost popup currently open. Typically called from inside a popup body, e.g. on "Close" menu item or after a click that should dismiss. ImGui: `CloseCurrentPopup()`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::CloseCurrentPopup` — manually close the popup we have begin-ed into.

#### `Ui.collapsingHeader`

```zig
    pub fn collapsingHeader(
        self: Ui,
        label: []const u8,
        open: *bool,
    ) bool
```

Click-to-toggle section header. When `*open` is true the caller should submit the section's child widgets; when false they're suppressed (caller is expected to gate them with `if`). Returns true when the section is open this frame (== `*open`). Pattern: if (ui.collapsingHeader("Advanced", &state.show_adv)) { _ = ui.checkbox("Verbose logging", &state.verbose); _ = ui.slider("Buffer size", &state.bufsz,.{.max = 1024 }); }

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::CollapsingHeader` — if returning 'true' the header is open. doesn't indent nor push on ID stack. user doesn't have to call TreePop().

#### `Ui.colorButton`

```zig
    pub fn colorButton(
        self: Ui,
        desc_id: []const u8,
        v: anytype,
        size: Vec2,
    ) bool
```

Compact colour swatch - a clickable rectangle that displays a colour and shows the RGBA breakdown as a tooltip on hover. Returns true on the frame the user clicks the swatch. `value` is `*const [4]f32`, `*const [3]f32`, or `*const Color`. ImGui: `ColorButton(desc_id, col, flags, size)`.

#### `Ui.colorEdit`

```zig
    pub fn colorEdit(
        self: Ui,
        label: []const u8,
        v: anytype,
        opts: ColorEditOpts,
    ) bool
```

Inline RGB or RGBA editor. Generic over `*[3]f32`, `*[4]f32`, or `*Color` (zimr's u8 RGBA). Renders three or four slim sliders side-by-side plus a small swatch.

#### `Ui.colorPicker`

```zig
    pub fn colorPicker(
        self: Ui,
        label: []const u8,
        v: anytype,
        opts: ColorPickerOpts,
    ) bool
```

Block-style color picker. Generic over `*[3]f32` (RGB), `*[4]f32` (RGBA), or `*Color` (zimr's u8 RGBA). Renders either a bar layout (hue gradient + SV box) or a wheel layout (hue ring + SV box inside) selectable via `opts.layout`. Returns true on any frame where the color changed. var rgba: [4]f32 =.{ 0.2, 0.6, 0.9, 1.0 }; _ = ui.colorPicker("Tint", &rgba,.{ .layout =.wheel, .alpha = true, });

#### `Ui.combo`

```zig
    pub fn combo(
        self: Ui,
        label: []const u8,
        current_index: *i32,
        items: []const []const u8,
        opts: ComboOpts,
    ) bool
```

Dropdown picker - click the closed row to expand a vertical list of items, click an item to select. Returns true on the frame the selection changed. const modes = [_][]const u8{ "Off", "Low", "Medium", "High" }; _ = ui.combo("Quality", &state.quality_idx, &modes,.{}); Single-slot: only one combo can be open at a time. Clicking outside the dropdown (or on the closed row again) closes it. **Real overlay popup** - the dropdown is recorded into the foreground draw list, replayed at endFrame on top of all windows. The layout below the combo does NOT reflow when the dropdown opens; the dropdown floats over whatever's there. This matches Dear ImGui's combo behavior. Multiple combos in the same window are stable: open one, the layout below stays put.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::Combo`

#### `Ui.currentItemFlags`

```zig
    pub fn currentItemFlags(self: Ui) ItemFlags
```

The current effective item flags - the top of `item_flags_stack`, or all-default if empty. Widgets consult this for cross-widget behavior gates.

#### `Ui.cursorScreenPos`

```zig
    pub fn cursorScreenPos(self: Ui) Vec2
```

Current widget anchor in screen pixels. In the post-308.5 layout model, sameLine writes directly to `cursor_pos`, so this is just a read; the function form is retained for callsite readability.

#### `Ui.dockBuilderDockWindow`

```zig
    pub fn dockBuilderDockWindow(
        self: Ui,
        window_name: []const u8,
        node_id: Id,
    ) void
```

Dock the window named `window_name` into the leaf node `node_id`. Creates the window if it doesn't exist yet (with default opts; the demo's subsequent `u.window(name,...)` call will pick up the same window via name lookup). No-op when the window is already docked into `node_id` (idempotent - safe to call from inside the demo's per-frame builder block without gating). When the window is docked elsewhere, it's first undocked then re-docked.

#### `Ui.dockBuilderFinish`

```zig
    pub fn dockBuilderFinish(self: Ui, node_id: Id) void
```

Finalize the layout. Currently a no-op marker - our `layoutSubtree` runs every frame in `dockSpaceImpl` regardless, so there's nothing to "commit". Reserved for future optimization where `dockBuilderFinish` would write into a frozen cache rather than re-walking every frame.

#### `Ui.dockBuilderGetNodeFlags`

```zig
    pub fn dockBuilderGetNodeFlags(
        self: Ui,
        node_id: Id,
    ) DockNodeFlags
```

Read the current flags on a dock node. Returns default-init (all false) for unknown ids - callers don't need to handle the null case.

#### `Ui.dockBuilderRemoveNode`

```zig
    pub fn dockBuilderRemoveNode(self: Ui, node_id: Id) void
```

Remove a dock subtree. Tears down `node_id` and every node beneath it; the parent (if any) collapses to a leaf with the surviving sibling's contents. Windows that were in the removed subtree have their `dock_node_id` cleared (they fall back to floating). Used by demos to reset a layout: typically called once, followed by a fresh sequence of split + dockWindow calls.

#### `Ui.dockBuilderSetCentralNode`

```zig
    pub fn dockBuilderSetCentralNode(
        self: Ui,
        node_id: Id,
    ) void
```

mark a node as "central" - the one that absorbs remaining space when its siblings are locked at fixed sizes. Pure marker; sets `is_central = true` in the node's flags. Today the layout doesn't yet special- case central nodes (the `size_ref` mechanism already gives the "fixed sibling + free central" UX); the flag reservation is for 5.5k persistence and future drop-zone behavior (`NoDockingOverCentralNode` semantics).

#### `Ui.dockBuilderSetNodeFlags`

```zig
    pub fn dockBuilderSetNodeFlags(
        self: Ui,
        node_id: Id,
        flags: DockNodeFlags,
    ) void
```

set the constraint flags on a dock node. Mirrors imgui's `DockBuilderGetNode(id)->LocalFlags =...` pattern, but in a single explicit call. No-op if the node id is unknown. Example: lock down a side panel so the user can't dock other windows over it, split it, or accidentally close its tabs: u.dockBuilderSetNodeFlags(side_id,.{ .no_split = true, .no_docking_over_me = true, .no_close_button = true, });

#### `Ui.dockBuilderSetSizeRef`

```zig
    pub fn dockBuilderSetSizeRef(
        self: Ui,
        parent_id: Id,
        side: enum { a, b },
        px: ?f32,
    ) void
```

lock one child of a SPLIT node to a fixed pixel size along the split axis. The other child absorbs the dockspace remainder when the host resizes. This is the "Tools panel stays 200px wide regardless of window size" UX. `parent_id`: the SPLIT node (NOT the child leaf). `side`: `.a` = top/left child; `.b` = bottom/right. `px`: target pixel size; `null` clears the lock and falls back to `ratio`-driven sizing. Locking both children at once is over-constrained; the layout falls back to `ratio` in that case (same behavior as no lock). No-op for leaf nodes or unknown ids. Splitter drag on a node with an active lock writes the new pixel size back into `size_ref` (preserving the lock semantic) instead of mutating `ratio`.

#### `Ui.dockBuilderSplitNode`

```zig
    pub fn dockBuilderSplitNode(
        self: Ui,
        node_id: Id,
        dir: Dir,
        ratio: f32,
    ) SplitResult
```

Split `node_id` along `dir` with the given `ratio`. Returns the two new child node ids. Existing window list (if any) is preserved on the side OPPOSITE `dir` - e.g. splitting `.left` with windows already docked puts the existing content on the right (b), with a fresh empty leaf on the left (a) waiting for new windows. Returns `.{.a = 0,.b = 0 }` on error (invalid node id, target is already a split, etc). Callers can detect by checking whether `a` is zero.

#### `Ui.dockSpace`

```zig
    pub fn dockSpace(
        self: Ui,
        str_id: []const u8,
        size: Vec2,
        opts: DockSpaceOpts,
    ) Id
```

Submit a dockspace within the current scope. Reserves `size` worth of layout space, looks up (or creates) the root `DockNode` for `str_id`, returns its `Id`. The returned id is stable across frames and can be passed to `dockBuilderDockWindow` / `dockBuilderSplitNode` to define the default layout. `size`: outer dimensions of the dockspace. Pass `Ui.contentRegionAvail()` to fill the remaining space of the containing window - typical pattern for "the whole window IS a dockspace." `opts.flags`: not yet used; reserved for future `passthru_central_node`, `no_split`, `no_resize` etc. matching imgui's `ImGuiDockNodeFlags_*`. What renders today (5.5b): a single empty container outline where docked windows WILL appear. 5.5c wires `Window.dock_node_id` so the actual docked-window rendering happens. 5.5d/e add splitters and drag-targets. Returns 0 if no `current_window` is set (must be called inside a `window()` scope).

#### `Ui.drag`

```zig
    pub fn drag(
        self: Ui,
        label: []const u8,
        v: anytype,
        opts: DragOpts,
    ) bool
```

Drag-to-edit numeric widget. Like `slider` but unbounded each pixel of mouse drag changes the value by `opts.speed`, hard-clamped only if `opts.min`/`opts.max` are set. Common uses: world coordinates, infinite-range tunables. var speed: f32 = 5.0; _ = ui.drag("Speed", &speed,.{.speed = 0.1 });

#### `Ui.drawListAllocator`

```zig
    pub fn drawListAllocator(self: Ui) Allocator
```

Allocator backing the DrawList's command list + variable payloads. Returns the UiContext's frame arena, which is reset at endFrame - so any slice you allocate through this is valid for exactly one frame. Demos that call `getDrawList().addPolyline(...)` pass the result here as the `alloc` argument.

#### `Ui.dummy`

```zig
    pub fn dummy(self: Ui, size: Vec2) void
```

Reserve a `size`-sized rectangle at the current cursor. Advances the layout cursor as if a widget that big had been drawn, and records it as the most-recent item so `isItem*` queries see a non-zero rect. Useful as a spacer or as a custom-render placeholder. ImGui: `Dummy(size)`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::Dummy` — add a dummy item of given size. unlike InvisibleButton(), Dummy() won't take the mouse click or be navigable into.

#### `Ui.editArrayList`

```zig
    pub fn editArrayList(
        self: Ui,
        label: []const u8,
        list_ptr: anytype,
    ) bool
```

Auto-build an editor UI for a `std.ArrayList(T)`. Renders a collapsible header with the count + an "Add" button that appends a default-initialized `T`, then one row per element with a "Remove" button. Each element is dispatched through the same field-type matrix as `editStruct` (struct → recursive editor, primitive → matching widget, etc.). Pass a pointer to your `std.ArrayList(T)`. The list's allocator is read from `ctx.gpa` (the same allocator used by every other persistent UI structure). Returns true on the frame ANY field changes OR the list grows/shrinks. var enemies: std.ArrayList(Enemy) =.empty; defer enemies.deinit(gpa); //... later, in update(): if (f.ui.editArrayList("Enemies", &enemies)) { // re-sort spatial index, etc. }

#### `Ui.editArrayListOpts`

```zig
    pub fn editArrayListOpts(
        self: Ui,
        label: []const u8,
        list_ptr: anytype,
        opts: anytype,
    ) bool
```

Same as `editArrayList` but accepts the same per-field opts struct as `editStructOpts`. Forwarded to each element when the element type is a struct so per-element fields can be constrained (e.g. `.health =.{.min = 0,.max = 100 }`).

#### `Ui.editStruct`

```zig
    pub fn editStruct(self: Ui, value_ptr: anytype) bool
```

Auto-build an editor UI for a struct via comptime reflection. Each field gets a widget chosen from its type: - `bool` → `checkbox` - `f32` / `f64` → `inputFloat` (or `slider` if opts gives min+max) - `i32` / `u32` / … → `inputInt` (or `slider` if opts gives min+max) - `[]const u8` → read-only `text` line (mutation TBD) - nested struct → `treeNode` + recursive `editStruct` - `enum` → `combo` over all enum values - `[3]f32` / `[4]f32`→ `colorEdit3` / `colorEdit4` (special-cased) - other arrays of float/int → one widget per element Returns true on the frame ANY field changed. var s: MySettings =.{}; if (f.ui.editStruct(&s)) { // re-apply settings... } `editStruct` dispatches to `editStructOpts` with empty opts.

#### `Ui.editStructOpts`

```zig
    pub fn editStructOpts(
        self: Ui,
        value_ptr: anytype,
        opts: anytype,
    ) bool
```

Same as `editStruct` but accepts a per-field options struct. `opts` is a struct literal whose field names match the value's fields; each entry is itself a struct with optional members: - `.min`, `.max` → switch to slider for numeric fields - `.fmt` → format string (default per type) - `.skip` → skip this field entirely - `.label` → override the displayed label - `.is_color` → force colorEdit dispatch for [3]/[4] f32 arrays Fields not mentioned in `opts` use defaults. Unknown opts fields are silently ignored - no @compileError - so a struct can grow without breaking existing call-sites. f.ui.editStructOpts(&state,.{ .speed =.{.min = 0,.max = 100,.fmt = "{d:.1}" }, .iterations =.{.min = 1,.max = 1000 }, .password =.{.skip = true }, });

#### `Ui.endCanvas`

```zig
    pub fn endCanvas(self: Ui, c: CanvasCtx) void
```

Close a canvas scope. Pops the clip rect that `beginCanvas` pushed, and truncates the transform stack back to its depth at begin time (so any pushTransforms the caller forgot to pop don't leak to the next canvas). Safe to call exactly once per matching begin.

#### `Ui.endChild`

```zig
    pub fn endChild(self: Ui) void
```

End the current child scope. Restores the parent's layout cursor and pops the clip rect from the draw list. Symmetric with `beginChild` - call EXACTLY once per `beginChild` that returned true. The `defer f.ui.endChild()` pattern is the right way to spell that.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::EndChild` — Windows Utilities

#### `Ui.endCombo`

```zig
    pub fn endCombo(self: Ui) void
```

Close a `beginCombo` scope. Pair with EXACTLY one `beginCombo` that returned true. ImGui: `EndCombo()`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::EndCombo` — only call EndCombo() if BeginCombo() returns true!

#### `Ui.endDisabled`

```zig
    pub fn endDisabled(self: Ui) void
```

Close the matching `beginDisabled` scope.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::EndDisabled` — Clipping

#### `Ui.endDragDropSource`

```zig
    pub fn endDragDropSource(self: Ui) void
```

Close a drag-drop source scope. Pairs with a `beginDragDropSource` that returned true.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::EndDragDropSource` — only call EndDragDropSource() if BeginDragDropSource() returns true!

#### `Ui.endDragDropTarget`

```zig
    pub fn endDragDropTarget(self: Ui) void
```

Close a drop-target scope. Pairs with a `beginDragDropTarget` that returned true.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::EndDragDropTarget` — only call EndDragDropTarget() if BeginDragDropTarget() returns true!

#### `Ui.endGroup`

```zig
    pub fn endGroup(self: Ui) void
```

Close the matching `beginGroup`. Computes the group's bounding rect, writes it into `last_item_*` for any follow-up `isItem*` query, and advances the parent cursor as if the group were a single item. ImGui: `EndGroup()`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::EndGroup` — unlock horizontal starting position + capture the whole group bounding box into one "item" (so you can use IsItemHovered() or layout primitives such as SameLine() on whole group, etc.)

#### `Ui.endItem`

```zig
    pub fn endItem(self: Ui, it: ItemCtx) void
```

Pair with `beginItem`. Advances the layout cursor by the item's size. Pass the `ItemCtx` returned from `beginItem` so the helper has the rect — and so a mistakenly-called `endItem` without a matching `beginItem` is a compile error (no ItemCtx in scope).

#### `Ui.endItemTooltip`

```zig
    pub fn endItemTooltip(self: Ui) void
```

Pair with `beginItemTooltip` when it returned true. Identical to `endTooltip` - separate name keeps the matched pair visually obvious in caller code.

#### `Ui.endListBox`

```zig
    pub fn endListBox(self: Ui) void
```

Close the matching `beginListBox` scope. Renders the border + advances the parent cursor. ImGui: `EndListBox()`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::EndListBox` — only call EndListBox() if BeginListBox() returned true!

#### `Ui.endMainMenuBar`

```zig
    pub fn endMainMenuBar(self: Ui) void
```

Close the main menu bar scope. Symmetric - call EXACTLY once per `beginMainMenuBar` that returned true. ImGui: `EndMainMenuBar()`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::EndMainMenuBar` — only call EndMainMenuBar() if BeginMainMenuBar() returns true!

#### `Ui.endMenu`

```zig
    pub fn endMenu(self: Ui) void
```

End a `beginMenu` scope. Symmetric - call EXACTLY once per `beginMenu` that returned true. ImGui: `EndMenu()`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::EndMenu` — only call EndMenu() if BeginMenu() returns true!

#### `Ui.endMenuBar`

```zig
    pub fn endMenuBar(self: Ui) void
```

Close the per-window menu bar scope. Symmetric - call EXACTLY once per `beginMenuBar` that returned true. ImGui: `EndMenuBar()`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::EndMenuBar` — only call EndMenuBar() if BeginMenuBar() returns true!

#### `Ui.endMultiSelect`

```zig
    pub fn endMultiSelect(self: Ui) *const MultiSelectIO
```

Close the multi-select scope and return the IO view with this loop's click-emitted requests. Call `applyRequests` on it to update storage.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::EndMultiSelect`

#### `Ui.endPopup`

```zig
    pub fn endPopup(self: Ui) void
```

Close the matching `beginPopup` scope. Symmetric - call EXACTLY once per `beginPopup` that returned true. `defer` is the recommended spelling. ImGui: `EndPopup()`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::EndPopup` — only call EndPopup() if BeginPopupXXX() returns true!

#### `Ui.endTabBar`

```zig
    pub fn endTabBar(self: Ui) void
```

Close the matching `beginTabBar` scope. Renders the bar→content separator line, GCs tabs the caller stopped submitting, advances the parent cursor past the tab bar.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::EndTabBar` — only call EndTabBar() if BeginTabBar() returns true!

#### `Ui.endTabItem`

```zig
    pub fn endTabItem(self: Ui) void
```

Close the matching `beginTabItem` scope. Symmetric - call EXACTLY once per `beginTabItem` that returned true. ImGui: `EndTabItem()`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::EndTabItem` — only call EndTabItem() if BeginTabItem() returns true!

#### `Ui.endTable`

```zig
    pub fn endTable(self: Ui) void
```

Close a table scope. Must match a `beginTable` that returned true.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::EndTable` — only call EndTable() if BeginTable() returns true!

#### `Ui.endTooltip`

```zig
    pub fn endTooltip(self: Ui) void
```

Close the tooltip block opened by `beginTooltip`. Safe to call when no tooltip is open (no-op).

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::EndTooltip` — only call EndTooltip() if BeginTooltip()/BeginItemTooltip() returns true!

#### `Ui.fillRect`

```zig
    pub fn fillRect(
        self: Ui,
        rect: Rectangle,
        c: Color,
    ) void
```

Convenience: draw a filled rectangle on the current window's draw list using the `Color` struct (not the packed `ColorU32` the underlying DrawList takes). Equivalent to `u.getForegroundDrawList().addRectFilled(rect, colorToU32(c))` but shorter at the call site — extension widgets that want to paint inside their own rect reach for this constantly. No-op if no window is currently open. Convenience: draw a filled rectangle on the current window's draw list using the `Color` struct (not the packed `ColorU32` the underlying DrawList takes). Equivalent to `u.getForegroundDrawList().addRectFilled(rect, colorToU32(c))` but shorter at the call site — extension widgets that want to paint inside their own rect reach for this constantly. No-op if no window is currently open.

#### `Ui.getBackgroundDrawList`

```zig
    pub fn getBackgroundDrawList(self: Ui) DrawListHandle
```

Get a handle on the BACKGROUND draw list - renders under all windows. Use for backdrops, grids, watermarks, debug overlays that should never occlude widgets. const dl = ui.getBackgroundDrawList(); dl.addRectFilled(.{.x = 0,.y = 0,.width = 800,.height = 600 }, 0xFF1F2937); dl.addText("debug",.{ 8, 8 }, 12, 0xFFE2E8F0); The handle is per-frame: it carries a pointer into the context's `background_dl` and the frame arena. Don't stash it across frames - the arena resets at endFrame and the pointer becomes invalid.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::GetBackgroundDrawList` — this draw list will be the first rendered one. Useful to quickly draw shapes/text behind dear imgui contents.

#### `Ui.getClipboardText`

```zig
    pub fn getClipboardText(self: Ui) []const u8
```

Return the most recently read clipboard text, or "" if no successful read has occurred yet. Reads are inherently async on the web platform - the imgui-style sync getter is a stub for surface parity; the actual async fetch happens via `runtime.getClipboardTextAsync()` with a polling handle. Long-form: store the result of the async read in `ctx.clipboard_cache_buf` and this getter returns that. Today: empty (no caching wired yet - that lands with the `InputTextCallbackData` paste plumbing in Phase A4). ImGui: `GetClipboardText() const char*`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::GetClipboardText`

#### `Ui.getContentRegionAvail`

```zig
    pub fn getContentRegionAvail(self: Ui) Vec2
```

Available content region from the current cursor to the right edge of the window's content area, and from the cursor to the bottom of the content area. Useful for sizing widgets that should fill remaining space. ImGui: `GetContentRegionAvail()`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::GetContentRegionAvail` — available space from current position. THIS IS YOUR BEST FRIEND.

#### `Ui.getCursorPos`

```zig
    pub fn getCursorPos(self: Ui) Vec2
```

Read the current layout cursor (next-widget position) for the active window. Useful before calling `setCursorPos` to stash + restore manual layout decisions, or to overlay custom rendering at a known point in the flow. Returns `(0, 0)` outside any window scope.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::GetCursorPos` — [window-local] cursor position in window-local coordinates. This is not your best friend.

#### `Ui.getCursorScreenPos`

```zig
    pub fn getCursorScreenPos(self: Ui) Vec2
```

Current layout cursor position in screen-space pixels. Useful when you want to place custom-rendered primitives at the spot the next widget would land. Returns `.{0,0}` when called outside a window block.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::GetCursorScreenPos` — cursor position, absolute coordinates. THIS IS YOUR BEST FRIEND (prefer using this rather than GetCursorPos(), also more useful to work with ImDrawList API).

#### `Ui.getCursorStartPos`

```zig
    pub fn getCursorStartPos(self: Ui) Vec2
```

Cursor position at the start of the current window's content area, in screen-space pixels. Useful as a reference point for computing relative offsets within a window — e.g. `getCursorPos() - getCursorStartPos()` is how far down the content area you've laid out so far. Returns `.{0,0}` when called outside a window block. Differs from raw ImGui `GetCursorStartPos()` which returns window-local coords; the rest of zimr's cursor accessors use screen-space, so this getter follows that convention. ImGui: `GetCursorStartPos()`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::GetCursorStartPos` — [window-local] initial cursor position, in window-local coordinates. Call GetCursorScreenPos() after Begin() to get the absolute coordinates version.

#### `Ui.getDrawList`

```zig
    pub fn getDrawList(self: Ui) ?*DrawList
```

Get the draw list of the currently-active window so the caller can record arbitrary `addLine` / `addCircleFilled` / etc. primitives inside it. Returns null when called outside a `ui.window(...)` block. The returned pointer is valid only for the rest of the current frame.

#### `Ui.getForegroundDrawList`

```zig
    pub fn getForegroundDrawList(self: Ui) DrawListHandle
```

Get a handle on the FOREGROUND draw list - renders above all windows + popups (but below tooltips and drag previews). Use for watermarks, custom HUDs, debug crosshairs. const dl = ui.getForegroundDrawList(); dl.addText("DEBUG BUILD",.{ 10, 10 }, 14, 0xFFEF4444); Like the background list, the handle is per-frame.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::GetForegroundDrawList` — this draw list will be the last rendered one. Useful to quickly draw shapes/text over dear imgui contents.

#### `Ui.getId`

```zig
    pub fn getId(self: Ui, str_id: []const u8) Id
```

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::GetID` — calculate unique ID (hash of whole ID stack + given parameter). e.g. if you want to query into ImGuiStorage yourself

#### `Ui.getItemID`

```zig
    pub fn getItemID(self: Ui) Id
```

Hashed ID of the most-recent widget (0 if none). Useful for debug + custom hover/active dispatch.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::GetItemID` — get ID of last item (~~ often same ImGui::GetID(label) beforehand)

#### `Ui.getItemRectMax`

```zig
    pub fn getItemRectMax(self: Ui) Vec2
```

Bottom-right of the most-recent widget's rectangle (screen px).

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::GetItemRectMax` — get lower-right bounding rectangle of the last item (screen space)

#### `Ui.getItemRectMin`

```zig
    pub fn getItemRectMin(self: Ui) Vec2
```

Top-left of the most-recent widget's rectangle (screen px). raylib parity: `GetItemRectMin`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::GetItemRectMin` — get upper-left bounding rectangle of the last item (screen space)

#### `Ui.getItemRectSize`

```zig
    pub fn getItemRectSize(self: Ui) Vec2
```

Width × height of the most-recent widget. ImGui: `GetItemRectSize`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::GetItemRectSize` — get size of last item

#### `Ui.getMouseCursor`

```zig
    pub fn getMouseCursor(self: Ui) MouseCursor
```

The cursor value most recently passed to `setMouseCursor`. Defaults to `.default` for a fresh `InputState`. Useful for stacked hover detection where multiple widgets may try to claim the cursor - read the current value, decide, then set.

#### `Ui.getMouseDragDelta`

```zig
    pub fn getMouseDragDelta(
        self: Ui,
        btn: MouseButton,
        lock_threshold: f32,
    ) Vec2
```

Cursor displacement since the most recent press of `button`, or `(0, 0)` if the button isn't held / threshold not yet crossed. Threshold semantics match `isMouseDragging` - pass `-1` for the imgui default.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::GetMouseDragDelta` — return the delta from the initial clicking position while the mouse button is pressed or was just released. This is locked and return 0.0f until the mouse moves past a distance threshold at least once (uses io.MouseDraggingThreshold if lock_threshold < 0.0f)

#### `Ui.getOrPutState`

```zig
    pub fn getOrPutState(
        self: Ui,
        comptime T: type,
        id: Id,
        opts: StateOpts,
    ) StateGetOrPut(T)
```

Look up (T, id) — return a slot pointer plus whether it existed. When `!found_existing`, the slot's contents are **undefined** (matches `std.HashMap.getOrPut` semantics) — the caller MUST write through `value_ptr` before reading.  Standard idiom for a type with a sensible zero / default:  const r = u.getOrPutState(MyKnobState, id,.{}); if (!r.found_existing) r.value_ptr.* =.{}; r.value_ptr.angle += delta;  For a type without a default, supply the seed explicitly:  const r = u.getOrPutState(DragState, id,.{}); if (!r.found_existing) r.value_ptr.* =.{ .origin = mouse_pos,.active = true, };  One hash on the outer map, one on the inner map. No allocation in the common case (slot already exists).  `opts.persist = true` enrolls T in the on-disk save/load path. Flag is sticky once set — re-passing `false` after a `true` does not disenroll, because some other call site may rely on the registration.

#### `Ui.getScrollMaxX`

```zig
    pub fn getScrollMaxX(self: Ui) f32
```

Maximum allowed horizontal scroll for the active window. Reads last frame's computed value (same convergence semantics as `getScrollMaxY`). Returns 0 if no window is active, the horizontal-scrollbar flag is unset, or content fits without X-overflow. Imgui: `GetScrollMaxX()`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::GetScrollMaxX` — get maximum scrolling amount ~~ ContentSize.x - WindowSize.x - DecorationsSize.x

#### `Ui.getScrollMaxY`

```zig
    pub fn getScrollMaxY(self: Ui) f32
```

Maximum allowed scroll for the active window - clamp upper bound enforced by end-of-frame. Reads last frame's computed value; for mid-frame programmatic scroll the previous frame's max is the best estimate (subsequent frames converge). Returns 0 if no window is active or if content fits without overflow. ImGui: `GetScrollMaxY()`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::GetScrollMaxY` — get maximum scrolling amount ~~ ContentSize.y - WindowSize.y - DecorationsSize.y

#### `Ui.getScrollX`

```zig
    pub fn getScrollX(self: Ui) f32
```

Current horizontal scroll offset of the active window in pixels. Returns 0 if no window is active OR if the window hasn't opted into `WindowFlags.horizontal_scrollbar` (scroll_x is forced to 0 in `closeWindow` when the flag is unset). Imgui: `GetScrollX()`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::GetScrollX` — get scrolling amount [0 .. GetScrollMaxX()]

#### `Ui.getScrollY`

```zig
    pub fn getScrollY(self: Ui) f32
```

Current vertical scroll offset of the active window in pixels. Returns 0 if no window is active. ImGui: `GetScrollY()`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::GetScrollY` — get scrolling amount [0 .. GetScrollMaxY()]

#### `Ui.getState`

```zig
    pub fn getState(
        self: Ui,
        comptime T: type,
        id: Id,
    ) ?*T
```

Read-only state lookup. Returns null if no slot exists for (T, id). Use when you want to react to existing state but not create it — most callers want `getOrPutState`.

#### `Ui.getTreeNodeToLabelSpacing`

```zig
    pub fn getTreeNodeToLabelSpacing(self: Ui) f32
```

Horizontal offset from the tree-node's start-X to the label start. Useful for `sameLine`-aligning content underneath a node's label column. Returns `font_size + 4` (triangle width + gap), matching the existing `treeNodeImpl` layout. Imgui: `GetTreeNodeToLabelSpacing()`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::GetTreeNodeToLabelSpacing` — horizontal distance preceding label when using TreeNode*() or Bullet() == (g.FontSize + style.FramePadding.x*2) for a regular unframed TreeNode

#### `Ui.getWindowContentRegionMax`

```zig
    pub fn getWindowContentRegionMax(self: Ui) Vec2
```

Bottom-right of the window's content region, in WINDOW-LOCAL coordinates (relative to the window's top-left corner). Returns `.{0,0}` when called outside a window block. Pair with `getWindowContentRegionMin` to compute the full content rect in window-local space. ImGui: `GetWindowContentRegionMax()`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::GetWindowContentRegionMax` — Content boundaries max for the window (roughly (0,0)+Size-Scroll), in window-local coordinates. You should never need this. Always use GetCursorScreenPos() and GetContentRegionAvail()!

#### `Ui.getWindowContentRegionMin`

```zig
    pub fn getWindowContentRegionMin(self: Ui) Vec2
```

Top-left of the window's content region, in WINDOW-LOCAL coordinates (relative to the window's top-left corner). Returns `.{0,0}` when called outside a window block. Matches ImGui's coordinate convention for this specific getter (window-local), even though most of zimr's cursor accessors use screen-space — the "window content region" framing implies window-local space, and matches what callers expect for fitting custom layouts into a window. ImGui: `GetWindowContentRegionMin()`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::GetWindowContentRegionMin` — Content boundaries min for the window (roughly (0,0)-Scroll), in window-local coordinates. You should never need this. Always use GetCursorScreenPos() and GetContentRegionAvail()!

#### `Ui.getWindowHeight`

```zig
    pub fn getWindowHeight(self: Ui) f32
```

Height of the current window in pixels. Shortcut for `getWindowSize()[1]`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::GetWindowHeight` — get current window height (IT IS UNLIKELY YOU EVER NEED TO USE THIS). Shortcut for GetWindowSize().y.

#### `Ui.getWindowPos`

```zig
    pub fn getWindowPos(self: Ui) Vec2
```

Position of the current window's top-left in screen pixels. ImGui: `GetWindowPos()`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::GetWindowPos` — get current window position in screen space (IT IS UNLIKELY YOU EVER NEED TO USE THIS. Consider always using GetCursorScreenPos() and GetContentRegionAvail() instead)

#### `Ui.getWindowSize`

```zig
    pub fn getWindowSize(self: Ui) Vec2
```

Outer size of the current window (including title bar) in pixels. ImGui: `GetWindowSize()`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::GetWindowSize` — get current window size (IT IS UNLIKELY YOU EVER NEED TO USE THIS. Consider always using GetCursorScreenPos() and GetContentRegionAvail() instead)

#### `Ui.getWindowWidth`

```zig
    pub fn getWindowWidth(self: Ui) f32
```

Width of the current window in pixels. Shortcut for `getWindowSize()[0]`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::GetWindowWidth` — get current window width (IT IS UNLIKELY YOU EVER NEED TO USE THIS). Shortcut for GetWindowSize().x.

#### `Ui.image`

```zig
    pub fn image(
        self: Ui,
        source: anytype,
        size: ?Vec2,
    ) void
```

Display a GPU texture inline at the current cursor. Accepts any of: - `u32` / `u32` - raw GL texture ID (legacy / advanced) - `Texture` / `*const Texture` - raylib-native, infers size - `RenderTexture` / `*const RenderTexture` - render target, infers size AND auto-flips V coordinates so the user sees it the right way up (RTs in OpenGL are origin-bottom-left) Pass `null` for `size` to use the texture's natural pixel size, or a `Vec2` to scale. No interaction - pair with `selectable` or `button` for clickable image variants. This is the bridge that makes recursive UI-on-render-texture composition feel natural: render a HUD to an RT, then `f.ui.image(my_rt, null)` displays it elsewhere.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::Image` — <-- 'border_col' was removed in favor of ImGuiCol_ImageBorder. If you use 'tint_col', use ImageWithBg() instead.

#### `Ui.imageButton`

```zig
    pub fn imageButton(
        self: Ui,
        str_id: []const u8,
        source: anytype,
        size: ?Vec2,
    ) bool
```

Clickable variant of `image`. Renders the texture as a button-sized rect (using the same `source` polymorphism as `image`) and returns true on the frame the user clicks it. `size: ?Vec2` - `null` means "use the texture's natural pixel dimensions". ImGui: `ImageButton(str_id, texture, size,...)`.

#### `Ui.indent`

```zig
    pub fn indent(self: Ui) void
```

Manually push a horizontal indent on the cursor. Pair with `unindent()`. Useful for visual grouping outside of trees.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::Indent` — move content position toward the right, by indent_w, or style.IndentSpacing if indent_w <= 0

#### `Ui.input`

```zig
    pub fn input(
        self: Ui,
        label: []const u8,
        v: anytype,
        opts: InputScalarOpts,
    ) bool
```

Text-entry float input - like `inputText` but typed scalar. Renders the current value formatted via `opts.fmt`; user types a new number and presses Enter to commit. Invalid input is silently rejected on commit (value stays at last good). Returns true on the frame the value changes. Text-entry numeric input, generic over scalar or array. Pass `*f32` / `*i32` for a single value; pass `*[N]f32` / `*[N]i32` for a multi-component widget (N narrow boxes side-by-side). Returns true on any frame the value(s) changed. ImGui: `InputFloat` / `InputInt` / `InputFloat3` / `InputInt2`, collapsed to one generic function. var pos: [3]f32 =.{ 0, 0, 0 }; _ = ui.input("position", &pos,.{});

#### `Ui.inputFloat`

```zig
    pub fn inputFloat(
        self: Ui,
        label: []const u8,
        v: *f32,
        opts: InputScalarOpts,
    ) bool
```

Text-entry float input. See `inputFloat`. ImGui: `InputFloat(label, v, step, step_fast, format, flags)`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::InputFloat`

#### `Ui.inputInt`

```zig
    pub fn inputInt(
        self: Ui,
        label: []const u8,
        v: *i32,
        opts: InputScalarOpts,
    ) bool
```

Text-entry int input. See `inputFloat`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::InputInt`

#### `Ui.inputText`

```zig
    pub fn inputText(
        self: Ui,
        label: []const u8,
        buf: []u8,
        len: *usize,
        opts: InputTextOpts,
    ) bool
```

Single-line text edit. Caller passes the backing buffer (`buf: []u8`) and a pointer to its current content length (`len: *usize`). We grow `len` up to `buf.len` on type, never realloc. Returns true on any frame where contents changed. Pattern: var name_buf: [64]u8 = undefined; var name_len: usize = 0; _ = ui.inputText("Name", &name_buf, &name_len,.{}); Supports: insert at cursor, backspace, delete, left/right arrows, home/end, click-to-position cursor, commit-on-Enter (defocuses), cancel-on-Escape (defocuses, no revert). No selection ranges or clipboard support.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::InputText`

#### `Ui.inputTextMultiline`

```zig
    pub fn inputTextMultiline(
        self: Ui,
        label: []const u8,
        buf: []u8,
        len: *usize,
        size: Vec2,
        opts: InputTextOpts,
    ) bool
```

Multi-line text editor. Same buffer-and-length contract as `inputText`, plus an explicit `size: Vec2` for the editor's rendered dimensions (in pixels - caller decides; `size.x <= 0` auto-fills the remaining row width). Returns true on any frame the buffer changed. Differences from `inputText`: - **Enter inserts a newline** (single-line variant defocuses on Enter). - **Up / Down arrows** move the cursor between lines, preserving column where possible. - **Click-to-place** computes the target line by y- distance from the editor's top, then the column by x- distance into that line. - **Escape defocuses** without committing. Limitations (deferred to later phases): - No selection (shift-arrow, mouse-drag). - No clipboard cut/copy/paste - lands in Phase A4 with `InputTextCallbackData`. - No undo/redo. - No tab character handling (Tab is consumed as a focus-traversal key by the runtime; pasting a string with `\t` works, typing one doesn't). - No scroll - caller wraps in `beginChild(...)` for scrollable editing of buffers taller than `size.y`.

#### `Ui.inputTextWithHint`

```zig
    pub fn inputTextWithHint(
        self: Ui,
        label: []const u8,
        hint: []const u8,
        buf: []u8,
        len: *usize,
        opts: InputTextOpts,
    ) bool
```

Like `inputText`, but `hint` is shown in dimmed `style.text_disabled` color when the buffer is empty AND the widget isn't focused. Disappears the moment the user types or focuses. Equivalent to setting `opts.hint` directly - provided for ImGui parity (`InputTextWithHint`). _ = ui.inputTextWithHint("Search", "type to search...", &state.query_buf, &state.query_len,.{});

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::InputTextWithHint`

#### `Ui.inspect`

```zig
    pub fn inspect(
        self: Ui,
        label: []const u8,
        value_ptr: anytype,
    ) bool
```

Reflection inspector (P1, AM-8). Wraps `editStruct` in a labeled, collapsible tree node — the right shape for a section header in a settings panel or dev-tools tab. if (u.inspect("Style", &ctx.style)) { // re-apply theme... } The treeNode collapses by default; opens once on first click, remembers per-frame open state. When closed, returns false and skips the field iteration entirely (zero per-frame cost for collapsed inspectors).  Use `editStruct(ptr)` directly when you DON'T want a header (e.g. when the inspector is already inside its own tab or window section), or `inspectWithAttrs` for per-field hints.

#### `Ui.inspectWithAttrs`

```zig
    pub fn inspectWithAttrs(
        self: Ui,
        label: []const u8,
        value_ptr: anytype,
        opts: anytype,
    ) bool
```

Labeled variant of `editStructOpts`. Same per-field opts shape; wraps in a collapsible tree node like `inspect`. if (u.inspectWithAttrs("Settings", &s,.{ .speed =.{.min = 0,.max = 100 }, .password =.{.skip = true }, })) {... }

#### `Ui.invisibleButton`

```zig
    pub fn invisibleButton(
        self: Ui,
        id_str: []const u8,
        size: Vec2,
    ) bool
```

Click-target rectangle with NO visual. Same hit-testing as `button` but doesn't draw a frame or label - useful for drag handles, hidden hit zones, "tap-anywhere-to-dismiss" overlays, or clickable banners where you draw your own visuals. Returns true on press (mouse-up while hovered). ImGui: `InvisibleButton(str_id, size, flags)`. Size semantics: a zero on either axis means "use a sensible default" - for X, fill the remaining row width to the right padding; for Y, the standard widget row height (`font_size + 2 * frame_padding[1]`). This mirrors imgui's `0 → -FLT_MIN → fill` convention. `id_str` disambiguates this hit zone from other invisible regions in the same window - it's hashed into the widget ID stack but never rendered.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::InvisibleButton` — flexible button behavior without the visuals, frequently useful to build custom behaviors using the public api (along with IsItemActive, IsItemHovered, etc.)

#### `Ui.isAltDown`

```zig
    pub fn isAltDown(self: Ui) bool
```

True if either left or right Alt is currently held.

#### `Ui.isCtrlDown`

```zig
    pub fn isCtrlDown(self: Ui) bool
```

True if either left or right Control is currently held. On macOS this is the literal Control key, NOT Cmd — platform-aware modifier remapping (imgui's KeyCtrl-on-mac = Cmd convention) is not yet done; callers on mac who want "command-or-control" should check both `isCtrlDown` and `isSuperDown`.

#### `Ui.isDisabled`

```zig
    pub fn isDisabled(self: Ui) bool
```

True if any active `beginDisabled` scope is currently open. Widgets check this to gate input + apply the alpha multiplier.

#### `Ui.isItemActivated`

```zig
    pub fn isItemActivated(self: Ui) bool
```

True if the most-recent widget became active this frame (transition from inactive → active). ImGui: `IsItemActivated`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::IsItemActivated` — was the last item just made active (item was previously inactive).

#### `Ui.isItemActive`

```zig
    pub fn isItemActive(self: Ui) bool
```

True if the user is currently interacting with the most- recently-submitted widget (e.g. a slider being dragged, a button being held). raylib parity: `IsItemActive`.
