→ **imgui:** `ImGui::IsItemActive` — is the last item active? (e.g. button being held, text field being edited. This will continuously return true while holding mouse button on an item. Items that don't interact will always return false)

#### `Ui.isItemClicked`

```zig
    pub fn isItemClicked(self: Ui) bool
```

True if the user clicked the most-recent widget this frame (mouse-down inside its rect on the same frame the click register). raylib parity: `IsItemClicked` (mouse button 0).

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::IsItemClicked` — is the last item hovered and mouse clicked on? (**)  == IsMouseClicked(mouse_button) && IsItemHovered()Important. (**) this is NOT equivalent to the behavior of e.g. Button(). Read comments in function definition.

#### `Ui.isItemDeactivated`

```zig
    pub fn isItemDeactivated(self: Ui) bool
```

True if the most-recent widget transitioned from active → inactive this frame. ImGui: `IsItemDeactivated`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::IsItemDeactivated` — was the last item just made inactive (item was previously active). Useful for Undo/Redo patterns with widgets that require continuous editing.

#### `Ui.isItemDeactivatedAfterEdit`

```zig
    pub fn isItemDeactivatedAfterEdit(self: Ui) bool
```

True if the most-recent widget transitioned from active → inactive this frame AND the user edited its value at any point during the activation. Useful for "commit on release" patterns. ImGui: `IsItemDeactivatedAfterEdit`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::IsItemDeactivatedAfterEdit` — was the last item just made inactive and made a value change when it was active? (e.g. Slider/Drag moved). Useful for Undo/Redo patterns with widgets that require continuous editing. Note that you may get false positives (some widgets such as Combo()/ListBox()/Selectable() will return true even when clicking an already selected item).

#### `Ui.isItemEdited`

```zig
    pub fn isItemEdited(self: Ui) bool
```

True if the user mutated the most-recent widget's value this frame (slider drag, checkbox toggle, inputText keystroke). ImGui: `IsItemEdited`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::IsItemEdited` — did the last item modify its underlying value this frame? or was pressed? This is generally the same as the "bool" return value of many widgets.

#### `Ui.isItemFocused`

```zig
    pub fn isItemFocused(self: Ui) bool
```

True if the most-recent widget is currently keyboard-focused. Equivalent to `isItemActive` since there's no keyboard nav.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::IsItemFocused` — is the last item focused for keyboard/gamepad navigation?

#### `Ui.isItemHovered`

```zig
    pub fn isItemHovered(self: Ui, opts: HoveredFlags) bool
```

Was the most recently submitted item under the mouse this frame? Use after a widget call to attach hover behavior: if (ui.button("Save",.{})) save(); if (ui.isItemHovered(.{})) ui.setTooltip("Ctrl+S",.{}); Opts-arg form: pass `.{}` for the default "directly hovered, not blocked" semantics, or set fields on `HoveredFlags` for relaxed checks: if (ui.isItemHovered(.{.allow_when_disabled = true }))... Today most `HoveredFlags` fields are accepted but NO-OP for items - they relax blocking checks zimr doesn't yet enforce (popup-blocking, active-item occlusion, per-item z-order, disabled-state, nav override). User code can set them today and pick up the behavior automatically when each machinery lands. Hover-delay flags are not on `HoveredFlags` yet - deferred until per-id hover-start timestamp state lands on `UiContext`. ImGui: `IsItemHovered(flags)`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::IsItemHovered` — is the last item hovered? (and usable, aka not blocked by a popup, etc.). See ImGuiHoveredFlags for more options.

#### `Ui.isItemToggledOpen`

```zig
    pub fn isItemToggledOpen(self: Ui) bool
```

True if the most-recently-submitted tree node was toggled this frame. Imgui: `IsItemToggledOpen()` (queries the ToggledOpen status flag on LastItemData).

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::IsItemToggledOpen` — was the last item open state toggled? set by TreeNode().

#### `Ui.isKeyDown`

```zig
    pub fn isKeyDown(self: Ui, key: KeyCode) bool
```

True while `button` is held and the cursor has moved more than `lock_threshold` pixels from where the button was pressed. Pass `-1` for the imgui-default threshold (6 px). Distinguishes click-vs-drag for custom widgets: if (ui.isMouseDragging(.left, -1)) { const d = ui.getMouseDragDelta(.left, -1); widget_pos.x += d.x; widget_pos.y += d.y; ui.resetMouseDragDelta(.left); } True while `key` is currently held. Reads the per-frame `InputSnapshot.keys` array — no syscall, no runtime input lookup, just an array index. Stable across the whole frame. Mirrors imgui's `IsKeyDown` (imgui.h:1097).

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::IsKeyDown` — is key being held.

#### `Ui.isKeyPressed`

```zig
    pub fn isKeyPressed(self: Ui, key: KeyCode) bool
```

True for exactly one frame: the frame `key` transitioned up→down. Edge event. Mirrors imgui's `IsKeyPressed` (imgui.h:1098). This is one-shot only; for key-repeat firing, use `isKeyPressedOrRepeat`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::IsKeyPressed` — was key pressed (went from !Down to Down)? Repeat rate uses io.KeyRepeatDelay / KeyRepeatRate.

#### `Ui.isKeyPressedOrRepeat`

```zig
    pub fn isKeyPressedOrRepeat(self: Ui, key: KeyCode) bool
```

`isKeyPressed` plus key-repeat: fires on initial press AND at the OS-defined repeat cadence while held. Used by text input for backspace/delete/arrow keys so the user can hold the key for accelerated motion/deletion. Mirrors imgui's `IsKeyPressed(key, repeat=true)` (the default `repeat=true` variant — `imgui.h:1098`).  Implementation routes through raylib's `isKeyPressedRepeat` for the held-and-repeating case (because raylib owns the OS-level repeat timer; mirroring it in zimr would drift). Initial-press case uses the snapshot for consistency with `isKeyPressed`.

#### `Ui.isKeyReleased`

```zig
    pub fn isKeyReleased(self: Ui, key: KeyCode) bool
```

True for exactly one frame: the frame `key` transitioned down→up. Edge event. Mirrors imgui's `IsKeyReleased` (imgui.h:1099).

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::IsKeyReleased` — was key released (went from Down to !Down)?

#### `Ui.isMouseDragging`

```zig
    pub fn isMouseDragging(
        self: Ui,
        btn: MouseButton,
        lock_threshold: f32,
    ) bool
```

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::IsMouseDragging` — is mouse dragging? (uses io.MouseDraggingThreshold if lock_threshold < 0.0f)

#### `Ui.isMouseHoveringRect`

```zig
    pub fn isMouseHoveringRect(
        self: Ui,
        x0: f32,
        y0: f32,
        x1: f32,
        y1: f32,
    ) bool
```

True if the current mouse position is inside the axis-aligned rectangle `(x0..x1, y0..y1)`. Edges are inclusive on near side, exclusive on far side (imgui semantics - half-open intervals so adjacent rects don't double-count). Coordinates are in the same space as the widget's draw calls.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::IsMouseHoveringRect` — is mouse hovering given bounding rect (in screen space). clipped by current clipping settings, but disregarding of other consideration of focus/window ordering/popup-block.

#### `Ui.isPopupOpen`

```zig
    pub fn isPopupOpen(self: Ui, str_id: []const u8) bool
```

True if the popup is currently open. ImGui: `IsPopupOpen(str_id)`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::IsPopupOpen` — return true if the popup is open.

#### `Ui.isShiftDown`

```zig
    pub fn isShiftDown(self: Ui) bool
```

True if either left or right Shift is currently held. Convenience for the very common case of testing a modifier without caring which side. Mirrors imgui's `io.KeyShift`.

#### `Ui.isSuperDown`

```zig
    pub fn isSuperDown(self: Ui) bool
```

True if either left or right Super (Windows / Command) is held.

#### `Ui.isWindowFocused`

```zig
    pub fn isWindowFocused(self: Ui, opts: FocusedFlags) bool
```

True if the current window has focus, optionally including children / parents per `FocusedFlags`. Pass `.{}` for the default "exactly me" semantics; set `.root_and_child_windows` for "anything in my hierarchy." - `child_windows`: true if THIS window OR any descendant has focus. Walks the focused window's parent chain. - `root_window`: walk to root of THIS window's parent chain before testing. - `root_window` + `child_windows`: "is anything in my whole hierarchy focused?" Docking-relevant combo. - `any_window`: true if ANY window in `UiContext` has focus. `no_popup_hierarchy` is accepted but no-op today (popup hierarchy linkage lands in a later phase). Always-false outside a window scope. ImGui: `IsWindowFocused(flags)`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::IsWindowFocused` — is current window focused? or its root/child, depending on flags. see flags for options.

#### `Ui.isWindowHovered`

```zig
    pub fn isWindowHovered(self: Ui, opts: HoveredFlags) bool
```

True if the mouse is over the current window's outer rect AND no later (higher-z) window covers that point, optionally including children / parents per `HoveredFlags`. Pass `.{}` for the default "exactly me" semantics. Hierarchy traversal (`child_windows`, `root_window`, `any_window`, `no_popup_hierarchy`) mirrors `isWindowFocused`. Implementation specifics: - `child_windows = true`: the mouse is over me OR any descendant. Because children are nested inside the parent's outer rect (zimr children don't escape parent bounds enforced by the defensive clamp in `beginChildImpl`), this is equivalent to `pointInRect(mouse, w.outer_rect)` for non-overlapping descendants. For the FULL imgui semantics we walk the hovered window's parent chain - handles the case where a hovered popup-of-child should count as "child." The `allow_when_blocked_by_*`, `allow_when_overlapped_by_*`, `allow_when_disabled`, and `no_nav_override` flags are accepted but no-op today - see `HoveredFlags` field docstrings for when each lands. ImGui: `IsWindowHovered(flags)`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::IsWindowHovered` — is current window hovered and hoverable (e.g. not blocked by a popup/modal)? See ImGuiHoveredFlags_ for options. IMPORTANT: If you are trying to check whether your mouse should be dispatched to Dear ImGui or to your underlying app, you should not use this function! Use the 'io.WantCaptureMouse' boolean for that! Refer to FAQ entry "How can I tell whether to dispatch mouse/keyboard to Dear ImGui or my application?" for details.

#### `Ui.itemAdd`

```zig
    pub fn itemAdd(
        self: Ui,
        rect: Rectangle,
        id: Id,
    ) bool
```

Register a widget rectangle with the given `id`, returning `true` if the widget should be drawn this frame. Returns `false` when the rect is fully outside the current clip rect (so the caller can skip drawing entirely — early exit). Side effects: sets `ctx.hovered_id = id` when the mouse is over the rect.  Mirrors imgui's `ItemAdd(const ImRect& bb, ImGuiID id)`. Today the clip-rect check is simple "is rect within window bounds"; future canvas / scroll-clip integration (Q4) will tighten this.  The CALLER computes the rect and the id (`u.getId(label)`). itemAdd doesn't claim the layout cursor — pair with `itemSize` after drawing.

#### `Ui.itemSize`

```zig
    pub fn itemSize(self: Ui, size: Vec2) void
```

Advance the layout cursor by `size`, marking that a widget of those dimensions occupied the current cursor position. Must be called by every widget at end-of-submission, AFTER drawing (so the cursor advance happens once).  Mirrors imgui's `ItemSize(ImVec2)`. Imgui's optional `text_baseline_y` is not surfaced — zimr widgets don't currently use cross-baseline alignment. Add if needed.

#### `Ui.labelText`

```zig
    pub fn labelText(
        self: Ui,
        label: []const u8,
        comptime fmt: []const u8,
        args: anytype,
    ) void
```

Render `label` on the left + formatted value on the right of the same row, with a small gap between. Common pattern for key/value displays ("Health: 100", "Status: ready"). The label is in normal text color; the formatted value uses it too. For value-emphasis, wrap the args side in your own `text` / `textColored` calls; this is the imgui-parity convenience for the common "Label: value" form.

#### `Ui.listBox`

```zig
    pub fn listBox(
        self: Ui,
        label: []const u8,
        current: *i32,
        items: []const []const u8,
    ) bool
```

Convenience - render a complete labelled list box that shows `items` as selectable rows; `current.*` is the selected index. Returns true on the frame the user changes selection. ImGui: `ListBox(label, current, items, items_count, height_in_items)`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::ListBox`

#### `Ui.menuItem`

```zig
    pub fn menuItem(
        self: Ui,
        label: []const u8,
        opts: MenuItemOpts,
    ) bool
```

A clickable menu entry. Returns true on the click frame. `opts.shortcut` is a display-only string (right-aligned in the row) - wiring the actual keyboard shortcut is the caller's responsibility. `opts.selected` shows a checkmark when true (toggle-style menu items). ImGui: `MenuItem(label, shortcut, selected, enabled)`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::MenuItem` — return true when activated.

#### `Ui.miniPlot`

```zig
    pub fn miniPlot(
        self: Ui,
        label: []const u8,
        rect: Rectangle,
        xs: []const f32,
        ys: []const f32,
    ) bool
```

Single-line plot in a canvas-clipped rectangle.  No axes, no title, no markers beyond a "latest value" dot — just enough to verify the pillars compose.  `xs` and `ys` must be the same length; `xs` must be monotonic ascending for the line to render sensibly (not enforced).  Returns `true` if the user pressed R while the plot was hovered (a courtesy signal — most callers ignore it).

#### `Ui.newLine`

```zig
    pub fn newLine(self: Ui) void
```

Drop the cursor down by one row's worth - useful between two `sameLine`-prefixed widgets when you want to break the row without inserting a separator.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::NewLine` — undo a SameLine() or force a new line when in a horizontal-layout context.

#### `Ui.openPopup`

```zig
    pub fn openPopup(self: Ui, str_id: []const u8) void
```

Mark a popup as "should be open from this frame onward". The popup itself doesn't render until matched with `beginPopup(str_id)`. `str_id` is hashed against the current window's id so two windows can have popups with identical labels without collision. Anchor position captured from the current mouse position - popup will appear at the cursor (matches ImGui's right-click-context-menu behaviour). ImGui: `OpenPopup(str_id)`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::OpenPopup` — call to mark popup as open (don't call every frame!).

#### `Ui.plotHistogram`

```zig
    pub fn plotHistogram(
        self: Ui,
        label: []const u8,
        values: []const f32,
        opts: PlotOpts,
    ) void
```

Bar-chart sparkline. Same API as `plotLines` but renders each sample as a filled column instead of connected lines. Suited to discrete-count data (histogram bins, per-second event counts, etc).

#### `Ui.plotLines`

```zig
    pub fn plotLines(
        self: Ui,
        label: []const u8,
        values: []const f32,
        opts: PlotOpts,
    ) void
```

Sparkline-style line plot. Takes a slice of `f32` samples; draws a connected line strip scaled to fit a row-height rectangle. Optional overlay text appears centered in the plot rect (imgui-style "current value" caption). When hovered, shows the value at the hovered index as a tooltip. Pattern (typical: a 60-frame FPS history): _ = ui.plotLines("fps", &state.fps_hist,.{ .overlay = "60.0 fps", .min = 0, .max = 120, }); Pass `min == max` to auto-scale to the data's observed range. `height` is the plot rect's height; `0` means use a sensible default of 80px.

#### `Ui.popFont`

```zig
    pub fn popFont(self: Ui) void
```

Pair with `pushFont`. Restores the most-recently-pushed font. Safe to call on an empty stack (no-op). ImGui: `PopFont()`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::PopFont`

#### `Ui.popId`

```zig
    pub fn popId(self: Ui) void
```

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::PopID` — pop from the ID stack.

#### `Ui.popItemFlag`

```zig
    pub fn popItemFlag(self: Ui) void
```

Pop the matching `pushItemFlag`. Always pair with a push; no-op when the stack is empty (defensive).

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::PopItemFlag` — Parameters stacks (current window)

#### `Ui.popItemWidth`

```zig
    pub fn popItemWidth(self: Ui) void
```

Pop the most-recent `pushItemWidth` value. Symmetric - call exactly once per push. ImGui: `PopItemWidth()`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::PopItemWidth`

#### `Ui.popRenderTexture`

```zig
    pub fn popRenderTexture(self: Ui, gl: *GlState) void
```

Pop the current render-texture surface. Must pair with a matching `pushRenderTexture` - flushes pending geometry to the RT, restores the outer matrix + framebuffer + viewport.

#### `Ui.popStyle`

```zig
    pub fn popStyle(self: Ui) void
```

Pair with `pushStyle`. Restores the most-recently-pushed field to its prior value. Safe to call on an empty stack (no-op).

#### `Ui.progressBar`

```zig
    pub fn progressBar(
        self: Ui,
        fraction: f32,
        size: Vec2,
        overlay: []const u8,
    ) void
```

Progress indicator. `fraction` clamped to [0, 1]. `size` passes 0 in either axis to mean "auto" (full content width; 1-row default height). `overlay` is an optional centred caption - pass `""` for none. ImGui: `ProgressBar(fraction, size_arg, overlay)`.

#### `Ui.pushFont`

```zig
    pub fn pushFont(self: Ui, font: ?*const Font) void
```

Push a font onto the style stack. Subsequent widgets render with `*font` until the matching `popFont()`. Use for code sections (mono font), section headers (larger / different face), etc. Currently `font_size` is NOT pushed alongside - fonts in zimr carry their own design size separately from the rendered size. To change rendered size while pushed, call `pushStyle( "font_size", new_size)` separately. ImGui's `PushFont(font, size)` does both; zimr keeps them separable so a single font can be scaled by callers without re-pushing. Convenience wrapper over `pushStyle("font", font)`. Pairs with `popFont()`. Pushing a null pointer reverts to the default cached font. ImGui: `PushFont(font)`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::PushFont` — Use NULL as a shortcut to keep current font. Use 0.0f to keep current size.

#### `Ui.pushId`

```zig
    pub fn pushId(self: Ui, str_id: []const u8) void
```

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::PushID` — push string into the ID stack (will hash string).

#### `Ui.pushIdInt`

```zig
    pub fn pushIdInt(self: Ui, int_id: i64) void
```

#### `Ui.pushItemFlag`

```zig
    pub fn pushItemFlag(self: Ui, flags: ItemFlags) void
```

Push an `ItemFlags` scope. Merged with the existing top-of-stack via `mergeItemFlags` (disabling AND repeating flags OR; auto_close_popups ANDs - disabling a parent's auto-close propagates). Use this for cross-widget behavior toggles. For example: ui.pushItemFlag(.{.button_repeat = true }); defer ui.popItemFlag(); if (ui.button("+",.{})) increment(); // auto-repeats while held ImGui: `PushItemFlag(flag, enabled)` / `PopItemFlag()`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::PushItemFlag` — modify specified shared item flag, e.g. PushItemFlag(ImGuiItemFlags_NoTabStop, true)

#### `Ui.pushItemWidth`

```zig
    pub fn pushItemWidth(self: Ui, width: f32) void
```

Push an item width onto the stack - every subsequent widget reads it as their preferred width until popped. Stacks, so nested `pushItemWidth` calls compose. ImGui: `PushItemWidth(width)`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::PushItemWidth` — push width of items for common large "item+label" widgets. >0.0f: width in pixels, <0.0f align xx pixels to the right of window (so -FLT_MIN always align width to the right side).

#### `Ui.pushRenderTexture`

```zig
    pub fn pushRenderTexture(
        self: Ui,
        gl: *GlState,
        rt: RenderTexture,
    ) void
```

Push a render texture as the current UI surface. Subsequent widget calls render to the RT. Pair with `popRenderTexture()`. Recursive composition is the whole point - push an RT, render a HUD to it, pop, then `image(rt, null)` displays it elsewhere (and the consumer can be in another RT, or screen, or on a material applied to a 3D mesh). Surfaces stack 8 deep.

#### `Ui.pushStyle`

```zig
    pub fn pushStyle(
        self: Ui,
        comptime field_name: []const u8,
        v: anytype,
    ) void
```

Temporarily override one field of `Style` until the matching `popStyle()`. Reflection-based: `field_name` is a comptime string that must name an actual field of `Style`; `value` is typed via `anytype` and must coerce to that field's type (compile error otherwise). ui.pushStyle("frame_padding",.{ 12, 8 }); defer ui.popStyle(); _ = ui.button("Chunky",.{}); Stack-cap: `style_stack`'s 32 slots. Every push must be paired with a pop; mismatched pushes/pops leave the style in a wrong state for the rest of the frame.

#### `Ui.radioButton`

```zig
    pub fn radioButton(
        self: Ui,
        label: []const u8,
        current: *i32,
        v: i32,
    ) bool
```

Mutually-exclusive selection: a small circle + label. Returns true if `*current` changed. Pattern (matches ImGui's per-button overload): _ = ui.radioButton("Easy", &state.diff, 0); _ = ui.radioButton("Hard", &state.diff, 1);

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::RadioButton` — use with e.g. if (RadioButton("one", my_value==1)) { my_value = 1; }

#### `Ui.resetMouseDragDelta`

```zig
    pub fn resetMouseDragDelta(self: Ui, btn: MouseButton) void
```

Reset the drag anchor for `button` to the current cursor position - subsequent `getMouseDragDelta` calls start fresh from here. Idiomatic for incremental drag handlers that consume the delta each frame.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::ResetMouseDragDelta` — IMGUI_API ImGuiMouseCursor GetMouseCursor();                                                // get desired mouse cursor shape. Important: reset in ImGui::NewFrame(), this is updated during the frame. valid before Render(). If you use software rendering by setting io.MouseDrawCursor ImGui will render those for you

#### `Ui.sameLine`

```zig
    pub fn sameLine(self: Ui, opts: SameLineOpts) void
```

Place the next widget on the same row as the last. Optional `offset_x` overrides the default cursor advance. Place the next widget on the same row as the previous one, just to its right (separated by `item_spacing[0]`, or at an explicit X if `opts.offset_x` is non-zero). Matches imgui's `SameLine` semantics - specifically: - cursor X = right edge of the previous row's items (`cursor_pos_prev_line[0]`) + spacing. - cursor Y = top of the previous row (`cursor_pos_prev_line[1]`). - `line_height` = `prev_line_height` so the row's max-height tracking continues accumulating as the next widget submits. The new widget submits at the resolved cursor; on its `advanceLayout`, `cursor_pos_prev_line` and `prev_line_height` are updated to include it, and the cursor wraps to the next row past the tallest widget on this row.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::SameLine` — call between widgets or groups to layout them horizontally. X position given in window coordinates.

#### `Ui.selectable`

```zig
    pub fn selectable(
        self: Ui,
        label: []const u8,
        selected: bool,
        opts: SelectableOpts,
    ) bool
```

List-item-style clickable text. Returns true on the click frame. When `selected` is true the row paints with the active highlight. Foundation for menu items, file pickers, list selectors.

#### `Ui.separator`

```zig
    pub fn separator(self: Ui) void
```

Vertical separator. Fills the content width at current cursor.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::Separator` — separator, generally horizontal. inside a menu bar or in horizontal layout mode, this becomes a vertical separator.

#### `Ui.separatorText`

```zig
    pub fn separatorText(self: Ui, label: []const u8) void
```

Section-header rule with a centered label. Renders as a short rule on the left, the `label` in normal text colour, then a long rule filling to the right edge of the window's work area. If `label` is empty, degrades to a plain full-width rule at the row's vertical centre (slightly heavier than `separator` - 2px vs 1px). ImGui: `SeparatorText(label)`. Use for top-level panel sections inside a window - like the "Controls / Log / Status" bands in the log viewer demo.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::SeparatorText` — currently: formatted text with a horizontal line

#### `Ui.serializeLayout`

```zig
    pub fn serializeLayout(self: Ui, gpa: Allocator) ![]u8
```

Serialize all currently-known windows' positions, sizes, scroll states, and user-resized flags into a binary buffer the caller owns and frees. Format is tagged with magic + version so future format changes can be detected. Pair with `applyLayout(bytes)` to restore. Useful for "remember window layout across launches". const buf = try f.ui.serializeLayout(gpa); defer gpa.free(buf); try std.fs.cwd().writeFile("layout.bin", buf);

#### `Ui.setClipboardText`

```zig
    pub fn setClipboardText(self: Ui, s: []const u8) void
```

Write text to the system clipboard. Sync from caller's perspective; the underlying browser API (`navigator.clipboard.writeText`) is async fire-and-forget and may silently fail if the document lacks focus or the user hasn't granted permission. On the host build this is a no-op. ImGui: `SetClipboardText(text)`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::SetClipboardText` — Settings/.Ini Utilities

#### `Ui.setCursorPos`

```zig
    pub fn setCursorPos(self: Ui, pos: Vec2) void
```

Force the layout cursor to a specific position within the current window. Use case: manually placing a widget at a pixel-precise spot, or restoring after a `getCursorPos` snapshot. No-op outside any window scope.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::SetCursorPos` — [window-local] "

#### `Ui.setDragDropPayload`

```zig
    pub fn setDragDropPayload(
        self: Ui,
        comptime T: type,
        v: *const T,
    ) void
```

Register a typed payload for the in-flight drag. Generic over `T` - the type tag is `@typeName(T)`, so the target side can match types at compile time. `value` is a pointer; the bytes are copied into the drag state's inline buffer. Must be called inside a `beginDragDropSource` scope that returned true. Payload size cap: `DragDropState.PAYLOAD_CAP` bytes. Oversize values silently fail to register - the source scope still renders preview, but no target will accept.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::SetDragDropPayload` — type is a user defined string of maximum 32 characters. Strings starting with '_' are reserved for dear imgui internal types. Data is copied and held by imgui. Return true when payload has been accepted.

#### `Ui.setItemTooltip`

```zig
    pub fn setItemTooltip(
        self: Ui,
        comptime fmt: []const u8,
        args: anytype,
    ) void
```

Show a tooltip on hover, automatically gated to "only if the last-submitted item is hovered this frame." Saves the two-line `if (isItemHovered()) setTooltip(...)` boilerplate at the call site - and that boilerplate is the typical pattern, so most tooltips should use this. ImGui: `SetItemTooltip(fmt,...)`. Was missing from the zimr surface before now (v2 audit §C23 listed it ❌); filled here because `textLinkOpenURL` needs it.

#### `Ui.setKeyboardFocusHere`

```zig
    pub fn setKeyboardFocusHere(self: Ui) void
```

Queue keyboard focus for the NEXT focusable widget submitted this frame. Call BEFORE the widget you want focused (e.g. before an `inputText` you want to be ready for typing the moment a modal opens). No-op if no focusable widget follows in the same frame. Today only `inputText` honors the flag. if (state.search_just_opened) { ui.setKeyboardFocusHere(); state.search_just_opened = false; } _ = ui.inputText("Search", &buf, &len,.{});

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::SetKeyboardFocusHere` — focus keyboard on the next widget. Use positive 'offset' to access sub components of a multiple component widget. Use -1 to access previous widget.

#### `Ui.setMouseCursor`

```zig
    pub fn setMouseCursor(self: Ui, cursor: MouseCursor) void
```

Set the canvas cursor style. Pass the imgui-style mouse-cursor enum - `default`, `pointing_hand`, `ibeam`, `resize_ew`, etc. Pairs naturally with hover detection: if (ui.isItemHovered(.{})) ui.setMouseCursor(.pointing_hand); The value persists until something else sets it (no per-frame reset), so most apps call this conditionally from their hover branches. No-op when the in-flight frame's input state isn't available - defensive for ctx-only test harnesses.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::SetMouseCursor` — set desired mouse cursor shape

#### `Ui.setNextItemOpen`

```zig
    pub fn setNextItemOpen(self: Ui, open: bool) void
```

Force the next tree node's open state. Cleared after consumption. Imgui: `SetNextItemOpen`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::SetNextItemOpen` — set next TreeNode/CollapsingHeader open state.

#### `Ui.setNextItemSelectionUserData`

```zig
    pub fn setNextItemSelectionUserData(self: Ui, data: u64) void
```

Tag the next widget with a selection-user-data value. In a MultiSelect scope, the widget's item-side hooks read this to identify the item for range-select bookkeeping and SetRange request emission. Outside a scope, the value is still stored (matches imgui - widgets pre-tag cheaply) but nothing consumes it.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::SetNextItemSelectionUserData`

#### `Ui.setNextItemWidth`

```zig
    pub fn setNextItemWidth(self: Ui, width: f32) void
```

Override the width of the next widget's primary input element. Cleared after the next widget consumes it. ImGui: `SetNextItemWidth(width)`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::SetNextItemWidth` — set width of the _next_ common large "item+label" widget. >0.0f: width in pixels, <0.0f align xx pixels to the right of window (so -FLT_MIN always align width to the right side)

#### `Ui.setNextWindowBgAlpha`

```zig
    pub fn setNextWindowBgAlpha(self: Ui, alpha: f32) void
```

Override the next window's background alpha - 0 = fully transparent, 1 = opaque. Cleared after the window opens. ImGui: `SetNextWindowBgAlpha(alpha)`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::SetNextWindowBgAlpha` — set next window background color alpha. helper to easily override the Alpha component of ImGuiCol_WindowBg/ChildBg/PopupBg. you may also use ImGuiWindowFlags_NoBackground.

#### `Ui.setNextWindowFocus`

```zig
    pub fn setNextWindowFocus(self: Ui) void
```

Raise the next window to top of z-order on this frame. ImGui: `SetNextWindowFocus()`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::SetNextWindowFocus` — set next window to be focused / top-most. call before Begin()

#### `Ui.setNextWindowPos`

```zig
    pub fn setNextWindowPos(
        self: Ui,
        pos: Vec2,
        opts: SetNextWindowOpts,
    ) void
```

Set the position the next `window(...)` call applies. Default is "every frame" (matches ImGui's `ImGuiCond_Always`); pass `.{.once = true }` to apply only on first creation. ImGui: `SetNextWindowPos(pos, cond)`.

#### `Ui.setNextWindowSize`

```zig
    pub fn setNextWindowSize(
        self: Ui,
        size: Vec2,
        opts: SetNextWindowOpts,
    ) void
```

Set the size the next `window(...)` call applies. See `setNextWindowPos` for the once-vs-always distinction. ImGui: `SetNextWindowSize(size, cond)`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::SetNextWindowSize` — set next window size. set axis to 0.0f to force an auto-fit on this axis. call before Begin()

#### `Ui.setNextWindowSizeConstraints`

```zig
    pub fn setNextWindowSizeConstraints(
        self: Ui,
        min: ?Vec2,
        max: ?Vec2,
    ) void
```

Constrain the next window's size to `[min, max]` on its next open + every subsequent frame. Either bound can be `null` for "no limit". Useful for chrome-bounded windows (e.g. "this window must be at least 300×200 so its content fits"). Constraints are also applied to user drag-resize gestures - the window simply can't grow/shrink past the bounds. Imgui: `SetNextWindowSizeConstraints` (`imgui.cpp:8664`). Imgui's custom-callback variant (for non-rectangular constraints) is deferred - see Step 1.x followup.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::SetNextWindowSizeConstraints` — set next window size limits. use 0.0f or FLT_MAX if you don't want limits. Use -1 for both min and max of same axis to preserve current size (which itself is a constraint). Use callback to apply non-trivial programmatic constraints.

#### `Ui.setScrollFromPosY`

```zig
    pub fn setScrollFromPosY(
        self: Ui,
        local_y: f32,
        ratio: f32,
    ) void
```

Like `setScrollHereY` but with an explicit content-area y-coordinate instead of the cursor. `local_y` is relative to the window's content-area top-left. ImGui: `SetScrollFromPosY(local_y, center_y_ratio = 0.5)`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::SetScrollFromPosY` — adjust scrolling amount to make given position visible. Generally GetCursorStartPos() + offset to compute a valid position.

#### `Ui.setScrollHereY`

```zig
    pub fn setScrollHereY(self: Ui, ratio: f32) void
```

Adjust scroll so the current cursor position appears at `ratio` within the visible vertical area: - 0.0 - cursor at the TOP of visible area - 0.5 - cursor CENTERED - 1.0 - cursor at the BOTTOM Idiomatic use: at end-of-frame in a log viewer, call `setScrollHereY(1.0)` to auto-scroll-to-bottom. ImGui: `SetScrollHereY(center_y_ratio = 0.5)`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::SetScrollHereY` — adjust scrolling amount to make current cursor position visible. center_y_ratio=0.0: top, 0.5: center, 1.0: bottom. When using to make a "default/current item" visible, consider using SetItemDefaultFocus() instead.

#### `Ui.setScrollX`

```zig
    pub fn setScrollX(self: Ui, x: f32) void
```

Set the horizontal scroll position directly. Clamped to `[0, scroll_max_x]`. No-op when the window hasn't opted into `WindowFlags.horizontal_scrollbar` (the next `closeWindow` would clobber any write to scroll_x back to 0 anyway). Imgui: `SetScrollX(scroll_x)`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::SetScrollX` — set scrolling amount [0 .. GetScrollMaxX()]

#### `Ui.setScrollY`

```zig
    pub fn setScrollY(self: Ui, y: f32) void
```

Set the vertical scroll position directly. Clamped to `[0, scroll_max_y]`. Useful for "remember scroll position" or "jump to top" buttons. ImGui: `SetScrollY(scroll_y)`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::SetScrollY` — set scrolling amount [0 .. GetScrollMaxY()]

#### `Ui.setTooltip`

```zig
    pub fn setTooltip(
        self: Ui,
        comptime fmt: []const u8,
        args: anytype,
    ) void
```

Submit a tooltip to be drawn above all other content at end of frame. Position is the current mouse position + (16, 16) offset. Last call this frame wins.

#### `Ui.setWindowFocus`

```zig
    pub fn setWindowFocus(self: Ui, name: []const u8) void
```

Raise an existing top-level window to focused. No-op if the window doesn't exist. Imgui: `imgui.cpp:12813`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::SetWindowFocus` — (not recommended) set current window to be focused / top-most. prefer using SetNextWindowFocus().

#### `Ui.setWindowPos`

```zig
    pub fn setWindowPos(
        self: Ui,
        name: []const u8,
        pos: Vec2,
    ) void
```

Set the position of an existing top-level window by name. No-op if no window with that name has been opened yet (matches imgui's `SetWindowPos(name,...)` which silently no-ops on FindWindowByName failure). Always-semantics applies unconditionally; ImGui's Cond gating is filed as a follow-up. Imgui: `imgui.cpp:8540`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::SetWindowPos` — (not recommended) set current window position - call within Begin()/End(). prefer using SetNextWindowPos(), as this may incur tearing and side-effects.

#### `Ui.setWindowSize`

```zig
    pub fn setWindowSize(
        self: Ui,
        name: []const u8,
        size: Vec2,
    ) void
```

Set the size of an existing top-level window by name. See `setWindowPos` re: no-op when window absent. Marks `user_resized = true` so the window won't auto-fit growth after this call. Imgui: `imgui.cpp:8586`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::SetWindowSize` — (not recommended) set current window size - call within Begin()/End(). set to ImVec2(0, 0) to force an auto-fit. prefer using SetNextWindowSize(), as this may incur tearing and minor side-effects.

#### `Ui.shortcut`

```zig
    pub fn shortcut(
        self: Ui,
        chord: KeyChord,
        opts: ShortcutOpts,
    ) bool
```

True if `chord` fires this frame. Edge-triggered on the key, level-triggered on modifiers. Pairs with conditional drawing to attach hotkey behavior to specific UI states: if (ui.shortcut(.{.key =.s,.ctrl = true },.{})) save(); if (ui.shortcut(.{.key =.escape },.{})) cancel(); Modifier strictness: Ctrl+S won't fire a bare-S shortcut. Pass `opts.repeat = true` to also fire on auto-repeat events (held key). See `runtime.input.KeyChord` and `ShortcutOpts` for the underlying types.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::Shortcut`

#### `Ui.showDemoWindow`

```zig
    pub fn showDemoWindow(
        self: Ui,
        state: *DemoState,
        p_open: ?*bool,
    ) void
```

Show the canonical zimr/imgui demo window. One call walks every widget category the port supports, organized into a tab bar (Widgets / Layout / Style / About). Useful as a living showcase, a smoke-test that every widget at least submits without panic, and a copy-paste reference for users learning the API. `state` carries the persistent values the demo's widgets drive - sliders, checkboxes, text buffers, etc. Pass a pointer to a `DemoState` initialized with `.{}` (all defaults). `p_open`, when non-null, is read at the top: if false, the demo doesn't render. When the user clicks the window's close button (not yet implemented for general windows), `*p_open` becomes false. Pass `null` to render unconditionally. var demo: ui.DemoState =.{}; //... in update(): f.ui.showDemoWindow(&demo, null);

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::ShowDemoWindow` — create Demo window. demonstrate most ImGui features. call this to learn about the library! try to make it always available in your application!

#### `Ui.slider`

```zig
    pub fn slider(
        self: Ui,
        label: []const u8,
        v: anytype,
        opts: SliderOpts,
    ) bool
```

Drag-to-edit slider. Generic over numeric pointer types: `*f32`, `*f64`, `*i32`, `*u32`, `*i64`, `*u64`, etc. Returns true if the value changed this frame. var f: f32 = 0.5; _ = ui.slider("opacity", &f,.{.min = 0,.max = 1 }); Comptime dispatch picks the right inner implementation float vs int - based on the pointed-to type.

#### `Ui.sliderAngle`

```zig
    pub fn sliderAngle(
        self: Ui,
        label: []const u8,
        rad: *f32,
        opts: SliderAngleOpts,
    ) bool
```

Slider over an angle stored as RADIANS, displayed and dragged in DEGREES. ImGui's `SliderAngle` convention: the math wants radians, the user wants degrees. Returns true when the value changed this frame. `min_deg` / `max_deg` are in degrees (default -360 / 360). _ = ui.sliderAngle("Rotation", &state.yaw_rad,.{});

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::SliderAngle`

#### `Ui.smallButton`

```zig
    pub fn smallButton(self: Ui, label: []const u8) bool
```

Compact button - zero vertical frame padding, just label-tall. Useful inline with text for actions like "?" or "[x]" markers, or in dense toolbars where vertical space matters. ImGui: `SmallButton(label)`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::SmallButton` — button with (FramePadding.y == 0) to easily embed within text

#### `Ui.spacing`

```zig
    pub fn spacing(self: Ui) void
```

Add empty space on the current line, then start a new line.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::Spacing` — add vertical spacing.

#### `Ui.splitter`

```zig
    pub fn splitter(
        self: Ui,
        str_id: []const u8,
        split_pos: *f32,
        axis: SplitterAxis,
        opts: SplitterOpts,
    ) bool
```

Draggable splitter bar between two adjacent panes. Returns `true` while the user is dragging (held). `split_pos` is the size of the FIRST pane (left for `.x`, top for `.y`). The widget mutates it on drag, clamped by `opts.min1` and `opts.min2` (against `total - min2 - bar_width`). Canonical usage with `beginChild`: const total_w = u.getContentRegionAvail()[0]; const total_h = u.getContentRegionAvail()[1]; const right_w = total_w - state.left_w - 4; if (u.beginChild("L",.{ state.left_w, total_h },.{})) { defer u.endChild(); //... left content } u.sameLine(.{}); _ = u.splitter("split", &state.left_w,.x,.{ .min1 = 80,.min2 = 100,.total_along_axis = total_w, }); u.sameLine(.{}); if (u.beginChild("R",.{ right_w, total_h },.{})) { defer u.endChild(); //... right content }

#### `Ui.spring`

```zig
    pub fn spring(
        self: Ui,
        label: []const u8,
        opts: SpringOpts,
    ) f32
```

Physical spring following `opts.target`.  Returns the current value.  Each call advances the spring by one `delta_time` step using semi-implicit Euler integration.  Pass a new `opts.target` each frame; the spring smoothly follows.  At rest, the call is cheap (zero work beyond a settle test).

#### `Ui.strokeRect`

```zig
    pub fn strokeRect(
        self: Ui,
        rect: Rectangle,
        c: Color,
    ) void
```

Convenience: stroked rectangle outline. See `fillRect`.

#### `Ui.style`

```zig
    pub fn style(self: Ui) *Style
```

Direct mutable access to the active style (Tier A - see `docs/raylib-ui-integration.md`). Lets users tweak colors without reaching into internal `ctx`. Pattern: f.ui.style().button = z.colors.amber_500;

#### `Ui.styleEditor`

```zig
    pub fn styleEditor(self: Ui) bool
```

Build a complete editor for the active `Style` via `editStruct`. Every Color slot becomes a `colorEdit` widget, every Vec2 becomes a side-by-side pair of inputFloats, every f32 becomes an inputFloat. Returns true on the frame any style field changed. if (f.ui.styleEditor()) { // restyle just happened - bump theme rev counter etc. }

#### `Ui.styleOverride`

```zig
    pub fn styleOverride(self: Ui, overrides: PartialStyle) StyleGuard
```

Override one or more style fields for a scope. Returns a `StyleGuard` that restores the prior values when `g.restore()` is called. Use with `defer`:  const g = u.styleOverride(.{.text = my_red }); defer g.restore(); u.text("warning",.{});  Only the fields you set on `overrides` are touched — null fields leave the live style alone. Nesting is safe: each guard saves the value live at the time IT ran, so multiple stacked overrides restore correctly in defer-stack order.

#### `Ui.tableGetColumnCount`

```zig
    pub fn tableGetColumnCount(self: Ui) ?u32
```

Number of columns configured at `beginTable`.  Null if no table is active.  Stable across the entire frame; reads the count the caller passed to `beginTable`, not the number of `tableSetupColumn` calls made so far.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::TableGetColumnCount` — return number of columns (value passed to BeginTable)

#### `Ui.tableGetColumnFlags`

```zig
    pub fn tableGetColumnFlags(self: Ui, index: u32) ?TableColumnFlags
```

Snapshot of column `index`'s queryable flag state.  Null if no active table or `index >= column_count`.  See `TableColumnFlags` docs re: what's included vs why some `TableColumnOpts` fields (sizing/width/weight) aren't roundtrippable through column state.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::TableGetColumnFlags` — return column flags so you can query their Enabled/Visible/Sorted/Hovered status flags. Pass -1 to use current column.

#### `Ui.tableGetColumnIndex`

```zig
    pub fn tableGetColumnIndex(self: Ui) ?u32
```

Index of the column currently being populated (0-based). Null if no table is active, or if no `tableNextColumn` has run yet this row (i.e. between `tableNextRow` and the first `tableNextColumn`).  Useful for per-cell branching when the loop body is shared across columns but renders slightly different content per column.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::TableGetColumnIndex` — return current column index.

#### `Ui.tableGetColumnName`

```zig
    pub fn tableGetColumnName(self: Ui, index: u32) ?[]const u8
```

Display name of column `index` as configured at `tableSetupColumn`, or null if no active table or if `index >= column_count`.  The returned slice points into the column's inline `name_buf` and is stable across the current frame.  Columns whose `tableSetupColumn` call passed an empty label return an empty slice (not null).

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::TableGetColumnName` — return "" if column didn't have a name declared by TableSetupColumn(). Pass -1 to use current column.

#### `Ui.tableGetSortSpecs`

```zig
    pub fn tableGetSortSpecs(self: Ui) ?*const TableSortSpecs
```

Return the active table's current sort spec, or null if no sort is configured (no columns clicked yet). Pointer is stable for the rest of the current frame. Read between `tableHeadersRow` and the first `tableNextRow` to sort the data before submitting it.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::TableGetSortSpecs` — get latest sort specs for the table (NULL if not sorting).  Lifetime: don't hold on this pointer over multiple frames or past any subsequent call to BeginTable().

#### `Ui.tableHeadersRow`

```zig
    pub fn tableHeadersRow(self: Ui) void
```

Render the table's header row. Call BEFORE the first `tableNextRow`. Each column gets a clickable header that cycles sort state when clicked. Shift-click appends as a secondary key (up to N=3 per decision Q8). Plain click either flips the direction (if already primary) or promotes that column to the new primary. Pattern: u.tableHeadersRow(); if (u.tableGetSortSpecs()) |specs| { std.mem.sort(Row, &rows, specs, sortCmp); } for (rows) |r| { u.tableNextRow(); ... }

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::TableHeadersRow` — submit a row with headers cells based on data provided to TableSetupColumn() + submit context menu

#### `Ui.tableNextColumn`

```zig
    pub fn tableNextColumn(self: Ui) bool
```

Advance to the next column within the current row. Sets the layout cursor to the column's x position; the next widget renders inside that column's clip rect. Returns true when the column is "in bounds" (i.e. we haven't run past `n_columns` for this row).

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::TableNextColumn` — append into the next column (or first column of next row if currently in last column). Return true when column is visible.

#### `Ui.tableNextRow`

```zig
    pub fn tableNextRow(self: Ui) void
```

Advance to the next data row. Resets the current column index to 0 and computes the row's y position based on the previous row's height.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::TableNextRow` — append into the first cell of a new row. 'min_row_height' include the minimum top and bottom padding aka CellPadding.y * 2.0f.

#### `Ui.tableSetCellBgColor`

```zig
    pub fn tableSetCellBgColor(self: Ui, color: Color) void
```

Override the background color of the CURRENT cell — the one just entered via `tableNextColumn`. Call AFTER `tableNextColumn` for the column you want to tint. Use cases: heat-map cells, flag specific values, draw attention to a single field without highlighting the whole row. The cell bg paints UNDER the cell's content (P7.2's 2-channel render) and OVER the row bg (cell-level overrides are more specific than row-level — so they win visually). No-op when called outside a table or before any `tableNextColumn`. Pass `Color.transparent` to clear a pending override that hasn't been painted yet. ImGui equivalent: `TableSetBgColor(ImGuiTableBgTarget_CellBg, color)` with the implicit "current column" target.

#### `Ui.tableSetRowBgColor`

```zig
    pub fn tableSetRowBgColor(self: Ui, color: Color) void
```

Override the current row's background color, overriding any alternating tint that `TableOpts.row_bg` would otherwise apply. Call AFTER `tableNextRow` and BEFORE `tableNextColumn` to set the bg for the row about to be populated. Use cases: highlight a selected row, flag a warning row in red, mark a "current user" row in accent, etc. Pass `Color.transparent` (alpha=0) to force NO background even when alternating tint would otherwise paint one.

#### `Ui.tableSetupColumn`

```zig
    pub fn tableSetupColumn(
        self: Ui,
        label: []const u8,
        opts: TableColumnOpts,
    ) void
```

Configure one column. Call between `beginTable` and the first `tableNextRow`; subsequent calls during data rows are no-ops. Order is left-to-right.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::TableSetupColumn`

#### `Ui.text`

```zig
    pub fn text(
        self: Ui,
        comptime fmt: []const u8,
        args: anytype,
    ) void
```

#### `Ui.textColored`

```zig
    pub fn textColored(
        self: Ui,
        color: Color,
        comptime fmt: []const u8,
        args: anytype,
    ) void
```

Like `text`, but with an explicit color. Convenience for status messages, error highlights, headings.

#### `Ui.textDisabled`

```zig
    pub fn textDisabled(
        self: Ui,
        comptime fmt: []const u8,
        args: anytype,
    ) void
```

Like `text`, but rendered in the dimmed `style.text_disabled` color. Use for hints, secondary info, captions - anything the eye should pass over before the main content.

#### `Ui.textLink`

```zig
    pub fn textLink(self: Ui, label: []const u8) bool
```

Click-styled text. Renders `label` in `style.text_link` with a 1px underline below, switches to `style.text_link_hovered` and shows the pointing-hand cursor while hovered, and returns true on press. Use for in-text links that trigger an in-app action (open a panel, focus a widget, etc.) - for "open this URL" links, use `textLinkOpenURL` instead. ImGui: `TextLink(label)`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::TextLink` — hyperlink text button, return true when clicked

#### `Ui.textLinkOpenURL`

```zig
    pub fn textLinkOpenURL(
        self: Ui,
        label: []const u8,
        url: []const u8,
    ) bool
```

Click-styled text that opens `url` in a new browser tab on press. Hovering shows a tooltip with the destination URL so the user always sees where the link goes before clicking. Right- click pops a "Copy link" context menu (currently desktop-only phone support arrives with step 2.2's long-press bridge). Returns true on press for the rare caller that wants to react in-app to the click in addition to opening the URL. ImGui: `TextLinkOpenURL(label, url)`. Implementation is a composer over public methods - `textLink` for click semantics, `setItemTooltip` for the URL preview, `beginPopupContextItem` + `menuItem` for the right-click menu. Browsers may block the `window.open` call if it doesn't fire inside a user-gesture handler (the `runtime.core.openURL` path goes through the JS bridge from `web.zig`); a click on this widget IS a user gesture so the normal path works.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::TextLinkOpenURL` — hyperlink text button, automatically open file/url when clicked

#### `Ui.textWrapped`

```zig
    pub fn textWrapped(
        self: Ui,
        comptime fmt: []const u8,
        args: anytype,
    ) void
```

Like `text`, but soft-wraps at the right edge of the available content area. Wraps on word boundaries when possible, falls back to mid-word splits if a single token is longer than the line. No allocation: writes into the per- frame stack buffer.

#### `Ui.treeNode`

```zig
    pub fn treeNode(
        self: Ui,
        label: []const u8,
        opts: TreeNodeOpts,
    ) bool
```

Recursive disclosure-triangle hierarchy node. Library-managed open state (persistent across frames in `UiContext.tree_open_state`). Returns true when open - the caller should submit child widgets inside the `if` block and pair with `treePop()` on the way out. Pattern: if (ui.treeNode("Player",.{})) { defer ui.treePop(); _ = ui.slider("HP", &state.hp,.{.min = 0,.max = 100 }); _ = ui.slider("MP", &state.mp,.{.min = 0,.max = 100 }); if (ui.treeNode("Inventory",.{})) { defer ui.treePop(); _ = ui.checkbox("Sword", &state.has_sword); } } Each open node bumps the window's `indent_x` so children visually nest. Nodes are stable across frames (open state keyed by hashed label + parent ID). Opts-arg form: pass `.{}` for the default click-toggle node, or set fields on `TreeNodeOpts` for flags like `.default_open`, `.leaf`, `.framed`, etc. See `TreeNodeOpts` for the full flag inventory. if (ui.treeNode("src",.{.default_open = true })) { defer ui.treePop(); _ = ui.treeNode("ui.zig",.{.leaf = true }); ui.treePop(); // pair with leaf } For `opts.leaf = true`, always returns true (no toggle, no arrow - caller still calls `treePop` if they push children, otherwise can skip the pop). Replaces the old `treeNode(label)` + `treeNodeEx(label, opts)` dual API.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::TreeNode`

#### `Ui.treeNodeGetOpen`

```zig
    pub fn treeNodeGetOpen(self: Ui, str_id: []const u8) bool
```

Query a tree node's stored open state without rendering. Useful for parent code that wants to know if a section is expanded. `str_id` must hash to the same id the tree node uses (i.e. its label inside the current id-stack scope). Returns `default_open` if no state stored. Imgui: `TreeNodeGetOpen(id)`.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::TreeNodeGetOpen` — retrieve tree node open/close state.

#### `Ui.treePop`

```zig
    pub fn treePop(self: Ui) void
```

Pair with `treeNode` on the way out. Decrements indent. Safe to call when the tree was closed (no-op). In the new layout model, this just decrements `indent_x` - the next widget's `advanceLayout` will reset the cursor X to `origin + indent` from the popped depth. No need to manually mutate `cursor_pos` here (the turn-308 hack of "also decrement cursor_pos[0]" is no longer needed, because the cursor is recomputed from origin+indent on every row).

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::TreePop` — ~ Unindent()+PopID()

#### `Ui.unindent`

```zig
    pub fn unindent(self: Ui) void
```

Pair with `indent()`. Decrements indent and pulls the current row's cursor back. Safe to call at zero indent (no-op).

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::Unindent` — move content position back to the left, by indent_w, or style.IndentSpacing if indent_w <= 0

#### `Ui.vSlider`

```zig
    pub fn vSlider(
        self: Ui,
        label: []const u8,
        size: Vec2,
        v: anytype,
        opts: SliderOpts,
    ) bool
```

Vertical slider - same semantics as `slider` but laid out top-to-bottom with `size.y` driving the bar's vertical span. Useful for mixers, level meters, etc. `value` is `*f32` or `*i32` (or any float/int via comptime dispatch). Returns true on the frame the value changes. ImGui: `VSliderFloat(label, size, v, v_min, v_max, format)` etc.

#### `Ui.value`

```zig
    pub fn value(
        self: Ui,
        label: []const u8,
        v: anytype,
    ) void
```

Render `"label: <formatted-v>"` as a one-line read-only display. `v` can be any of: - `bool` → "true" / "false" - signed/unsigned int → decimal - float → "{d:.3}" (3 decimal places) - enum → `@tagName(v)` - `[]const u8` → the string bytes, passthrough - anything else → `{any}` fallback (ugly but readable) ImGui ships four overloads (`Value(prefix, bool)`, `Value(prefix, int)`, `Value(prefix, unsigned)`, `Value(prefix, float, format)`) because C++ doesn't have comptime dispatch. Zig does, so one signature covers all four imgui overloads plus enums and strings - net more capability, fewer functions. For a custom format (e.g. `"{d:.5}"`), reach for `Ui.text("{s}: {d:.5}",.{label, v})` directly; that's the same character count and zero new API.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::Value`

#### `Ui.wantCaptureKeyboard`

```zig
    pub fn wantCaptureKeyboard(self: Ui) bool
```

Returns true if the UI currently wants to consume keyboard input - i.e., an `inputText` is focused. Game-input handlers should gate themselves on `!f.ui.wantCaptureKeyboard()` to avoid double-firing while the user types into a UI field.

#### `Ui.wantCaptureMouse`

```zig
    pub fn wantCaptureMouse(self: Ui) bool
```

Returns true if the UI currently wants to consume mouse input - i.e., the pointer is over a UI window or a UI widget is being interacted with. Game-input handlers (camera drag, world-click selection) should gate themselves on `!f.ui.wantCaptureMouse()` to avoid clicking through windows.

#### `Ui.window`

```zig
    pub fn window(
        self: Ui,
        title: []const u8,
        opts: WindowOpts,
    ) ?WindowHandle
```

Open a window scope. Returns a handle if the window is "alive" this frame (visible + uncollapsed). Caller pattern: if (f.ui.window("Stats",.{})) |w| { defer w.close(); f.ui.text("FPS: {d}",.{f.clock.fps()}); } Every window is uncollapsed and at a default position; the optional return type carries the API shape for collapse / dismiss / hide flags.

### `ui.UiContext`

#### `UiContext.beginFrame`

```zig
    pub fn beginFrame(
        self: *UiContext,
        f: *Frame,
        shapes_state: *const ShapesTextureState,
        font_cache: *const FontCache,
    ) Ui
```

Stamp the per-frame input snapshot and canvas dimensions; reset transient frame state. Returns the `Ui` handle the caller embeds in `Frame`. Line-spacing for UI text comes from `self.style.line_spacing` (Style is the user-facing knob). No runtime-level setting exists - this avoids the per-tick stamp that earlier turns' scaffolding required.

#### `UiContext.beginFrameRaw`

```zig
    pub fn beginFrameRaw(
        self: *UiContext,
        snapshot: InputSnapshot,
        input_state: ?*InputState,
        canvas_w: u32,
        canvas_h: u32,
        gl: *GlState,
        shapes_state: *const ShapesTextureState,
        font_cache: *const FontCache,
    ) Ui
```

Test-friendly form of `beginFrame` that takes pre-built snapshot + dims + GL primitives directly, skipping the `*Frame` plumbing. Inline tests use this so they don't have to construct a fake `Frame` (with all its loader / clock / rng / log vtables). Production code uses `beginFrame`; this is the underlying body.

#### `UiContext.deinit`

```zig
    pub fn deinit(self: *UiContext) void
```

#### `UiContext.endFrame`

```zig
    pub fn endFrame(
        self: *UiContext,
        f: *Frame,
        shapes_state: *const ShapesTextureState,
        font_cache: *const FontCache,
    ) void
```

Called after the user's frame callback returns. Replays all window draw lists in submission order, then the foreground draw list (popups + tooltips on top). This is where deferred rendering finally hits rlgl. Reads `self.frame_windows` / `self.frame_popups` / `self.foreground_dl` (recorded command lists for this frame), `self.pending_tooltip` (single-slot live tooltip), `shapes_state` (passed to each `DrawList.render`), `font_cache.font` (default-font fallback for text cmds). Mutates `f.gl.*` (every draw command flushes here) and `self.*` (snapshots active_id / clears per-frame state). `f.window` is read-only only push_clip needs canvas dims.

→ **imgui:** `ImGui::EndFrame` — ends the Dear ImGui frame. automatically called by Render(). If you don't need to render data (skipping rendering) you may call EndFrame() without Render()... but you'll have wasted CPU already! If you don't need to render, better to not create any windows and not call NewFrame() at all!

#### `UiContext.init`

```zig
    pub fn init(gpa: Allocator) UiContext
```

```zig
    pub fn name(self: *const Window) []const u8
```


## **codecs.zig** — `png`, `gltf`, `truetype`, `rectpack`, `code_point`

### `codecs.audio`

#### `audio.canonicalFromWave`

```zig
    pub fn canonicalFromWave(wave: Wave) CanonicalWave
```

Adapt a raylib-shaped `Wave` back to a `CanonicalWave` for re-encoding.  Borrows the Wave's `data` pointer (no allocation) - the returned CanonicalWave does NOT own its samples and its `deinit` MUST NOT be called.  The Wave continues to own the memory. This adapter is for read-only use cases like `wav.encode` the encoder doesn't free the source.  For long-lived ownership transfer, copy the bytes manually.

#### `audio.toCanonical`

```zig
    pub fn toCanonical(
        gpa: Allocator,
        reader: *Io.Reader,
        target_rate: u32,
    ) ![]f32
```

End-to-end pipeline: decode any supported format, convert to interleaved-stereo `f32`, resample to `target_rate`.  Returns the resampled f32 buffer; caller frees via `gpa.free`. This is the path used by `loadAudioBuffer` when loading a sound: decode → canonical f32 stereo → upload to AudioContext at the device sample rate. `target_rate` is typically `web.audio.getSampleRate(ctx_id)` - the AudioContext's native rate (so Web Audio doesn't need to do its own resample).  Passing 0 yields an empty result. Returns `error.OggRequiresAsyncDecode` if the bytes sniff as OGG - those need the Web Audio async decode path, not this sync pipeline.

#### `audio.waveFromCanonical`

```zig
    pub fn waveFromCanonical(
        gpa: Allocator,
        c: CanonicalWave,
    ) !Wave
```

Wrap a `CanonicalWave` in a raylib-shaped `Wave` extern struct. Allocates a new copy of the sample bytes - the returned Wave owns its `data` pointer and the caller is responsible for freeing it via `gpa.free(@as([*]u8, @ptrCast(wave.data))[0..byte_len])`. Convention: `Wave.sampleSize` reflects the canonical sample size (8/16/32).  raylib treats `sampleSize == 32` as float (the runtime mixer hands 32-bit data to JIT-style float ops); `8` and `16` are PCM int.  `canonicalFromWave` reverses this.

### `codecs.code_point`

#### `code_point.codepointAtIndex`

```zig
    pub fn codepointAtIndex(bytes: []const u8, index: uoffset) ?CodePoint
```

Return the codepoint at `index`, even if `index` is in the middle of that codepoint.

#### `code_point.decode`

```zig
    pub fn decode(bytes: []const u8, offset: uoffset) ?CodePoint
```

Removed.  Use `decodeAtIndex` or `decodeAtCursor`.

#### `code_point.decodeAtCursor`

```zig
    pub fn decodeAtCursor(bytes: []const u8, cursor: *uoffset) ?CodePoint
```

Decode the CodePoint, if any, at `bytes[cursor.*]`.  After, the cursor will point at the next potential codepoint index.

#### `code_point.decodeAtIndex`

```zig
    pub fn decodeAtIndex(bytes: []const u8, index: uoffset) ?CodePoint
```

Decode the CodePoint, if any, at `bytes[idx]`.

### `codecs.gltf`

#### `gltf.meshesFromGltf`

```zig
    pub fn meshesFromGltf(
        gpa: Allocator,
        data: Data,
    ) Error![]Mesh
```

Extract every primitive of every mesh in `data`, returning a flat `[]types.Mesh` slice.  Each glTF primitive becomes one mesh. Caller owns the slice + each mesh's contents (free via `gpa.free(slice)` after unloading each mesh).

#### `gltf.parse`

```zig
    pub fn parse(gpa: Allocator, bytes: []const u8) Error!Data
```

Parse a glTF document from raw bytes.  Auto-detects GLB vs JSON by examining the first 4 bytes.  Returns a heap-allocated `Data` which the caller MUST `deinit`.

#### `gltf.readAccessor`

```zig
    pub fn readAccessor(
        comptime T: type,
        gpa: Allocator,
        data: Data,
        accessor: Accessor,
    ) Error![]T
```

Read all values from `accessor` as a heap-allocated `[]T`. `T` must match the underlying byte width: read u16 indices as `u16`, vec3 positions as `f32` (and walk by 3 elements), etc. Length of the returned slice = `accessor.count * components`, where `components` is determined by `accessor.type_kind`. Caller owns the slice; free with `gpa.free(slice)`.

### `codecs.jpeg`

#### `jpeg.decode`

```zig
    pub fn decode(allocator: Allocator, bytes: []const u8) Error!Image
```

#### `jpeg.isJpeg`

```zig
    pub fn isJpeg(bytes: []const u8) bool
```

### `codecs.png`

#### `png.decode`

```zig
    pub fn decode(allocator: Allocator, png_bytes: []const u8) Error!Image
```

Decode PNG bytes into an RGBA8 image.  Caller owns `pixels` release with `Image.deinit(allocator)`.

#### `png.encode`

```zig
    pub fn encode(
        allocator: Allocator,
        pixels: []const u8,
        width: u32,
        height: u32,
    ) EncodeError![]u8
```

Encode an RGBA8 pixel buffer into PNG bytes.  `pixels.len` must equal `width * height * 4` exactly.  Returns a heap-owned slice - release with `allocator.free`. The fast path (no compression cost analysis) is fine for our use case: small assets, screenshot-style output, and image-editor "save current canvas" buttons.  Production-grade compression (PNGOUT-style filter selection + max chain length) is intentionally out of scope.

#### `png.loadAsync`

```zig
    pub fn loadAsync(allocator: Allocator, url: []const u8) LoadHandle
```

Begin loading `url` as a PNG.  Returns a handle to poll.  The allocator is used for the decoded Image when the fetch resolves. `LoadHandle` is small and Copy-able; pass by value.

#### `png.pollLoad`

```zig
    pub fn pollLoad(handle: LoadHandle) LoadStatus
```

Check progress on a `loadAsync` handle.  On `.ok`, ownership of the returned `Image` transfers to the caller (release with `Image.deinit(allocator)` AND `releaseLoad(handle)`).  On `.failed`, the underlying fetch is auto-released; the caller must NOT call `releaseLoad`.

#### `png.releaseLoad`

```zig
    pub fn releaseLoad(handle: LoadHandle) void
```

Release a still-pending or ok-but-not-yet-polled load handle.  Safe to call multiple times.  No-op if the fetch already resolved and `pollLoad` already returned `.ok` or `.failed`.

### `codecs.rectpack`

#### `rectpack.pack`

```zig
    pub fn pack(
        rects: []Rect,
        width: u32,
        padding: u32,
    ) Result
```

Pack `rects` into an atlas of `width` pixels wide.  Sorts `rects` by height descending in-place, then assigns each rect an (x, y) on a shelf.  Returns the resulting atlas dimensions. `padding` is added between rects on the same shelf and between shelves, so the caller doesn't need to inflate input widths / heights.

#### `rectpack.suggestAtlasWidth`

```zig
    pub fn suggestAtlasWidth(rects: []const Rect) u32
```

Estimate a reasonable atlas width for a set of rects, sized to be a square-ish power-of-two with ~25% slack.  Useful default when the caller doesn't have a strong opinion about atlas dimensions.

#### `truetype.load`

```zig

```

#### `truetype.loadFontFromTtf`

```zig
    pub fn loadFontFromTtf(
        gpa: Allocator,
        ttf_bytes: []const u8,
    ) !Font
```

zimr-canonical entry point for parsing a TTF/OTF blob. The caller retains ownership of `ttf_bytes` - the returned `Font` borrows them and the bytes must outlive any rasterization or metrics calls.  This matches the upstream contract; we re-export it here under a name that matches the rest of zimr's `loadX` family. The `gpa` parameter is reserved for future use - atlas-baking and font-table caching arcs may need allocations.  Today upstream's `load` is allocation-free, so `gpa` is unused; the signature is laid out now so the eventual atlas-bake API drops in cleanly.
