## **entities.zig** — archetype ECS: `Entities(T)`, `World`, `Entity`, `CmdBuf`, `Node`, `Tag`

#### `ChangeArchImmediateOptions`

```zig
    pub fn ChangeArchImmediateOptions(Add: type) type
```

Options struct for `changeArchImmediate`.  Built at comptime so that an `Add` struct whose fields all have defaults gives an Options whose `add` itself has a default - letting users write `.{ .remove = ... }` without filling in `add`.

#### `Entities`

```zig
pub fn Entities(comptime T_primary: type) type
```

A pool-anchored ECS world parameterized on a primary component type.  Every entity carries the primary through the fast pool deref path (2 dependent loads); optional secondaries attach via the inlined archetype ECS. `T_primary = struct {}` (empty struct) is fine -- gives an ECS-only world where the primary is zero-sized and `spawn(.{})` is the spawn verb.  See `gpu.Resources` for a typical use with non-trivial primary types.

#### `Handle`

```zig
pub fn Handle(comptime T_primary: type) type
```

Phantom-typed reference to one entity slot in `Entities(T)`. 8 bytes (4 of payload + zero-sized stamp in release builds; 8 bytes in debug for the stamp).  Methods that operate on the slot (`deref`, `destroy`, `isValid`, `get`, `attach`, `attachAll`, `detach`) take a `*Entities(T)` and access the storage directly.

#### `NodeWithOptions`

```zig
pub fn NodeWithOptions(node_options: NodeOptions) type
```

Build a parent/child tree node parameterized on the name field type.  Most code wants `Node` (the default specialization with `?[:0]const u8` names) - call this directly only when you have a specific reason to vary. A node component, attached to an entity, places that entity in a tree: each node carries handles for parent, first_child, prev_sib, next_sib (intrusive linked list of siblings).  The tree's anchor is a `Tree` value held in user state - multiple Trees per world are fine, one per scene-graph instance. Mutation has two flavors that mirror the core ECS: `*Immediate` ops apply now (load-time setup), the command-buffer ops queue for `Node.Exec.afterCmdBuf` (frame-rate-safe under iteration).

#### `Ref`

```zig
pub fn Ref(T: type) type
```

A typed reference into ECS-managed data.  Two flavors held in one struct: 1. **Path ref** - points to a `T`-shaped sub-field at byte `offset` inside component `loc` on entity `entity`. Resurrected on each `get` by re-fetching the component slice and adding the offset.  Survives chunk compaction; goes null if the entity / component is gone. 2. **Copy ref** - owns a `T` by value.  Useful when a system accepts "T or a reference to T" as input - UI sliders, animation curves, anything where the caller sometimes wants a literal and sometimes wants a live-tracking reference.  The discriminator is `loc == typeId(CopyTag)`; that's why the `data` union is untagged (we'd be paying for a tag bit we already have). See `GenericRef` if you want a system to be portable across different Ref implementations (e.g. one with extra invalidation tracking).

#### `SlotMap`

```zig
pub fn SlotMap(comptime Value: type, comptime options: SlotMapOptions) type
```

A persistent-key handle table. `init` reserves space; `put` returns a generation-tagged key; `get` validates the key's generation against the slot's; `remove` invalidates a key by bumping the generation (and retires the slot if it overflows).

#### `VoaUninitResult`

```zig
    pub fn VoaUninitResult(View: type) type
```

What `viewOrAddUninitImmediate*` returns: the materialised view, plus a bitset of which components in the View were added by THIS call (caller must initialise those before dropping the view).

#### `addPtr`

```zig
    pub fn addPtr(
        self: @This(),
        cb: *CmdBuf,
        T: type,
        comp: *const T,
    ) void
```

Like `add`, but always passes the component by pointer. The pointer must outlive the buffer flush (typical: a static const, or chunk-arena memory).  Prefer `add`.

#### `addPtrOrErr`

```zig
    pub fn addPtrOrErr(
        self: @This(),
        cb: *CmdBuf,
        T: type,
        comp: *const T,
    ) error
```

`addPtr` returning `error.EcsCmdBufOverflow`.

#### `addVal`

```zig
    pub fn addVal(
        self: @This(),
        cb: *CmdBuf,
        T: type,
        comp: T,
    ) *T
```

Like `add`, but always copies by value into the command buffer.  The returned pointer is valid until the next `clear()` (which happens during `Exec.immediate`).  Prefer `add` unless you need the returned pointer.

#### `addValOrErr`

```zig
    pub fn addValOrErr(
        self: @This(),
        cb: *CmdBuf,
        T: type,
        comp: T,
    ) error
```

`addVal` that returns `error.EcsCmdBufOverflow` instead of panicking.  See `CmdBuf` doc for what "undefined state on error" means.

#### `arch`

```zig
    pub fn arch(self: @This(), es: *const Registry) CompFlag.Set
```

The entity's archetype, or the empty arch if it's destroyed or uncommitted.

#### `assertMatch`

```zig
    pub fn assertMatch(
        comptime context_desc: []const u8,
        handle_stamp: Stamp,
        world: Stamp,
    ) void
```

In debug, verify that a handle's stamp matches the world's stamp. Panics with a useful message if they differ.  Skips the check when `handle_stamp == nil_stamp` - nil and externally-constructed handles deliberately bypass the check. `context_desc` is a short string identifying the handle type at the call site, e.g. `"Handle(Texture)"` or `"Ref(GpuMesh)"`.  Shows up in the panic message to point the user at the right line. In release builds this is a no-op and compiles to nothing.

#### `changeArchAnyImmediate`

```zig
    pub fn changeArchAnyImmediate(
        self: @This(),
        es: *Registry,
        gpa: Allocator,
        changes: ChangeArchAnyImmediateOptions,
    ) error
```

Runtime-typed cousin of `changeArchImmediate`.  Use when the component list is built dynamically (level-loader, scripting, etc.) rather than fixed at the call site.

#### `changeArchImmediate`

```zig
    pub fn changeArchImmediate(
        self: @This(),
        es: *Registry,
        gpa: Allocator,
        Add: type,
        changes: ChangeArchImmediateOptions(Add),
    ) bool
```

Add the listed components, then remove the listed flags. `Add` is a tuple or struct of components-to-add (fields may be optional, letting the caller decide at runtime whether each should actually be added).  Returns true on success, false if the entity doesn't exist. `gpa` is needed because adding a component may register a fresh type with the world's flag table.  Invalidates pointers.

#### `changeArchImmediateOrErr`

```zig
    pub fn changeArchImmediateOrErr(
        self: @This(),
        es: *Registry,
        gpa: Allocator,
        Add: type,
        changes: ChangeArchImmediateOptions(Add),
    ) error
```

`changeArchImmediate` returning errors instead of panicking.

#### `changeArchUninitImmediateOrErr`

```zig
    pub fn changeArchUninitImmediateOrErr(
        self: @This(),
        es: *Registry,
        options: ChangeArchUninitImmediateOptions,
    ) error
```

Like `changeArchOrErr`, but the added components are left uninitialised - even if they had values before.  Useful when the caller is going to overwrite the slots immediately (e.g. `viewOrAddImmediate`'s second pass) and the init-then-overwrite would just be wasted work. On failure, internal allocator state may have shifted chunk lists created mid-call don't get rolled back even if no chunks could be allocated for them.  In practice this is fine because immediate arch changes happen at level-load time when failures are fatal anyway. Could go slightly faster with comptime-known types (the donor mirrors that split with `changeArchImmediate` vs `changeArchAnyImmediate`) - `@memcpy` with comptime length is a bit faster.  In practice nobody bulk-changes-arch at frame rate, so the win wouldn't show up.

#### `checkAssertions`

```zig
    pub fn checkAssertions(
        self: *Chunk,
        es: *const Registry,
        mode: enum {
            /// Default: an empty chunk is a bug - empty chunks
            /// would have been returned to the 
            default,
            /// For freshly-allocated chunks and chunks about to
            /// be cleared, skip the "must be non-empty" 
            allow_empty,
        },
    ) void
```

Walk the chunk's invariants and assert each.  No-op when `runtime_safety` is off.  Sprinkled around mutation entry points to catch linked-list corruption early.

#### `checkGenericRef`

```zig
pub fn checkGenericRef(F: GenericRef) void
```

Comptime check: does `F` look like a valid `GenericRef`? Asserts the shape of `get` and `getConst` only.  Init functions (`path`, `copy`, etc.) vary between implementations and aren't part of the interface.

#### `clear`

```zig
    pub fn clear(self: *Chunk, es: *Registry) void
```

Empty the chunk's entity data and return it to the cpool's free list.  Unwires this chunk from both the archetype's main list AND its available-list, then nukes the header so reuse starts fresh.

#### `commit`

```zig
    pub fn commit(self: @This(), cb: *CmdBuf) void
```

Queue this entity to be committed.  Implicit during add / remove / cmd, so really only needed when you want an empty entity in chunk storage.

#### `commitOrErr`

```zig
    pub fn commitOrErr(self: @This(), cb: *CmdBuf) error{EcsCmdBufOverflow}!void
```

`commit` returning `error.EcsCmdBufOverflow`.

#### `committed`

```zig
    pub fn committed(self: @This(), es: *const Registry) bool
```

True if the entity exists AND has chunk storage attached. Reserved entities return false here.

#### `compsFromId`

```zig
    pub fn compsFromId(
        self: *Chunk,
        es: *const Registry,
        id: TypeId,
    ) ?[]u8
```

Like `view`, but for a single runtime-typed component rather than a comptime View struct.  Returns the raw `[]u8` covering all entities in the chunk for that comp; caller `@ptrCast`s back to the real type.  Null when the component isn't registered with this world or isn't in the chunk's archetype.

#### `destroy`

```zig
    pub fn destroy(self: @This(), cb: *CmdBuf) void
```

Queue this entity for destruction at the next `Exec.immediate`. Destroying an entity that no longer exists is a no-op.

#### `destroyImmediate`

```zig
    pub fn destroyImmediate(self: @This(), es: *Registry) bool
```

Like `destroy`, but immediate (no command buffer).  Returns true if the entity actually existed before this call destroying a non-existent entity is a no-op and returns false.  Invalidates pointers.

#### `destroyOrErr`

```zig
    pub fn destroyOrErr(self: @This(), cb: *CmdBuf) error{EcsCmdBufOverflow}!void
```

`destroy` that surfaces the buffer-overflow error instead of panicking.  See `CmdBuf` doc for "undefined state on error".

#### `encodeAddPtr`

```zig
    pub fn encodeAddPtr(
        cb: *CmdBuf,
        entity: Entity,
        T: type,
        comp: *const T,
    ) error
```

Encode adding a component to an entity by pointer.

#### `encodeAddVal`

```zig
    pub fn encodeAddVal(
        cb: *CmdBuf,
        entity: Entity,
        T: type,
        comp: T,
    ) error
```

Encode adding a component to an entity by value. Returns a pointer to the encoded value that's valid until the command buffer is cleared.

#### `encodeCommit`

```zig
    pub fn encodeCommit(
        cb: *CmdBuf,
        entity: Entity,
    ) error
```

Encode committing an entity.

#### `encodeDestroy`

```zig
    pub fn encodeDestroy(
        cb: *CmdBuf,
        entity: Entity,
    ) error
```

Encode destroying an entity.

#### `encodeExtPtr`

```zig
    pub fn encodeExtPtr(
        cb: *CmdBuf,
        T: type,
        payload: *const T,
    ) error
```

Encode an extension command by pointer.

#### `encodeExtVal`

```zig
    pub fn encodeExtVal(
        cb: *CmdBuf,
        T: type,
        payload: T,
    ) error
```

Encode an extension command by value. Returns a pointer to the encoded value that's valid until the command buffer is cleared.

#### `encodeRemove`

```zig
    pub fn encodeRemove(
        cb: *CmdBuf,
        entity: Entity,
        id: TypeId,
    ) error
```

Encode removing a component from an entity.

#### `eql`

```zig
    pub fn eql(self: @This(), other: @This()) bool
```

#### `exists`

```zig
    pub fn exists(self: @This(), es: *const Registry) bool
```

True if the entity hasn't been destroyed.  Doesn't check commitment - a reserved-but-uncommitted entity still "exists" in the sense that its handle is live.

#### `format`

```zig
    pub fn format(self: @This(), writer: *Io.Writer) Io.Writer.Error!void
```

Default formatting.

#### `get`

```zig
    pub fn get(
        self: @This(),
        es: *const Registry,
        T: type,
    ) ?*T
```

Get a pointer to component `T` on this entity, or `null` if it isn't attached / the entity is destroyed. We could route this through `compsFromId` for a smaller implementation, but benchmarks show a measurable speedup from doing the offset math inline - `get` is the inner- loop accessor and the indirection costs more than it saves.  See `Registry.getComp` for the variant that takes an arbitrary component pointer instead of a handle.

#### `getId`

```zig
    pub fn getId(
        self: @This(),
        es: *const Registry,
        id: TypeId,
    ) ?[]u8
```

`get` for runtime-typed component IDs.  Returns the raw bytes - `@ptrCast` to the right type at the call site.

#### `getOrAddImmediate`

```zig
    pub fn getOrAddImmediate(
        self: @This(),
        es: *Registry,
        gpa: Allocator,
        T: type,
        default: T,
    ) ?*T
```

Single-component shorthand for `viewOrAddImmediate`.  Get `*T` if it exists, else add it with `default` and return the new pointer.  Most common use case for "ensure this component exists" patterns.

#### `getOrAddImmediateOrErr`

```zig
    pub fn getOrAddImmediateOrErr(
        self: @This(),
        es: *Registry,
        gpa: Allocator,
        T: type,
        default: T,
    ) error
```

`getOrAddImmediate` that surfaces errors.  `gpa` is for flag-table growth on first use of `T`.

#### `has`

```zig
    pub fn has(
        self: @This(),
        es: *const Registry,
        T: type,
    ) bool
```

True if the entity has component `T`.  False on destroyed or unregistered-component-type.

#### `hasId`

```zig
    pub fn hasId(
        self: @This(),
        es: *const Registry,
        id: TypeId,
    ) bool
```

`has` for runtime-typed component IDs.

#### `iterator`

```zig
    pub fn iterator(self: *@This(), es: *const Registry) Iterator
```

Iterate every committed entity in this chunk.  Locks the world's pointer-generation counter - any mutation during iteration trips a runtime-safety assert in `next`.

#### `next`

```zig
    pub fn next() Stamp
```

Acquire the next process-unique stamp.  Monotonic ordering is sufficient - we only need uniqueness, not happens-before semantics. In release builds returns `{}` and compiles to nothing.

#### `remove`

```zig
    pub fn remove(
        self: @This(),
        cb: *CmdBuf,
        T: type,
    ) void
```

Queue a component for removal at the next flush.  No-op if the component isn't present or the entity is gone. Performance note: see `add` - alternating add/remove on different entities defeats batching.

#### `removeId`

```zig
    pub fn removeId(
        self: @This(),
        cb: *CmdBuf,
        id: TypeId,
    ) error
```

`remove` for runtime-typed component IDs that surfaces the buffer-overflow error instead of panicking.

#### `reserve`

```zig
    pub fn reserve(cb: *CmdBuf) Entity
```

Pops a reserved entity. Pops a reserved entity off the command buffer's reserve. Reserved entities have a persistent key but no chunk storage; they behave as empty entities and don't show up in iteration.  You can commit explicitly via `commit`, but add/remove also commits implicitly.

#### `reserveImmediate`

```zig
    pub fn reserveImmediate(es: *Registry) Entity
```

Like `reserve`, but reserves directly from the world instead of pulling from a command-buffer reserve.  Prefer `reserve`.  Doesn't invalidate pointers; not thread-safe.

#### `reserveImmediateOrErr`

```zig
    pub fn reserveImmediateOrErr(es: *Registry) error{EcsEntityOverflow}!Entity
```

`reserveImmediate` returning `error.EcsEntityOverflow`.

#### `reserveOrErr`

```zig
    pub fn reserveOrErr(cb: *CmdBuf) error{EcsReservedEntityUnderflow}!Entity
```

`reserve` that surfaces the underflow error.  Underflow happens when the buffer's reserve is empty - usually means you didn't pre-fill the buffer for the burst of work this frame.

#### `swapRemove`

```zig
    pub fn swapRemove(
        self: *@This(),
        es: *Registry,
        index_in_chunk: Entity.Location.IndexInChunk,
    ) void
```

Drop the entity at `index_in_chunk` from this chunk and fill the hole by moving the last entity into the slot. Standard SoA swap-remove: O(1), reorders entities, updates the moved entity's `Location.index_in_chunk` in the handle table. Internal - external code should use `Entity.destroy` / `Entity.destroyImmediate` instead, which know about archetype membership and pointer-lock invalidation.

#### `toOptional`

```zig
    pub fn toOptional(self: @This()) Optional
```

Wrap as `Optional`.

#### `view`

```zig
    pub fn view(
        self: @This(),
        es: *const Registry,
        View: type,
    ) ?View
```

Materialise a View for this entity, or `null` if it doesn't exist or is missing any required components.  See `viewLib` for what a "View" is - basically a struct of pointers / optional pointers / `Entity` fields whose names match component types in this world. We pre-check the entity's archetype against the view's requirement bitset before walking the View fields.  For large views this is measurably faster than letting each per-field flag lookup discover absence on its own; the pre-check has no measurable cost when it's not needed.

#### `view`

```zig
    pub fn view(
        self: *@This(),
        es: *const Registry,
        View: type,
    ) ?View
```

Materialize a `View` (struct of slices) over every entity in this chunk.  Returns `null` if any required component in `View` isn't part of this chunk's archetype. `View` shape: each field is `[]T`, `[]const T`, `?[]T`, or `[]const Entity.Index`.  Optional slice fields are filled with `null` for components the chunk doesn't carry.

#### `viewOrAddImmediate`

```zig
    pub fn viewOrAddImmediate(
        self: @This(),
        es: *Registry,
        gpa: Allocator,
        View: type,
        comps: anytype,
    ) ?View
```

Like `view`, but adds any missing non-optional components on the spot, taking their initial values from `comps` (a struct of pointer-typed fields whose names match the View).  Use when you want "give me a handle to position+velocity, adding them with these defaults if absent". `gpa` is needed because adding a component may register a fresh type with the world's flag table.  Invalidates pointers.

#### `viewOrAddImmediateOrErr`

```zig
    pub fn viewOrAddImmediateOrErr(
        self: @This(),
        es: *Registry,
        gpa: Allocator,
        View: type,
        comps: anytype,
    ) error
```

`viewOrAddImmediate` that surfaces errors instead of panicking.

#### `viewOrAddUninitImmediate`

```zig
    pub fn viewOrAddUninitImmediate(
        self: @This(),
        es: *Registry,
        gpa: Allocator,
        View: type,
    ) ?VoaUninitResult(View)
```

Like `viewOrAddImmediate` but doesn't initialise the added components - returns `(view, uninitialized_set)` so caller can fill them in directly without re-fetching.

#### `viewOrAddUninitImmediateOrErr`

```zig
    pub fn viewOrAddUninitImmediateOrErr(
        self: @This(),
        es: *Registry,
        gpa: Allocator,
        View: type,
    ) error
```

`viewOrAddUninitImmediate` that surfaces errors.  `gpa` covers flag-table growth on first use of any component type in `View`.

#### `Any.as`


Returns the component as the given type if it matches its ID, or null otherwise.

#### `Any.bytes`

```zig
    pub fn bytes(self: @This()) [*]const u8
```

Similar to `constSlice`, but returns the data as a many item pointer.

#### `Any.constSlice`

```zig
    pub fn constSlice(self: @This()) []const u8
```

Returns the component as a constant slice of `u8`s.

#### `Any.init`

```zig
    pub fn init(T: type, ptr: *const T) @This()
```

Initialize a component from a pointer to a component type.

### `entities.Arches`

#### `Arches.clear`

```zig
    pub fn clear(self: *@This()) void
```

Drop every archetype but keep the underlying allocation. Equivalent to "freshly initialized" for usage but skips the ensureTotalCapacity round-trip.

#### `Arches.deinit`

```zig
    pub fn deinit(self: *@This(), gpa: Allocator) void
```

Free the map.  After this `self.*` is undefined.

#### `Arches.getOrPut`

```zig
    pub fn getOrPut(
        self: *@This(),
        es: *const Registry,
        cpool: *const ChunkPool,
        arch: CompFlag.Set,
    ) error
```

Find or create the chunk list for `arch`. The `getOrPutAssumeCapacity` + `errdefer swapRemove` shape works around the standard library not having a "get-or-put that returns OOM" - we always have one slot of headroom (reserved in `init`), insert into it tentatively, and swap-remove on error.  Pointers stay valid because swapRemove only moves the slot we just inserted.

#### `Arches.indexOf`

```zig
    pub fn indexOf(lists: *const @This(), self: *const ChunkList) ChunkList.Index
```

Reverse-lookup a chunk list's index in this map.  Same shape as `ChunkPool.indexOf` - the index IS the position of the value in the underlying ArrayHashMap's value slice.

#### `Arches.init`

```zig
    pub fn init(gpa: Allocator, capacity: u32) Allocator.Error!@This()
```

Pre-allocate the archetype map and lock its pointers. We over-reserve by one slot so `getOrPut` can stage a tentative insert into the spare slot, then either keep it or `swapRemove` on the error path.

#### `Arches.iterator`

```zig
    pub fn iterator(
        self: @This(),
        es: *const Registry,
        options: IteratorOptions,
    ) Iterator
```

Walk every archetype matching `options`.  Locks the world's pointer generation; mutating ops during iteration trip the safety check in `Iterator.next`.

### `entities.ChunkList`

#### `ChunkList.append`

```zig
    pub fn append(
        self: *@This(),
        es: *Registry,
        e: Entity,
    ) error
```

Append `e` to this archetype.  Bumps `len` of whichever chunk currently has space; reserves a fresh chunk on the cpool overflow path.  The returned `Entity.Location` points at the newly-occupied slot.

#### `ChunkList.checkAssertions`

```zig
    pub fn checkAssertions(self: *const @This(), es: *const Registry) void
```

Internal-consistency check.  No-op outside runtime-safety builds.  Asserts the head/tail/avail invariants - head==null implies tail==null and avail==null; head!=null implies tail is reachable; the avail head's prev_avail is null.

#### `ChunkList.init`

```zig
    pub fn init(
        es: *const Registry,
        cpool: *const ChunkPool,
        arch: CompFlag.Set,
    ) error
```

Build a fresh chunk list for `arch`.  No chunks committed - that happens lazily in `reserve`.  This call just computes the per-archetype layout (component offsets, capacity per chunk) and stashes it on the list. Errors when an archetype's combined size won't fit in a single chunk (`EcsChunkOverflow`).  That's a sizing problem with `ChunkPool.cap.chunk` - make chunks bigger.

#### `ChunkList.iterator`

```zig
    pub fn iterator(self: *const @This(), es: *const Registry) Iterator
```

Iterator over the chunks in this list, in head→tail order. Mutating pointers during iteration trips the pointer-generation guard.

### `entities.ChunkPool`

#### `ChunkPool.clear`

```zig
    pub fn clear(self: *@This()) void
```

Reset to "freshly initialized": empty free list, zero reserved.  The arena bytes stay; existing chunks become reusable on the next `reserve`.

#### `ChunkPool.deinit`

```zig
    pub fn deinit(self: *@This(), gpa: Allocator) void
```

Free the arena.  After this `self.*` is undefined.

#### `ChunkPool.indexOf`

```zig
    pub fn indexOf(self: *const @This(), chunk: *const Chunk) Chunk.Index
```

Reverse-lookup a chunk's index in the arena.  The chunk must come from `self.buf`; out-of-cpool pointers trip the asserts.

#### `ChunkPool.init`

```zig
    pub fn init(gpa: Allocator, cap: Capacity) Allocator.Error!@This()
```

Allocate the chunk arena and return a fresh cpool.  Single upfront allocation; `deinit` is the only call that hits the allocator again.

#### `ChunkPool.reserve`

```zig
    pub fn reserve(
        self: *@This(),
        es: *const Registry,
        list: ChunkList.Index,
    ) error
```

Hand out a chunk.  Tries the free list first (LIFO), falls back to bumping into the arena.  Returns `EcsChunkPoolOverflow` when both are exhausted - that's the signal to grow `cap.chunks` in `Registry.init`.

### `entities.CmdBuf`

#### `CmdBuf.clear`

```zig
    pub fn clear(self: *@This(), es: *Registry) void
```

Reset the buffer for reuse and refill the reserved-entity cpool to capacity.  Called automatically by `Exec.immediate` after a successful run.

#### `CmdBuf.clearOrErr`

```zig
    pub fn clearOrErr(self: *@This(), es: *Registry) error{EcsEntityOverflow}!void
```

Like `clear` but surfaces the entity-cpool refill error instead of panicking.

#### `CmdBuf.deinit`

```zig
    pub fn deinit(
        self: *@This(),
        gpa: Allocator,
        es: *Registry,
    ) void
```

Free all four buffers and destroy the pre-reserved entities.  Caller must pass the same `gpa` and `es` they passed to `init`.

#### `CmdBuf.extPtr`

```zig
    pub fn extPtr(
        self: *@This(),
        T: type,
        payload: *const T,
    ) void
```

Force pass-by-reference encoding.  The caller's `payload` must outlive the buffer.  Prefer `ext` unless you want the no-copy guarantee.

#### `CmdBuf.extPtrOrErr`

```zig
    pub fn extPtrOrErr(
        self: *@This(),
        T: type,
        payload: *const T,
    ) error
```

`extPtr` with error return.  See `extValOrErr`.

#### `CmdBuf.extVal`

```zig
    pub fn extVal(
        self: *@This(),
        T: type,
        payload: T,
    ) *T
```

Force pass-by-value encoding.  Returns a pointer into the command buffer's `data` arena (valid until `clear`). Prefer `ext` unless you specifically want the in-buffer pointer back.

#### `CmdBuf.extValOrErr`

```zig
    pub fn extValOrErr(
        self: *@This(),
        T: type,
        payload: T,
    ) error
```

Like `extVal` but surfaces overflow as a return error. On error the buffer is in an undefined state - see the `CmdBuf` top-level doc.

#### `CmdBuf.init`

```zig
    pub fn init(options: InitOptions) error{ OutOfMemory, EcsEntityOverflow }!@This()
```

Allocate the four backing buffers and pre-reserve the entity-handle cpool.  Errors on allocator OOM and on entity cpool exhaustion (the world's `cap.entities` ceiling).

#### `CmdBuf.isEmpty`

```zig
    pub fn isEmpty(self: @This()) bool
```

True when no commands are queued AND the reserved-entity cpool is full (i.e. nothing has been encoded since the last `clear`).

#### `CmdBuf.iterator`

```zig
    pub fn iterator(self: *const @This()) Iterator
```

Iterate the encoded subcommands in insertion order. Each `next()` returns one `Batch` - either a contiguous run of arch-change ops on the same entity, or a single extension command.

#### `CmdBuf.updateStats`

```zig
    pub fn updateStats(self: *const CmdBuf) void
```

Emit a one-line warning if `worstCaseUsage` is past `warn_ratio`.  Called automatically at the end of `Exec.immediate`; user code can call it ad-hoc to check pressure mid-frame.

#### `CmdBuf.worstCaseUsage`

```zig
    pub fn worstCaseUsage(self: @This()) f32
```

The fullest of the four internal buffers, as a 0..1 fraction.  Buffers explicitly initialised with capacity 0 score 0.  `reserved` is inverted: `items.len` shrinks as the cpool is consumed, so its usage is `(capacity - items.len) / capacity`, not `items.len / capacity` like the others.

### `entities.PointerLock`

#### `PointerLock.check`

```zig
    pub fn check(self: @This(), generation: Generation) void
```

Asserts that pointers have not been invalidated since this lock was created.

### `entities.Registry`

#### `Registry.ForEachChunkOptions`

```zig
    pub fn ForEachChunkOptions(f: type) type
```

Options for `forEachChunk`.

#### `Registry.Iterator`

```zig
    pub fn Iterator(View: type) type
```

See `Registry.iterator`.

#### `Registry.chunkIterator`

```zig
    pub fn chunkIterator(
        self: *const @This(),
        options: ChunkIteratorOptions,
    ) ChunkIterator
```

Returns an iterator over all the chunks with at least the components in `require_comps` in an implementation defined order. Invalidating pointers while iterating results in safety checked illegal behavior.

#### `Registry.count`

```zig
    pub fn count(self: *const @This()) usize
```

Number of committed entities (excludes reserved-but-uncommitted).

#### `Registry.deinit`

```zig
    pub fn deinit(self: *@This(), gpa: Allocator) void
```

Free everything `init` allocated, leaving `self` in `undefined` state.  Caller must pass the same `gpa` they passed to `init`.

#### `Registry.destroyArchImmediate`

```zig
    pub fn destroyArchImmediate(self: *@This(), arch: CompFlag.Set) void
```

Destroys all entities matching the given arch immediately. Their handles become invalid (any later use is detected by generation mismatch). Invalidates pointers.

#### `Registry.forEach`

```zig
    pub fn forEach(
        self: *@This(),
        comptime updateEntity: anytype,
        ctx: params(@TypeOf(updateEntity))[0],
    ) void
```

Run `updateEntity` once per entity matching the unpacked-tuple view.  `updateEntity` takes `ctx` first, then a flat list of component pointers / optional component pointers / `Entity` args; the View shape is derived from those param types. Compared to `forEachView`: catches unused components at compile time (Zig's unused-param error) but the update fn can't be a method on a struct. Invalidating pointers inside `updateEntity` triggers safety-checked illegal behavior.

#### `Registry.forEachChunk`

```zig
    pub fn forEachChunk(
        self: *@This(),
        comptime updateChunk: anytype,
        options: ForEachChunkOptions(@TypeOf(updateChunk)),
    ) void
```

Run `updateChunk` once per chunk matching the requested view.  `updateChunk` takes `ctx` first, then a flat list of component **slices** (one per chunk), optional component slices, or `[]const Entity.Index`. Use this when batch-processing a whole chunk at once buys you something - vectorisable inner loops, single-shot memcpy, etc.  Otherwise prefer `forEach`.

#### `Registry.forEachView`

```zig
    pub fn forEachView(
        self: *@This(),
        comptime updateView: anytype,
        ctx: Tuple(params(@TypeOf(updateView))[1..]),
    ) void
```

Run `updateView` once per entity matching `View`.  `View` is derived from the first param of `updateView`; remaining params come from `ctx` (an anonymous tuple). Prefer this over `forEach` when your update logic wants helper methods on a struct (View can be any struct shape); downside is you don't get unused-param errors for fields you forgot to read. Invalidating pointers inside `updateView` triggers safety-checked illegal behavior.  The implementation only uses public ecs methods - fork into user code if your use case wants different iteration shape.

#### `Registry.forEachViewWithOptions`

```zig
    pub fn forEachViewWithOptions(
        self: *@This(),
        comptime updateView: anytype,
        ctx: Tuple(params(@TypeOf(updateView))[1..]),
        options: IteratorOptions,
    ) void
```

Like `forEachView` plus an `IteratorOptions` arg (currently just `skip` - the comp set to filter out).

#### `Registry.forEachWithOptions`

```zig
    pub fn forEachWithOptions(
        self: *@This(),
        comptime updateEntity: anytype,
        ctx: params(@TypeOf(updateEntity))[0],
        options: IteratorOptions,
    ) void
```

Like `forEach` plus an `IteratorOptions` arg.

#### `Registry.getAllCompTypes`

```zig
    pub fn getAllCompTypes(self: *const @This()) []const TypeId
```

Slice of every TypeId registered with this world, in registration order.  Suitable for introspection / dumps.

#### `Registry.getComp`

```zig
    pub fn getComp(
        es: *const Registry,
        from: anytype,
        Result: type,
    ) ?*Result
```

Given a pointer to one component of an entity, fetch another component on the same entity.  Returns `null` if `Result` isn't attached.  Like `Entity.get` but skips the handle-table indirection (you already have a pointer into the entity's chunk).

#### `Registry.getCompFlag`

```zig
    pub fn getCompFlag(self: *const @This(), id: TypeId) ?CompFlag
```

Look up the CompFlag for a component type ID in this world. `null` if the component hasn't been registered with this world yet - call `registerComponent` to register.

#### `Registry.getCompFromAny`

```zig
    pub fn getCompFromAny(
        self: *const Registry,
        from_comp: Any,
        get_comp_id: TypeId,
    ) ?[]u8
```

`getComp` for runtime-typed component pointers.  Returns the raw bytes (`[]u8` of `get_comp_id.size`) so the caller can `@ptrCast` to the right type - `getComp` does that cast automatically.

#### `Registry.getCompType`

```zig
    pub fn getCompType(self: *const @This(), flag: CompFlag) TypeId
```

Recover the TypeId that owns the given flag in this world. Asserts the flag is in-range - caller must have obtained it from this same world (flag layouts differ between worlds).

#### `Registry.getEntity`

```zig
    pub fn getEntity(es: *const Registry, from_comp: anytype) Entity
```

Reverse-lookup an Entity from a pointer to one of its non-zero-sized components.  Useful inside `forEachChunk` when you have `*Position` and want the entity it belongs to. Why non-zero-sized: zero-sized components have no per- instance storage so all "instances" share an address; reverse-lookup is ambiguous.  We could pack the entity handle into the pointer value (typical ptr is 64-bit, `Entity` fits) but that breaks `[]ZeroSized` slicing we chose to keep slicing.  This is a deliberate trade-off in the donor; if you later want zero-sized reverse lookup the path is "panic on zero-size, switch to packed representation, ensure live entities never serialize as fully-zero pointer bits".

#### `Registry.getEntityFromAny`

```zig
    pub fn getEntityFromAny(es: *const Registry, from_comp: Any) Entity
```

`getEntity` for runtime-typed component pointers.

#### `Registry.init`

```zig
    pub fn init(options: InitOptions) Allocator.Error!@This()
```

Allocate the preallocated pools and return a fresh world. All cap fields are committed upfront; the world will not ask the allocator for more memory after this call.

#### `Registry.iterator`

```zig
    pub fn iterator(self: *const @This(), View: type) Iterator(View)
```

Iterate every entity carrying at least the components in `View`.  `View` is a struct whose fields are component pointers (`*Position`, `*const Velocity`), optional component pointers (`?*Color`), or `Entity`.  Each `next()` returns one filled-in `View`. Iteration order is "chunk order" - implementation defined in general, but with one useful guarantee: entities added to a fresh archetype with no intervening deletions iterate in insertion order.  Useful for transient event queues that are spawned + drained in one go. Mutating `Registry` from inside an iterator (add / remove / destroy) triggers safety-checked illegal behavior - the pointer-lock generation check catches it.

#### `Registry.iteratorWithOptions`

```zig
    pub fn iteratorWithOptions(
        self: *const @This(),
        View: type,
        options: IteratorOptions,
    ) Iterator(View)
```

`iterator` plus a `skip` filter.  Registry carrying any component in `options.skip` are excluded.

#### `Registry.recycleArchImmediate`

```zig
    pub fn recycleArchImmediate(self: *@This(), arch: CompFlag.Set) void
```

Like `destroyArchImmediate`, but the handles get returned to the slot map intact instead of being marked invalid - older references to the same Entity will silently rebind to whatever the slot is reused for.  Faster than destroy because it skips the generation-bump per entity, but only safe when you can prove no stale references remain (event pools, transient queues). Invalidates pointers.

#### `Registry.recycleImmediate`

```zig
    pub fn recycleImmediate(self: *@This()) void
```

Recycles all entities. Invalidates pointers.

#### `Registry.registerComponent`

```zig
    pub fn registerComponent(
        self: *@This(),
        gpa: Allocator,
        id: TypeId,
    ) error
```

Register a component type with this world if it isn't already.  Idempotent - re-registering returns the existing flag.  Errors on flag exhaustion (more than `CompFlag.max` distinct components in this world) and on hashmap OOM. Reads/mutates `self.flag_table`, `self.reverse_table`, `self.reverse_len`.  Allocates from `gpa` for hashmap growth.

#### `Registry.reserved`

```zig
    pub fn reserved(self: *const @This()) usize
```

Number of entities reserved via `reserve` but not yet committed via `commit` / `add` etc.

#### `Registry.updateStats`

```zig
    pub fn updateStats(self: *@This()) void
```

Emit a warning if any preallocated cpool has crossed the `warn_ratio` threshold, or if entity slots have been saturated since the last call.  Each cpool warns at most once per world (`warned_*` flags); the saturation warning is rate-limited to one log per increment. Recommended call site: once per frame.

### `entities.Tag`

#### `Tag.eql`

```zig
    pub fn eql(lhs: @This(), rhs: @This()) bool
```

Tag equality = TypeId equality.  Don't try to fold this into a packed-struct bitwise compare - see https://github.com/ziglang/zig/issues/26044 for why packed-struct equality on this shape is unsafe.

#### `Tag.findAncestorOf`

```zig
    pub fn findAncestorOf(
        self: @This(),
        es: *const Registry,
        node: *Node,
    ) Entity.Optional
```

Walk `node`'s ancestors and return the nearest one that carries a matching Tag, or `.none` if none do. Workhorse for "find the enclosing X" scenegraph queries.

#### `Tag.init`

```zig
    pub fn init(T: type) @This()
```

Build a Tag from a marker type.  Pass an empty struct (`struct {}`) - only its identity matters.

#### `Tag.matches`

```zig
    pub fn matches(
        self: @This(),
        es: *const Registry,
        entity: Entity,
    ) bool
```

True if `entity` carries a Tag matching `self`.  False for entities with no Tag, or with a Tag of a different kind.

### `entities.TypeInfo`

#### `TypeInfo.checkType`

```zig
    pub fn checkType(T: type) void
```

Asserts at compile time that mr_ecs's runtime type information supports this type.

### `entities.meta`

#### `meta.offsetOf`

```zig
    pub fn offsetOf(T: type, comptime path: []const u8) comptime_int
```

Like `@offsetOf` but the path can dot-traverse nested structs.  `offsetOf(Line, "start.x")` walks Line→start (Vec2), then Vec2→x.  Escaped field names (`@"..."`) are not supported - assertion catches the `@` if you try.

### `entities.viewLib`

#### `viewLib.Slice`

```zig
    pub fn Slice(EntityView: type) type
```

Lift an entity-View type to its slice-View counterpart. `struct { p: *Position, v: *const Velocity, e: Entity }` → `struct { p: []Position, v: []const Velocity, e: []const Entity.Index }` (as a tuple-struct).  Used by `Chunk.view` to materialise the slice-shaped view a chunk wants.

#### `viewLib.Tuple`

```zig
    pub fn Tuple(types: []const type) type
```

Build a tuple type from a slice of types.  Like `std.meta.Tuple` but skips the `setEvalBranchQuota` call (we control the call sites and they're shallow).

#### `viewLib.Unwrap`

```zig
    pub fn Unwrap(T: type) type
```

`?T` → `T`, anything else → unchanged.

#### `viewLib.UnwrapField`

```zig
    pub fn UnwrapField(T: type, options: ViewOptions) type
```

Pull the underlying component type out of a view-field type.  `?*const Position` → `Position`; `[]const Velocity` → `Velocity`; `Entity` → `Entity`; `[]const Entity.Index` → `Entity.Index`.

#### `viewLib.index`

```zig
    pub fn index(
        View: type,
        es: *const Registry,
        slices: anytype,
        i: u32,
    ) View
```

Build one entity-View from an index into the per-chunk slice-View `slices`.  Used inside `Iterator(View).next` to materialise each step.

#### `viewLib.params`

```zig
    pub fn params(T: type) [@typeInfo(T).@"fn".len]type
```

Type list of `T`'s function-type parameters.  Used by `forEach` / `forEachView` to derive the View shape from the user's update function signature.

## **rlsw.zig** — software renderer (CPU rasterizer, `rlsw.h`-shape)

#### `Handle`

```zig
pub fn Handle(comptime T: type) type
```

#### `addVertexGradPC`

```zig
pub fn addVertexGradPC(out: *Vertex, gradients: *const Vertex) void
```

Position+color step; texcoord untouched.  Donor: `sw_add_vertex_grad_PC`.

#### `addVertexGradPCT`

```zig
pub fn addVertexGradPCT(out: *Vertex, gradients: *const Vertex) void
```

Advance `out` by exactly one step's worth of gradient.  Called once per pixel inside the span-fill loop.  Donor: `sw_add_vertex_grad_PCT`, line 1328.

#### `addVertexGradScaledPC`

```zig
pub fn addVertexGradScaledPC(
    out: *Vertex,
    gradients: *const Vertex,
    scale: f32,
) void
```

Position+color scaled step; texcoord untouched.  Donor: `sw_add_vertex_grad_scaled_PC`.

#### `addVertexGradScaledPCT`

```zig
pub fn addVertexGradScaledPCT(
    out: *Vertex,
    gradients: *const Vertex,
    scale: f32,
) void
```

Advance `out` by `scale` steps' worth of gradient.  Used at the start of each scanline to skip the substep distance to the first pixel center (so the interpolated values align with pixel centers, not span boundaries).  Donor: `sw_add_vertex_grad_scaled_PCT`, line 1347.

#### `getVertexGradPC`

```zig
pub fn getVertexGradPC(
    out: *Vertex,
    a: *const Vertex,
    b: *const Vertex,
    scale: f32,
) void
```

Position+color gradient; texcoord untouched.  Donor: `sw_get_vertex_grad_PC`.

#### `getVertexGradPCT`

```zig
pub fn getVertexGradPCT(
    out: *Vertex,
    a: *const Vertex,
    b: *const Vertex,
    scale: f32,
) void
```

Compute per-step gradient = `(b - a) * scale`, attribute by attribute. Used at span-fill setup: passing `scale = 1/(x_end - x_start)` yields the per-pixel delta to advance.  Donor: `sw_get_vertex_grad_PCT`, line 1309.

#### `lerpVertexPCT`

```zig
pub fn lerpVertexPCT(
    out: *Vertex,
    a: *const Vertex,
    b: *const Vertex,
    t: f32,
) void
```

Linearly interpolate between `a` and `b` at parameter `t ∈ [0, 1]`. `t = 0` returns `a`; `t = 1` returns `b`.  Used by the Sutherland- Hodgman clipper when an edge crosses a clip plane - the new vertex at the crossing point gets correctly-interpolated position, color, and texcoord. Donor: `sw_lerp_vertex_PCT`, line 1288.  Always interpolates all three attribute groups (no `_PC` lerp variant - the clipper runs before the rasterizer dispatches on texture state, so it has to produce texcoords whether or not they'll end up used).

#### `pixelFormatFromFormatAndType`

```zig
pub fn pixelFormatFromFormatAndType(
    format: Format,
    dt: DataType,
) ?PixelFormat
```

Translate a `(format, data_type)` pair from `texImage2D` into the internal `PixelFormat` that names a specific bit layout.  Returns `null` for combinations we don't support (the caller maps null to `err_code = .invalid_enum`).  Donor: `sw_pixel_get_format` (lines 1720-1796), but rewritten as a Zig switch tree for compile-time exhaustiveness - every `Format` × `DataType` combination is reached or explicitly mapped to `null`.

### `rlsw.Context`

#### `Context.begin`

```zig
    pub fn begin(self: *Context, mode: DrawMode) void
```

Open an immediate-mode recording.  Records `.invalid_operation` if already active (donor + GL spec) or if the framebuffer is incomplete.  Recomputes `mat_mvp` and runs `cleanRasterState` before accepting vertices.  Donor: `swBegin` + `sw_immediate_begin`.

#### `Context.bindTexture`

```zig
    pub fn bindTexture(self: *Context, handle: Handle(Texture)) void
```

Bind a texture handle as the current sampler source.  `.nil` clears the binding (subsequent draws sample untextured even if `.texture_2d` is enabled - `cleanRasterState` strips it). Invalid handles record `.invalid_value` and leave the binding unchanged.  Donor: `swBindTexture`.

#### `Context.blendFunc`

```zig
    pub fn blendFunc(
        self: *Context,
        src: BlendFactor,
        dst: BlendFactor,
    ) void
```

Set the source / destination blend factors.  The actual blend recipe lookup (`blend_func` table indexing + `blend_flags` classification) lands in alongside the future the rasterizer's blend stage.  For now this stores the factors - observable via `src_factor` / `dst_factor` - and is a no-op for `blend_flags` / `blend_func`.  Donor: `swBlendFunc`.

#### `Context.cleanRasterState`

```zig
    pub fn cleanRasterState(self: *Context) void
```

Compute `raster_state` from `user_state` based on which capabilities the current resources actually support.  Strips: - `.depth_test` if no complete depth attachment is present. - `.texture_2d` if no texture is bound, or the bound texture is incomplete, or has a depth (non-color) format. All other capabilities pass through unchanged.

#### `Context.clear`

```zig
    pub fn clear(self: *Context, mask: ClearMask) void
```

Wipe one or both framebuffer attachments.  `mask.color` fills the color attachment with `clear_color` (encoded into the attachment's pixel format via the dispatch table); `mask.depth` fills the depth attachment with `clear_depth`.  Both bits clear is allowed; neither bit is a no-op.  Calling inside a `begin`/`end` pair sets `err_code` to `invalid_operation` and does nothing.  Donor: `swClear`. Targets whichever Texture `color_buffer` / `depth_buffer` point at.  When those are null (the post-`init` state, before any FBO bind) we fall back to the Context's own default framebuffer attachments - the same Texture the rebind path in `deleteFramebuffers` would install.

#### `Context.clearColor`

```zig
    pub fn clearColor(self: *Context, c: Color) void
```

Set the color used by `clear({ .color = true })`.  Stores the value; doesn't actually wipe anything until `clear` runs. Donor: `swClearColor`.

#### `Context.clearDepth`

```zig
    pub fn clearDepth(self: *Context, depth: f32) void
```

Set the depth value used by `clear({ .depth = true })`.  Stores; doesn't wipe.  Donor: `swClearDepth`.  Caller is responsible for keeping `depth` in `[0, 1]`; the depth-format writers will truncate / scale according to bit width regardless, but values outside that range have no defined meaning.

#### `Context.color3f`

```zig
    pub fn color3f(
        self: *Context,
        r: f32,
        g: f32,
        b: f32,
    ) void
```

Set the current vertex color (3-component float; alpha = 1). Donor: `swColor3f`.

#### `Context.color4f`

```zig
    pub fn color4f(
        self: *Context,
        r: f32,
        g: f32,
        b: f32,
        a: f32,
    ) void
```

Set the current vertex color (4-component float).  Donor: `swColor4f`.

#### `Context.color4ub`

```zig
    pub fn color4ub(
        self: *Context,
        r: u8,
        g: u8,
        b: u8,
        a: u8,
    ) void
```

Set the current vertex color (4-component byte; normalised to `[0, 1]` floats internally).  Donor: `swColor4ub`.

#### `Context.colorBufferBytes`

```zig
    pub fn colorBufferBytes(self: *const Context) []const u8
```

Borrow the color attachment's pixel slice.  Used by hosts that need to upload the rlsw output to a GL texture for display (e.g. `z.textures.updateTexture(tex, ctx.colorBufferBytes().ptr)`). The slice is valid until the next `resize`; no ownership transfer. When `color_buffer` is null (post-`init` state, before any FBO bind) returns the default framebuffer's color attachment.

#### `Context.colorBufferBytesMut`

```zig
    pub fn colorBufferBytesMut(self: *Context) []u8
```

Mutable variant of `colorBufferBytes`: returns the same slice but as `[]u8`.  Callers that want to write pixels directly (e.g. the software-shader dispatcher in `src/rlsw_shader.zig`) use this instead of `colorBufferBytes` + `@constCast`, which would be UB.  Caller owns the responsibility for not stepping on the rasterizer's toes — typical use is OUTSIDE of `begin`/`end` blocks, with no draw calls in flight.  The software-shader dispatch path bypasses the rasterizer entirely (it writes pixels per-fragment from a user-supplied kernel), so this is the intended seam.

#### `Context.colorBufferDims`

```zig
    pub fn colorBufferDims(self: *const Context) Vec2i
```

Width / height of the active color attachment in texels. Together with `colorBufferBytesMut` this gives the dispatcher everything it needs to address pixels by (x, y) without peeking at private state.

#### `Context.colorBufferFormat`

```zig
    pub fn colorBufferFormat(self: *const Context) PixelFormat
```

The pixel format of the active color attachment.  Used by the software-shader dispatcher to pick the correct `rlsw_pixel.writeColor` codec.

#### `Context.cullFace`

```zig
    pub fn cullFace(self: *Context, face: Face) void
```

Choose which face direction `cull_face` discards.  Donor: `swCullFace`.  `front` and `back` are the only two values; the donor's enum-validity check is unnecessary in Zig (the `Face` enum can't hold an invalid value).

#### `Context.currentMatrix`

```zig
    pub fn currentMatrix(self: *Context) *Matrix
```

Returns a pointer to the active matrix-stack top (the matrix that `loadIdentity` / `translatef` / `multMatrixf` would modify next).  Cheaper alternative to caching the pointer in a `current_matrix` field - that field would be self-referential and break on `Context` move/copy.  See the field-cluster comment in the matrix-stack section above.

#### `Context.currentMatrixConst`

```zig
    pub fn currentMatrixConst(self: *const Context) *const Matrix
```

Const variant of `currentMatrix` for tests / readers.  Same body shape; const pointer out.  Method overload via separate name (Zig doesn't do C++ overloading on const-ness).

#### `Context.deinit`

```zig
    pub fn deinit(self: *Context, gpa: Allocator) void
```

Free the Context's heap allocations.  After this returns, `self` is in an invalid state - using it again is undefined behavior.  Donor: `swClose`.

#### `Context.deleteFramebuffers`

```zig
    pub fn deleteFramebuffers(
        self: *Context,
        handles: []const Handle(Framebuffer),
    ) void
```

Free `handles.len` framebuffer slots.  Mirror of `deleteTextures`: per-handle validation, `err_code` updates without short-circuiting, and active-binding cleanup. Deleting the currently-bound framebuffer rebinds the default framebuffer (`bound_framebuffer = .nil`), repointing `color_buffer` and `depth_buffer` at the Context's owned color/depth attachments.  Donor: `swDeleteFramebuffers`.

#### `Context.deleteTextures`

```zig
    pub fn deleteTextures(
        self: *Context,
        gpa: Allocator,
        handles: []const Handle(Texture),
    ) void
```

Free a slice of texture handles.  Per-texture pixel storage (allocated by `texImage2D`) is freed via `gpa`; the pool slot is then released.  Invalid handles record `.invalid_value` and the loop continues.  Calling inside `begin`/`end` records `.invalid_operation` and frees nothing.  If a deleted texture was bound (texture / color / depth attachment in the active framebuffer state), the alias is cleared.  Donor: `swDeleteTextures`.  Recently added the `gpa` parameter and the pixel-free step.

#### `Context.disable`

```zig
    pub fn disable(self: *Context, cap: Capability) void
```

Remove `cap` from `user_state`.  Donor: `swDisable`.

#### `Context.enable`

```zig
    pub fn enable(self: *Context, cap: Capability) void
```

Add `cap` to `user_state`.  The cleanup pass at `begin` time (`cleanRasterState`) is what decides whether the capability actually takes effect on the next draw - adding a capability here doesn't necessarily add it to `raster_state`.  Donor: `swEnable`.

#### `Context.end`

```zig
    pub fn end(self: *Context) void
```

Close the immediate-mode recording.  Records `.invalid_operation` if not active.  Donor: `swEnd` + `sw_immediate_end`.

#### `Context.frustum`

```zig
    pub fn frustum(
        self: *Context,
        left: f64,
        right: f64,
        bottom: f64,
        top: f64,
        near_plane: f64,
        far_plane: f64,
    ) void
```

Post-multiply the active matrix by a perspective frustum. Args are `f64` per the donor (intermediate precision matters for tight near/far ratios).  Donor: `swFrustum`.

#### `Context.genFramebuffers`

```zig
    pub fn genFramebuffers(self: *Context, out: []Handle(Framebuffer)) void
```

Allocate `out.len` framebuffer handles.  See `genTextures` for error semantics.  Donor: `swGenFramebuffers`.

#### `Context.genTextures`

```zig
    pub fn genTextures(self: *Context, out: []Handle(Texture)) void
```

Allocate `out.len` texture handles and write them into `out`. On pool exhaustion sets `err_code` to `out_of_memory` and returns early - handles after the failure point are left unwritten (caller's existing memory).  Calling inside a `begin`/`end` pair sets `err_code` to `invalid_operation` and writes nothing.  Donor: `swGenTextures`.

#### `Context.getFramebuffer`

```zig
    pub fn getFramebuffer(
        self: *const Context,
        handle: Handle(Framebuffer),
    ) ?*Framebuffer
```

Look up a framebuffer-pool slot.  See `getTexture` for semantics.

#### `Context.getTexture`

```zig
    pub fn getTexture(self: *const Context, handle: Handle(
        Texture,
    )) ?*Texture
```

Look up a texture-pool slot.  Returns null for invalid, freed, or stale handles, and for `Handle.nil`.  Used by the gen/delete and bind paths; exposed publicly so future future binding code (sampler setup, framebuffer attachments) can reach into the pool with the right type at every site.

#### `Context.init`

```zig
    pub fn init(
        gpa: Allocator,
        width: i32,
        height: i32,
    ) !Context
```

Initialize a Context backed by a `width × height` RGBA8 color buffer + D32 depth buffer.  Donor: `swInit`. The defaults match the donor: depth-test off, blend off, scissor off, cull-face off, clear color (0, 0, 0, 0), clear depth 1.0; modelview / projection / texture stacks at depth 1 (identity).  After this, the user typically calls `enable` for the capabilities they want and sets up the projection. Allocates four buffers: framebuffer color pixels, framebuffer depth pixels, and the two object pools' backing storage (each pool owns three sub-buffers).  All are owned by the returned Context and freed by `deinit` using the same `gpa`.  Partial allocation failures unwind cleanly via `errdefer`.

#### `Context.lineWidth`

```zig
    pub fn lineWidth(self: *Context, width: f32) void
```

Set the line-rendering thickness in pixels.  Donor: `swLineWidth`, which rounds to the nearest integer (lines are integer-pixel-thickness in the rasterizer); we match.

#### `Context.loadIdentity`

```zig
    pub fn loadIdentity(self: *Context) void
```

Replace the active matrix with the identity matrix.  Donor: `swLoadIdentity`.  Marks MVP dirty unless the active stack is texture (matching donor + the design of `mat_mvp`).

#### `Context.matrixMode`

```zig
    pub fn matrixMode(self: *Context, mode: MatrixMode) void
```

Switch which stack subsequent matrix ops target.  Donor: `swMatrixMode`.  No `invalid_enum` path (Zig's typed enum rejects bad values at compile time).

#### `Context.multMatrix`

```zig
    pub fn multMatrix(self: *Context, mat: *const Matrix) void
```

Pre-multiply the active matrix by an arbitrary `mat`.  Donor: `swMultMatrixf`.

#### `Context.normal3f`

```zig
    pub fn normal3f(
        self: *Context,
        x: f32,
        y: f32,
        z: f32,
    ) void
```

Renderer-polymorphic trait method: `normal3f`. rlsw is an unlit rasterizer - it has no lighting calculation and ignores per-vertex normals.  This method exists for API parity with `rlgl.GlState.normal3f` so generic `drawing.zig` paths compile against either backend.

#### `Context.ortho`

```zig
    pub fn ortho(
        self: *Context,
        left: f64,
        right: f64,
        bottom: f64,
        top: f64,
        near_plane: f64,
        far_plane: f64,
    ) void
```

Post-multiply the active matrix by an orthographic projection. Donor: `swOrtho`.

#### `Context.pointSize`

```zig
    pub fn pointSize(self: *Context, size: f32) void
```

Set the point sprite radius derived from a diameter `size`. Donor: `swPointSize`, which floors `size * 0.5` (point sprites are integer-pixel disks); we match.

#### `Context.polygonMode`

```zig
    pub fn polygonMode(self: *Context, mode: PolyMode) void
```

Set the polygon-rasterization mode (`point` / `line` / `fill`). Donor: `swPolygonMode`.  Same enum-validity note as `cullFace`.

#### `Context.popMatrix`

```zig
    pub fn popMatrix(self: *Context) void
```

Pop the top matrix.  The slot one below becomes "current" for future ops.  Sets `is_dirty_mvp = true` for projection / modelview pops because the cached MVP is now stale.  Stack underflow (counter already 1, the implicit identity slot) records `.stack_underflow` and writes nothing.  Donor: `swPopMatrix`.

#### `Context.pushMatrix`

```zig
    pub fn pushMatrix(self: *Context) void
```

Push the current matrix onto the stack.  Equivalent to duplicating the top - the next matrix op writes through the duplicate, leaving the previous slot intact for later `popMatrix` to revert.  Stack overflow records `.stack_overflow` and writes nothing.  Donor: `swPushMatrix`.

#### `Context.readPixels`

```zig
    pub fn readPixels(
        self: *const Context,
        x: i32,
        y: i32,
        w: i32,
        h: i32,
        dst: []u8,
    ) usize
```

Copy a sub-rectangle of the color attachment into a caller- owned buffer.  Mirrors `glReadPixels(x, y, w, h, GL_RGBA, GL_UNSIGNED_BYTE, ...)` - out-of-bounds requests are clipped to the framebuffer; pixels outside the available region stay zero. `dst` must hold at least `w * h * 4` bytes.  Returns the number of bytes actually written (may be less than the slice length if `(x + w, y + h)` extends past the framebuffer). The Y axis matches the framebuffer's natural top-down layout - no flip applied. Tradeoffs vs. `colorBufferBytes()`: `colorBufferBytes` returns a borrowed slice over the WHOLE buffer with zero copies - use it for full-frame uploads to a GL texture.  `readPixels` is for sub-rectangle reads where you want the bytes laid out contiguously in your own buffer, e.g. saving a screenshot region or computing a per-pixel diff.

#### `Context.resize`

```zig
    pub fn resize(
        self: *Context,
        gpa: Allocator,
        width: i32,
        height: i32,
    ) !void
```

Resize the default framebuffer.  Reallocates color + depth pixel storage, updates viewport (full canvas), updates scissor (full canvas).  Other state - bound textures, matrix stacks, blend setup - is preserved.  Donor: `swResize`.

#### `Context.rotate`

```zig
    pub fn rotate(
        self: *Context,
        angle_deg: f32,
        x: f32,
        y: f32,
        z: f32,
    ) void
```

Pre-multiply the active matrix by an axis-angle rotation. `angle` is in DEGREES (donor convention; matches GL).  Donor: `swRotatef`.

#### `Context.scale`

```zig
    pub fn scale(
        self: *Context,
        x: f32,
        y: f32,
        z: f32,
    ) void
```

Pre-multiply the active matrix by a scale.  Donor: `swScalef`.

#### `Context.scissor`

```zig
    pub fn scissor(
        self: *Context,
        x: i32,
        y: i32,
        w: i32,
        h: i32,
    ) void
```

Set the scissor rectangle in pixels.  `(x, y)` is the bottom-left; `w` / `h` are extents.  Recomputes both the pixel-space scissor (`sc_min`, `sc_max`) and its clip-space projection (`sc_clip_min`, `sc_clip_max`) used by the clipper to reject triangles before perspective divide.  Donor: `swScissor`.  The Y-flip in the clip-space mapping mirrors the donor (pixel space has +Y down; clip space has +Y up).

#### `Context.setTexture`

```zig
    pub fn setTexture(self: *Context, id: u32) void
```

Renderer-polymorphic trait method: `setTexture(id)` with a `u32` ID.  This is rlgl's native binding shape; on rlsw a `u32` doesn't unambiguously identify a `Texture` in the pool (handles are slot+generation), so we treat all non-zero IDs as "unbind the current texture" - subsequent sampling reads white and vertex_color stays unmodulated. This means UI rendering on rlsw (where rlgl glyph atlas IDs get passed through) sees no texture → glyph quads come out as solid colored rects, which is the desired placeholder behaviour.  Real rlsw texture binding stays via `bindTexture(handle)` for callers with native Handle access.

#### `Context.texCoord2f`

```zig
    pub fn texCoord2f(
        self: *Context,
        u: f32,
        v: f32,
    ) void
```

Set the current vertex texcoord, transformed by the active texture matrix (top of `stack_texture`).  Donor: `swTexCoord2f` + `sw_immediate_set_texcoord`.  The texture- matrix transform is applied immediately, not at vertex-push time - this matches the donor and lets the texture stack itself stay out of the per-vertex hot path.

#### `Context.texImage2D`

```zig
    pub fn texImage2D(
        self: *Context,
        gpa: Allocator,
        width: i32,
        height: i32,
        format: Format,
        data_type: DataType,
        data: ?[]const u8,
    ) !void
```

Allocate per-texture pixel storage and upload `data` into the currently-bound texture.  Format is derived from `(format, data_type)` via `pixelFormatFromFormatAndType`; if the combo isn't supported, records `.invalid_enum` and writes nothing.  If `data` is null, the storage is allocated and zeroed (matches donor: `swTexImage2D` with `data=NULL` produces an all-zero texture).  Calling without a bound texture is a silent no-op (donor matches).  Calling inside `begin`/`end` records `.invalid_operation` and writes nothing.  Donor: `swTexImage2D`. `gpa` must be the same allocator passed to `init` / `deleteTextures`; texture pixel storage is owned by the Context and released by `deleteTextures` (or `deinit` once the pool's per-slot free path is wired).

#### `Context.texParameter`

```zig
    pub fn texParameter(self: *Context, param: TextureParam) void
```

Set a single sampler parameter on the currently-bound texture. Donor: `swTexParameteri`.  No-op if no texture is bound (donor matches).  Invalid combinations don't compile (the `param` enum determines the value type, so `.min_filter` only takes a `Filter`, etc.).

#### `Context.translate`

```zig
    pub fn translate(
        self: *Context,
        x: f32,
        y: f32,
        z: f32,
    ) void
```

Pre-multiply the active matrix by a translation.  Donor: `swTranslatef`.

#### `Context.vertex2f`

```zig
    pub fn vertex2f(
        self: *Context,
        x: f32,
        y: f32,
    ) void
```

Submit a 2D vertex.  Z = 0, W = 1 (donor matches; the donor's `swVertex2f` builds `{ x, y, 0, 1 }` then routes through `sw_immediate_push_vertex`).  Records `.invalid_operation` if not inside `begin`/`end`.

#### `Context.vertex3f`

```zig
    pub fn vertex3f(
        self: *Context,
        x: f32,
        y: f32,
        z: f32,
    ) void
```

Submit a 3D vertex.  W = 1.  Donor: `swVertex3f`.

#### `Context.viewport`

```zig
    pub fn viewport(
        self: *Context,
        x: i32,
        y: i32,
        w: i32,
        h: i32,
    ) void
```

Set the viewport rectangle in pixels.  `(x, y)` is the bottom-left corner; `w` and `h` are extents.  Recomputes `vp_size`, `vp_half`, `vp_center` - the three fields the vertex transform reads.  Donor: `swViewport`.

### `rlsw.RasterCfg`

#### `RasterCfg.simdEligible`

```zig
    pub fn simdEligible(comptime self: RasterCfg) bool
```

Whether this cfg's per-pixel work has no branches that prevent 4-wide SIMD vectorization of the inner loop. Today: a kernel is SIMD-eligible when none of `depth_test`, `texture`, or `blend` are active.  That leaves only color interpolation + byte-pack + 4-byte write per pixel - all trivially vectorizable. Future expansions (each unlocks more SIMD-eligible cfgs): - DEPTH SIMD: vector load 4 stored z-values, vector compare, masked vector store.  Easy on wasm SIMD; just not done yet. - BLEND SIMD: vector load 4 destination colors via v128 load, vector mix, vector store.  Easy too. - TEX SIMD: needs per-lane gather (each lane samples a different texel address).  Wasm SIMD gather support is weak; this one is genuinely harder. `cull_back` doesn't affect the inner loop - it's a per- triangle decision that gates entry to the kernel.  So cull_back-true kernels are SIMD-eligible iff their other axes are.

## **renderer_trait.zig** — comptime `gl: anytype` trait + `GlAdapter`/`SwAdapter`

#### `assertIsGlContext`

```zig
pub fn assertIsGlContext(gl: anytype) void
```

Comptime trait check: error out at compile time if `gl` doesn't expose the renderer interface.  Place this at the top of any `fn drawX(gl: anytype, ...)` function - costs zero runtime, catches typos at the trait boundary instead of buried in use sites. Pattern: ```zig fn drawCube(gl: anytype, t: f32) void { assertIsGlContext(gl);  // comptime - no runtime cost gl.clear(.{ .color = true, .depth = true }); // ... } ```

## **gpu.zig** — ref-based GPU resources: `Resources`, `Mesh`, `Texture2D`, `Shader`, `Material`, loaders, `drawMesh`, `realizeMaterial`

#### `attachMetadata`

```zig
pub fn attachMetadata(
    gpa: Allocator,
    world: anytype,
    ref: anytype,
    metadata: anytype,
) !void
```

Attach one or more metadata components to an existing resource entity.  Decouples metadata from the spawn moment - callers can tag textures / meshes with `BundleTag`, `HotReloadable`, etc. AFTER load time. `ref` is any handle type (`TextureHandle`, `MeshHandle`, ...); `world` is the matching `Entities(T)`.  `metadata` is an anonymous struct whose fields are the components to add.  Multi-component attach is one archetype transition. Example: ```zig const tex = try gpu.loadFromMemory(gpa, &resources.textures, png_bytes); try gpu.attachMetadata(gpa, &resources.textures, tex, .{ .source = .{ .bytes = png_bytes, .encoding = .png }, .bundle = .{ .id = current_level_id }, }); ```

#### `beginShaderMode`

```zig
pub fn beginShaderMode(
    gl: *GlState,
    shaders: *const Entities(GpuShader),
    shader: ShaderHandle,
) void
```

Bind a custom shader as the current draw shader.  Pairs with `endShaderMode`.  All subsequent draws use this shader until end.

→ **raylib:** `BeginShaderMode` — Begin custom shader drawing

#### `beginTextureMode`

```zig
pub fn beginTextureMode(
    gl: *GlState,
    targets: *const Entities(GpuRenderTexture),
    target: RenderTextureHandle,
) void
```

Begin rendering to a render-texture (push FBO bind + viewport). Pairs with `endTextureMode`.

→ **raylib:** `BeginTextureMode` — Begin drawing to render texture

#### `bindMaterialTexture`

```zig
pub fn bindMaterialTexture(
    mat: *Material,
    slot: usize,
    ref: TextureHandle,
    world: *const Entities(GpuTexture),
) void
```

Write a texture entity's GL info into a material's map slot one slot at a time.  Useful when composing a wire-format material by hand instead of going through `realizeMaterial`. If `ref` is `TextureHandle.nil` or refers to a destroyed entity, the function is a no-op and `mat.maps[slot].texture` is left untouched - preserving whatever the slot held before.


Camera-facing textured quad at a 3D position.  Same uniform deref → convert → delegate pattern as the 2D texture family.

→ **raylib:** `DrawBillboard` — Draw a billboard texture

#### `drawMesh`

```zig
pub fn drawMesh(
    gl: *GlState,
    worlds: *const Resources,
    mesh: MeshHandle,
    material: Material,
    transform: Matrix,
) void
```

Draw a mesh with a material at a transform.  Both refs deref here. Nil refs (or stale ones from destroyed entities) skip the draw no error, just nothing rendered. Per call: 1 mesh deref + 1 material deref + 1 shader deref + 12 texture derefs (all material slots).  All O(1) ECS lookups. Stack-allocates a `[12]types.MaterialMap` scratch (~144 bytes).

→ **raylib:** `DrawMesh` — Draw a 3d mesh with material and transform

#### `drawMeshInstanced`

```zig
pub fn drawMeshInstanced(
    gl: *GlState,
    gpa: Allocator,
    meshes: *const Entities(GpuMesh),
    textures: *const Entities(GpuTexture),
    shaders: *const Entities(GpuShader),
    mesh: MeshHandle,
    material: WireMaterial,
    transforms: []const Matrix,
) !void
```

Draw a mesh N times with per-instance transforms.  GPU instancing path; allocates an instance VBO inside the call (hence the `gpa`).

→ **raylib:** `DrawMeshInstanced` — Draw multiple mesh instances with material and different transforms

#### `drawMeshWireFormat`

```zig
pub fn drawMeshWireFormat(
    gl: *GlState,
    meshes: *const Entities(GpuMesh),
    mesh: MeshHandle,
    material: WireMaterial,
    transform: Matrix,
) void
```

Wire-format draw path - takes a raylib-shape `Material` value directly.  Used by callers that hold materials as values rather than refs (today: `gltf_model_refs`, which works with the `Model.materials` slice from `loadModelFromGltfMemory`).

#### `drawMeshWires`

```zig
pub fn drawMeshWires(
    gl: *GlState,

    tint: Color,

```

Draw a mesh as wireframe - every edge as a line.  Walks CPU vertex data; meshes whose `vertices` is null are silently skipped.
