#### `loadFontDefault`

```zig
pub fn loadFontDefault(gpa: Allocator, world: *Entities(
    GpuFont,
)) !FontHandle
```

Load the bundled default font as an entity.  Distinct from the global `font_cache` default-font path: that lives on `Frame` and is implicitly used by `z.text.draw`.  This is for callers who want the default font as a regular `FontHandle` ref - e.g. to swap between fonts dynamically without going through the cache. **STUB.**  The default-font atlas is currently owned by `FontCache`; re-loading it as a separate entity would duplicate GPU state.  Returning an error until a shared design lands.

#### `loadFontFromMemory`

```zig
pub fn loadFontFromMemory(
    gpa: Allocator,
    world: *Entities(GpuFont),
    ttf_bytes: []const u8,
    font_size: i32,
    codepoints: []const u21,
    padding: i32,
) !FontHandle
```

Load a TTF font from in-memory bytes.  Bakes a glyph atlas at the given pixel size for the requested codepoints, uploads it to GPU, and spawns an entity carrying `GpuFont`. The codepoints slice declares which characters to bake into the atlas - typically ASCII + extended Latin (32..255) for a Latin alphabet, or a custom set for non-Latin scripts.  `padding` is the per-glyph margin in pixels (small positive number, e.g. 4, prevents bleed when the atlas is sampled with bilinear filtering). On error, no entity is created; the GPU and CPU buffers allocated mid-load are rolled back.

→ **raylib:** `LoadFontFromMemory` — Load font from memory buffer, fileType refers to extension: i.e. '.ttf'

#### `loadFromImage`

```zig
pub fn loadFromImage(
    gpa: Allocator,
    world: *Entities(GpuTexture),
    image: Image,
) !TextureHandle
```

Upload a CPU-side `types.Image` to GPU and spawn an entity.  Caller owns the types.Image data and is responsible for freeing it after this returns (we don't take ownership - the bytes get copied to GPU). Symmetric with `loadFromMemory` but skips the decode step; useful for procedural images (`genImageColor`, `genImagePerlin`) and for uploading rasterized text (`imageDrawText`).

#### `loadFromMemory`

```zig
pub fn loadFromMemory(
    gpa: Allocator,
    world: *Entities(GpuTexture),
    bytes: []const u8,
) !TextureHandle
```

Decode + GPU-upload a texture from in-memory bytes (PNG/JPEG via the codecs.png module).  Spawns an entity in `world` carrying just `GpuTexture` - no metadata. No `gl` parameter - the wasm GL upload path goes through the JS bridge and doesn't need `*GlState`. On error, no entity is created and the GPU upload is rolled back.

#### `loadFromMemoryRetained`

```zig
pub fn loadFromMemoryRetained(
    gpa: Allocator,
    world: *Entities(GpuTexture),
    bytes: []const u8,
    encoding: SourceBytesEncoding,
) !TextureHandle
```

Like `loadFromMemory`, plus retains the source bytes as a `SourceBytes` component so the texture can be re-uploaded after WebGL2 context loss without re-fetching.  Caller's `bytes` slice must outlive the texture entity - typically `@embedFile`'d data or long-lived asset bundles. The entity ends up in the {GpuTexture, SourceBytes} archetype. `TextureHandle.deref(world)` resolves it either way.

#### `loadMeshesFromGltfMemory`

```zig
pub fn loadMeshesFromGltfMemory(
    gpa: Allocator,
    world: *Entities(GpuMesh),
    glb_bytes: []const u8,
) !ModelMeshes
```

Parse a glTF/GLB file from memory and spawn one mesh entity per primitive.  Returns a `ModelMeshes` holding refs to all the spawned entities. Materials are NOT loaded by this call - only mesh geometry. Callers that need the glTF's materials (PBR maps, base color factors, normal maps) should go through the `f.loadModelFromMemory(...)` path which builds `Material` arrays. On any partial-load failure (parse, GPU upload, ECS spawn), every successfully-spawned mesh entity is destroyed before returning the error.  The world's state is unchanged.

#### `loadModelFromGltfMemory`

```zig
pub fn loadModelFromGltfMemory(
    gpa: Allocator,
    gl: *GlState,
    meshes_world: *Entities(GpuMesh),
    textures_world: *Entities(GpuTexture),
    bytes: []const u8,
) !Model
```

Full glTF load: meshes AND textures spawn as ECS entities, with materials kept in raylib-shape so the renderer's existing draw path works unchanged.  This is the recommended verb for any glTF asset that wants to participate in metadata-based queries (asset bundles, hot-reload, source-bytes retention). Compared to `loadMeshesFromGltfMemory`: also uploads textures and builds materials.  Compared to the legacy `f.loadModelFromMemory`: textures become refs (visible to `unloadAllTextures`, taggable via `attachMetadata`). Each glTF texture is uploaded exactly once, even if multiple materials reference it - fixes a pre-existing inefficiency in the legacy path (which uploads per-material-map). On any partial-load failure, every successfully-spawned entity is destroyed before returning the error.  The worlds' state is unchanged.

#### `loadRenderTexture`

```zig
pub fn loadRenderTexture(
    gpa: Allocator,
    world: *Entities(GpuRenderTexture),
    width: i32,
    height: i32,
) !RenderTextureHandle
```

Allocate a render-texture (FBO + color + depth attachments) and spawn an entity carrying `GpuRenderTexture`.  types.Color attachment is RGBA8; depth is a 24-bit renderbuffer.

→ **raylib:** `LoadRenderTexture` — Load texture for rendering (framebuffer)

#### `loadShaderFromMemory`

```zig
pub fn loadShaderFromMemory(
    gpa: Allocator,
    world: *Entities(GpuShader),
    gl: *GlState,
    vs_source: []const u8,
    fs_source: []const u8,
) !ShaderHandle
```

Compile + link a vertex/fragment shader pair from in-memory GLSL. Spawns an entity carrying `GpuShader`.  On error (compile/link failure), the GL program is rolled back.

→ **raylib:** `LoadShaderFromMemory` — Load shader from code strings and bind default locations

#### `meshToRaylib`

```zig
pub fn meshToRaylib(m: GpuMesh) WireMesh
```

Inverse of `meshFromRaylib`.  Used by the cleanup walker to hand a `types.Mesh` back to `drawing.models.unloadMesh`, and exposed publicly for renderer/scene code that needs the wire format.

#### `realizeMaterial`

```zig
pub fn realizeMaterial(
    gl: *const GlState,
    gpu_mat: *const GpuMaterial,
    worlds: *const Resources,
    maps_scratch: *[MAX_MATERIAL_MAPS]WireMaterialMap,
) WireMaterial
```

Realize a `GpuMaterial` into the `Material` wire format that `drawing.models.drawMesh` (the rlgl-side draw path) expects. Every per-draw call goes through this step: deref the shader ref, deref each per-slot texture ref, fill a stack-allocated maps array, return a `Material` whose `.maps` points at that stack array. Lifetime: the returned `Material` borrows from `maps_scratch`. `maps_scratch` must outlive any use of the returned value - easiest pattern is to stack-allocate it in the caller and pass `&scratch`. Nil texture refs (slot left unset, or pointing at a destroyed entity) substitute rlgl's default 1×1 white texture so the shader always samples a valid surface - sampling white means the slot's color tint passes through unmodified.  Nil shader refs substitute id=0/locs=null, which `drawing.models.drawMesh` treats as "no shader bound" and skips the draw entirely. Performance: per call, 1 shader deref + 12 texture derefs (all slots - branching to skip nils would likely cost more than the wasted derefs).  Each deref is an O(1) ECS lookup.

#### `spawnCustomMaterial`

```zig
pub fn spawnCustomMaterial(
    gpa: Allocator,
    world: *Entities(
        GpuMaterial,
    ),
    args: struct {
        shader: ShaderHandle,
        params: [4]f32 = @splat(0),
        transparent: bool = false,
        double_sided: bool = false,
    },
) !Material
```

Spawn a custom-shader material - the escape hatch.  Caller picks the shader; `params` is shader-defined.  Maps left at defaults (no textures bound).

#### `spawnLitMaterial`

```zig
pub fn spawnLitMaterial(
    gpa: Allocator,
    world: *Entities(
        GpuMaterial,
    ),
    args: struct {
        shader: ShaderHandle = ShaderHandle.nil,
        albedo: Color = .{ .r = 255, .g = 255, .b = 255, .a = 255 },
        albedo_map: TextureHandle = TextureHandle.nil,
        metallic: f32 = 0.0,
        metallic_map: TextureHandle = TextureHandle.nil,
        roughness: f32 = 1.0,
        roughness_map: TextureHandle = TextureHandle.nil,
        normal_map: TextureHandle = TextureHandle.nil,
        emissive: Color = .{ .r = 0, .g = 0, .b = 0, .a = 255 },
        emissive_map: TextureHandle = TextureHandle.nil,
        ao_map: TextureHandle = TextureHandle.nil,
        transparent: bool = false,
        double_sided: bool = false,
    },
) !Material
```

Spawn a PBR-lit material: albedo color + map, metalness scalar + map, roughness scalar + map, normal map, emissive color + map, ambient-occlusion map. Passing `.{}` gives a plain white opaque lit material with no textures.

#### `spawnSpriteMaterial`

```zig
pub fn spawnSpriteMaterial(
    gpa: Allocator,
    world: *Entities(
        GpuMaterial,
    ),
    args: struct {
        shader: ShaderHandle = ShaderHandle.nil,
        tint: Color = .{ .r = 255, .g = 255, .b = 255, .a = 255 },
        color_map: TextureHandle = TextureHandle.nil,
        transparent: bool = true,
    },
) !Material
```

Spawn a sprite material.  Same shape as unlit but transparent by default (sprites typically have alpha).

#### `spawnUnlitMaterial`

```zig
pub fn spawnUnlitMaterial(
    gpa: Allocator,
    world: *Entities(
        GpuMaterial,
    ),
    args: struct {
        shader: ShaderHandle = ShaderHandle.nil,
        tint: Color = .{ .r = 255, .g = 255, .b = 255, .a = 255 },
        color_map: TextureHandle = TextureHandle.nil,
        transparent: bool = false,
    },
) !Material
```

Spawn an unlit material.  Solid color (or texture) with no lighting math.

#### `spawnWireframeMaterial`

```zig
pub fn spawnWireframeMaterial(
    gpa: Allocator,
    world: *Entities(
        GpuMaterial,
    ),
    args: struct {
        shader: ShaderHandle = ShaderHandle.nil,
        color: Color = .{ .r = 255, .g = 255, .b = 255, .a = 255 },
        line_thickness: f32 = 1.0,
    },
) !Material
```

Spawn a wireframe material.  Outline-only, no texture. `line_thickness` lives in `params[0]` (no dedicated field on `GpuMaterial`); the renderer reads it from there.

#### `unloadAllFonts`

```zig
pub fn unloadAllFonts(gpa: Allocator, world: *Entities(
    GpuFont,
)) void
```

Free every font's atlas + glyph metadata, then deinit `world`.

#### `unloadAllMaterials`

```zig
pub fn unloadAllMaterials(gpa: Allocator, world: *Entities(
    GpuMaterial,
)) void
```

Release every material entity in the world.  Materials don't own GL resources directly - their texture/shader refs do - so this is pure ECS teardown.  No `gl` parameter, no per-entity walk. Symmetric in shape with `unloadAllTextures` et al. but minus the rlgl side-trip.

#### `unloadAllMeshes`

```zig
pub fn unloadAllMeshes(
    gl: *GlState,
    gpa: Allocator,
    world: *Entities(
        GpuMesh,
    ),
) void
```

Free every mesh entity's GL VAO/VBO ids + CPU vertex buffers, then deinit `world`.

#### `unloadAllRenderTextures`

```zig
pub fn unloadAllRenderTextures(gpa: Allocator, world: *Entities(
    GpuRenderTexture,
)) void
```

Free every render-texture's FBO + attachments, then deinit `world`.

#### `unloadAllShaders`

```zig
pub fn unloadAllShaders(
    gl: *GlState,
    gpa: Allocator,
    world: *Entities(
        GpuShader,
    ),
) void
```

Free every shader entity's program id + locs allocation, then deinit `world`.

#### `unloadAllTextures`

```zig
pub fn unloadAllTextures(gpa: Allocator, world: *Entities(
    GpuTexture,
)) void
```

Free every texture entity's GL id, then deinit `world`'s ECS structures.  After this call, `world` is consumed (no further use is valid). Doesn't take `gl` - `rlUnloadTexture` on the wasm backend uses the global JS bridge, no `*GlState` required.  Symmetric with `loadFromMemory` not taking `gl`.

#### `unloadBundle`

```zig
pub fn unloadBundle(
    gl: *GlState,
    gpa: Allocator,
    world: anytype,
    bundle_id: u32,
) error
```

Destroy every entity in `world` whose `BundleTag.id` matches `bundle_id`, with appropriate GL cleanup per entity kind. Pairs with `attachMetadata(gpa, world, ref, .{ .bundle = BundleTag{...} })` - load a level, tag its resources with a bundle id, drop the whole bundle in one call when leaving the level. Works on any world: texture, mesh, shader, or mixed.  For each matched entity, the canonical GPU component (GpuTexture, GpuMesh, GpuShader, GpuRenderTexture) determines the GL cleanup action before the entity record is destroyed.  Entities lacking any canonical component get destroyed without GL cleanup suitable for pure-data bundles too. Implementation: collect-then-destroy.  Mid-iteration destroy would trip ECS's pointer-generation check (the world's archetype layout shifts when entities die).  We collect Entity values during forEach, release the iterator, then destroy in a second pass.

#### `unloadFont`

```zig
pub fn unloadFont(
    gpa: Allocator,
    world: *Entities(
        GpuFont,
    ),
    font: FontHandle,
) void
```

Destroy one font entity - releases atlas GL id + frees glyph metadata buffers, then removes the entity.

→ **raylib:** `UnloadFont` — Unload font from GPU memory (VRAM)

#### `unloadMesh`

```zig
pub fn unloadMesh(
    gpa: Allocator,
    world: *Entities(
        GpuMesh,
    ),
    mesh: MeshHandle,
) void
```

Destroy one mesh entity - frees GL VAO/VBO + CPU vertex buffers, then removes the entity.

→ **raylib:** `UnloadMesh` — Unload mesh data from CPU and GPU

#### `unloadRenderTexture`

```zig
pub fn unloadRenderTexture(world: *Entities(GpuRenderTexture), target: RenderTextureHandle) void
```

Destroy one render-texture entity - frees FBO + attachments.

→ **raylib:** `UnloadRenderTexture` — Unload render texture from GPU memory (VRAM)

#### `unloadShader`

```zig
pub fn unloadShader(
    gl: *GlState,
    gpa: Allocator,
    world: *Entities(
        GpuShader,
    ),
    shader: ShaderHandle,
) void
```

Destroy one shader entity - frees program id + locs allocation.

→ **raylib:** `UnloadShader` — Unload shader from GPU memory (VRAM)

#### `unloadTexture`

```zig
pub fn unloadTexture(world: *Entities(GpuTexture), tex: TextureHandle) void
```

Destroy one texture entity - frees its GL id AND removes the entity from `world`.  Use for mid-loop replacement (e.g. the readback example re-uploads once a second; each new texture supersedes the old one, which gets destroyed via this call). No-op for a stale ref (deref → null).

→ **raylib:** `UnloadTexture` — Unload texture from GPU memory (VRAM)

#### `updateTexture`

```zig
pub fn updateTexture(
    world: *const Entities(GpuTexture),
    tex: TextureHandle,
    pixels: ?*const anyopaque,
) void
```

Re-upload pixel data to an existing texture.  Used for animated textures (procedural noise that varies per frame, video frames, etc.) - cheaper than allocating a new GPU texture each frame. Caller must ensure the pixel buffer's dimensions and format match the original upload; mismatch = silent garbage on most drivers.

→ **raylib:** `UpdateTexture` — Update GPU texture with new data (pixels should be able to fill texture)

### `gpu.Model`

#### `Model.deinit`

```zig
    pub fn deinit(
        self: *Model,
        gpa: Allocator,
        meshes_world: *Entities(GpuMesh),
        textures_world: *Entities(GpuTexture),
    ) void
```

Release every entity + every legacy allocation owned by the model.  After this, refs are invalid and the slices are freed.  Don't reuse the value. Cleanup order: textures last (so material maps remain valid during any intermediate read), but in practice the order doesn't matter - nothing reads the materials during teardown.

### `gpu.ModelMeshes`

#### `ModelMeshes.deinit`

```zig
    pub fn deinit(
        self: *ModelMeshes,
        gpa: Allocator,
        world: *Entities(
            GpuMesh,
        ),
    ) void
```

Bulk teardown: destroy every mesh entity, free the slice. Call once when the model is no longer needed.  After this, every ref in `refs` is invalid (deref → null) and the slice itself has been freed - don't reuse the value.

### `gpu.Resources`

#### `Resources.deinit`

```zig
    pub fn deinit(
        self: *Resources,
        gl: *GlState,
        gpa: Allocator,
    ) void
```

Walk every sub-world, release the GL ids, deinit the Entities.  After this call the worlds are consumed. `gl` is required because shaders release through the stateful rlgl path.  Textures release through the wasm bridge (`rlUnloadTexture` is stateless).  Materials don't touch GL.

#### `Resources.init`

```zig
    pub fn init(gpa: Allocator) !Resources
```

Initialize all six sub-worlds with default capacity.  On failure, any successfully-initialized worlds are released before the error returns.

## **scene.zig** — components (`Transform`, `MeshDraw`, lights, `Camera`), `compile()`, `RenderList`

#### `compile`

```zig
pub fn compile(
    arena: Allocator,
    es: *Entities,
    opts: CompileOptions,
) Error!RenderList
```

Walk the ECS world once, produce a flat `RenderList` describing what to draw this frame.  Call from your update function: var list = try z.scene.compile(f.scratch, &es, .{ .cam_entity = my_camera_entity, .viewport   = .{ .width = 800, .height = 450 }, }); try renderer.render(f.gl, &resources, &list, .{ .surface = .screen }); All allocations come from `arena`; the returned list is valid until that arena resets.  If `arena` is `f.scratch`, that's end-of-frame - perfect.

### `scene.Frustum`

#### `Frustum.fromViewProj`

```zig
    pub fn fromViewProj(vp: Matrix) Frustum
```

Extract frustum planes from a view*projection matrix.  The derivation is the standard Gribb-Hartmann method: each plane is a row-combination of the view-proj matrix.  Result planes are normalized so `.n.length == 1`, which lets us compare signed distance to a sphere radius directly.

#### `Frustum.intersectsSphere`

```zig
    pub fn intersectsSphere(self: Frustum, sphere: Sphere) bool
```

True if the world-space sphere lies at least partially inside the frustum.  Conservative (false-positive on near misses) but never rejects something visible - the standard outcome for a per-plane sphere test.

### `scene.SceneTransform`

#### `SceneTransform.at`

```zig
    pub fn at(x: f32, y: f32, z: f32) SceneTransform
```

#### `SceneTransform.identity`

```zig
    pub fn identity() SceneTransform
```

#### `SceneTransform.matrix`

```zig
    pub fn matrix(self: SceneTransform) Matrix
```

Compose into a single 4×4 matrix. Computes math `T * R * S` - applied to a vertex, scale happens in local space first, then rotation, then translation to parent space.  This matches `drawing.zig`'s `matrixFromTransform` and the column-vector convention the renderer uses throughout; it also makes `m12/m13/m14` the genuine world translation, which `worldMatrixOf`'s callers rely on. (Was `S * R * T` pre-fix - a latent order bug surfaced by the Phase-B migration and resolved in the B4-discrepancies investigation.  See matrix-fix-plan.md.)

### `scene.Viewport`

#### `Viewport.aspect`

```zig
    pub fn aspect(self: Viewport) f32
```


Spawn the built-in shaders, materials, and skybox mesh that the renderer needs.  Entities live in the user's `Resources`; the returned struct holds refs. On partial-load failure, every successfully-spawned entity is destroyed before returning the error.  `resources` is left unchanged.


## shader_runtime.zig

#### `BoundShader`

```zig
pub fn BoundShader(comptime Iface: type) type
```

Phantom-typed wrapper providing type-checked uniform setting for a shader described by `Iface`.  Constructed via `bind(Iface, sh)`.  All methods are comptime-dispatched against `Iface.Uniforms`. Mistakes are caught at compile time:  ```zig pbr.set(.metallic_factor, 0.8);     // ok pbr.set(.metallic_facter, 0.8);     // compile error: no such field pbr.set(.view_pos, "string");       // compile error: type mismatch ```

#### `LoadedShader`

```zig
pub fn LoadedShader(comptime Iface: type) type
```

Result type for `loadShaderWithUbo`.  Bundles the engine `Shader` handle (used for draw calls) with the typed `UniformBuffer(Iface.Ubo)` (used for per-frame uniform pushes).

#### `bind`

```zig
pub fn bind(comptime Iface: type, sh: Shader) BoundShader(Iface)
```

Wrap a `Shader` with a phantom interface type for type-checked uniform operations.  The returned `BoundShader(Iface)` provides `.set(.field, value)` and `.upload(uniforms)` that are checked against `Iface.Uniforms` at compile time.  Zero runtime cost.  The wrapper holds only the underlying `Shader` value; the type parameter is purely a comptime tag.

#### `loadShader`

```zig
pub fn loadShader(
    comptime Iface: type,
    gl: *GlState,
    gpa: Allocator,
    vs_src: []const u8,
    fs_src: []const u8,
) !Shader
```

Load and link a shader program described by `Iface`.  Performs the underlying GL setup (compile, link, locate uniforms) via the existing `loadShaderFromMemory` path, then runs schema-driven one-time setup:  1. Sampler bindings — walks `Iface.Samplers` (if present) and calls `glUniform1i` for each sampler uniform to bind it to the declared texture-unit slot.  Done once at load time; the shader's `metallic_roughness` uniform sampler stays bound to slot 1 (or wherever the schema put it) for the lifetime of the program.  This replaces today's hardcoded friendly-name fallback in `loadShaderFromMemory` (which we shipped this week as a stopgap).  Returns an UNTYPED `Shader` — the interface is consumed for setup only.  To USE the shader for typed uniform operations, wrap it via `bind(Iface, sh)` at the call site.  In debug builds, after Phase 6 ships, this also runs the active-uniform validation check against the schema.

→ **raylib:** `LoadShader` — Load shader from files and bind default locations

#### `loadShaderWithUbo`

```zig
pub fn loadShaderWithUbo(
    comptime Iface: type,
    gl: *GlState,
    gpa: Allocator,
    vs_src: []const u8,
    fs_src: []const u8,
    initial_ubo: Iface.Ubo,
    binding_point: u32,
) !LoadedShader(Iface)
```

Compose `loadShader` + `UniformBuffer.create` + `UniformBuffer.attach` into a single entry point for shaders that use the typed-UBO pattern (mandelbrot, shader_uniforms, etc.).  The caller passes the initial UBO value and binding point; we return both the shader and the pre-attached buffer.  Without this helper, the caller has to: 1. `loadShader(ShaderIO, ...)` — samplers auto-bound. 2. `UniformBuffer(ShaderIO.Ubo).create(initial, binding_point)`. 3. `ub.attach(shader.id)` — match the program's UBO block by laid-out size against `@sizeOf(ShaderIO.Ubo)`.  The match-by-size attach means there's no GLSL block-name string to keep in sync.  If the shader has exactly one UBO (the case for every typed shader in zimr today) the block is unambiguous; multi-UBO shaders would need an explicit disambiguator.  Requires the shader_io to declare a `Ubo` struct.  If it doesn't, this call is a `@compileError` pointing the caller at the regular `loadShader` (no-UBO path).  Returns a small `LoadedShader(ShaderIO)` struct holding `.shader` and `.ub`.  Use `.shader` for draw calls (`beginShaderMode(f.gl, loaded.shader)`) and `.ub.push(...)` per frame for uniform updates.

---

## shader_interface.zig

#### `Attr`

```zig
pub fn Attr(comptime elem: ElemKind, comptime loc: u32) type
```

Returns a marker type describing a vertex attribute: its GLSL element type and its `layout(location = N)` annotation.  ```zig pub const Attributes = struct { vertex_position: zimr.shader.Attr(.vec3, 0), vertex_tex_coord: zimr.shader.Attr(.vec2, 1), vertex_normal: zimr.shader.Attr(.vec3, 2), vertex_tangent: zimr.shader.Attr(.vec4, 4), }; ```  Schemas describe BINDINGS, not engine policy.  Whether the engine auto-generates missing tangents is a separate concern handled in `mesh_prep.zig` by field-name convention — see plan.md §"Engine policy: schema-driven auto-fulfillment of missing mesh attributes".

#### `Sampler2D`

```zig
pub fn Sampler2D(comptime tag: MaterialMapIndex) type
```

Returns a marker type for a 2D texture sampler bound to the given material-map slot.  Use as a FIELD TYPE in a shader's `Samplers` struct, never as a value:  ```zig pub const Samplers = struct { base_color: zimr.shader.Sampler2D(.albedo), metallic_roughness: zimr.shader.Sampler2D(.metalness), }; ```  The returned type carries the slot number as a comptime constant, readable via `@field(SamplerT, "slot")`.  Engine `loadShader` walks the schema's `Samplers` struct and binds each sampler uniform to its declared slot at shader-load time.  For samplers not tied to a `MaterialMapIndex` (e.g. shadow maps, secondary render targets), use `Sampler2D_atSlot(N)` instead.

#### `Sampler2D_atSlot`

```zig
pub fn Sampler2D_atSlot(comptime n: u32) type
```

Marker type for a sampler bound to an explicit texture-unit slot, not a material-map index.  Used for samplers that the engine binds outside the material-map loop (shadow maps live at slot 8 by convention, render-target inputs typically at slot 10+, etc.).

#### `isReservedName`

```zig
pub fn isReservedName(comptime name: []const u8) bool
```

Comptime check: is the given name in the reserved set?

#### `merge`

```zig
pub fn merge(comptime structs: anytype) type
```

Comptime helper that takes a tuple of struct types and produces a new struct type with all their fields combined.  Default values from the source structs flow through.  Field-name collisions across source structs are a compile error.  Used to compose common uniform sets into per-shader `Uniforms` structs without re-declaring shared fields in every shader:  ```zig const lighting = @import("../common/lighting.zig"); const fog = @import("../common/fog.zig");  pub const Uniforms = zimr.shader.merge(.{ lighting.Lighting, fog.Fog, struct { metallic_factor: f32 = 1.0, roughness_factor: f32 = 1.0, }, }); ```  Implementation: walk each source struct's fields, collect them into a single flat list, check for name collisions, build a new struct via `@Struct` (Zig 0.16's type-construction builtin).

## uniform_buffer.zig

#### `UniformBuffer`

```zig
pub fn UniformBuffer(comptime T: type) type
```

Returns a UBO-wrapper type for the uniform struct `T`.  `T` must be an `extern struct` whose total size is a multiple of 16 bytes (std140 requirement).  Both checks fire at comptime.

## rlsw_shader.zig

#### `VertexOut`

```zig
pub fn VertexOut(comptime ShaderModule: type) type
```

A single vertex's transformed Out + interpolation context. The caller indexes into the array by vertex ID.

#### `dispatchFragmentShader`

```zig
pub fn dispatchFragmentShader(
    ctx: *Context,
    comptime ShaderModule: type,
    base_io: ShaderModule.Io,
    rect: Rect,
) void
```

Dispatch a fragment shader over every pixel in `rect`.  `ShaderModule` is comptime — typically the result of `@import( "mandelbrot_fs.zig")`.  It must declare `pub const Io = struct { ... }`, `pub const Out = struct { out_color: Vec4, ... }`, and `pub fn shaderMain(io: Io) Out`.  Codegen emits the `Io`/`Out` types into `io.zig`, and the shader source defines `shaderMain` — so any shader on the new shape is dispatch-ready for free.  `base_io` is the template Io.  The dispatcher overrides `frag_tex_coord` per-pixel (computed from the rect-relative position); every other field is copied verbatim.  Typical usage: - Set `base_io.u` to the UBO struct you'd push to the GPU. - Leave `base_io.frag_tex_coord = undefined` — it'll be overwritten. - Set `base_io.frag_color = .{1, 1, 1, 1}` (engine default).  Pixel coordinates: `frag_tex_coord` is normalized to [0, 1] over the rect's bounds, matching the GPU pipeline's varying behavior when a fullscreen quad spans the rect's UVs.  For mandelbrot specifically: `Ubo.resolution` should be set to `(rect.w, rect.h)` so the screen-to-complex math produces the same view as the GPU side.  Writes go to the rlsw context's active color attachment via `colorBufferBytesMut`.  Rect is clipped to the framebuffer; out- of-bounds pixels are dropped (not wrapped).  No depth test, no blending — overwrites whatever's there.

#### `dispatchVertexShader`

```zig
pub fn dispatchVertexShader(
    comptime ShaderModule: type,
    vertex_outs: []ShaderModule.Out,
    base_io: ShaderModule.Io,
    vertex_count: u32,
    comptime fillAttrs: fn (u32, *ShaderModule.Io) void,
) void
```

Run a VS over every vertex of an indexed mesh.  Writes results into `vertex_outs` (caller-allocated, length == vertex_count).  `vertex_attrs_fn` is a comptime callback that produces the per-vertex Io.  Lets the caller decide how to source attributes (interleaved buffer, separate streams, generated procedurally, etc.) without forcing a single layout.  Pattern: ```zig var outs: [vertex_count]ShaderModule.Out = undefined; dispatchVertexShader(ShaderModule, &outs, base_io, vertex_count, struct { pub fn attrs(vid: u32, io: *ShaderModule.Io) void { io.vertex_position = positions[vid]; io.vertex_tex_coord = uvs[vid]; } }.attrs); ```

#### `rasterizeTriangles`

```zig
pub fn rasterizeTriangles(
    comptime VsModule: type,
    comptime FsModule: type,
    ctx: *Context,
    vertex_outs: []const VsModule.Out,
    indices: []const u32,
    base_fs_io: FsModule.Io,
    comptime connect: fn (VsModule.Out, *FsModule.Io) void,
) void
```

Rasterize an indexed triangle mesh.  `vertex_outs` comes from dispatchVertexShader; `indices` references it.  For each triangle, the rasterizer: 1. Performs clip-space → NDC → screen-space division. 2. Computes triangle bounding box, clips to viewport. 3. For each pixel in the box, computes barycentric coords via edge functions. 4. If all three are non-negative (inside the triangle), perspective-correctly interpolates the varyings and calls the FS.  `connect(vs_out)` builds the FS Io from a single interpolated VS Out — caller-supplied so the FS can have a different Io shape than the VS Out (e.g. additional uniforms not in the VS).

---

#### `angle2`

```zig
pub fn angle2(v0: Vec, v1: Vec) f32
```

Signed angle (radians) from `v0` to `v1` in the 2D plane (lanes 0,1), in `(-pi, pi]`.  Positive is counter-clockwise.  This is `atan2(cross2, dot2)` - inherently a plain scalar, so unlike the reductions above it returns `f32`, not a splatted `F32x4`.

#### `angle3`

```zig
pub fn angle3(v0: Vec, v1: Vec) f32
```

Unsigned angle (radians) between two 3D vectors (lanes 0,1,2), in `[0, pi]`.  `atan2(|cross|, dot)` - more robust than `acos(dot)` for near-parallel and near-antiparallel inputs.  Returns `f32` (an angle is inherently scalar).


Approximate-equal predicate.  Polymorphic over scalar (`f32`, `comptime_float`, ...) and vector (`Vec`, `Vec2`, `F32x8`, `F32x16`).  For vectors, returns true only if every lane is within `eps`.  Returns `bool` so it composes in `or`/`and` (use `expectVecApproxEqAbs` for assertion-style throws).

#### `floatEqualsEps`

```zig
pub fn floatEqualsEps(x: f32, y: f32, eps: f32) bool
```

Epsilon float compare with a magnitude-relative tolerance.  Returns `true` when `x` and `y` are within `eps * max(1, |x|, |y|)` of each other - i.e. the tolerance grows with the operands so it stays meaningful for large values.  (zimr's old raylib-derived `floatEquals` returned `i32` 0/1; the zmath-style version returns `bool` - callers use it in `if`/`and` directly.)

#### `floatToHalf`

```zig
pub fn floatToHalf(x: f32) u16
```

`f32` -> IEEE-754 half (`f16`) bit pattern.  Zig has native `f16`, so this is just a narrowing cast plus a bitcast - but it is wrapped so call sites that move 16-bit float data (vertex attributes, compact textures) read as intent rather than as a cast soup.

#### `lineAngle2`

```zig
pub fn lineAngle2(start: Vec, end: Vec) f32
```

Angle (radians) of the directed line segment `start -> end`, measured from the +x axis, counter-clockwise positive, in `(-pi, pi]`. NOTE: raylib's `Vector2LineAngle` negates the `atan2` result, making *its* angle increase clockwise - raylib's own source carries a TODO questioning that.  zimr does NOT keep that: every other angle in this library (`angle2`, `rotate2`, the matrix rotations) is counter-clockwise positive, and a 2D library with one function rotating the other way is a footgun.  Code ported from raylib that relied on the old sign must negate the result.

#### `luminance8`

```zig

```

Perceptual luminance of an 8-bit RGB triple, returned 8-bit. Integer fixed-point form of the Rec.601 weights (77, 150, 29 - these sum to 256, so the `>> 8` is an exact average-by-256).  Kept as a separate function from `luminance` because the all-integer path avoids float round-trips for the very common u8-colour case.

#### `mulMat`

```zig
pub fn mulMat(a: Mat, b: Mat) Mat
```

Compose two matrices: `A * B`.  Applied to a vector `v` later, `mulMatVec(mulMat(A, B), v)` equals `mulMatVec(A, mulMatVec(B, v))` — B is applied to v first, then A.  The mental model is the same as GLSL: build MVP as `mulMat(proj, mulMat(view, model))` and the resulting matrix transforms model-space → clip-space when multiplied against a position.

#### `mulMatPoint`

```zig
pub fn mulMatPoint(m: Mat, p: Vec3) Vec
```

Transform a 3D point through a mat4 with implied `w = 1` — the standard "transform a position" operation, matching GLSL's `M * vec4(p, 1.0)`.  Returns a vec4; caller divides by `.w` for the perspective divide if needed.

#### `mulMatScalar`

```zig
pub fn mulMatScalar(m: Mat, s: f32) Mat
```

Multiply matrix `M` by scalar `s` (componentwise on every element of every column).  Renamed from the `(Mat, f32)` branch of the legacy polymorphic `mul`.

#### `mulMatVec`

```zig
pub fn mulMatVec(m: Mat, v: Vec) Vec
```

Multiply matrix `M` by column vector `v`: `M * v`.  This is the GLSL `vec4 result = M * v;` operation — same arithmetic, just named explicitly because Zig has no operator overloading.  Internally identical to the legacy private `vecMulMat(v, M)`: the column-major M*v computation equals the row-major v*M computation when the same bytes are interpreted under the dual conventions (commutative scalar mul + matching indexed reads).  See `src/notes/math_unification.md`.

### `zimrmath.OrthoBasis3`

#### `OrthoBasis3.barycenter3`

```zig
pub fn barycenter3(
    p: Vec,
    a: Vec,
    b: Vec,
    c: Vec,
) Vec
```

Barycentric coordinates of point `p` with respect to triangle `(a, b, c)`, all in 3D (lanes 0,1,2).  Returns `(u, v, w)` in lanes 0,1,2 with `u + v + w == 1`; `p == u*a + v*b + w*c`.

#### `OrthoBasis3.matrixCompose`

```zig
pub fn matrixCompose(
    t: Vec,
    rotation: Quat,
    scale: Vec,
) Mat
```

Build a transform matrix from translation, rotation, and scale — the standard TRS composition.  Under the column-major M*v convention, the composition that applies "scale, then rotate, then translate" to a point is `mulMat(T, mulMat(R, S))`. Provided as one call because "compose a transform" is a frequent intent.  `scale` and `t` (translation) use lanes 0,1,2.

→ **raylib:** `MatrixCompose`

#### `OrthoBasis3.matrixFrustum`

```zig
pub fn matrixFrustum(
    left: f32,
    right: f32,
    bottom: f32,
    top: f32,
    near: f32,
    far: f32,
) Mat
```

General perspective frustum matrix from the six clip-plane coordinates (right-handed, looking down -z).  Uses the OpenGL `[-1, 1]` clip-space depth convention - this is raylib's `MatrixFrustum`, and it pairs with zmath's `perspectiveFovRhGl` / `orthographic*Gl` builders, NOT the `[0, 1]`-depth `perspectiveFovRh`. For a symmetric frustum prefer `perspectiveFovRhGl`.

→ **raylib:** `MatrixFrustum`

#### `OrthoBasis3.orthoNormalize3`

```zig
pub fn orthoNormalize3(v1: Vec, v2: Vec) OrthoBasis3
```

Gram-Schmidt-style orthonormalization of two 3D vectors.  Returns `tangent` = normalized `v1`, and `bitangent` = a unit vector orthogonal to `tangent` lying in the `v1`/`v2` plane.  Mirrors raylib's `Vector3OrthoNormalize`, but value-in / value-out (no in-place pointer mutation - the zmath style).

#### `OrthoBasis3.rotateByAxisAngle3`

```zig
pub fn rotateByAxisAngle3(
    v: Vec,
    axis: Vec,
    angle: f32,
) Vec
```

Rotate a 3D vector (lanes 0,1,2) around an arbitrary axis by `angle` radians, via the Euler-Rodrigues formula.  `axis` need not be normalized - it is normalized internally.  This is the direct vector rotation; for rotating many vectors by the same rotation, build a `Mat` (`matFromAxisAngle`) or `Quat` once instead.

#### `OrthoBasis3.unproject3`

```zig
pub fn unproject3(
    source: Vec,
    projection: Mat,
    view: Mat,
) Vec
```

Unproject a point from normalized device / screen space back to world space, given the projection and view matrices.  `source` is the NDC point (lanes 0,1,2); the result is the world-space point (lanes 0,1,2, perspective-divided). Under the column-major M*v convention, the world→clip matrix is `mulMat(projection, view)` (projection applied last; pre-Stage-2 this was `mul(view, projection)` under row-major v*M).

### `zimrmath.OrthoBasis3.TRS`

#### `OrthoBasis3.TRS.binding`

```zig
pub fn binding(
    comptime ptr: anytype,
    comptime set: u32,
    comptime bind: u32,
) void
```

Set the SPIR-V `DescriptorSet` + `Binding` decorations on a sampler or UBO variable.  Same call-site rule as `location`.  `set` is the descriptor set number (usually 0 for shader-local resources, 1+ for shared engine resources).  `bind` is the binding number within the set.

#### `OrthoBasis3.TRS.clamp01`

```zig
pub fn clamp01(v: f32) f32
```

`max(0, min(1, x))`.  Matches `shadermath.clamp01`.

#### `OrthoBasis3.TRS.distance`

```zig
pub fn distance(a: anytype, b: @TypeOf(a)) f32
```

Distance between two same-typed vectors.

#### `OrthoBasis3.TRS.dot`

```zig
pub fn dot(a: anytype, b: @TypeOf(a)) f32
```

Generic dot product over `@Vector(N, f32)`.  Use the arity-suffixed `dot2`/`dot3`/`dot4` for SIMD-hot CPU code; use this for general code and anything intended to read identically on the GPU side.

#### `OrthoBasis3.TRS.fract`

```zig
pub fn fract(v: f32) f32
```

`x - floor(x)`.  Range `[0, 1)` for positive inputs.  Matches `shadermath.fract`.

#### `OrthoBasis3.TRS.length`

```zig
pub fn length(v: anytype) f32
```

Vector length: `sqrt(dot(v, v))`.

#### `OrthoBasis3.TRS.location`

```zig
pub fn location(comptime ptr: anytype, comptime n: u32) void
```

Set the SPIR-V `Location` decoration on a stage input/output variable.  Called with comptime-known args; the body emits `OpDecorate %target Location N` and gets stripped during dead- code elimination.  `n` is the location number (0-based).  For vertex attributes, match the raylib reserved layout: position=0, texcoord=1, normal=2, color=3, tangent=4, texcoord2=5.

#### `OrthoBasis3.TRS.matrixDecompose`

```zig
pub fn matrixDecompose(m: Mat) TRS
```

Decompose an affine transform matrix into translation, rotation, and scale (the inverse of `matrixCompose`).  Handles non-uniform scale and shear by Gram-Schmidt orthonormalizing the basis; the sign of the determinant is folded into the scale so the extracted rotation is a proper rotation (no reflection). zmath's `Mat` is row-major, so the transform's basis vectors are rows 0,1,2 and the translation is row 3 - this reads those directly.  (raylib's `MatrixDecompose` is written for a column-major matrix and famously extracts the wrong vectors when ported naively; reading rows here is correct *because* the layout is row-major, not a bug.)

→ **raylib:** `MatrixDecompose`

#### `OrthoBasis3.TRS.mix`

```zig
pub fn mix(a: f32, b: f32, t: f32) f32
```

Linear interpolation, GLSL spelling.  Alias for `lerp(a, b, t)`. Matches `shadermath.mix`.

#### `OrthoBasis3.TRS.normalize`

```zig
pub fn normalize(v: anytype) @TypeOf(v)
```

Normalize to unit length.  Returns the input type.

→ **raylib:** `Normalize`

#### `OrthoBasis3.TRS.quatCubicHermite`

```zig
pub fn quatCubicHermite(
    q1: Quat,
    tangent1: Quat,
    q2: Quat,
    tangent2: Quat,
    t: f32,
) Quat
```

Cubic Hermite spline interpolation between quaternions `q1` (t=0) and `q2` (t=1) with endpoint tangents.  Result is renormalized. raylib's `QuaternionCubicHermiteSpline`.

#### `OrthoBasis3.TRS.quatFromEulerXYZ`

```zig
pub fn quatFromEulerXYZ(e: Vec) Quat
```

Quat from Euler angles, **XYZ** order (rotate about X, then Y, then Z).  This is raylib's `quaternionFromEuler` convention. `e` holds the x,y,z angles in lanes 0,1,2.

#### `OrthoBasis3.TRS.quatFromEulerZXY`

```zig
pub fn quatFromEulerZXY(e: Vec) Quat
```

Quat from Euler angles, **ZXY** order (rotate about Z, then X, then Y).  This is zmath's `quatFromRollPitchYaw` convention. `e` holds the x,y,z angles in lanes 0,1,2.

#### `OrthoBasis3.TRS.quatFromTo`

```zig
pub fn quatFromTo(from: Vec, to: Vec) Quat
```

Shortest-arc unit quaternion that rotates direction `from` onto direction `to` (both lanes 0,1,2; neither need be normalized). raylib's `QuaternionFromVector3ToVector3`.

#### `OrthoBasis3.TRS.quatToEulerXYZ`

```zig
pub fn quatToEulerXYZ(q: Quat) Vec
```

Euler angles (x,y,z in lanes 0,1,2) from a quaternion, **XYZ** order - the inverse of `quatFromEulerXYZ`.  raylib's `quaternionToEuler` convention.

#### `OrthoBasis3.TRS.quatToEulerZXY`

```zig
pub fn quatToEulerZXY(q: Quat) Vec
```

Euler angles (x,y,z in lanes 0,1,2) from a quaternion, **ZXY** order - the inverse of `quatFromEulerZXY`.  zmath's `quatToRollPitchYaw` produces the same triple (it returns a `[3]f32`; this returns it in a `Vec` for consistency with the rest of the euler API).

#### `OrthoBasis3.TRS.smoothstep`

```zig
pub fn smoothstep(e0: f32, e1: f32, v: f32) f32
```

`0` below `e0`, `1` above `e1`, smooth Hermite interpolation between.  Matches `shadermath.smoothstep`.

#### `OrthoBasis3.TRS.step`

```zig
pub fn step(edge: f32, v: f32) f32
```

`0` if `v < edge`, `1` otherwise.  GLSL semantics.  Matches `shadermath.step`.

#### `OrthoBasis3.TRS.sw`

```zig
pub fn sw(v: anytype, comptime chars: []const u8) @Vector(len, f32)
```

General swizzle: build a new vector from named components of `v`. `chars` is a comptime string of `x`/`y`/`z`/`w` characters.  String length 2 returns `Vec2`, length 4 returns `Vec`.  (Length 3 also works on the GPU side via `shadermath.sw`; here it returns `@Vector(3, f32)` since `math.zig` has no `Vec3` alias.)  Note: the single-component swizzles `x`/`y`/`z`/`w` that `shadermath.zig` exposes do NOT exist in `math.zig` — they'd collide with the many local `const x: f32 = ...` bindings in vendored zmath code.  For shared code (a function callable from both CPU and GPU), use direct index access `v[0]` / `v[1]` / etc — idiomatic Zig, works on both sides.

## Angle units (the rad revolution)
- Radian arguments are suffixed `_rad`; degree arguments `_deg`. No bare angle args.
- The whole geometry/math/draw layer is radians: shapes2d arcs (`start_angle_rad`/`end_angle_rad`), wgpu_app arcs, ui DrawList `addArc`/`addNgon` (`rotation_rad`), drawRectanglePro/drawTexturePro/drawPoly (`rotation_rad`), zimrmath constructors (`rotationX(angle_rad)`, `matFromAxisAngle(axis, angle_rad)`, `matFromRollPitchYaw(pitch_rad, yaw_rad, roll_rad)`, `quatFromElAz(elevation_rad, azimuth_rad)`, `rotate2`, `modAngle32`), updateCameraPro pitch/yaw/roll, Camera2D.rotation.
- Convert at the UI edge with the zm helpers `radFromDeg` / `degFromRad`. Clean turns read as `pi/4`, `pi/2`, `pi`, `tau` (a full turn is `tau`, never `radFromDeg(360)`).
- Custom `DEG2RAD`/`deg2rad`/`radToDeg`-style conversions are BANNED by the linter (`custom-degrad`) - use the zm helpers.
- Deliberate degree exceptions, suffixed `_deg`: Camera3D `fovy_deg`, plot3d `elevation_deg`/`azimuth_deg`, gesture `angle_deg` (raylib-compat), text2d/raster GL-rotate primitives (`angle_deg`, radian public API converts at the boundary). HSV hue stays 0-360 (color convention). Box2D joint `target_angle`/`lower_angle`/`upper_angle` are radians but keep Box2D names (parity) - FLAGGED.
